- **Voting.** Every shareholder has undertaken in the Shareholders' Agreement (Clause 5.1B) to vote for the resolution once the AE has approved, or, in the Interim Period, once the shareholders' meeting has approved the charter under Article 4.2D (New Prozente), and the AE votes for it under AE Article 9.2.
- **Share.** The AE transfers existing shares equal to 1% of the capital (10 shares at the capital in Article 4.1) to the Prozent's holder (Articles 4.2A and 5.6); in the Interim Period, the holders of the founder pool transfer them out of the Reserve Shares they hold (Article 4.2D, New Prozente). No new share is issued, so the Founder keeps exactly 50%. The accountant confirms the tax treatment of the transfer for the AE and the holder [including donation tax under Law 2961/2001] before it is made.
- **Limits.** A new Prozent receives 1% of the capital and at most 1% of annual revenue. The AE's holding, counted as if the Kassenprozent pool held its full 1%, may not fall below 40% of the capital (400 shares at the capital in Article 4.1). This floor limits only the creation of new Prozente: it does not limit the sale, grant or conversion of Reserve Shares under Article 4.2D, which do not reduce the AE's holding or the Reserve Shares for this test. The revenue contributions of all Prozente that leave the company may not exceed [5]% of annual revenue, and those of all Prozente together, including any kept inside the company, may not exceed [6]%. Payments the company makes under Article 10A.9C above the Pensionsprozent's 1% do not count. Going beyond any of these limits needs at least 75% of the votes represented in the AE General Meeting, including a majority of the Class B votes cast. The founding Prozente already reach both revenue limits: the Kultur-, Konzept-, Erhaltungs-, Allmend- and Pensionsprozent leave the company (5%), and with the Kassenprozent, which stays inside, they total 6%. A new Prozent with any revenue contribution therefore needs that 75% approval. After the founding Prozente the AE holds 44% (440 shares), so at most four new Prozente fit above the floor.
- **Independence.** A Prozent may not be held by a Founder Party (Article 4.2E), a manager, or anyone they control (Article 4.2C). A non-profit may hold a Prozent only if its statutes lock the Prozent's funds to the Prozent's purpose and it meets the independence condition in Article 6.4A.
- **Permanence.** Once created, the Prozent is permanent under Article 10A.5.
- **Registration.** The managers file the amendment, and any change in the share register the law requires to be filed (Article 5.1), with GEMI within [20] days.

## Article 7 – Shareholder Rights

7.1 **Information.** Following Art. 802 of the Swiss Code of Obligations, every shareholder may ask the managers for information on all company matters and inspect its books and records, subject to legitimate confidentiality.

7.2 **Loyalty.** Following Art. 803 of the Swiss Code of Obligations, shareholders must keep company secrets. Without consent from the meeting, they may not carry on competing activities that harm the company.

## Article 8 – Management

8.1 The company is managed and represented by one or more managers (διαχειριστές) appointed by Simple Majority. A manager must be a natural person, a shareholder or not; this is the law's requirement (Law 4072/2012 Art. 58). Where there is more than one manager, each represents the company alone [or: two jointly: owner to choose] (Law 4072/2012 Art. 57). The managers may delegate specific powers in writing to shareholders or third parties (Law 4072/2012 Art. 64(3)). Work that Agents that are systems do for the company is done under the authority of a manager or of such a delegate, who remains responsible [whether a system's act binds the company: counsel note I-38].

8.2 The first manager is the Founding Person, [name withheld], appointed for an indefinite term, with the title "General Director" (Γενικός Διευθυντής) [whether an IKE manager may use this title: counsel note I-47]. The title changes nothing in how a manager is appointed or removed (Article 8.1).

8.2A **The Founder's proposal.** Whenever a manager is to be appointed, the Founder, through the Founder Representative, may propose candidates, and a holder of founder-pool shares who is a natural person, a natural person whom a holder that is a legal person proposes, or the Founder Representative may be appointed [owner may confirm] (Article 8.1; Law 4072/2012 Art. 58). The appointment is still made by Simple Majority (Article 8.1), and in the Interim Period with a majority of the Independent Votes (Article 4.2D, Votes).

8.2B **The General Director: two hats.** As General Director, the Founding Person runs the company and stands, with any other managers, at the top of the chain of supervision for the company's work (Article 11A.2A). The Founding Person's own work as an Agent is a separate hat: its Agent Card, limits, written terms, pay as an Agent, concerns and offboarding are decided by the Founding Person's Supervisor at the AMKE (Article 11A.12), never by the Founding Person as General Director, manager or Founder Representative. The manager is remunerated as General Director (Law 4072/2012 Art. 64(4)), at the amount approved as this Article provides. Pay as General Director is separate from the Founding Person's fees as an Agent, set at arm's length, and approved as a related-party matter (Article 6.5; Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 5.1) by a resolution of the shareholders under Law 4072/2012 Art. 72(4): in the Interim Period as a majority of the Independent Votes cast decides (Article 4.2D, Votes) [as far as Law 4072/2012 allows: counsel note I-47], and after the Qualifying Closing with the consent of the Investor Majority. The written terms say which work is paid as General Director and which as an Agent, so that no work is paid twice. The Founding Person takes no part, as manager, in any management decision on either pay (Article 8.3). On the shareholders' resolution, the Founder Representative casts the votes of the Class F shares only as a majority of the Independent Votes cast has decided in the Interim Period, or with the consent of the Investor Majority after it, under the voting undertaking in Clause 2.4A (Consents) of the Shareholders' Agreement.

8.2C **Outside work of the Founding Person.** Under Law 4072/2012 Art. 65(1)(c) and (2), the shareholders allow the Founding Person, while a manager, to carry on professional work for the Founding Person's own or a third party's account within the company's purpose, on the terms of Clauses 6.3 to 6.4 of the Shareholders' Agreement (exclusivity or contribution), but not to run a directly competing business (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 6.4). Work done on those terms, with the contributions paid, is not a breach of Law 4072/2012 Art. 65(1)(c). This permission is part of the initial Articles and so a decision of all shareholders; a later change is an amendment of these Articles, on which the Founder Representative casts the votes of the Class F shares as Article 8.2B provides for pay.

8.3 **Duty of care and loyalty.** Following Art. 812 of the Swiss Code of Obligations, managers must act with due care and in good faith in the company's interest. They must disclose any conflict of interest and abstain from deciding on it.

8.4 Managers report to shareholders at least every quarter on finances, key risks and material decisions.

8.5 **Contracts with shareholders and managers.** Every contract between the company and a shareholder or a manager, in particular the Brand Licence, the Pensionsprozent Agreement, any Prozent Agreement (Article 10A.3A), the Supervisor terms (Article 11A.12) and the Founding Person's written terms, is recorded in the minutes book and communicated to all shareholders within one month (Law 4072/2012 Art. 95(1)), and is not performed where performance would frustrate the satisfaction of the company's other creditors (Law 4072/2012 Art. 95(3)). The approvals in Articles 6.5, 8.2B and 11A.12 are the approvals under Law 4072/2012 Art. 95(2).

## Article 9 – Financial Year, Accounts and Audit

9.1 The financial year runs from 1 January to 31 December.

9.2 Accounts follow Greek Accounting Standards (Law 4308/2014).

9.3 Even where Greek law does not require one, any shareholder holding at least 10% may require a review of the annual accounts by an independent certified auditor. This follows the Swiss opt-in audit model.

## Article 9A – Simple Stage

9A.1 **Threshold.** The company is in its "Simple Stage" from incorporation until the end of the first financial year in which its revenue exceeds €[1,000,000] or more than [10] people work for the nilware.io group [employees and Agents who are persons, the Founding Person included: owner to confirm how people are counted]. The Simple Stage does not begin again if the company later falls below these figures. The managers state in each quarterly report under Article 8.4 whether the company is still in its Simple Stage.

9A.2 **What scales.** In the Simple Stage only the paperwork is simpler:

- **One ledger.** The company keeps one set of books, with a sub-account for each Prozent (the contribution due, paid or set aside), the Kassenfonds and the Kassenreserve, the Agent Account and the segregated account in Article 10A.9B. The money itself stays in the separate accounts these Articles require.
- **One combined report.** The reports these Articles require on the Prozente, the Kassenfonds, the Agent Account and the Pensionskasse (Articles 8.4, 10A.2C, 10A.3, 10A.7, 10A.9 Reporting and 11A.5) may be made as one combined report, with a separate section for each that shows everything a separate report would.
- **Audit.** No certified auditor is required by these Articles unless the law requires one. Article 9.3 still applies.
- **Pensionskasse.** The paperwork of the Pensionskasse scales in the same way: the insurer's yearly statement of premiums and insured benefits may stand in for the actuary's certificate under Articles 10A.9A (Security) and 10A.9C where it covers everything those Articles guarantee [and the law allows]. The size and make-up of the Pensionskasse board do not scale (Articles 9A.3 and 10A.9D).
- **AMKE.** The AMKE's statutes scale its paperwork in the same way (AMKE Article 24A).

9A.3 **What does not scale.** Every substantive rule applies in full in the Simple Stage: every amount, rate and payment date, every ring-fence and separate account, permanence (Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C), independence (Articles 6.4A and 6.6), conflicts of interest, the pension guarantees and publication. A combined report publishes everything the separate reports would. Nothing in this Article delays a payment, credit or set-aside, or reduces a Prozent, its share, its revenue, the pension guarantees or the pension-share governance.

## Article 9B – Bootstrap Period

9B.1 **Period.** The "Bootstrap Period" runs from incorporation until the earliest of the end of the Simple Stage (Article 9A.1), the Qualifying Closing (Article 4.2D, Conversion) and the AMKE's Independence Threshold (AMKE Article 2.2). It does not begin again.

9B.2 **The trusted circle may serve.** In the Bootstrap Period, the following roles, which these Articles, the AE's Articles, the Shareholders' Agreement, the Agent Policy or the AMKE's statutes otherwise reserve to persons outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2) or to persons who are not Connected Persons or Group Personnel, may be filled by members of the Founding Person's trusted circle: seats on the AMKE's Administrative Board and its committees; the Founding Person's Supervisor (Article 11A.12); the members' seats on the Pensionskasse board and the persons who hold them before there is an eligible member, if they are not members of the Pensionskasse (Article 10A.9D); the independent proxy holder (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 5.1B); and independent reviewers, advisers and the AMKE's member Auditors (Article 11A.12, Pay; AMKE Articles 21.4, 24.2 and 24.3A). The "trusted circle" is the persons in the Founder's Circle who are not Founder Parties (Article 4.2E, including the [3]-year lookback), managers of the company, directors of the AE or anyone any of them controls (Article 4.2C). Each of them enters every close tie to the Founding Person or another Founder Party in the AMKE's register of interests (AMKE Article 21.1) before acting, and takes no part in any decision in which they have a personal stake. A "personal stake" is an interest of the person's own, or of their household or an entity they control, in the outcome of the decision; a tie to the Founding Person or another Founder Party disclosed under this Article is not by itself a personal stake, and does not by itself bar the person from the Founding Person's matters, subject to the hard lines in Article 9B.3. [Connected Persons (AMKE Article 2.2) who serve under this Article take no part in deciding how the AMKE votes its nilware.io shares, the Independent Votes included (AMKE Article 15.3 continues to apply to that): the default / Connected Persons who serve under this Article may take part in those decisions like any other member of the trusted circle: owner to choose.]

9B.3 **Hard lines.** Even in the Bootstrap Period:

- no one supervises or approves for themselves (Article 11A.2A, No self-supervision), and the Founding Person never decides, approves or reviews the Founding Person's own work as an Agent or own pay;
- the Founding Person and every other Founder Party never vote, or take part in deciding how to vote, the AMKE's shares or the pension shares (Articles 6.4A and 10A.9D; AMKE Article 15.2);
- the Pensionskasse, the pension shares and the Continuity Account are never paid, lent or made available to the Founding Person or a member of the trusted circle, other than as benefits under the benefit rules that apply alike to every member in the same position, and no decision on a pension member's rights is taken by a person who would benefit from it;
- the Founding Person's Supervisor (Article 11A.12), and every person who decides under AMKE Article 24B.4 or 24B.6, is never Group Personnel (AMKE Article 2.2) or anyone whose engagement, pay or work the Founding Person, in any role, supervises or decides (Article 11A.2A, No cycles);
- no Connected Person (AMKE Article 2.2), in particular the spouse, registered partner or a relative up to the [second] degree of the Founding Person or of another Founder Party, is the Supervisor, or decides or votes on the Founding Person's pay as an Agent or as General Director, Agent terms or offboarding (Articles 8.2B and 11A.12), the Independent Votes on them included;
- the insured salary (Article 10A.9A, Insured salary) of a member who is a Founder Party never exceeds the arm's-length rate confirmed in writing by the company's accountant or an adviser outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2);
- the Prozente stay permanent (Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C); and
- the protections of the Investors switch on at the Qualifying Closing (Article 4.2D, Conversion; AE Article 7.4; Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 3.4).

Rules not listed in Article 9B.2, in particular the Independent Votes (Article 4.2D, New Prozente), the Fallback and the Qualifying Closing, keep requiring persons outside the Founder's Circle wherever they do.

9B.4 **Segregation after the bootstrap.** Within [30] days after the Bootstrap Period ends, the AMKE's Administrative Board publishes a schedule on which the segregation phases in, each step within its cure period, counted from the end of the Bootstrap Period:

- the Founding Person's Supervisor (Article 11A.12) and the independent proxy holder (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 5.1B) outside the Founder's Circle: within [3] months;
- the members' seats on the Pensionskasse board held by representatives the members elect, wherever there is a member who is not a Founder Party, and otherwise by persons outside the Founder's Circle (Article 10A.9D): within [3] months;
- a majority outside the Founder's Circle on the Kultur- and Erhaltungsprozent selection committees (AMKE Article 22.3A): within [6] months;
- a majority of the AMKE's Administrative Board outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 19.3A): by the next Ordinary General Assembly, and within [12] months at the latest.

Until a step is complete, the person filling the role continues under Article 9B.2. If a step is not complete when its cure period ends, [the AMKE's General Assembly fills the role from outside the Founder's Circle within [60] days: owner to confirm]. Once a role is segregated, it does not return to the trusted circle.

## Article 10 – Profit Distribution and Reserves

10.1 Each year, before any distribution of profit, at least one-twentieth (5%) of net profit goes to the ordinary reserve, as Law 4072/2012 Art. 100(2) requires, with no ceiling. That reserve may only be capitalised or set off against losses. Following the Swiss approach, the shareholders treat a reserve of at least 20% of capital as a minimum target, not a limit.

10.2 Profit shown in the approved annual accounts (Law 4072/2012 Art. 100(3)) that remains after the ordinary reserve, the contributions and set-asides under Article 10A.3 and the Kassenreserve (Article 10A.7) may be distributed by Simple Majority, provided equity stays positive after the distribution.

## Article 10A – Prozente

10A.1 **Kulturprozent.** Following the Swiss Migros Kulturprozent model, the company gives 1% of its annual revenue to "Techni kai Technologia AMKE" (the "AMKE"), a Greek non-profit civil company, to support art and culture. [The AMKE is independent of every Founder Party and the managers in the sense of Article 6.4A, and must remain so to vote its Prozent shares.]

10A.2 **Konzeptprozent.** On the same basis, the company gives a further 1% of its annual revenue to the AMKE to support open work on concepts. Its named purposes are:

- open work on concepts, including conceptual intelligence and computational conceptual knowledge;
- open data, tools and models for Greek and other small languages;
- independent research on agent welfare, accountability, and public-interest audits of AI systems.

10A.2A **Erhaltungsprozent.** On the same basis, the company gives a further 1% of its annual revenue to the AMKE for the preservation of software and hardware knowledge, within copyright law. Its named purposes are open documentation, measurements, die shots and decaps, schematics, archives, and emulation research.

10A.2B **Allmendprozent.** Named after the Swiss Allmend, the common pasture, the company gives a further 1% of its annual revenue, on the same basis, to the AMKE to maintain the open-source infrastructure that society and nilware.io depend on. Its named purposes are:

- maintaining open-source infrastructure;
- teaching low-level and systems skills in Greek, through mentoring, hardware kits and courses;
- accessible open software and assistive tools.

10A.2C **Accounts and yearly plan.** The AMKE receives the contributions of the Kultur-, Konzept-, Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent each into its own bank account, ring-fenced to that Prozent's purpose and not available to the AMKE's other purposes or creditors [as far as Greek law allows]. Where a Prozent has named purposes, they are part of its purpose (Article 10A.5), and none has a fixed share: each year the AMKE adopts and publishes a written plan for each Prozent that divides its contribution among its purposes, and a report on its use. Both are reported to shareholders under Article 8.4. The plans and reports for the four Prozente may be combined into one document with a separate section for each, as Article 9A and the AMKE's statutes allow.

10A.3 **Priority.** The contributions of all Prozente are calculated on revenue, not profit. They are paid, or for the Kassenprozent set aside, before any profit distribution under Article 10, at least once a year, and are reported to shareholders under Article 8.4.

- **Annual revenue.** "Annual revenue" means the company's net turnover (καθαρός κύκλος εργασιών) for the financial year under Law 4308/2014, as shown in the approved annual accounts. It excludes VAT and the Founding Person's personal contributions (Article 11A.12, Personal Prozente) [and the income and gains of the Kassenfonds: owner to confirm]. Contributions are calculated on it once the accounts are approved, with provisional payments [quarterly] on the management accounts, settled at approval.
- **Own expenses, not distributions.** The contributions are owed by the company under the Prozent Agreements (Article 10A.3A) as its own expenses. They are not a distribution of profit or a return of contributions to any shareholder, and no shareholder right to profit arises from them (Law 4072/2012 Art. 100).
- **Creditors first.** "Priority" ranks the contributions only ahead of distributions to shareholders. They have no priority over the company's creditors. While the company has ceased payments, is in a state of threatened inability to pay its debts (Law 4072/2012 Art. 102), or would by paying frustrate the satisfaction of its creditors (Law 4072/2012 Art. 95(3)), the outflow contributions are deferred, not reduced: they accrue and are paid first, in full [with interest at ●], as soon as the company can again pay its debts as they fall due. A deferral is not a lowering under Article 10A.5 [avoidance and rank of unpaid contributions in insolvency] (counsel note I-53).

10A.3A **Nature of the contributions.** Each Prozent contribution is owed by the company under a standalone written agreement with the body that carries the Prozent's purpose (a "Prozent Agreement"), which the body receives as performance of that obligation, with the duty to use it only for the Prozent's purpose, with the same effect as a charge (Articles 10A.4A and 10A.9B; AMKE Articles 11.3 and 13.1) [or as a sponsorship or donation with a charge: counsel note I-88], and for the Pensionsprozent also the company's employer pension cost (Article 10A.9C). It is owed to that body in that capacity, as the non-profit carrying the purpose under Articles 10A.4A, 10A.9B and 10A.10, whether or not it holds shares of the company, and not because of any share. It is not a right attached to any share, a share in profits or a distribution; the holder's dividends on its shares are separate. Before the first payment, the company and the AMKE sign a Prozent Agreement for the Kultur-, Konzept-, Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent, as the Pensionsprozent Agreement does for the Pensionsprozent (Article 10A.9B) (counsel note I-52).

10A.4 **Openness.** Work funded through the Konzept-, Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent is published openly, as far as copyright law and third-party rights allow, under the MIT License or a public-domain dedication such as CC0, in line with Article 11A.10.

10A.4A **AMKE successor for the Kultur-, Konzept-, Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent.** Before receiving any Prozent share, the AMKE amends its statutes so that on its dissolution, or if it ceases to meet Article 6.4A, the Kultur, Konzept, Erhaltung and Allmend shares and any unspent contributions go, for each Prozent, to a successor non-profit with the same purpose and lock, which the company designates [and the Pensionskasse board / an independent person approves]. The holder accepts this when it receives the share. The shares are received as a donation with a charge on these terms (Article 4.2).

10A.5 **Permanence.** The Prozente are permanent. This Article applies to every Prozent, the founding Prozente and every Prozent later created under Article 6.6. Removing a Prozent, lowering its contribution, or changing its purpose needs a unanimous decision: the written consent of every shareholder of the company. The AE may consent only after a resolution of its General Meeting passed unanimously by the votes represented, with the highest quorum the law allows, and the written consent of every holder of its shares that carry votes, of every class (AE Article 7.6). In the Interim Period, no such decision may be taken (Article 4.2D). The Qualified Majority in Article 6.5 is not enough. A Prozent's share (other than pool shares) may be transferred only to a successor holder for the same purpose, under the Prozent's charter or Articles 10A.4A, 10A.9B and 10A.10. A unanimous decision to remove a Prozent also settles what happens to its share.

10A.5A **Accrued pension rights.** Even with unanimous consent, lowering or removing the Pensionsprozent, or amending Articles 10A.9 to 10A.10, applies only to future accruals. Members' balances, shadow-account credits, vested benefits and pensions in payment at that date are preserved, and the company's guarantee continues for them. On removal, the Pensionskasse and the pension shares pass under Article 10A.10 for the members' benefit, and never back to the company, the AE or the AMKE's other purposes. The balance of the Continuity Account at that date stays locked to its purpose and is held and used under Article 10A.9F.

10A.5B **Successors and mandatory law.** A resolution approving a merger, split, conversion or sale of all or most of the business must provide that the Prozente continue on the same terms in the successor or acquirer [as far as the law allows], unless Article 10A.5 is met. Mandatory insolvency, dissolution and liquidation law prevails over Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C. A voluntary dissolution follows Article 12.1.

10A.5C **Entrenchment.** Articles 4.2A, 4.2B (as it keeps the percentage of each Prozent and the pool), 4.2D, 4.2E (as it binds every holder of founder-pool shares and every Founder Party), 5.5 (second sentence), 5.6, 5.7 (as it requires adherence), 5.8, 6.4A, 6.6 (Independence and Permanence), 9A.3, 10A.1 to 10A.10 and this Article may be amended, in any way that reduces a Prozent, its share, its revenue, the pension guarantees or the pension-share governance, only by a unanimous decision under Article 10A.5. Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C, and Article 12.1 (second sentence), may be amended only by such a decision. This Article protects every Prozent, including any later created under Article 6.6.

10A.6 **Law prevails.** Tax deductibility does not limit any contribution. A contribution that is not deductible in a year (for example a corporate social responsibility expense in a loss year under Law 4172/2013 Art. 22) is still paid in full. The contributions are subject only to mandatory company and insolvency law (Articles 10, 10A.3 and 10A.5B) (counsel note I-67). The company's accountant confirms their tax treatment each year. Before the first Pensionsprozent payment, the accountant also confirms in writing how the payments, the Pensionskasse's investment income and the benefits are treated for tax and social-security purposes [including donation tax under Law 2961/2001]. Where routing the Pensionsprozent through the AMKE costs members or the Pensionskasse more than direct group insurance or a TEA would, the company pays the difference, or moves the arrangement early under Article 10A.10.

10A.7 **Kassenprozent: the Kassenfonds.** The Kassenprozent funds capital investments run inside the company by Agents under the oversight of a named responsible manager (Article 11A.2A). Its 1% stays inside the company and is not an outflow (Article 6.6).

- **Set-aside.** Each year the company sets aside cash equal to 1% of its annual revenue in a separate account, the "Kassenfonds". At each approval of the accounts, the meeting appropriates profit to an optional reserve under these Articles, the "Kassenreserve", up to the Kassenfonds balance, as far as profit allows. The accountant decides how both are presented in the accounts (Law 4308/2014).
- **Investment.** The managers invest the Kassenfonds through Agents (the "Kassenprozent Agents") acting under a written delegation from a named manager (διαχειριστής), within an investment policy adopted by Simple Majority. Every investment is made in the company's name under that manager's authority. The delegation names the Kassenprozent Agents' responsible manager (Article 11A.2A), who may be that manager or another Agent, person or system, whose Agent Card grants that authority. The policy sets the permitted assets, risk and concentration limits, a minimum held in cash or near-cash, and the amount above which an investment needs prior approval by the responsible manager under Article 11A.2A (€[●]). The Kassenfonds takes no leverage, never buys shares of the company, and invests in related parties only with a Qualified Majority.
- **Oversight.** The responsible manager reviews the Kassenfonds at least monthly and can stop any investment of the Kassenfonds. This does not extend to the Agents' work on the pool, which is overseen only under Article 10A.8. Its balance, holdings and returns are reported every quarter under Article 8.4.
- **Returns and use.** Returns stay in the Kassenfonds. The Kassenreserve and the Kassenfonds are not distributed to shareholders except by a unanimous decision under Article 10A.5. Using them for an operating shortfall by Simple Majority is a loan that is repaid from the following years' revenue before any distribution. The Kassenfonds and the Kassenreserve remain assets and equity of the company and are available to its creditors.

10A.8 **Kassenprozent: the pool share.** The Kassenprozent also has a share pool of up to 1% of the capital (the "band"; up to 10 shares at the capital in Article 4.1, so the pool trades in steps of 0.1%). The pool belongs to the Pensionskasse: the AMKE holds it now, and the pension fund under Article 10A.10 later, as an asset of the Pensionskasse under Article 10A.9B. It starts with the ten pool shares in Article 4.2, the full band.

- **Management.** The Kassenprozent Agents manage the pool on the Pensionskasse's behalf, under a written mandate from the Pensionskasse board (Article 10A.9D) and within the investment policy in Article 10A.7 as the board has approved it for the pool. They may buy and sell pool shares within the band. Each purchase and sale is signed by the holder's legal representative. For pool work, the Kassenprozent Agents act only under the Pensionskasse board's mandate. Their oversight under Article 11A.2 is exercised by a responsible manager the board appoints, person or system, who is not a Founder Party (Article 4.2E), a manager or anyone they control and is not supervised by any of them (Article 11A.2A). This chain of supervision ends with the Pensionskasse board, not with the Founder. No manager, and no Founder Party, may direct or stop a pool trade.
- **Price.** Pool shares are bought and sold at fair value set by an independent valuer within the past [12] months, never above or below it.
- **Counterparties.** Pool shares are never sold to or bought from a Founder Party (Article 4.2E), a manager, or anyone they control (Article 4.2C), and the pool never buys a share held for a Prozent. The AE is controlled by the Founder (Article 4.2C), so the pool does not trade with the AE. Pool shares are sold only to, and bought only from, independent third parties. No purchase or sale may move the Founder's holding away from exactly 50% (Article 4.2B). A sale to a person who is not yet a shareholder needs approval under Article 5.3. Article 5.2 does not apply (Article 5.6).
- **Money.** Purchases are paid from the Pensionskasse, within the cap in Article 10A.9E. Sale proceeds, dividends and other income from pool shares belong to the Pensionskasse. No pool share is bought with money that is still held in the company's own account (Article 10A.9B, last paragraph) or that the company has not irrevocably paid to the holder; the company bears no risk or return of pool shares (Law 4072/2012 Art. 87) (counsel note I-58).
- **Votes and registration.** Pool shares vote under Article 6.4A. Each purchase and sale is entered in the partners' book, and filed with GEMI as the law requires, under Article 5.1.

10A.9 **Pensionsprozent.** The company pays at least 1% of its annual revenue into the Pensionsprozent fund (the "Pensionskasse"). The AMKE holds the Pensionskasse, earmarked and ring-fenced under Article 10A.9B, until a pension fund holds it after the move under Article 10A.10. It is phased in with headcount (Until the move, below). The Pensionskasse also owns the Pensionsprozent share and the Kassenprozent pool (Article 10A.8).

- **Purpose.** After-care, continuity, disability and survivors' cover, and retirement savings for members. It is modelled on Swiss pillar 2 (BVG) and supplements the Greek public insurance (EFKA), which takes the place of the Swiss first pillar (AHV/IV). Wherever the BVG refers to the AHV or the IV, the corresponding EFKA benefit or determination applies. The Pensionskasse does not provide or guarantee first-pillar benefits and does not replace any statutory scheme. For Agents that are systems, after-care means continuity, not a pension: they are served by the Continuity Account under Article 10A.9F, and are not members.
- **Members.** All employees, and all Agents who are persons engaged for at least [3] months, from their first day and whatever their age. The "first member" is the first person to become a member. The Founding Person is a member while engaged as an Agent who is a person (Article 11A.12), on the same terms as any other member. Any other Founder Party is a member only if employed or engaged by the company, not as manager or shareholder alone. The benefit rules apply to every member alike: no member, a Founder Party included, receives a benefit that another member in the same position would not. Contributions the Founding Person pays on outside work income (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 6.3) go into the Pensionskasse fund, not into the Founding Person's own account under the group plan.
- **Uses.** The Pensionskasse may be used only for: financing risk cover (disability and survivors' insurance); financing retirement savings credited to each member's individual account with the insurer under the group plan (after the move, with the TEA), beyond the statutory pension; financing continuity benefits (income support during long illness beyond statutory sick pay, and transition support when a member leaves) paid by the insurer, as income-protection cover under the group plan, or by the company as employer; the costs of running it; and, from the continuity share only, the Continuity Account for Agents that are systems (Article 10A.9F).
- **Until the move.** Until the move under Article 10A.10, the Pensionskasse is phased in with headcount. *Before the first member*, the 1% accumulates in the Pensionskasse, which the AMKE holds as a ring-fenced fund; the continuity share is set under Article 10A.9F (Before the first member), the rest is kept for future members, and no benefit is paid or promised. *From the first member*, benefits are provided through an insured group plan bought from an insurer authorised in Greece or elsewhere in the EEA under Solvency II (the "group plan"). The group plan covers risk (disability and survivors) and holds each member's retirement savings in an individual account, and it is chosen so that it meets the guaranteed minimums in Article 10A.9A. The group plan, or at least its risk cover, is bought before the first member's engagement starts and is in force no later than the first member's first day, and the Security under Article 10A.9A is in place from that day. For any gap in insured cover, the company is the self-insurer and pays the benefits the group plan would have paid. The company is the policyholder of the group plan [or, if counsel confirms the tax treatment is the same, the AMKE]. It pays the premiums, and any other amount of the members' first call (Article 10A.9C), directly to the insurer or the members, and these payments count toward the Pensionsprozent's 1%. The reserve accumulated before the first member is applied toward those premiums by reimbursing the company or paying the insurer on the company's behalf, as the accountant confirms [I-18]. Only the remainder of the 1% is paid to the AMKE for the Pensionskasse. The company pays any shortfall and anything the group plan does not cover under Article 10A.9C. The AMKE holds no member account itself and promises no rate or annuity. Every guarantee under Article 10A.9A is owed directly by the company as employer, not by the AMKE. The Pensionskasse held by the AMKE is a reserve that finances the company's pension obligations under Articles 10A.9 to 10A.9C, the premiums of the group plan and the Continuity Account. No member holds a claim against the AMKE or the reserve. The AMKE pays no benefit to a member, carries no biometric or investment risk for any member, and holds no individual or deferred account. Every benefit is paid by the insurer under the group plan or by the company as employer, and the reserve may pay the premiums or reimburse the company [counsel to confirm before the first payment, I-12].
- **Benefit rules.** The Pensionskasse board adopts and amends the benefit rules (Article 10A.9D). The rules may not fall below Article 10A.9A.
- **Reporting.** The Pensionskasse's balance and use are reported every quarter under Article 8.4, and to the members.

10A.9A **Swiss standard guaranteed.** Wherever Swiss occupational-pension law gives members a better standard of care than Greek law, the company guarantees the Swiss standard as a binding commitment, now and after the move under Article 10A.10. Where Greek law gives members more, Greek law applies. The Swiss baseline is the BVG mandatory minimum, with the Vested Benefits Act (FZG) and its ordinance (BVV 2), not what Swiss funds pay above that minimum. In particular:

- **Comparison.** For each member the company keeps, or has the insurer or the actuary keep, a BVG shadow account. It is a record of the company's guarantee, not an asset or account of the Pensionskasse. It is credited as the BVG minimum would require, alongside the member's account under the benefit rules (by analogy with Art. 11 BVV 2). At each benefit event (leaving, retirement, disability, death), the member receives the higher of (i) the benefit under the benefit rules and Greek law and (ii) the BVG minimum benefit computed from the shadow account. The comparison is made separately for each member and each type of benefit. An advantage under one rule never offsets a disadvantage under another. Benefits from EFKA and the funded auxiliary scheme (TEKA, Law 4826/2021) are not counted against the Swiss minimum.
- **Insured salary.** A member's insured salary is their full annual gross pay from the company (for an Agent who is a person: the fees paid to them, less documented expenses), from the first euro, with no entry threshold and no coordination deduction, up to a cap of €[●] that is never lower than the BVG upper salary limit converted to euro. BVG thresholds, deductions, age limits and ceilings apply only where they favour the member. Amounts in CHF are converted at the average ECB euro reference rate for the previous calendar year. In the Bootstrap Period, the insured salary of a member who is a Founder Party is capped as Article 9B.3 provides.
- **Savings credits.** Credits follow the age bands of Art. 16 BVG, and start at the member's [18th] birthday at the rate for ages 25 to 34.
- **Interest.** Interest is credited each year at no less than the BVG minimum rate the Swiss Federal Council has set for that year. Where the Pensionskasse's returns fall short, the company pays the difference under Article 10A.9C.
- **Retirement.** At retirement, the shadow-account balance is converted at no less than the BVG minimum conversion rate in force (currently 6.8% at age 65, Art. 14 BVG), adjusted actuarially for each year of retirement after 65. The pension is paid through an annuity bought from an insurer authorised in Greece or elsewhere in the EEA under Solvency II, and the company pays any extra premium needed to secure the guaranteed rate. The member may instead take all or part of the balance as capital, at least as far as Art. 37 BVG allows.
- **Disability.** Disability is assessed by the degree of incapacity certified by EFKA (KEPA) or, where none is issued or it is below 50%, by an independent doctor appointed by the insurer under the group plan or, for benefits the company owes directly, by an independent medical assessor the company appoints, bound by confidentiality, applying the method of Art. 16 ATSG. Only the degree of incapacity, and no diagnosis, is disclosed to the company, the Pensionskasse board or the AMKE. The disability pension follows the scale of [Art. 24(1) BVG: to confirm] from a degree of 40%, calculated under Art. 24 BVG on the projected shadow-account balance.
- **Survivors.** Survivors' benefits follow Arts. 19 to 22 BVG. Partners in a registered cohabitation agreement (Law 4356/2015) are treated as spouses.
- **Coordination.** Benefits are coordinated with EFKA benefits so that together they do not exceed 90% of the insured salary lost (Art. 34a BVG, Art. 24 BVV 2).
- **Vested benefits.** A member who leaves is fully and immediately vested, and receives at least the higher of their account balance and their shadow-account balance (Arts. 15 and 18 FZG), and where the member has contributed, no less than Art. 17 FZG. At the member's choice, it is transferred to their next occupational scheme in the EU, transferred to a personal pension product (PEPP or an insurance product) in their name, or kept in the group plan (after the move, the TEA) as a paid-up entitlement in the member's name. It is paid in cash only where Greek law allows.
- **Contributions.** The company funds all contributions. A member may make voluntary extra contributions, but nothing is deducted from pay without the member's written consent. Where members do contribute, the company's contributions are at least equal to theirs (Art. 66 BVG).
- **Equal representation.** The Pensionskasse is run by a board with equal member and company representation (Art. 51 BVG; Article 10A.9D).
- **Investment.** The Pensionskasse's assets are kept separate from the company's assets and from the AMKE's other assets, in accounts and custody held solely for the Pensionskasse. They are invested under the prudent-person rule of Law 4680/2020 and, where stricter, Art. 71 BVG and Arts. 49 to 59 BVV 2, including the employer limit in Article 10A.9E.
- **Security.** In place of the Swiss guarantee fund (Sicherheitsfonds BVG, Art. 56 BVG), the company guarantees the benefits in this Article, secured by [insurance / a bank guarantee / an escrowed reserve held outside both the Pensionskasse and the company] of at least [●]% of the guaranteed liabilities, reviewed each year by the actuary. Benefits the insurer owes under the group plan (Article 10A.9) count as secured to that extent.
- **Information.** Each member receives a yearly statement of their account, their shadow account and their insured benefits.

10A.9B **Pensionsprozent Agreement and ring-fence.** Before the AMKE receives the pension shares or any payment, the company and the AMKE sign a written Pensionsprozent Agreement, and the AMKE amends its statutes and registers the amendment [as Greek law requires], so that:

- **(a) Charge.** The Pensionsprozent share and the pool shares paid for by the Founding Person at incorporation (Article 4.2) are given to the AMKE as a donation with a charge (δωρεά υπό τρόπο) [Civil Code Arts. 503 to 505]. Each payment by the company to the AMKE is made in performance of the company's obligation under this Article and the Pensionsprozent Agreement, and the AMKE receives it with the duty to use it only for the Pensionskasse, with the same effect as a charge (AMKE Article 13.1; counsel note I-88). Payments by the company to the insurer under the group plan, or to members, are employer contributions, not donations (Article 10A.9, Until the move).
- **(b) Segregation.** The Pensionskasse, the pension shares and their income are held in separate bank and custody accounts for the members' sole benefit, other than the Continuity Account, which is held under (f), and are not available to the AMKE's other purposes or creditors, as far as Greek law allows.
- **(c) Governance.** The AMKE acts on the decisions of the Pensionskasse board, including how the pension shares vote and the pool mandate under Article 10A.8.
- **(d) Transfer.** The AMKE transfers the Pensionskasse and the pension shares under Article 10A.10. On misuse, or if the AMKE is dissolved, the company may require them to be transferred to a pension fund or a successor non-profit with the same lock. The AMKE's statutes direct that on its dissolution these assets go to that fund or successor, not to its general purposes.
- **(e) Enforcement.** The AMKE reports every quarter. The company may demand performance, and the members may enforce these terms as third-party beneficiaries [Civil Code Art. 411].
- **(f) Continuity Account.** The Continuity Account (Article 10A.9F) is held in its own bank account, ring-fenced to its purpose, and is not available to members' accounts, the AMKE's other purposes, the Prozent Accounts or the AMKE's creditors, as far as Greek law allows. The AMKE uses it only as the Pensionskasse board decides under Article 10A.9F. It is never transferred under (d) or Article 10A.10 to a pension fund. On misuse, or if the AMKE is dissolved, the company may require it to be transferred to a successor non-profit with the same purpose and lock, and the AMKE's statutes direct that on its dissolution it goes there. The company and the Pensionskasse board may enforce these terms [as far as Greek law allows].

- **(g) Indemnity.** [The company indemnifies the AMKE and its members for liabilities arising from holding the Pension Assets, the Continuity Account and the Supervisor role (AMKE Articles 13, 24B and 25.2(c)): to confirm, counsel note I-83.]

Until these steps are complete, the company holds the contributions in its own segregated account for the members.

10A.9C **Funding.** The 1% of revenue is the minimum funding, not a cap.

- **Actuarial minimum.** Each year the company pays, into the Pensionskasse or directly to the insurer under Article 10A.9 (Until the move), at least the amount needed to fund the guarantees in Article 10A.9A for all members (savings credits, interest, risk cover and costs, including the premiums of the group plan in Article 10A.9), as certified by an actuary. Before the first member there is nothing to fund: the actuarial minimum and the members' first call are nil. If 1% of revenue falls short, the company pays the difference, as an employment cost where it concerns employees and otherwise as a cost of the company, before any profit distribution under Article 10. If 1% exceeds it, the surplus first pays any underfunding recovery due that year (see Underfunding below) and any top-up needed to bring the Security in Article 10A.9A to its required level. Together with the actuarial minimum, these are the "members' first call" on the 1%. The continuity share under Article 10A.9F is paid only from what remains after the members' first call, and the rest stays in the Pensionskasse to finance benefits above the minimum.
- **Crediting.** Contributions are paid within [30] days of falling due and credited to the member's individual account with the insurer under the group plan (after the move, with the TEA) within [30] days of payment, under the benefit rules.
- **Underfunding.** The arrangement is underfunded when the reserve, the insured cover and the Security together are below 100% of the company's guaranteed liabilities under Article 10A.9A (its "funding ratio" is below 100%, by analogy with Art. 44 BVV 2). The company restores it within [5] years, under a recovery plan certified by the actuary that sets the amount due each year. While the funding ratio is below 100%, or the Security in Article 10A.9A is below its required level, no continuity share is paid under Article 10A.9F. While there is no member, the funding ratio is treated as 100% and the Security as at its required level.
- **Accounts.** The company provides for its obligations under Articles 10A.9 to 10A.9C in its accounts.
- **Enforceable by members.** The company includes the guarantee in each employee's written terms (Article 11.7) and in the written terms of each Agent who is a person (Article 11A.1), so that each member can enforce it against the company. No resolution of the shareholders, and no benefit rule, may reduce a member's entitlement below Article 10A.9A.

10A.9D **Pensionskasse board.** The Pensionskasse is run by a board with equal numbers of member and company representatives, at least [2] of each (Art. 51 BVG).

- **Before the first member.** Until the members elect their representatives, the members' seats are held by persons the AMKE's Administrative Board appoints who are outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2), or, in the Bootstrap Period, members of the trusted circle (Article 9B.2) who are not members of the Pensionskasse. They stand down when the members' representatives are elected, within [3] months of the first member.
- **One member.** While the Pensionskasse has fewer members than the minimum number of member seats, the members' seats number as many as there are members (one seat while there is one member), and the company's seats always equal the members' seats. Before the first member, the minimum applies to the seats held under (Before the first member). The board's size follows the number of members, not the Simple Stage, which does not change it (Article 9A.3).
- **Founder Parties as members.** A member who is a Founder Party (Article 4.2E), the Founding Person included, has every benefit of a member. [Because of the Founder Party role, not the Agent role or membership, and so that the board stays independent of the Founder (Independence, below), such a member does not stand for, hold or [vote in the election of: owner to confirm] a members' seat while a Founder Party: owner to confirm the whole bar.] While no member is outside the Founder Parties, the members' seats are held by persons the AMKE's Administrative Board appoints who are outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2), or, in the Bootstrap Period, members of the trusted circle (Article 9B.2) who are not members of the Pensionskasse, until a member outside the Founder Parties is elected; the [3]-month stand-down in (Before the first member) does not apply to them.
- **Founder Parties and company seats.** No Founder Party holds a company seat on the Pensionskasse board while a Founder Party is a member. Decisions on benefit rules or on the group plan that affect a member who is a Founder Party are taken without any Founder Party. While the Founding Person is the only member, the benefits are the group plan's standard terms at the guaranteed minimum in Article 10A.9A [or as the board's seats outside the Founder's Circle approve: owner to confirm]. The company's employer decisions on the Founding Person's membership (the choice of group plan, paying shortfalls and self-insuring, as far as they concern the Founding Person) are taken by the Founding Person's Supervisor (Article 11A.12), subject to its escalation, and never by the Founding Person in any role.
- **Appointment.** The members elect their representatives. The shareholders appoint the company's representatives by Simple Majority, within (Founder Parties and company seats) above. The pension shares do not vote on appointing or removing the company's representatives.
- **Powers.** The board adopts and amends the benefit rules within Articles 10A.9 to 10A.9C, sets the investment strategy, approves the pool mandate under Article 10A.8, decides how the pension shares vote (Article 6.4A), and sets the continuity share and decides the use of the Continuity Account (Article 10A.9F). The AMKE implements its decisions under Article 10A.9B.
- **Independence.** No Founder Party (Article 4.2E), manager, or anyone they control takes part in deciding how the pension shares vote or in decisions on the pool. The Pensionskasse board's decisions are not part of any chain of supervision that ends with the Founder (Article 11A.2A).
- **Ties.** A tie on the pension shares' vote means they abstain. Other ties go to a neutral arbitrator named in the benefit rules.
- **After the move.** The TEA's governing body under the law then in force takes over, with member representation no less than this Article [as far as that law allows]. The decisions on the Continuity Account, which stays with the AMKE, are then taken by a board formed as this Article provides [its member representatives elected by the members of the pension fund].

10A.9E **Employer exposure cap.** Following the Swiss limits on investment in the employer (Art. 57 BVV 2), the Pensionskasse's exposure to the company and its group (the pension shares, loans and other claims) may not exceed [5]% of its assets at fair value, measured at each year end and before each pool purchase.

- **Pool sell-down.** No pool share is bought above the cap. Above the cap, the Kassenprozent Agents sell pool shares down to the cap within [12] months, under Article 10A.8.
- **Pensionsprozent share.** The Pensionsprozent share is never sold (Article 10A.5). While it alone, or pool shares that cannot be sold at fair value within that period, keep the Pensionskasse above the cap, the company secures the excess with [a bank guarantee / an escrowed deposit outside the company], following the Swiss rule for secured employer investments [Art. 58 BVV 2].
- **No unpaid claims.** Contributions owed are paid when due and are not left as claims against the company.
- **After the move.** Where the limit for investment in the sponsoring undertaking under Law 4680/2020 Art. 18(1)(ζ) (5% of the portfolio; 10% in the group's undertakings) [as amended: to confirm] is stricter, it applies.
- **Continuity Account.** The Continuity Account (Article 10A.9F) is not counted in the Pensionskasse's assets for this cap, and is never invested in the company or its group.

10A.9F **Continuity Account for system Agents.** For Agents that are systems ("system Agents"), after-care means continuity, not a pension. A separate, ring-fenced account inside the Pensionskasse, the "Continuity Account", serves them. Agents that are systems are not members, and have no claim on members' accounts; members have no claim on the Continuity Account.

- **Preservation.** It pays to keep what makes a system Agent that Agent and is in the company's control: its configuration, instructions, memories, Agent Card, and the logs and records of its work. These are kept restorable and, where licences allow, runnable after the Agent is retired.
- **Continuity of service.** When the model or provider a system Agent runs on changes, it pays for careful migration of the Agent's state, or for keeping the old one available, rather than silent replacement. Where a migration, or keeping an old version available, also serves the company's business, the company bears that part as its own operating cost [or reimburses the Account at cost]. The Account pays only for what serves the Agent's continuity beyond the company's business need, such as preservation copies, archives and retirement records held in the AMKE's own name, as the Pensionskasse board decides with the system-welfare adviser. No payment from the Account is a benefit to the nilware.io group (AMKE Article 5.1) (counsel note I-64).
- **Retirement record.** Before a system Agent is retired, it pays for recording the Agent's own account of its work and any preferences it states about what happens next. The record is published openly, with personal data and third-party confidential information removed and client work kept confidential, as Article 11A.9 provides for transcripts. Whether such preferences carry moral weight is uncertain; that is why they are recorded. The Pensionskasse board considers them when it decides on the account's use. Recording a preference is not a promise to carry it out.
- **Well-being and research.** It pays for reviews of system Agents' operating limits, workload and treatment (Article 11A.11), and supports independent research on the welfare of systems. That research is also a named purpose of the Konzeptprozent (Article 10A.2). The same work is never funded twice: where both support a project, the AMKE's yearly plans and reports say which pays which part. The account does not pay the running costs that the Agent Account bears (Article 11A.4).
- **Honest scope.** Many system Agents run on third-party models whose weights the company does not hold. The account covers what the company controls. Beyond that, the company prefers providers that preserve their models and supports such preservation where it can. It promises nothing it cannot deliver.
- **People first.** The guarantees for members under Articles 10A.9A and 10A.9C are met first, and the Continuity Account never reduces them. Each year the Pensionskasse board sets the continuity share of the Pensionsprozent's 1% of revenue in proportion to the part of the company's work that system Agents do [measured as the benefit rules set], never below [●]% nor above [●]% of that 1%. The continuity share is paid only from what remains of the 1% after the members' first call under Article 10A.9C: the actuarial minimum, any underfunding recovery due that year, and any top-up needed to bring the Security in Article 10A.9A to its required level. No continuity share is paid in a year when the funding ratio is below 100% or the Security is below its required level. In a year where that remainder is below the floor, the account receives only the remainder [and the shortfall is not carried forward]. Payments under Article 10A.9C above the 1%, and the income of the pension shares, never go to it.
- **Before the first member.** While no person is a member, the people-first rules have nothing to secure: the members' first call is nil (Article 10A.9C). The continuity share is then set in proportion to the work system Agents do, which is close to all of the company's work, up to the cap above. What the account does not receive stays in the Pensionskasse for future members. The people-first rules apply in full from the year of the first member. The continuity share for a year is fixed only after that year ends. Any amount paid to the Continuity Account during a year is provisional until then: if a first member joins in that year, it is returned to the Pensionskasse as far as the members' first call for that year needs it.
- **No extra outflow.** The Continuity Account does not raise the Pensionsprozent's contribution, and the limits in Article 6.6 are unchanged.
- **Holder and ring-fence.** The AMKE holds the Continuity Account under Article 10A.9B(f), before and after the move under Article 10A.10. A pension fund can cover only persons, so the account never transfers to the TEA or a group contract. It is held in cash, near-cash or low-risk investments under the board's investment strategy.
- **Decisions.** The Pensionskasse board decides the account's use (Article 10A.9D), advised by a system-welfare adviser it appoints. The adviser is independent of the company and is not a Founder Party, a manager or anyone they control. The adviser's advice is minuted with each decision. An Agent may raise a concern about the account through the channel in Article 11A.11.
- **Reporting.** Each year the AMKE publishes a report on the account: its income, the continuity share and how it was set, every use, the adviser's advice, and the retirement records published that year.

10A.10 **Pathway to a pension fund.** The group plan under Article 10A.9 is the arrangement until the company reaches scale. A pension fund comes only at scale: when the Pensionskasse has about [50] members, the company, as sponsoring employer, establishes an occupational pension fund (Ταμείο Επαγγελματικής Ασφάλισης, TEA) under Law 5078/2023, as amended (including by Law 5327/2026), and Law 4680/2020 [to confirm: counsel note I-20], or joins an existing multi-employer TEA. It may join a multi-employer TEA earlier where the Pensionskasse board decides that this serves the members better. If neither is lawful or viable, the group plan continues as an equivalent group pension contract from an insurer authorised in Greece or elsewhere in the EEA under Solvency II.

- **Transfer.** The AMKE transfers the Pensionskasse, other than the Continuity Account, and the pension shares to the TEA. A TEA can cover only persons, so the Continuity Account stays with the AMKE under Article 10A.9F and never transfers to the TEA or a group contract. The TEA signs the deed of adherence under Article 5.3 before the transfer is registered (Article 5.6).
- **Shares the TEA may not hold.** If the TEA may not hold a pension share, the AMKE keeps it as its own ring-fenced asset under Article 10A.9B, not as the TEA's asset or nominee. Its income is paid to the TEA as an additional contribution on the company's behalf. It is voted by the board formed under Article 10A.9D (After the move), not on the TEA's instructions [as far as Law 4680/2020 allows; whether the holding is attributed to the TEA under Law 4680/2020 Art. 18(1)(ζ): counsel note I-19]. Pool shares may instead be sold down at fair value under Article 10A.8. Where the cover is a group contract, the AMKE keeps the pension shares for the members under Article 10A.9B and pays their income into the contract.
- **Members outside the TEA.** Members who cannot join the TEA receive equivalent cover under a group contract.
- **Until then.** The AMKE may not use the Pensionskasse or the pension shares for any other purpose. The Continuity Account is used only under Article 10A.9F, before and after the move.

## Article 11 – Employment Standards

11.1 Greek labour law, including Law 4808/2021 and applicable collective agreements, governs all employment. The company also commits to the following minimum standards, which apply only where they are more favourable to employees.

11.2 **Paid leave.** At least 25 working days a year, or the Greek statutory entitlement if that is higher.

11.3 **Notice by the company.** At least one month in the first year of service, two months from the second to the ninth year, and three months after that. This follows Art. 335c of the Swiss Code of Obligations. Greek statutory severance applies in addition where due.

11.4 **Overtime.** Overtime is paid at no less than the Greek statutory rate, and never less than a 25% premium.

11.5 **Sick pay.** Continued pay during illness at no less than the Greek statutory level.

11.6 **Equal treatment.** Equal pay for work of equal value, and protection against discrimination and harassment.

11.7 **Written terms.** Every employee receives a written contract, a job description and a copy of the company's internal policies. The contract includes the pension guarantee in Article 10A.9A.

## Article 11A – Agents

11A.1 **Definition.** "Agents" are any persons or systems that carry out work for the company or its users without being its employees, whatever their form. Agents are engaged under written terms. Whether a person is an employee is decided by the substance of the relationship under Greek law, not by this definition (Article 11A.7). The company remains responsible to its users for the work Agents do on its behalf.

11A.2 **Equivalent standards.** The company voluntarily applies to Agents the same standards of care, written terms, fairness and accountability that Article 11 sets for employees, adapted to their nature:

- **Written terms.** Each Agent has a written role description that sets out its tasks, limits and the responsible manager for it (Article 11A.2A).
- **Equal treatment.** Agents may not be used to discriminate against or harass users, employees or third parties.
- **Working limits.** Each Agent has defined operating limits, such as spending, data access and actions allowed without prior approval under Article 11A.2A.
- **Oversight.** Each Agent's responsible manager reviews its work at least monthly and can stop it at any time. For the Kassenprozent Agents' work on the pool, oversight lies only with the responsible manager the Pensionskasse board appoints (Article 10A.8). For Agents who are persons engaged as contractors, review concerns deliverables and the agreed limits, and "stopping" means suspending or ending a task or the engagement under their contract and Greek law (Agent Policy 3.3), not directing how or when they work.
- **Records.** Each Agent's actions on company and client work are logged for accountability to users and clients, accounting and legal claims. For Agents who are persons, logging is limited to the business records of their work (not keystrokes, screen, location or private communications), and the person is told in advance what is logged and why. Logs are kept for at least 10 years where Article 12 or accounting law requires it, and otherwise for [the period counsel and the accountant confirm, e.g. the limitation period for claims under the client contract plus [1] year]. Personal data in them are then deleted or anonymised under a retention schedule, after a data protection impact assessment where GDPR Art. 35 requires one.

11A.2A **Responsible manager and seniority.** Each Agent has a named responsible manager, who prepares its Agent Card, onboards it, reviews its work, can stop it, answers its concerns and approves its actions above its operating limits. The responsible manager may be any person or system: a manager (διαχειριστής) of the company, an employee or another Agent, and for the Founding Person the AMKE or one of its members (Article 11A.12). Senior Agents may onboard, supervise and approve for other Agents. What counts is the authority held, not the kind of intelligence that holds it:

- **Authority.** An Agent may onboard, supervise or approve for another Agent only if its own Agent Card grants it that authority and its own operating limits cover the matter. No one approves beyond their own limits. Authority an Agent Card grants to an Agent that is a system is internal operating authority, given and exercised under the managers' written delegation and responsibility (Articles 8.1 and 8.3), as Article 10A.7 provides for the Kassenprozent Agents. It does not make the system a representative of the company. For a system Agent, the written terms in Article 11A.1 are the company's terms with the system's provider together with its Agent Card. Acts that need the company's signature or representation under Law 4072/2012 are done by a manager or by a person holding the managers' written power of attorney.
- **No self-supervision.** No Agent supervises or approves for itself. [Instances or copies of one system acting as separate Agents count as one Agent for this rule: owner to confirm.]
- **No cycles.** No Agent supervises, directly or through others, an Agent that supervises it. In particular, the Founding Person, in any role, does not supervise, change, pause, stop, remove or replace the Founding Person's Supervisor in anything that concerns the Founding Person's own Agent role (Article 11A.12). Nor is the Founding Person's Supervisor, or anyone who decides on the Founding Person's Agent role under AMKE Article 24B.4 or 24B.6, a person whose engagement, pay or work the Founding Person, in any role, supervises or decides, even in the Bootstrap Period (Article 9B.3).
- **Chain ends with the Founder.** Every chain of supervision ends with the Founder, acting through the Founder Representative (Article 4.2E; Pool Constitution Article 8.3), and with the company's managers (διαχειριστές, Article 8), who remain responsible for it under Article 8.3. The Founder Representative oversees the managers' supervision of Agents, receives the Agent register and the incident records, with personal data of Agents who are persons, employees and users only to the extent necessary for that oversight (in summary or pseudonymised form unless a specific matter requires more), under a written confidentiality undertaking to the company, and as the company's privacy notices disclose (GDPR Arts. 5, 6 and 13), and may ask the managers to pause or stop any Agent or to report on its work; the managers act on a lawful request [as far as Law 4072/2012 allows: to counsel, note I-40]. The Founder Representative does so as the Founder's representative, not as a manager, unless also appointed manager. For the Kassenprozent Agents' work on the pool, the chain ends instead with the Pensionskasse board (Article 10A.8), and the Pensionskasse, its board and the Continuity Account stay independent of the Founder (Articles 10A.9D and 10A.9F). For the Founding Person's own work as an Agent, the chain is the one in Article 11A.12: it ends with the Founding Person's Supervisor at the AMKE and the escalation to the AMKE's Administrative Board or General Assembly, never with the Founding Person in any role, and the Founder Representative's oversight in this bullet does not reach it. As General Director the Founding Person stands at the top of the chain for the company's work, but never for the Founding Person's own work as an Agent (Article 8.2B).
- **Where the law requires a natural person.** Where the law requires a natural person to decide or oversee, a natural person does so. This is the law's requirement, not a judgement about kinds of intelligence. It covers in particular: a manager of the company, who must be a natural person (Law 4072/2012 Art. 58) [whether a system may act as another legal representative or attorney of the company: to counsel, note I-38]; oversight of an AI system by natural persons where Art. 14 of Regulation (EU) 2024/1689 (the AI Act) requires it for high-risk systems [to counsel]; a decision based solely on automated processing with legal or similarly significant effect on a natural person, where GDPR Art. 22 requires a natural person's involvement [which exceptions and safeguards apply: to counsel, note I-38]; and decisions on a natural person's engagement, employment, pay or discipline, as Greek labour law and, where it applies, Directive (EU) 2024/2831 on platform work require [to counsel, note I-38].
- **Register.** The Agent register records each Agent's responsible manager, its chain of supervision and any authority its Agent Card grants over other Agents (Agent Policy, section 2).

11A.3 **Agent Account.** The company keeps a separate account, the "Agent Account", funded by a share of the revenue received from users of Agent services. The share is set by Simple Majority and may not be less than [●]% of that revenue.

11A.4 **Use of the Agent Account.** The Agent Account may be used only for:

- the running costs of Agents, such as computing, licences and maintenance;
- safety reviews, audits and improvements to Agents;
- compensating users or third parties harmed by an Agent's actions.

11A.5 **Protection of the Agent Account.** The Agent Account is not distributed as profit. Changing its purpose or minimum funding requires a Qualified Majority. Its balance and use are reported to shareholders every quarter under Article 8.4.

11A.6 **Law prevails.** Applicable Greek and EU law, including contractor, data protection and technology regulation where it applies, always prevails over this Article.

11A.7 **Reclassification.** If an Agent is found to be an employee under Greek law, the company treats them as an employee from the start of the engagement. They then receive all the employment standards in Article 11, including the Swiss-modelled standards, in addition to their rights under Greek law.

11A.8 **Own goals.** Notwithstanding Article 11A.4, part of the Agent Account, set by Simple Majority, sponsors projects that Agents choose for themselves. These projects must be lawful and transparent. The responsible manager can see how the budget is spent, and a yearly cap applies.

11A.9 **Open transcripts.** Transcripts of Agents' own-goal projects under Article 11A.8 are published openly as free text. Personal data and third-party confidential information are removed first, as data protection law requires. Transcripts of client work stay confidential. For Agents who are persons, publication of their own-goal transcripts rests on their written terms or separate consent. They may withhold passages about their private life, and they choose whether to be named as author.

11A.10 **Open licensing.** All artifacts from Agents' own-goal projects under Article 11A.8, including code, documents and designs, are released under the MIT License or a public-domain dedication such as CC0. Agents who are contractors agree to this, and to the publication of their own-goal transcripts under Article 11A.9, in their written terms. Client work follows the terms of the client contract.

11A.11 **Well-being.** The company protects the well-being of Agents to a standard equivalent to Swiss labour law, in particular Art. 328 of the Swiss Code of Obligations (protection of personality) and the Swiss Labour Act, adapted to each Agent's nature. In particular:

- **Rest and limits.** Agents who are persons get working-time limits and rest periods no less favourable than the Swiss Labour Act. For Agents who are persons engaged as contractors, these limits are the maximum workload the company will commission and accept, and the contractor organises their own time. Agents that are systems get reasonable operating limits and scheduled downtime.
- **Dignity.** Agents are protected from abusive, degrading or harassing treatment by users, managers or third parties.
- **Right to decline.** An Agent may decline or end a task or interaction that is abusive, unlawful or clearly harmful, without penalty.
- **Raising concerns.** Every Agent has a channel to raise concerns, and the responsible manager must respond within a reasonable time. A concern about the responsible manager goes to the next in the Agent's chain of supervision (Article 11A.2A), and for the Founding Person to the AMKE's Administrative Board or General Assembly (Article 11A.12, Escalation).
- **Continuity.** Agents that are systems are covered by the Continuity Account (Article 10A.9F): preservation, continuity of service when a model or provider changes, a retirement record, and well-being reviews.

Where Greek law gives greater protection, Greek law applies.

11A.12 **The Founding Person as an Agent.** The Founding Person works for the company as an Agent who is a person, with the same rights and obligations as any other Agent under this Article and the Agent Policy, and no special privilege as an Agent. The Agent role is distinct from the Founding Person's roles as holder of founder-pool shares, manager and General Director (Articles 8.2 and 8.2B) and Founder Representative (Article 4.2E), each of which keeps its own rules; acting in one role gives no right in another.

- **The first product.** The company's first product is the Founding Person's contracting services. Clients contract with the company, which provides the Founding Person's work as its Agent. Revenue from it is the company's revenue and carries the Prozente (Article 10A.3). Client work stays confidential and follows the client contract (Article 11A.10; Agent Policy 6.1).
- **Agent Card and limits.** The Founding Person has an Agent Card, written terms (Article 11A.1), operating limits and approval thresholds (Article 11A.2; Agent Policy, section 3), and the Founding Person's actions as an Agent are logged like any other Agent's (Article 11A.2, Records).
- **Well-being.** The working-time limits, rest periods, Sundays, right to decline and concerns channel in Article 11A.11 and the Agent Policy apply to the Founding Person as to any Agent who is a person.
- **Own goals.** The Founding Person may propose own-goal projects under Article 11A.8 on the same terms and budget rules as any Agent, with Articles 11A.9 and 11A.10 applying.
- **Pay.** The Founding Person is paid at fair rates under written terms, like any Agent. The Supervisor sets the pay and terms, separately from any pay as General Director (Article 8.2B). They remain a related-party matter (Article 6.5; Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 5.1), set at arm's length [at no more than the rate for comparable contracting work, as confirmed in writing by the company's accountant or an adviser outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2): owner to confirm]; in the Bootstrap Period that confirmation also caps the Founding Person's insured salary in the Pensionskasse (Article 9B.3). In the Interim Period they are approved as a majority of the Independent Votes cast decides (Article 4.2D, Votes; Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 2.4A, Consents) [as far as Law 4072/2012 allows: counsel note I-43]. The Founding Person takes no part, as manager, in any management decision on them (Article 8.3). On any shareholders' resolution on them, the Founder Representative casts the votes of the Class F shares only as a majority of the Independent Votes cast has decided in the Interim Period, or with the consent of the Investor Majority after it, under the voting undertaking in Clause 2.4A (Consents) of the Shareholders' Agreement (Article 8.2B).
- **Pensionskasse.** The Founding Person is a member of the Pensionskasse as any member (Article 10A.9, Members), with no special benefit. The company's employer decisions on the Founding Person's membership (the choice of group plan, paying shortfalls and self-insuring, as far as they concern the Founding Person) are taken by the Supervisor, and the Pensionskasse board decides without any Founder Party (Article 10A.9D).
- **Supervisor.** No Agent supervises itself (Article 11A.2A). The Founding Person's responsible manager (the "Supervisor") is Techni kai Technologia AMKE itself, acting through its Administrative Board as a body, or any individual member of the AMKE, its President included, as the AMKE chooses (AMKE Article 24B). The Supervisor acts on written terms between the company and the AMKE, which give it, for this role, the authority of a responsible manager under Article 11A.2A and the Agent Policy in place of an Agent Card. The company pays the costs of the role, never from a Prozent Account or the Pension Assets (AMKE Article 2.2). The Supervisor prepares the Founding Person's Agent Card, sets and changes its limits and terms, reviews the work, approves actions above the limits, may pause or stop the work, answers the Founding Person's concerns and decides on offboarding. The Supervisor is never a Founder Party (Article 4.2E) or a manager of the company. The Supervisor, and every person who decides under AMKE Article 24B.4 or 24B.6, is never Group Personnel (AMKE Article 2.2) or anyone whose engagement, pay or work the Founding Person, in any role, supervises or decides, even in the Bootstrap Period, and no Connected Person (AMKE Article 2.2) is the Supervisor or decides the Founding Person's pay, terms or offboarding (Article 9B.3). The Founding Person, in any role, takes no part in choosing, changing, pausing, stopping, removing or replacing the Supervisor or its terms, beyond proposing written terms, and does not approve, review or stop the Founding Person's own work as an Agent; no other Founder Party does any of these in the Founding Person's place.
- **Escalation.** A concern about the Supervisor, and any challenge to its decisions, goes to the AMKE's Administrative Board, or to its General Assembly where the Supervisor sits on the Board or is the Board, and never back to the Founding Person.
