Changing, pausing, stopping, removing or replacing the Supervisor, or changing its terms, is decided by the AMKE in the same way (AMKE Article 24B).
- **Conflicts.** A Supervisor who has a close tie to the Founding Person (of the kinds in the definition of Close Associate of the Founder, AMKE Article 2.2) discloses it in the AMKE's register of interests (AMKE Article 21.1); the tie, once disclosed, does not by itself bar the Supervisor from the Founding Person's matters, and the AMKE's members outside the Founder's Circle may take over any specific decision (AMKE Article 24B.6). A Supervisor with a personal stake in a decision (Article 9B.2) discloses it in the same way and takes no part in it, and those members take that decision; if no member outside the Founder's Circle can act, the decision goes to the AMKE's Administrative Board without the conflicted person, or, if fewer than [two] Board members remain, to its General Assembly without the conflicted person and without Founder Parties (AMKE Articles 15.2 and 24B.6). No Connected Person decides the Founding Person's pay, terms or offboarding (Article 9B.3).
- **Chain of supervision.** In every state, whether the Founding Person is the General Director and only manager, one of several managers or no manager, and whether or not the Founding Person is the Founder Representative, the chain of supervision for this Agent role ends with the Supervisor and the escalation under (Escalation), and never with the Founding Person in any role. While the Founding Person is a manager or the Founder Representative, no other Founder Party who is a manager or the Founder Representative stands in the chain [owner to confirm], and the Founder Representative's oversight under Article 11A.2A does not extend to this role. Where the law requires a natural person to decide or oversee (Article 11A.2A), a member of the AMKE who is a natural person does so: the Supervisor, if a natural person, or otherwise a member the AMKE's Administrative Board names.
- **Bootstrap.** In the Bootstrap Period the Supervisor may be a member of the trusted circle (Article 9B.2); after it, the Supervisor is outside the Founder's Circle on the schedule in Article 9B.4.
- **Personal Prozente.** The company receives into the Kassenfonds the Kassenprozent part of the contributions the Founding Person pays on work income earned outside nilware.io (Shareholders' Agreement, Clauses 6.3 to 6.3B). They are not revenue of the company, do not count toward the limits in Article 6.6 and do not reduce any contribution the company owes. The managers report their total, not the Founding Person's income, under Article 8.4.
- **Reclassification.** Article 11A.7 applies to the Founding Person as to any Agent [how it applies to a manager and shareholder engaged as an Agent: counsel note I-43].

## Article 12 – Dissolution and Liquidation

12.1 The company is dissolved by a Qualified Majority resolution or as provided by law. A voluntary dissolution, other than one mandatory law requires, needs a unanimous decision under Article 10A.5 [as far as Law 4072/2012 allows].

12.2 The managers act as liquidators unless the meeting appoints others. Any surplus is distributed in proportion to shareholdings.

12.3 **Jurisdiction.** The liquidation is carried out under Greek law; court matters under Law 4072/2012 go to the magistrates' court of the company's seat (Law 4072/2012 Art. 48(1)).

12.4 **Swiss-law advisor.** The liquidators must appoint at least one advisor qualified in Swiss company law. The advisor reviews each stage of the liquidation and reports in writing to the shareholders. The aim is that the liquidation meets Swiss standards wherever Greek law allows.

12.5 **Swiss standards applied.** Unless Greek law requires otherwise, the liquidators shall:

- publicly invite creditors to register their claims, at least three times, following Art. 742 of the Swiss Code of Obligations;
- distribute no assets to shareholders until at least one year after the last creditor call, following Art. 745 of the Swiss Code of Obligations, unless Greek law sets a longer period;
- keep full records of the liquidation for at least 10 years.

12.6 If Greek law and these Swiss standards conflict, Greek law prevails. The advisor records the conflict and how it was resolved.

## Article 13 – Disputes

13.1 Disputes between shareholders, or between shareholders and the company, go first to mediation under Greek Law 4640/2019.

13.2 If mediation fails, matters under Law 4072/2012 go to the magistrates' court of the company's seat (Law 4072/2012 Art. 48(1)), and other disputes to the competent courts of [the seat]. [An arbitration clause can be adopted only in these initial Articles, or later by unanimous amendment (Law 4072/2012 Art. 48(2)): include one now / none: owner to choose.]

## Article 14 – Final Provisions

14.1 If any provision is invalid, the rest remains in force. The invalid provision is replaced by the valid one that comes closest to its purpose.

14.2 Matters not covered here are governed by Law 4072/2012 and Greek law in general.

---

*Signatures of the founding shareholders: [●]*


<!-- articles/02-nilware-holdings-ae.md -->

# Articles of Association – nilware.io Holdings AE (Draft)

*Société Anonyme (Ανώνυμη Εταιρεία) under Greek Law 4548/2018. Companion to the Articles of nilware.io IKE; where the two must match, they are drafted to do so. Swiss-modelled standards apply only where Greek law allows. Draft for review by a Greek lawyer; not legal advice.*

## Article 1 – Name, Seat, Term

1.1 The company is a Société Anonyme named "nilware.io Holdings AE" [name to be confirmed with GEMI]. It uses the name "nilware.io" under a sublicence from nilware.io IKE, which holds it under licence from the founder pool (IKE Article 1.1), and changes its name if that sublicence ends.

1.2 Its registered seat is in the Municipality of [Athens], Greece. The term is indefinite.

1.2A **Formation for the first investment round.** The company is formed only when the first investment round needs it, no later than the first subscription of Class B shares, because it needs share capital of at least €25,000 (Article 4.1). Until the Qualifying Closing, the IKE shares it is to hold are the Reserve Shares (IKE Article 4.2D): 440 shares, 44% of the IKE's capital, owned at incorporation by the Founding Person as first holder of the founder pool (IKE Article 4.2E), as compensation for the Founding Person's time invested before investors, which carry no votes, whoever holds them, and which their holders may sell or grant to anyone (IKE Article 4.2D, Sales and grants). The company receives the Reserve Shares only at the Qualifying Closing: the first closing of a bona fide investment round in which investors outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2) subscribe Class B shares of at least €[●] in cash at that same closing (IKE Article 4.2D, Conversion). At it, every Reserve Share, whoever holds it, is contributed to the company in kind, and its holder receives the conversion shares in Article 4.3A at the Conversion Ratio; new money in the round buys new shares at the round price. Reserve Shares then held by holders of the founder pool are converted in the same way and at the same Conversion Ratio, into the non-voting Class C shares in Article 4.3B, or, at each such holder's option and only as far as the terms of the round provide the cash for it, bought by the company for cash at the round price per IKE share, paid only out of the round's proceeds at that closing, the company never owing a deferred balance (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 3.2; IKE Article 4.2D, Conversion). A purchase of Reserve Shares for cash within two years of the company's formation is made only after a resolution of the General Meeting approving it, taken before the purchase, and a valuation of those Reserve Shares under Articles 17 and 18 of Law 4548/2018, whose report is published (Law 4548/2018 Art. 19(2)); without them the purchase is void (Law 4548/2018 Art. 19(1)). The approval may be on the agenda of the General Meeting that resolves the round's increase (Article 4.3A, Issue resolution), and the same valuers may make both valuations. The price never exceeds that valuation, and if the valuation is below the round price the cut-back in Clause 3.2 of the Shareholders' Agreement applies to the excess [whether the selling holders vote on the approval: counsel note I-70]. Only Class B shares subscribed in cash by investors outside the Founder's Circle count toward the minimum, counted [after deducting / before deducting: owner to choose] any cash price the company pays for Reserve Shares (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 3.1A); if they do not reach it on their own, there is no Qualifying Closing and nothing converts. Subscriptions of Class B shares at a closing are conditional on the minimum being reached at that closing, and the money is held in escrow and refunded if it is not reached by [date], as Clause 3.1A of the Shareholders' Agreement provides; the company issues no Class B shares at a closing below the minimum [unless the owner chooses the alternative in that Clause]. Reserve Shares the company holds before the Qualifying Closing stay without votes, and the Interim Period rules of IKE Article 4.2D keep applying until it; after it, the Reserve Shares the company holds convert into ordinary voting shares, and the company again holds all of them. It also signs the deed of adherence under IKE Article 5.3 and adheres to the Shareholders' Agreement (Clause 2.4A). It is bound from its formation by every decision taken under IKE Article 4.2D in the Interim Period, by every sale and grant of Reserve Shares made under it, and by every Prozent created in it.

## Article 2 – Purpose

2.1 The company's purpose is to raise capital from investors and to hold and manage its stake in nilware.io IKE, being all shares not held by the Founder (the founder pool, IKE Article 4.2E), by the Prozente or by the Kassenprozent pool (initially 440 shares, 44%; IKE Article 4.2B). As part of that purpose, it transfers IKE shares equal to 1% of the IKE's capital, without payment, to the holder of each new Prozent approved under Article 7.5, and it pays for the IKE shares the Prozente and the pool receive on an IKE capital increase (IKE Article 4.2B). While the Founder controls it (IKE Article 4.2C), it does not buy IKE shares from, or sell them to, the Kassenprozent pool (IKE Article 10A.8).

2.2 It may also invest in other companies with the approval of a Qualified Majority under Article 7.4.

2.3 The company shares the agentic principle of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 2.2). It has no employees of its own unless the law requires it.

## Article 3 – Governing Law and Precedence

3.1 These Articles are governed by Greek law. Mandatory provisions of Law 4548/2018 always prevail.

3.2 Swiss-modelled standards in these Articles apply as additional commitments, only where they give shareholders equal or greater protection than Greek law.

## Article 4 – Share Capital and Share Classes

4.1 The share capital is €[25,000 minimum], divided into [●] registered shares of €[●] each, fully paid in on incorporation.

4.2 **Class A shares** are ordinary voting shares. They are founder-pool shares (IKE Article 4.2E): they are held only by holders of the founder pool, initially the Founding Person, [name withheld], and pass with the founder-class shares of the IKE under the Constitution of the Founder Pool (Pool Constitution Article 9.2). Any other person admitted to Class A by Qualified Majority holds them in the founder pool, after adhering to it. Like every founder-pool share they are freely transferable, and Articles 6.1 and 6.2 do not apply to them (IKE Article 5.7). All Class A shares vote as one block, cast by the Founder Representative as the founder pool decides (IKE Article 4.2E, One block). Each holder of Class A shares has granted the Founder Representative an irrevocable proxy for this (Pool Constitution Article 3.2), and the company admits the Founder Representative last notified to the Board as proxy for every holder of Class A shares.

4.3 **Class B shares** are preference shares for investors under Art. 38 of Law 4548/2018. They carry:

- a priority right to dividends of [●]% a year, non-cumulative unless the investment terms say otherwise;
- priority on return of capital in a liquidation, up to the amount invested;
- voting rights only on the Reserved Matters in Article 7.4, on new Prozente under Article 7.5 and on the matters in Article 7.6, unless the issue resolution grants full voting rights.

4.3A **Conversion shares.** The shares the company issues at the Qualifying Closing in exchange for Reserve Shares contributed in kind (IKE Article 4.2D, Conversion) are the "conversion shares". The company issues them at the Conversion Ratio: [●] shares for each Reserve Share, the same for every Reserve Share and every holder, fixed in these Articles at the company's formation and in any case before any investor subscribes in the round [owner to fix]. Conversion shares issued for Reserve Shares held by holders of the founder pool at the Qualifying Closing are Class C shares (Article 4.3B). The others are [Class B shares on the same terms as those the round's investors subscribe, pari passu: the default / shares of a separate class whose rights the term sheet sets: owner to choose in the term sheet]. The contribution is valued, before the resolution issuing the conversion shares and no more than six months before the contribution, by two certified auditors or an audit firm, or by two independent certified valuers, who meet Law 4548/2018 Art. 17(4) [or under Law 4548/2018 Art. 18(2), if counsel advises: counsel note I-44] (Law 4548/2018 Arts. 17 and 27(3)). The Conversion Ratio is a maximum: if the valuation per Reserve Share is below the issue value of the conversion shares the ratio gives, the share premium on the conversion shares is reduced first and then the ratio, the same for every Reserve Share and every holder, so that no share is issued below par (Law 4548/2018 Art. 35(2)) and no contribution is counted above its valuation (Law 4548/2018 Art. 17(7)). The existing shareholders' pre-emption right does not extend to the conversion shares, which are issued for a contribution in kind (Article 5.2). Conversion shares do not count toward the minimum subscription that makes a closing the Qualifying Closing. Conversion shares issued to a Founder Party, to any other person who was in the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2) at the Qualifying Closing, or to a person who received the Reserve Shares they were issued for without payment (the "excluded conversion shares") are, where they would otherwise be Class B shares, issued as Class B-X shares (Article 4.3C), and so are not counted for a majority of the Class B votes, under Article 7.4 or any other, for the Investor Majority (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 3.4) or for the Class B appointment right and its threshold (Article 8.2). Conversion shares received for an unvested Reserve Share stay subject to the same vesting, and the return right then applies to them [in favour of the grantor / in favour of the company: owner to choose]; the grant terms and Schedule 1 of the Shareholders' Agreement say so (IKE Article 4.2D, Conversion). Every holder of conversion shares adheres to the Shareholders' Agreement (Clause 12.3).

- **Issue resolution.** The Class B shares of the round, the conversion shares and the Class C shares are issued at the Qualifying Closing by one resolution of the General Meeting passed with the increased quorum and majority of Law 4548/2018 Arts. 130(3) and 132(2), approved by any class whose rights it affects (Law 4548/2018 Art. 25(3)), and stating the particulars in Law 4548/2018 Arts. 17(1) and 25(1) for each contributor and each subscriber [or by the Board under an authorisation under Law 4548/2018 Art. 24: counsel to choose]. That resolution, the approval of any cash purchase of Reserve Shares (Article 1.2A) and the exclusion of pre-emption for the round (Article 5.2) need no Class B approval under Articles 5.1 and 7.4, because no Class B shares held by investors outside the Founder's Circle exist before it; they need the written consent of [the round's lead investors: owner to choose] instead. Every holder of Class A shares votes for them (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 2.4A).

4.3B **Class C shares (non-voting).** Class C shares are issued only as conversion shares, at the Conversion Ratio, for Reserve Shares held by holders of the founder pool at the Qualifying Closing, other than those a holder sells to the company for cash under Article 1.2A (IKE Article 4.2D, Conversion). They are preference shares without voting rights under Law 4548/2018 Art. 38(1) and (4). Their purpose is that the founder pool keeps the value of the Reserve Shares no investor bought but can never outvote the Investors on their protections.

- **Economic rights.** Class C shares carry, share for share, the rights to dividends, other distributions, return of capital and liquidation proceeds [of preference shares that rank after the Class B dividend priority and liquidation preference and, before the Class A shares, take a priority return of the capital and share premium paid up on them on a capital reduction or liquidation [and a non-cumulative priority dividend of [●]% of that amount], and otherwise take part pro rata with all shareholders (Articles 4.3, 10.4 and 11.2), so that they rank after the Investors' Class B priorities (counsel note I-69) / of the shares named in Article 4.3A for holders outside the founder pool, Class B priorities included where those are Class B shares, so that they rank with the Investors' Class B priorities: owner to choose], whether or not any conversion shares are issued to holders outside the founder pool (Articles 10.4 and 11.2). Class C shares never rank ahead of the Class B dividend priority or liquidation preference of the shares the Investors subscribe in cash. They also carry, share for share, the same share as those shares of the proceeds of any sale of the company or of its shares, the tag-along and drag-along in Article 6.3 included; and the same information rights.
- **No votes.** Class C shares carry no vote at the General Meeting on any matter and are never counted for any quorum, majority or class vote, other than the separate class meetings of their own class below: not for a majority of the Class B votes under Article 7.4 or any other, the votes represented or the quorum under Article 7.5, the resolution under Article 7.6, the Investor Majority (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 3.4), the Class B appointment right or its threshold (Article 8.2), or the minimum subscription that makes a closing the Qualifying Closing (IKE Article 4.2D, Conversion). They vote only in the separate class meetings Law 4548/2018 requires: on an abolition or limitation of their preference (Law 4548/2018 Art. 38(8)) and on a capital increase, or an authorisation of one, that affects their rights (Law 4548/2018 Art. 25(3)). Their holders may attend General Meetings without being counted for quorum (Law 4548/2018 Art. 124(1)). How they vote in those class meetings is governed by the Shareholders' Agreement (Clause 2.4A) [owner to confirm] (counsel note I-75).
- **Not founder-pool shares.** Class C shares are not Class A shares and not founder-pool shares (IKE Article 4.2E). Each holder holds them outright, and they take no part in the Founder's block vote.
- **Transfers.** Their holders may sell, grant, give, pledge or bequeath Class C shares, in whole or in part, to anyone. Articles 6.1 and 6.2 do not apply to them. Every acquirer adheres to the Shareholders' Agreement before the transfer is registered (Clause 12.3).
- **Conversion in a buyer's hands.** A Class C share held by a buyer outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2) who is a "qualifying buyer" of it converts, at that buyer's written request to the Board, one for one into [a Class B share on the same terms as those the round's investors subscribed: owner to confirm] [counsel note I-49]. This is a conversion right under Law 4548/2018 Art. 38(3), granted in these Articles and repeated in the resolution issuing the Class C shares (Article 4.3A, Issue resolution); it takes effect when the Board confirms the qualifying buyer's certificate and records the conversion. A holder is a qualifying buyer of a share only while it paid for it at least [the round price / fair value as a valuer who is not a Founder Party (IKE Article 4.2E) determines: owner to choose], and is not a nominee of a Founder Party, has no agreement or understanding with a Founder Party on voting it, proxies over it, its reacquisition or options over it, and did not receive financing for it from a Founder Party. The holder certifies this to the Board with its request and again whenever the Board asks. A Class C share held by anyone else stays a Class C share. A share so converted converts mandatorily into a Class B-X share (Article 4.3C) if it ceases to be held by a person outside the Founder's Circle who is a qualifying buyer of it, or if its holder does not give the certificate when asked.

4.3C **Class B-X shares.** Class B-X shares carry the same rights as Class B shares in every economic right (Articles 4.3, 5.3, 10.4 and 11.2), and the same votes as Class B shares in the two-thirds under Article 7.4 and under Articles 7.5 and 7.6. They are never counted for a majority of the Class B votes voting as a separate class, under Article 7.4 or any other, or for the Investor Majority (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 3.4), and they take no part in the Class B appointment right or its threshold (Article 8.2). They are a separate class because every share of a class carries the same rights (Law 4548/2018 Arts. 36(1) and 38(2)); their purpose is that the separate Class B vote and the Class B Board seat stay with investors outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2).

- **Issue.** Excluded conversion shares that would otherwise be Class B shares are issued as Class B-X shares (Article 4.3A).
- **Mandatory conversion.** Under Law 4548/2018 Art. 38(3), a Class B share converts mandatorily, one for one, into a Class B-X share when a Founder Party (IKE Article 4.2E), or a person who was one in the previous [3] years, acquires it; a Class B share converted from a Class C share converts in the same way where Article 4.3B provides. [A Class B-X share converts back into a Class B share at the written request of a holder outside the Founder's Circle who acquired it for value and is a qualifying buyer of it (Article 4.3B), on the same certificate: owner to confirm.]
- **Effect.** Each conversion takes effect when the Board records the triggering fact, and the Board files the resulting capital article with GEMI [whether a mandatory conversion triggered by a change of holder is a lawful term of conversion under Law 4548/2018 Art. 38(3): counsel note I-71].
- **Transfers.** Articles 6.1 to 6.3 apply to Class B-X shares as to Class B shares, and for Article 6.1 the two classes count as one.

4.4 All shares are registered. Bearer shares are not permitted.

## Article 5 – Capital Increases and Pre-emption

5.1 Capital increases are decided by the General Meeting by Qualified Majority, except the increase at the Qualifying Closing, which follows Article 4.3A (Issue resolution).

5.2 Existing shareholders have the pre-emption right in Article 26 of Law 4548/2018, in proportion to their holdings. It does not extend to increases in kind, including the conversion shares (Article 4.3A). Where the company has several classes, it may issue shares of one class only, and holders of that class then have priority (Law 4548/2018 Art. 26(1)). The right may be limited or excluded by a General Meeting resolution passed with the increased quorum and majority of Law 4548/2018 Arts. 130(3) and 132(2), on a written Board report on the reasons and the issue price, published as the law requires (Law 4548/2018 Art. 27(1)), and, after the Qualifying Closing, by Qualified Majority. At the Qualifying Closing, the exclusion in favour of the round's investors needs no Class B approval (Article 4.3A, Issue resolution).

5.3 **Anti-dilution.** Class B shareholders are protected against issues below the price they paid, on a broad-based weighted-average basis, through the conversion right of the Class B shares, which these Articles set (Law 4548/2018 Art. 38(3)). Each Class B share converts into ordinary voting shares of [Class O: owner to name], never Class A shares (Article 4.2), on [an exit / a listing / the holder's request: owner to choose], at the Class B conversion ratio, initially [one] for one. If the company issues shares for a price per share below the Class B issue price, the conversion ratio becomes the old ratio multiplied by (A + C) / (A + B), where A is the number of shares in issue immediately before the issue, counted as if every convertible share had converted, B is the number of shares that the total consideration for the issue would buy at the Class B issue price, and C is the number of shares actually issued. The issues listed in Clause 4.3 of the Shareholders' Agreement do not trigger an adjustment. Issues by nilware.io IKE below the look-through Class B price follow Clause 4.2A of the Shareholders' Agreement [counsel note I-76].

## Article 6 – Transfers

6.1 **Right of first offer.** A holder who wants to transfer shares notifies the Board and the other holders of the same class of the price and terms agreed with a proposed buyer. The other holders may accept, pro rata to their holdings of that class, within 30 days of the notice, at that price and on those terms. Shares not accepted may then be transferred to that buyer within [60] days of the end of that period, at no lower price and on terms no more favourable to the buyer (Law 4548/2018 Art. 43(2)(a)). This Article and Article 6.2 do not apply to Class A shares (Article 4.2) or Class C shares (Article 4.3B).

6.2 Transfers to third parties need the Board's approval (Law 4548/2018 Art. 43(1)). The Board decides within [15] working days of the notice under Article 6.1, or, where no offer under Article 6.1 is needed, of the holder's notice of the proposed transfer, and may refuse only on [the ground that the transferee is a competitor of nilware.io IKE or has not adhered to the Shareholders' Agreement: owner to confirm]. If the Board does not decide within that deadline, the transfer is free (Law 4548/2018 Art. 43(2)). Under Law 4548/2018 Art. 43(4), Articles 6.1 and 6.2 do not apply to a transfer on death, on attachment or in insolvency.

6.3 **Tag-along and drag-along.** These rights are set by these Articles (Law 4548/2018 Art. 43(2)(c) and (d)). They match IKE Articles 5.4 and 5.5 in substance but apply by their own terms, not by reference.

- **Tag-along.** If one or more shareholders propose to transfer shares that would give the buyer, alone or in concert with others, more than half of the votes at the General Meeting, every other shareholder may sell its shares to the same buyer at the same price per share and on the same terms, adjusted only for the Class B priorities (Articles 4.3, 10.4 and 11.2) and, for Class C shares, as Article 4.3B provides. The sellers notify the other shareholders of the offer at least [30] days before completion; each may join by notice within [20] days of that notice, and the sellers may not complete unless the buyer also buys the shares of those who join.
- **Drag-along.** If holders of at least 75% of the capital accept a bona fide offer for all the shares of the company, they may require the other shareholders, by notice of at least [30] days, to sell their shares to that buyer at the same price per share and on the same terms, adjusted only for the Class B priorities and, for Class C shares, as Article 4.3B provides, with completion no earlier than the end of that notice period.

On a change of control of the founder pool, the holders of Class B shares have the tag-along in IKE Article 5.4A and Clause 9.1A of the Shareholders' Agreement [owner may confirm].

## Article 7 – General Meeting

7.1 The ordinary General Meeting is held by 10 September each year to approve the accounts.

7.2 Notice is given at least 20 days in advance, the Swiss standard, and never less than Greek law requires. Meetings may be held electronically.

7.3 Ordinary resolutions pass by more than 50% of the votes represented.

7.4 **Reserved Matters.** The following need at least two-thirds of the votes represented, and a majority of Class B votes voting as a separate class. A majority of the Class B votes, under this Article or any other, cannot be given, and is never deemed given, while no Class B shares are held by investors outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2); the resolutions at the Qualifying Closing need none (Article 4.3A, Issue resolution). Class B-X shares (Article 4.3C), which are the excluded conversion shares (Article 4.3A) and the Class B shares acquired by a Founder Party (IKE Article 4.2E) or by a person who was one in the previous [3] years, vote in the two-thirds but are never counted for a majority of the Class B votes, under this Article or any other, or for the Investor Majority (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 3.4). Class C shares carry no vote and are never counted, under this Article or any other (Article 4.3B). The creation of a new Prozent of nilware.io IKE is not a Reserved Matter; it follows Article 7.5. Removing a Prozent, lowering its contribution or changing its purpose follows Article 7.6, and Article 7.6 itself may be amended only as it provides.

- amending these Articles;
- capital increases or reductions, and issuing new share classes, together with any separate class approval Law 4548/2018 Arts. 25(3) and 38(8) require;
- mergers, splits, conversion or dissolution;
- selling or pledging the stake in nilware.io IKE;
- investing in any company other than nilware.io IKE;
- related-party transactions above €[●], in addition to the Board's authorisation that Articles 99 and 100 of Law 4548/2018 require for every such transaction; at the General Meeting the counterparty and the shareholders related to it under Law 4548/2018 Art. 99(2) do not vote and are not counted for quorum or majority (Law 4548/2018 Art. 100(5)) [and Law 4548/2018 Arts. 99 to 101 also apply to the company's general director and managers (Law 4548/2018 Art. 99(2)(c)), and the minority percentage in Law 4548/2018 Art. 100(3) and (4) is 1%: owner to choose].

7.5 **New Prozente of nilware.io IKE.** The General Meeting approves a new Prozent of nilware.io IKE, and its Prozent charter, by at least two-thirds of the votes represented, all share classes voting together (IKE Article 6.6). Class B and Class B-X shares vote on this matter; Class C shares do not and are not counted (Article 4.3B). The approval is a precondition: the IKE shareholders' meeting then passes the amendment under IKE Article 6.6. A charter approved under this Article only adds a Prozent. It may not change any existing Prozent, IKE Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C, or the limits in IKE Article 6.6; any such change follows Article 7.6 or 7.4.

- **Quorum.** Shares representing at least half of the paid-up capital entitled to vote on this matter, all classes counted, must be present or represented, at the first and at any repeat meeting.
- **Greek law.** Where Law 4548/2018 requires a higher quorum or majority, that applies.
- **Limits.** Going beyond the limits in IKE Article 6.6 needs at least 75% of the votes represented, including a majority of the Class B votes cast.
- **The share.** Transferring IKE shares equal to 1% of the IKE's capital to the holder of a new Prozent approved under this Article, and paying for the IKE shares the Prozente and the pool receive on an IKE capital increase (IKE Article 4.2B), are not a sale of the stake under Article 7.4. The Board decides them, and authorises them under Articles 99 to 101 of Law 4548/2018 where those apply.

7.6 **Permanence of the Prozente.** The Prozente of nilware.io IKE are permanent (IKE Article 10A.5). The Board may vote for, or consent to, any of the following only after both (a) a resolution passed unanimously by the votes represented at a General Meeting at which shares representing at least two-thirds of the paid-up capital entitled to vote are present or represented, the highest quorum Law 4548/2018 Art. 130(5) allows these Articles to set, and (b) the written consent of every holder of shares that carry votes, of every class, delivered to the Board before it votes (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 5.1C); the Board has no authority to vote or consent otherwise. Class C shares, which carry no votes, are not counted (Article 4.3B) [whether limb (b) is lawful as a limit on the Board's authority: counsel note I-73]:

- removing a Prozent, lowering its contribution or changing its purpose;
- amending the IKE Articles listed in IKE Article 10A.5C in any way that reduces a Prozent, its share, its revenue, the pension guarantees or the pension-share governance;
- a voluntary dissolution of nilware.io IKE, other than one mandatory law requires (IKE Article 12.1).

This Article may be amended only by a resolution under (a) together with the consents under (b). The separate class votes that Law 4548/2018 Arts. 25(3) and 38(8) give the holders of Class C shares apply (Article 4.3B). Mandatory insolvency, dissolution and liquidation law prevails.

## Article 8 – Board of Directors

8.1 The Board has [3 to 5] members, the exact number fixed by the General Meeting within that range (Law 4548/2018 Art. 77(3)), elected by the General Meeting for up to [5] years, except the member appointed under Article 8.2.

8.2 Holders of Class A shares, as one block through the Founder Representative (IKE Article 4.2E), propose candidates for a majority of the Board, and every shareholder party to the Shareholders' Agreement undertakes to vote for those candidates at the General Meeting (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 5.1F). The Class A shares do not use the direct appointment right in Article 79 of Law 4548/2018 for these seats: it is capped at two-fifths of the Board, and holders who use it take no part in electing the other members [or, if the owner prefers: direct appointment of two-fifths of the Board under Law 4548/2018 Art. 79 plus the voting undertaking for the rest: owner to choose]. Holders of Class B shares, other than Class B-X shares (Article 4.3C), who together hold at least [●]% of the capital have the right under Article 79 of Law 4548/2018 to appoint [1] member of the Board directly, a person outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2), and to remove and replace that member, by a joint written act notified to the company at least three full days before the General Meeting [the joint act may be signed by holders of a majority of those shares for all of them: counsel to confirm under Law 4548/2018 Art. 79(5)]. The General Meeting elects the other members. Class B and Class B-X shares, which vote only on the matters in Article 4.3, do not vote in that election, holders who use the appointment right take no part in it in any case (Law 4548/2018 Art. 79(2)), and Class C shares never vote, so the holders of Class A shares elect them. Class C shares take no part in the appointment right and do not count toward its threshold (Article 4.3B).

8.3 Directors owe duties of care and loyalty under Arts. 96 to 98 of Law 4548/2018, applied to the standard of Art. 717 of the Swiss Code of Obligations. Conflicts must be declared and the director abstains.

8.4 The Board reports to shareholders every quarter on the company's finances and on nilware.io IKE's performance.

## Article 9 – Voting at nilware.io IKE

9.1 The company exercises its vote in nilware.io IKE through the Board.

9.2 On any Qualified Majority matter of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 6.5), the Board may vote in favour only with the prior approval required for Reserved Matters under Article 7.4. This passes the investors' protection through to the IKE. While no Class B shares are held by investors outside the Founder's Circle, that approval cannot be given (Article 7.4), and the Board may not vote in favour. For a new Prozent approved under Article 7.5, the Board votes for the resolutions that implement it, as IKE Article 6.6 and the Shareholders' Agreement provide, and no Reserved Matters approval is needed. On the matters listed in Article 7.6, Article 7.6 applies.

## Article 10 – Accounts, Audit, Profits

10.1 The financial year runs from 1 January to 31 December. Accounts follow Law 4308/2014.

10.2 The accounts are audited by a certified auditor every year, whether or not the law requires it. While nilware.io IKE is in its Simple Stage (IKE Article 9A), an audit is needed only where the law requires it or the Investor Majority asks for one (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 8.1).

10.3 At least 5% of annual profit goes to a statutory reserve until it reaches one-third of the share capital, as Greek law requires.

10.4 Subject to the minimum dividend under Article 161 of Law 4548/2018, unless the General Meeting reduces or waives it with the quorum and majority that Article requires [and any separate class vote: counsel note I-77], profits are distributed first to Class B and Class B-X under Articles 4.3 and 4.3C, then to all shareholders pro rata. Class C shares take part as Article 4.3B provides: [preference shares that rank after the Class B dividend priority and liquidation preference and, before the Class A shares, take a priority return of the capital and share premium paid up on them on a capital reduction or liquidation [and a non-cumulative priority dividend of [●]% of that amount], and otherwise take part pro rata with all shareholders (Articles 4.3, 10.4 and 11.2), so that they rank after the Investors' Class B priorities (counsel note I-69) / the shares named in Article 4.3A for holders outside the founder pool, Class B priorities included where those are Class B shares, so that they rank with the Investors' Class B priorities: owner to choose], whether or not any conversion shares are issued to holders outside the founder pool, and never ahead of the Class B dividend priority of the shares the Investors subscribe in cash.

## Article 11 – Dissolution and Liquidation

11.1 Liquidation follows Article 12 of the IKE Articles: Greek law, Athens jurisdiction, a Swiss-law advisor, and Swiss creditor-protection standards where Greek law allows.

11.2 Surplus goes first to Class B and Class B-X up to the amount invested, then to all shareholders pro rata. Class C shares take part as Article 4.3B provides: [preference shares that rank after the Class B dividend priority and liquidation preference and, before the Class A shares, take a priority return of the capital and share premium paid up on them on a capital reduction or liquidation [and a non-cumulative priority dividend of [●]% of that amount], and otherwise take part pro rata with all shareholders (Articles 4.3, 10.4 and 11.2), so that they rank after the Investors' Class B priorities (counsel note I-69) / the shares named in Article 4.3A for holders outside the founder pool, Class B priorities included where those are Class B shares, so that they rank with the Investors' Class B priorities: owner to choose], whether or not any conversion shares are issued to holders outside the founder pool, and never ahead of the liquidation preference of the shares the Investors subscribe in cash.

## Article 12 – Disputes and Final Provisions

12.1 Disputes go first to mediation under Law 4640/2019, then to the courts of Athens, or to arbitration if all parties agree.

12.2 If any provision is invalid, the rest remains in force and the invalid provision is replaced by the closest valid one.

---

*Signatures of the founding shareholders: [●]*


<!-- agreements/03-shareholders-agreement.md -->

# Shareholders' Agreement – nilware.io Group (Draft)

*Between the holders of the founder pool (together the "Founder", acting through the Founder Representative, IKE Article 4.2E), initially [name withheld], who is also a party in person as the "Founding Person" for Clauses 6 and 12, nilware.io Holdings AE (the "AE", from its formation, Clause 2.4A), nilware.io IKE (the "IKE"), Techni kai Technologia AMKE (the "AMKE", as to Clauses 2, 5.1A to 5.1E, 6.3 to 6.3C, 11.0 and 12) and the investors listed in Schedule 1 (the "Investors"). Governed by Greek law; Swiss-modelled standards apply where stated. Draft for review by a Greek lawyer; not legal advice.*

## 1 – Purpose and Precedence

1.1 This Agreement sets out how the parties own, govern and fund the nilware.io group.

1.2 If this Agreement conflicts with the Articles of the AE or the IKE, the parties will vote to amend the Articles to match this Agreement. Until then, the Articles bind third parties.

## 2 – Group Structure

2.1 The Founder, the founder pool, holds founder-class shares (Class F) of the IKE equal to exactly 50% of its capital (initially 500 of its 1,000 shares), with votes equal to 50% of the total capital and never more, cast as one block by the Founder Representative (IKE Article 4.2E); on any capital increase the holders of founder-pool shares subscribe the new Class F shares needed to stay at exactly 50%, or the increase is otherwise structured that way (IKE Article 4.2B). In the Interim Period these are 500 of the 560 votes in existence, but every majority is counted on the total capital and also needs a majority of the Independent Votes, so the Founder cannot pass anything alone (IKE Article 4.2D, Votes). The AMKE holds 1% (10 shares) each for the Kulturprozent, the Konzeptprozent, the Erhaltungsprozent, the Allmendprozent and the Pensionsprozent, and the 1% (10 shares) of Kassenprozent pool shares, the last two for the Pensionskasse (IKE Articles 10A.8 and 10A.9B). The AE holds all remaining shares, initially 44% (440 shares; until the Qualifying Closing, they are Reserve Shares without votes, Clause 2.4A). Each new Prozent receives existing shares equal to 1% of the IKE's capital (10 shares), transferred by the AE (IKE Article 4.2A) or, in the Interim Period, by the holders of the founder pool out of the Reserve Shares they hold (IKE Article 4.2D). The Kassenprozent holds no share of its own; the pool shares are an asset of the Pensionskasse.

2.2 The Founder holds the Class A shares of the AE, as founder-pool shares (AE Article 4.2). Investors hold Class B shares.

2.2A **The founder pool.** The founder-pool shares (IKE Article 4.2E) are freely transferable, by sale, gift or inheritance, in whole or in part (IKE Article 5.7). Every holder of them is a party to this Agreement as part of the Founder, by deed of adherence under Clause 12.3 before its holding is registered, and is bound by every Clause that binds the Founder, Clauses 5.1B to 5.1D included. The holders vote as one block, as a majority among them decides, through the Founder Representative, a natural person, under the Constitution of the Founder Pool. Every rule of this Agreement that keeps a person or a vote independent of the Founder applies to every Founder Party (IKE Article 4.2E). The personal obligations of the Founding Person (Clauses 6.1 to 6.5 and the IP assignment) stay with the Founding Person and do not pass with the shares. In particular, the exclusivity-or-contribution obligation in Clause 6.3 binds the Founding Person only; a later holder of founder-pool shares is bound by it only if it also works for the group and opts in by written deed (Clause 6.3C).

2.3 The IKE is the operating company. The AE is a holding and fundraising vehicle and runs no business of its own.

2.3A **The brand.** The nilware.io name, the marks "NILWARE.IO" and "∅ nilware.io" and the domain nilware.io (the "Brand Assets") belong to the Founder, the founder pool, and are inseparable from the Class F shares (Pool Constitution Article 2A). The IKE uses them under the exclusive, perpetual, royalty-free, worldwide licence in the Brand Licence Agreement (the "Brand Licence"), with the right to sublicense them to the AE and the other group companies and to the AMKE and the Prozente to describe themselves. The Brand Licence ends only if the IKE stops honouring the Prozente or the permanence rules (IKE Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C), as an arbitrator establishes after written notice and a [90]-day cure period, followed by a [12]-month transition to a new name; it does not end on a change of control, an exit, liquidation or insolvency, or at the Founder's discretion (Brand Licence Clauses 6 and 7). Schedule 1 discloses it to the Investors.

2.3B **The first product.** The first product of nilware.io is the Founding Person's contracting services. Clients contract with the IKE, and the IKE provides the Founding Person's work as an Agent of the IKE (IKE Article 11A.12). Revenue from it is the IKE's revenue and carries the Prozente (IKE Article 10A.3). Work for clients follows the client contracts (IP Assignment Agreement, its section 7 on future IP). The business plan in Schedule 2 starts from this product.

2.4 The parties will not change this structure without approval under the Reserved Matters in Clause 5, except for new Prozente under Clause 5.1A and dealings of the Kassenprozent pool under IKE Article 10A.8. Removing a Prozent, lowering its contribution or changing its purpose follows Clause 5.1C.

2.4A **Before the AE.** The AE is formed only for the first investment round (AE Article 1.2A). Until the Reserve Shares are contributed to the AE at the Qualifying Closing and the contribution is registered (IKE Article 4.2D, the "Interim Period"), whether or not the AE has been formed earlier:

- **Parties.** The Founder (initially the Founding Person as sole holder of the founder pool), the Founding Person in person, the IKE and the AMKE sign this Agreement at the incorporation of the IKE. The AE and the Investors join by deed of adherence under Clause 12.3, and so do every later holder of founder-pool shares (Clause 2.2A) and every person who acquires Reserve Shares (IKE Article 4.2D, Sales and grants). Until then, amendments under Clause 12.2 need the written consent of the Founder and the AMKE, and Clauses 5.1C and 5.1D keep their own rule.
- **Reserve Shares.** The Reserve Shares are owned outright by their holders (initially the Founding Person, as compensation for the time invested before investors, sweat equity), and their dividend and liquidation rights belong to each holder for the shares it holds (IKE Article 4.2D). Those held by holders of the founder pool are founder-pool shares. They carry no votes, whoever holds them, until the AE holds them after the Qualifying Closing; no Founder Party and no other holder ever votes them, and they are not part of the Founder's 50% for Clauses 2.1 and 9.2. Each holder of the founder pool, the Founding Person included, may independently sell or grant any part of the Reserve Shares it holds to anyone, with the proceeds to the seller (IKE Article 4.2D, Sales and grants); a sale or grant changes no vote. Every holder, whoever it is, takes them subject to IKE Article 4.2D, adheres to this Agreement and to every permanence rule, and contributes them to the AE at the Qualifying Closing. [If the IKE Articles cannot create a class without votes (counsel note I-35), each holder undertakes not to vote them and grants the proxy holder in Clause 5.1B an irrevocable proxy that suspends their votes, with the same effect.]
- **Consents.** In the Interim Period, a consent that Clause 5.1 gives to the Investor Majority is given, for the pay and benefits of the Founding Person (the Founding Person's pay as General Director and fees as an Agent included, IKE Articles 8.2B and 11A.12) and of any other Founder Party, by a majority of the Independent Votes cast under IKE Article 4.2D, whether or not any Investor has adhered, and so is the consent to a change of the Brand Licence under Clause 5.1. The matters in IKE Article 6.5 cannot be passed in the Interim Period, other than those IKE Article 4.2D (What the AE's rules forbid) allows. The other matters in Clause 5.1 need no consent until then.
- **Leaver terms.** Where Clauses 6.1 and 6.2 name the AE, [the independent proxy holder in Clause 5.1B] directs the votes; distributions that would be due to the AE are paid by the IKE, under the assignment of distributions in Clause 6.2B, to the Escrow Holder, who holds them until the Qualifying Closing and then pays them to the AE (an AE not yet formed cannot receive them, and the IKE does not withhold them itself); and any payment due from the AE is due at the Qualifying Closing. Clause 6.2A applies to the Reserve Shares.
- **Option pool.** No option over shares of the IKE or the AE is satisfied in the Interim Period. Options may be granted in it only with the consent under (Consents) above, to be satisfied from the AE's shares after the Qualifying Closing (Clause 7.1). Separately, holders of Reserve Shares may grant them, with optional vesting, as an option pool for Agents and employees (IKE Article 4.2D, Sales and grants; Clause 7.1).
- **Deadlock.** Clause 10.1 applies to a resolution blocked twice in the Interim Period, including an ordinary resolution of the IKE that is not a Reserved Matter. Clause 10.2 does not. Where no Independent Vote is cast, or the Independent Votes tie, on a resolution the law requires (the Required Matters in IKE Article 4.2D, Fallback), the independent proxy holder in Clause 5.1B casts the votes of the Reserve Shares on that matter only, under the neutral rule in that Article, taking no instruction from any Founder Party; if the proxy holder cannot act, Clause 10.1 applies and then the court procedure that Article names. For this, for the change of name under Clause 5.1E and for the contribution at the Qualifying Closing, each holder of Reserve Shares grants the proxy holder an irrevocable proxy over them on signing or adhering, on the terms of the Proxy bullet in Clause 5.1B, including its successor rule. The Fallback gives no Founder Party a vote or control over the Reserve Shares.
- **Conversion.** At the Qualifying Closing (IKE Article 4.2D, Conversion: the first closing of a bona fide round in which investors outside the Founder's Circle subscribe Class B shares of at least €[●] in cash at that same closing), every Reserve Share, whoever holds it, is contributed to the AE in kind in exchange for AE shares at the Conversion Ratio fixed in advance (AE Article 4.3A), the same for every Reserve Share; the AE shares a holder outside the founder pool receives are [Class B on the same terms as the round's investors, pari passu: the default / a separate class: owner to choose in the term sheet]. New money in the round buys new AE shares at the round price. Reserve Shares then held by holders of the founder pool convert like every other, at the same Conversion Ratio, into the AE's non-voting Class C shares (AE Article 4.3B), with, share for share, the same economic rights as [preference shares that rank after the Class B dividend priority and liquidation preference and, before the Class A shares, take a priority return of the capital and share premium paid up on them on a capital reduction or liquidation [and a non-cumulative priority dividend of [●]% of that amount], and otherwise take part pro rata with all shareholders (AE Articles 4.3, 4.3B, 10.4 and 11.2), so that they rank after the Investors' Class B priorities (counsel note I-69) / the shares named in AE Article 4.3A for holders outside the founder pool, Class B priorities included where those are Class B shares, so that they rank with the Investors' Class B priorities: owner to choose], whether or not any conversion shares are issued to holders outside the founder pool, never ahead of the Investors' Class B priorities, but no vote, other than any separate vote of the class that Law 4548/2018 requires on a resolution that harms its own rights (AE Article 4.3B; counsel note I-49), and never counted toward any AE vote, a Class B approval, the Investor Majority (Clause 3.4) or the Qualifying Closing minimum (Clause 3.1A); at each such holder's option they are instead sold to the AE for cash at the round price, only as far as the terms of the round provide the cash, payable only out of the round's proceeds at that closing (Clause 3.2). Class C shares may later be sold or granted by their holders to anyone, and in the hands of a buyer outside the Founder's Circle may convert into [Class B: owner to confirm], and count, only while that buyer is a qualifying buyer under Clause 3.4 (AE Article 4.3B). If the round's investors outside the Founder's Circle do not reach the Qualifying Closing minimum on their own, nothing converts (Clause 3.1A). Each holder of Reserve Shares makes the contribution, and signs whatever it needs, under the proxy, penalty and specific performance in Clause 5.1B and the proxy in (Deadlock) above; Clause 6.2A applies instead to a bad leaver's Reserve Shares. AE shares received for an unvested Reserve Share stay subject to the same vesting, and the return right then applies to them [in favour of the grantor / in favour of the AE: owner to choose], as the grant terms and Schedule 1 say (IKE Article 4.2D, Conversion; AE Article 4.3A). The term sheet may provide that up to [●]% of the round is invested in the IKE as new capital (Clause 3.2). The AE may be formed earlier (AE Article 1.2A), but Reserve Shares it holds before the Qualifying Closing stay without votes, and this Clause 2.4A keeps applying until the Interim Period ends. Each party votes for, and signs, whatever IKE Article 4.2D requires for the contribution and for the Reserve Shares to convert into ordinary voting shares in the AE's hands, as Clause 5.1B provides for new Prozente. From then every Clause of this Agreement on the AE, the Class B shares and the Investors applies. Each holder of Class A shares votes at the AE's General Meeting for the resolution issuing the Class B shares of the round, the conversion shares and the Class C shares, for the exclusion of the pre-emption right in favour of the round's investors and for any approval of a cash purchase of Reserve Shares (AE Articles 1.2A, 4.3A, Issue resolution, and 5.2). [Each holder of Class C shares votes in any separate class meeting that Law 4548/2018 Arts. 25(3) and 38(8) give it for a resolution that has the approval in AE Article 7.4, the separate Class B majority included, under the proxy, penalty and specific performance in Clause 5.1B: owner to confirm] (counsel note I-75).

## 3 – Investment

3.1 Each Investor subscribes for the Class B shares in Schedule 1, at the price stated there.

3.1A **The Qualifying Closing minimum.** The round becomes the Qualifying Closing only if Investors outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2) subscribe Class B shares of at least €[●] in cash at that closing on their own (IKE Article 4.2D, Conversion). The minimum is counted on the Investors' cash subscriptions [after deducting / before deducting: owner to choose] any cash price the AE pays for Reserve Shares under Clause 3.2. Conversion shares, Class C shares (AE Article 4.3B), any cash price for Reserve Shares under Clause 3.2 and any subscription by a person in the Founder's Circle do not count toward it. Subscriptions at a closing are conditional on the minimum being reached at that closing: until it is, the AE issues no Class B shares for them and before the increase is resolved, subscription money may be held under a subscription and escrow agreement [with a bank or notary, not controlled by a Founder Party: owner to confirm]; at the closing it is paid into the AE's special capital account at a credit institution, as Law 4548/2018 Art. 20(3) requires for capital paid in cash. The General Meeting resolution on the Class B increase states the minimum, allows partial coverage only down to that minimum (Law 4548/2018 Art. 28(1)), and sets a payment period ending no later than [date] and in any case within four months of the resolution's registration with GEMI (Law 4548/2018 Art. 20(2)); if the minimum is not paid in within that period, the increase fails, every subscription lapses and every payment is refunded in full. [Alternative, owner to choose instead: Class B shares subscribed at a closing below the minimum stay in issue and [count / do not count] toward a later closing's minimum.] If the minimum is not reached, there is no Qualifying Closing: nothing converts, every Reserve Share, whoever holds it, stays without votes in the IKE, and Clause 2.4A keeps applying. Schedule 1 discloses the outcome for Investors' subscriptions (Clause 4.3).

3.2 **Proceeds.** At the Qualifying Closing the Reserve Shares are contributed to the AE in exchange for AE shares (IKE Article 4.2D, Conversion), and the round's new money buys new AE shares at the round price; the proceeds stay with the AE, except the cash price for Reserve Shares that a holder of the founder pool elects to sell to the AE instead of converting them into Class C shares (IKE Article 4.2D, Conversion; AE Article 4.3B). That option exists only if, and as far as, the terms of the round provide the cash for it, and the price is payable only out of the round's proceeds at that closing. If the holders' elections exceed the cash the round provides, the number of Reserve Shares sold at the round price is reduced to what it covers, in proportion for each electing holder, and the rest convert into Class C shares at the Conversion Ratio. The AE never owes a holder of Reserve Shares a deferred balance. The AE invests the rest of the proceeds, and the proceeds of later rounds, in the IKE as capital (up to [●]% of the first round, as the term sheet provides, under IKE Articles 4.2B and 6.5), a shareholder loan or both, as the AE Board decides with Reserved Matters approval. Schedule 1 shows the split. Within two years of the AE's formation, a cash purchase of Reserve Shares also needs the prior approval of the AE's General Meeting and the valuation in AE Article 1.2A (Law 4548/2018 Art. 19); the price never exceeds that valuation, and any excess is cut back in the same way as an election that exceeds the cash.

3.3 **Use of funds.** The proceeds invested in the IKE under Clause 3.2, and only those, are used for the IKE's business plan in Schedule 2, which starts from the first product in Clause 2.3B; any cash price paid for Reserve Shares under Clause 3.2 is not. Any change of more than [20]% between budget lines needs Investor Majority approval.

3.4 "Investor Majority" means Investors holding more than 50% of the Class B shares. Class B shares acquired by a Founder Party (IKE Article 4.2E), including a person who was one in the previous [3] years, convert into Class B-X shares (AE Article 4.3C) and are not counted, for the Investor Majority or for a Class B approval under the AE Articles (AE Article 7.4). Neither are the excluded conversion shares, which are issued as Class B-X shares (AE Articles 4.3A and 4.3C): conversion shares held by a person who was in the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2) at the Qualifying Closing, or by a person who received the Reserve Shares they were issued for without payment. Class C shares (AE Article 4.3B) carry no vote and are never counted for the Investor Majority, a Class B approval or the Class B appointment right (AE Article 8.2); a Class C share converted into a Class B share in a buyer's hands counts only while it is held by a person outside the Founder's Circle who is a qualifying buyer of it: a holder that paid for it at least [the round price / fair value as a valuer who is not a Founder Party (IKE Article 4.2E) determines: owner to choose], and is not a nominee of a Founder Party, has no agreement or understanding with a Founder Party on voting it, proxies over it, its reacquisition or options over it, and did not receive financing for it from a Founder Party. The holder certifies this to the AE Board at its request; a converted share that fails the test, or whose holder does not certify when asked, converts into a Class B-X share (AE Articles 4.3B and 4.3C). An Investor Majority consent or approval, and a Class B approval under the AE Articles, cannot be given, and is not deemed given under Clause 5.2, while no Class B shares are held by Investors outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2).

## 4 – Valuation and Anti-Dilution

4.1 The pre-money valuation of the group for this round is €[●].

4.2 If the AE or the IKE issues new shares below the price paid by Investors, the Investors' Class B entitlement is adjusted on a broad-based weighted-average basis. An AE cannot simply re-rate shares already in issue, so the adjustment works through the AE Articles: the Class B shares are convertible preference shares (Law 4548/2018 Art. 38(3)), each converting into [one] ordinary voting share of a separate class [Class O: owner to name] (never Class A, which is reserved to the founder pool) on [an exit / a listing / the holder's request: owner to choose], and whenever the AE issues shares below the Class B issue price the conversion ratio is adjusted by the broad-based weighted-average formula written out in the AE Articles (AE Article 5.3). The issues listed in Clause 4.3 do not trigger an adjustment.

4.2A **Issues by the IKE.** If the IKE issues shares to third parties below the price per IKE share implied by the Class B issue price on a look-through basis, the parties will [adjust the Class B conversion ratio on the AE's look-through value / procure a compensatory issue of AE shares to the Class B holders, paid up at least at nominal value, with the pre-emption right excluded under Articles 26 and 27 of Law 4548/2018: owner to choose], and vote and sign whatever that needs [counsel to confirm (counsel note I-76)].

4.3 Shares transferred by the AE under the option pool in Clause 7, shares transferred by the AE to a Prozent under IKE Article 6.6, shares issued to the Prozente and the pool to keep their percentage under IKE Article 4.2B, every sale and grant of Reserve Shares under IKE Article 4.2D, their contribution to the AE at the Qualifying Closing, the conversion shares the AE issues for them at the Conversion Ratio (AE Article 4.3A), the Class C shares among them and the conversion of a Class C share into a Class B share in a buyer's hands (AE Article 4.3B), their conversion into ordinary voting shares and the transfer of a new Prozent's share out of them under IKE Article 4.2D, and shares issued with Investor Majority consent do not trigger an adjustment. Anti-dilution protects Investors from their subscription. Schedule 1 discloses plainly that the Reserve Shares were issued to the Founding Person as compensation for time invested before investors, that their economic rights belong to their holders until the Qualifying Closing, and that at it every Reserve Share is contributed to the AE for AE shares at the Conversion Ratio (Clause 2.4A, IKE Article 4.2D). It states: the holders of the founder pool and their shares, and the Constitution of the Founder Pool; the subscription price paid for the Reserve Shares; every sale and grant of Reserve Shares before the Qualifying Closing, with the holder, the number of shares, the price, if any, and any vesting; the Conversion Ratio and the AE shares each holder of Reserve Shares receives, and any cash price paid under Clause 3.2; that the Reserve Shares held by the founder pool at the Qualifying Closing become non-voting Class C shares of the AE with economic rights (dividends, liquidation, sale proceeds) defined on their own terms in AE Article 4.3B, whether or not other conversion shares are issued, and ranking [after / with: owner to choose, as in AE Articles 10.4 and 11.2], and never ahead of, the Investors' Class B dividend priority and liquidation preference, or are sold to the AE for cash at the round price only where the round provides the cash, so that the founder pool keeps the value of the Reserve Shares no Investor bought but its Class C shares never vote, other than any separate vote of the class that Law 4548/2018 requires on a resolution that harms its own rights (AE Article 4.3B; counsel note I-49), or count toward any AE vote, Class B approval, the Investor Majority or the Qualifying Closing minimum, and cannot outvote the Investors on their protections; that Class C shares may be sold or granted to anyone and, in the hands of a buyer outside the Founder's Circle, may convert into [Class B: owner to confirm] shares, and count, only while the buyer is a qualifying buyer (Clause 3.4); that the Investors outside the Founder's Circle must reach the Qualifying Closing minimum on their own, counted [after deducting / before deducting: owner to choose] any cash price for Reserve Shares, and that if they do not nothing converts and the Reserve Shares stay without votes in the IKE, and what happens to the Investors' subscriptions at that closing (Clause 3.1A); the part of the round, if any, that is new capital of the IKE (Clause 3.2); the distributions paid on the Reserve Shares to date; the Founder's economic interest until the Qualifying Closing (94% at incorporation, less Reserve Shares sold or granted outside the founder pool); that before the Qualifying Closing the Founder held 500 of the 560 votes in existence, with every majority counted on the total capital and needing a majority of the Independent Votes; and that after it the Founder, as Class A holder nominating a majority of the AE Board (AE Article 8.2, IKE Article 4.2C), directs the AE's votes in the IKE, initially 44% of the capital (440 shares), on every matter that is not a Reserved Matter. The Kassenprozent pool does not trade with the AE while the Founder controls it (IKE Articles 4.2C and 10A.8).

## 5 – Reserved Matters

5.1 Except as Clauses 5.1A and 5.1C provide for the Prozente, the following need Investor Majority consent, at both AE and IKE level. That consent cannot be given while no Class B shares are held by Investors outside the Founder's Circle (Clause 3.4); in the Interim Period, Clause 2.4A (Consents) applies:

- any matter listed in Article 6.5 of the IKE Articles or Article 7.4 of the AE Articles;
- annual budget approval, and any spending more than [15]% above budget;
- borrowing over €[●];
- hiring employees, given the agentic principle in IKE Article 2.2;
- changing the Agent Account minimum or purpose under IKE Article 11A;
- the pay and benefits of the Founding Person and of any other Founder Party (IKE Article 4.2E), including the Founding Person's pay as General Director (IKE Article 8.2B) and, separately, the Founding Person's written terms and fees as an Agent (Clause 6.5, IKE Article 11A.12), each set at arm's length;
- any amendment or waiver of the Brand Licence that harms the IKE, and any assignment, abandonment or encumbrance of a Brand Asset, other than a transfer to a holder of record under Pool Constitution Article 2A.3 (Brand Licence Clauses 8.1 and 9.2).

5.1A **New Prozente.** The creation of a new Prozent, including the amendment of the IKE Articles, the transfer of its share by the AE (or, in the Interim Period, by the holders of the founder pool out of the Reserve Shares they hold) and the admission of its holder, is not a Reserved Matter at AE or IKE level. It needs no consent other than the AE's approval under AE Article 7.5 (in the Interim Period, the approval of the IKE meeting under IKE Article 4.2D, New Prozente), after which the IKE shareholders' meeting passes the amendment under IKE Article 6.6, as the parties undertake in Clause 5.1B. The AE's approval needs at least two-thirds of the votes represented, all classes together, or at least 75% including a majority of the Class B votes cast to go beyond the limits of IKE Article 6.6.

5.1B **Voting undertaking.** Once the AE has approved a new Prozent under AE Article 7.5, or, in the Interim Period, once the IKE meeting has approved the charter under IKE Article 4.2D (New Prozente), each party votes its shares in the IKE for the resolutions needed to amend the IKE Articles and register the transfer of shares equal to 1% of the IKE's capital from the AE, or in the Interim Period the transfer of Reserve Shares from the holders of the founder pool, to the Prozent's holder, and signs whatever is needed to file them with GEMI. Each party also votes its IKE shares, on every capital increase of the IKE, for the limitation of the pre-emption right that IKE Article 4.2B requires, so that the Founder stays at exactly 50% and each Prozent and the pool keep their percentage.

- **Proxy.** Each party grants an independent proxy holder, [●] (a notary or lawyer who is not, and was not in the previous [3] years, a Founder Party (IKE Article 4.2E), is not a manager of the IKE or anyone they control within IKE Article 4.2C, and, after the Bootstrap Period, is outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2) on the schedule in IKE Article 9B.4; in the Bootstrap Period a member of the trusted circle may hold it, with disclosure and recusal under IKE Article 9B.2), an irrevocable proxy, given also in the interest of the other parties [Civil Code Art. 218], to vote its IKE shares for these resolutions. The proxy is not given to the IKE managers. If the proxy holder must be replaced (IKE Article 9B.4, death, incapacity or resignation), [the AMKE's General Assembly, without Founder Parties (AMKE Article 15.2) / the President of the Athens Bar Association: owner to choose] names a successor who meets this Clause, and each party and every holder of Reserve Shares grants the successor the same irrevocable proxy within [14] days; the proxy is also granted in advance to whoever holds the office from time to time [as far as Civil Code Art. 218 allows: counsel note I-46]. Until the successor acts, the proxy holder is treated as unable to act (Clause 2.4A, Deadlock).
- **Penalty.** A party that breaches this Clause pays a penalty of €[●] (Civil Code Art. 404), without prejudice to specific performance, including a court judgment that stands in for the party's vote (Code of Civil Procedure Art. 949).
- **Exception.** The undertaking does not apply to a charter that breaches IKE Article 6.6 or Greek law. A resolution under IKE Article 6.6 only adds a Prozent. It may not change any existing Prozent, IKE Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C, or the limits in IKE Article 6.6; any such change follows Clause 5.1C or the Reserved Matters, and the undertaking does not cover it.

5.1C **Permanence.** The Prozente are permanent. No party votes for, or consents to, removing a Prozent, lowering its contribution or changing its purpose, amending the IKE Articles listed in IKE Article 10A.5C in any way that reduces a Prozent, its share, its revenue, the pension guarantees or the pension-share governance, or a voluntary dissolution of the IKE that mandatory law does not require (IKE Article 12.1), unless every IKE shareholder consents in writing and the AE's General Meeting has so resolved unanimously and every holder of AE shares that carry votes, of every class, has consented in writing (IKE Article 10A.5, AE Article 7.6). Each holder of AE shares that carry votes gives or withholds that written consent to the AE Board itself, before the Board votes, and the AE Board does not vote or consent without it. Accrued pension rights are preserved in any case (IKE Article 10A.5A). Mandatory insolvency, dissolution and liquidation law prevails.

5.1D **Adherence and entrenchment.** The AMKE is bound by Clauses 5.1B and 5.1C for all its IKE shares. It votes the pension shares as the Pensionskasse board decides (IKE Article 10A.9D), and the Pensionsprozent Agreement (IKE Article 10A.9B) makes the board's decisions subject to these Clauses [as far as mandatory pension law allows]. Every person who becomes an IKE shareholder, including under IKE Article 5.6, adheres to Clauses 5.1B to 5.1D (Clause 12.3, IKE Articles 5.3 and 5.6). Despite Clause 12.2, Clauses 5.1C and 5.1D may be amended only with the written consent of every party.

5.1E **The Brand Licence.** The terms of the Brand Licence are a protected arrangement of the group. The Founder, and every holder of founder-pool shares, honours it and does not end, suspend or restrict it other than under Brand Licence Clause 7. No party votes for, or consents to, a change of the Brand Licence that harms the IKE without the consent in Clause 5.1. No Founder Party causes, votes for or procures a Prozent Breach (Brand Licence Clause 7.1), and the Founder cannot rely on one that a Founder Party, or a manager proposed or nominated by one, caused: it gives only specific performance. The AMKE, any Investor or the independent proxy holder in Clause 5.1B may cure a Prozent Breach on the IKE's behalf, at the IKE's cost (Brand Licence Clause 7.2). In a dispute under Brand Licence Clause 7, the IKE acts and agrees on the arbitrator only through managers who are not Founder Parties and were not proposed or nominated by one, or failing them through the independent proxy holder or a nominee of the Investor Majority, and the AMKE may join (Brand Licence Clause 10.2). If the Brand Licence ends, each party votes its IKE shares for the change of the IKE's name and distinctive title under IKE Article 1.1A within the transition period, under the proxy, penalty and specific performance in Clause 5.1B, and, in the Interim Period, the independent proxy holder in Clause 5.1B casts the votes of the Reserve Shares for it under the proxy in Clause 2.4A (IKE Article 4.2D, Votes). This Clause binds every party and everyone who adheres under Clause 12.3, as part of every adherence to Clauses 5.1B to 5.1D.

5.1F **Board of the AE.** Each party that holds AE shares carrying votes in the election of the AE Board votes them at every General Meeting of the AE for the candidates that the holders of Class A shares propose, through the Founder Representative, for a majority of the Board (AE Article 8.2), under the proxy, penalty and specific performance in Clause 5.1B. This Clause does not cover the member the Class B holders appoint directly (AE Article 8.2).

5.2 Consent is not unreasonably withheld and is deemed given if no reply is received within [10] working days.

## 6 – Founding Person's Commitments

6.0 **Personal.** The commitments in this Clause 6 are personal to the Founding Person (IKE Article 4.2E). Leaving means the Founding Person ceasing to work for the group in every role in which the Founding Person works for it, as Agent and as manager. The founder pool continues whatever happens to the Founding Person: its other holders keep their shares and rights, and the block keeps voting under the Constitution of the Founder Pool. A Founding Person who is a bad leaver may not be the Founder Representative or a member of the Founder Council [for [●] years: owner to decide], and while the Founding Person is the only holder an interim Founder Representative is chosen under the fixed rule in the Constitution of the Founder Pool (Pool Constitution Article 14.3).

6.1 **Vesting.** The Class F shares issued to the Founding Person at incorporation (equal to 50% of the IKE's capital, initially 500 shares) vest over [4] years, with a [1]-year cliff. Vesting applies to the economic and voting rights on the shares, not to their ownership: the Founder holds exactly 50% at all times (IKE Article 4.2B), and no leaver event leads to a transfer of a founder-pool share. Unvested shares the Founding Person has given away without payment [in the [12] months before the bad-leaver date, or at any time while unvested: owner may confirm] stay unvested in the hands of anyone who holds them, and the assignment and proxy below travel with them. If the Founding Person leaves before full vesting, then from the leaving date, on the unvested shares:

- **Economic rights.** Dividends and other distributions are paid to [the AE / the other shareholders pro rata, the Prozent shares and the pool excluded], under the assignment of distributions the Founding Person grants on signing (Clause 6.2B), binding on anyone who receives the shares without payment;
- **Voting rights.** Their votes are cast, outside the Founder's block (IKE Article 4.2E, One block), as [the independent proxy holder in Clause 5.1B / the Investor Majority] directs, under an irrevocable proxy granted on signing [as far as Law 4072/2012 allows].

[Or another mechanism the owner chooses, provided it transfers no share.]

6.2 **Good and bad leaver.** A good leaver keeps all rights on vested shares. On unvested shares Clause 6.1 applies, and [the AE] pays the holder the fair value of the rights given up. If the Founding Person is a bad leaver, dismissed for serious misconduct or in material breach, the economic and voting rights on all the Class F shares the Founding Person holds, or has given away without payment [in the [12] months before the bad-leaver date, or at any time while unvested: owner may confirm], are lost under Clause 6.1, against payment of the lower of their nominal and fair value. In neither case is a founder-pool share transferred.

6.2A **Reserve Shares.** The Reserve Shares (IKE Article 4.2D) are for time already invested and are not subject to vesting under Clauses 6.1 and 6.2. If the Founding Person is a bad leaver in the Interim Period, the Reserve Shares the Founding Person holds, or has given away without payment [in the [12] months before the bad-leaver date, or at any time while unvested: owner may confirm], other than grants made as pay for work for the nilware.io group (IKE Article 4.2D, Sales and grants), are not converted at the Conversion Ratio: at the Qualifying Closing they are transferred to the AE [or to a successor holding vehicle the IKE designates] at the lower of their nominal value and their fair value, payable out of the round's proceeds, in place of the conversion. Reserve Shares the Founding Person sold for value before the bad-leaver date are not affected. From the bad-leaver date, distributions on the Reserve Shares this Clause reaches are paid by the IKE to the Escrow Holder for the eventual buyer, under the assignment of distributions in Clause 6.2B, which binds anyone who received the shares without payment, and the Founding Person, and anyone who received them without payment, receives none of them. [Alternative, owner to choose instead: the IKE Articles provide, under Law 4072/2012 Art. 100(4), that for at most ten years from the bad-leaver date the Reserve Shares this Clause reaches take no part in profits, their share being held by the IKE in a segregated account for the eventual buyer (IKE Article 4.2D, Economic rights); without such an Article the IKE may not withhold them (counsel note I-79).] If no Qualifying Closing takes place within [●] years of the bad-leaver date, the IKE designates a successor holding vehicle, and those Reserve Shares are offered to it at the same price, with the distributions held by the Escrow Holder for it [owner to decide].

6.2B **Assignment of distributions.** A declared distribution is the holder's own claim against the IKE, so the economics in Clauses 2.4A (Leaver terms), 6.1 and 6.2A work by assignment, not by the IKE withholding payment.
