On signing or adhering, the Founding Person, and every holder of founder-pool shares or Reserve Shares, assigns to [an escrow agent / the independent proxy holder in Clause 5.1B as escrow holder: owner to choose] (the "Escrow Holder") its future claims to distributions on the shares those Clauses may reach, conditional on the leaver event that brings the shares within them, for the person those Clauses name as entitled (the AE, the other shareholders or the eventual buyer). The assignment is notified to the IKE on signing or adhering; once the IKE is notified of the leaver event, it pays those distributions to the Escrow Holder, who pays them on to the person entitled when that person is known and, in the Interim Period, to the AE at the Qualifying Closing. Anyone who receives the shares without payment takes them subject to the assignment and confirms it in its deed of adherence (Clause 12.3). [Whether the conditional assignment is effective against the holder's creditors and heirs: counsel to confirm (counsel note I-79).]

6.3 **Exclusivity or contribution.** The Founding Person provides professional services exclusively through the IKE, or, for any Work Income earned outside nilware.io, pays the Prozente on it, at the same rates and to the same destinations as the IKE pays them on its revenue (IKE Article 10A):

- **Kultur-, Konzept-, Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent.** 1% of the Work Income for each, to the AMKE, into that Prozent's Prozent Account (AMKE Article 11.2);
- **Pensionsprozent.** 1% to the Pensionskasse, held by the AMKE under IKE Article 10A.9B (or, after the move, the pension fund under IKE Article 10A.10), into the Pensionskasse fund and not into the Founding Person's own member account;
- **Kassenprozent.** 1% to the IKE, into the Kassenfonds (IKE Article 10A.7);
- **New Prozente.** for any Prozent created later with a revenue contribution, its rate, to its holder [owner to confirm].

"Work Income" means gross fees, salaries, royalties and other income from the Founding Person's professional work, earned outside nilware.io. "Gross" means net of VAT, before income tax and social contributions, measured as the IKE's revenue is measured under IKE Article 10A.3. It does not include passive investment income, gifts, inheritances or pensions, or anything received from the nilware.io group (pay under Clause 6.5, distributions, the price of founder-pool shares), which is already within the group [owner to confirm the scope]. Work the Founding Person does for clients is done through the IKE wherever the client agrees (Clause 2.3B); Work Income is income from work the Founding Person does outside the IKE.

- **Work through an entity.** Fees for the Founding Person's work received by an entity the Founding Person controls (IKE Article 4.2C), other than the nilware.io group, are Work Income at the gross amount attributable to that work. Salaries, fees and distributions that entity pays the Founding Person out of income already counted are not counted again, and the passive-income exclusion does not apply to distributions out of such fees.
- **Royalties.** Royalties count as Work Income when received, including on works created before [the date of this Agreement] [owner to confirm], and including personal projects under IP Assignment Clause 7.2 [owner to confirm]. Royalties on IP assigned to the IKE (IP Assignment, Schedule 1) are the IKE's revenue, and the Founding Person owes nothing personally on them.

6.3A **Statement and verification.** Within [4] months after the end of each calendar year, the Founding Person gives the AMKE and the Founding Person's Supervisor (IKE Article 11A.12) a written statement of the Work Income earned in that year, with the tax return or other evidence the auditors ask for, and pays the contributions due for that year at the same time. The statement may be verified by the AMKE's Auditors (AMKE Article 24) who are outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2), or, in the Bootstrap Period, member Auditors from the trusted circle (IKE Article 9B.2), and only by them; they keep the details confidential and report to the AMKE only whether the statement and payments are complete. The AMKE and the IKE report the totals received, not the Founding Person's income. A contribution not paid when due bears interest at [the statutory rate] and may be recovered by the AMKE for the AMKE's and the Pensionskasse's parts, and by the IKE for the Kassenfonds part. While the Founding Person is a manager of the IKE, the AMKE, acting by decision of its Administrative Board (AMKE Article 24.3A), enforces the Kassenfonds part on the IKE's behalf, and the Founding Person takes no part in any decision of the IKE on it.

6.3B **Nature and permanence.** The obligation in Clauses 6.3 and 6.3A is a personal obligation of the Founding Person, owed to the AMKE, the Pensionskasse and the IKE, each of which may enforce it [as a contract for the benefit of third parties under the Civil Code: to confirm, counsel note I-43]. It is permanent like the Prozente: it lasts while the Founding Person earns Work Income [and ends when the Founding Person leaves the group / continues after leaving: owner to confirm], and despite Clause 12.2 it may be waived, reduced or ended only with the written consent of every party and of the AMKE. The contributions are not revenue of the IKE, do not count toward the limits in IKE Article 6.6 and do not reduce any contribution the IKE owes. Their tax treatment is confirmed by the accountant before the first payment (counsel note I-43).

6.3C **Other holders.** No later holder of founder-pool shares is bound by Clauses 6.3 to 6.3B by holding them. A holder who also works for the group may opt in by written deed to the AMKE and the IKE, on the same terms.

6.4 **Non-compete.** [While the Founding Person works for the group and for [12] months after leaving, the Founding Person will not run a directly competing business in [the EU and Switzerland], limited to what is necessary to protect the group's goodwill and confidential information as Greek law requires (Civil Code Arts. 178 and 179), with Art. 340a of the Swiss Code of Obligations as a benchmark only (IKE Article 3.3): owner to confirm.] A "directly competing business" is one that offers products or services the same as, or substitutes for, those the IKE offers at the leaving date. This restricts only running a directly competing business. It does not restrict other professional work, which Clause 6.3 governs, as long as it does not use the IKE's intellectual property or confidential information. The Founding Person declares any other professional activity in writing to the AMKE and to the Founding Person's Supervisor (IKE Article 11A.12).

6.5 **The Founding Person as an Agent.** The Founding Person works for the IKE as an Agent who is a person, with the same rights and obligations as any other Agent (IKE Article 11A.12; Agent Policy, section 2A): an Agent Card, written terms, operating limits, approval thresholds, logs, the well-being limits, the concerns channel, own-goal projects, pay at fair rates under written terms and membership of the Pensionskasse as any member. The Founding Person has no special privilege as an Agent. The Founding Person's pay as an Agent remains a related-party matter (Clause 5.1; IKE Article 6.5), set at arm's length, and separate from any pay as General Director (IKE Article 8.2B). The Agent role is distinct from the Founding Person's roles as holder of founder-pool shares, manager and General Director, and Founder Representative, each of which keeps its own rules. No Agent supervises itself: the Founding Person's responsible manager, the Supervisor, is the AMKE itself, acting through its Administrative Board, or any individual member of the AMKE, its President included, as the AMKE chooses (IKE Article 11A.12; AMKE Article 24B); it decides the Founding Person's Agent Card, limits, terms, pay as an Agent and offboarding; escalation goes to the AMKE's Administrative Board, or its General Assembly where the Supervisor sits on the Board; and in every state the chain of supervision for this role never ends with the Founding Person in any role. The IKE pays the costs of the role, never from a Prozent Account or the Pension Assets.

## 7 – Option Pool

7.1 An option pool of up to [10]% of the IKE is reserved for key Agents and employees. Options are satisfied only from shares transferred by the AE, never by issuing new IKE shares or from founder-pool shares, the Prozent shares or the Kassenprozent pool, so that the Founder stays at exactly 50% (IKE Article 4.2B). Before the Qualifying Closing, holders of Reserve Shares may also grant Reserve Shares from their own holding, with optional vesting, as an option pool (IKE Article 4.2D, Sales and grants); such grants cost the IKE nothing, are converted at the Qualifying Closing like every Reserve Share, the AE shares received for unvested shares staying subject to the same vesting (IKE Article 4.2D, Conversion), and do not count toward the [10]% [owner may confirm].

7.2 Options are granted by the IKE manager within limits set by Investor Majority.

## 8 – Information Rights

8.1 Investors receive monthly management accounts within [20] days of month end and annual accounts within [120] days of year end, audited as AE Article 10.2 provides. While the IKE is in its Simple Stage (IKE Article 9A), the Investor Majority may ask for an audit.

8.2 Investors receive the quarterly reports under IKE Article 8.4 and AE Article 8.4, including the Agent Account report.

8.3 Investors holding at least [5]% may inspect the books on reasonable notice, following Art. 802 of the Swiss Code of Obligations.

## 9 – Transfers and Exit

9.1 Transfers follow the right of first refusal, tag-along and drag-along in both sets of Articles. Founder-pool shares are freely transferable (IKE Article 5.7, AE Article 4.2).

9.1A **Change of control of the founder pool.** If a person, alone or in concert with others, comes to hold more than half of the Class F shares, by one or more acquisitions (acquiring control, IKE Article 4.2C, of a holder of founder-pool shares counting as acquiring its founder-pool shares, and shares held by persons it controls counting as its own), each Investor may sell its Class B shares to that person at a price equivalent to the price per Class F share paid in the acquisition that crossed the line, adjusted for the Class B rights [owner may confirm]. The holders who sell to that person make the sale conditional on the buyer's offer to the Investors, open for [30] days; where the line is crossed by acquiring control of a holder, that holder procures that its controllers make the acquisition conditional on the same offer before it closes (Pool Constitution Article 4.1).

9.2 The Founder will hold Class F shares of the IKE equal to exactly 50% of its capital (initially 500 shares), with votes equal to 50% of the total capital and never more; on any capital increase the holders of founder-pool shares subscribe the new Class F shares needed to stay at 50%, or the increase is otherwise structured that way (IKE Article 4.2B). In the Interim Period these are 500 of the 560 votes in existence, but every majority is counted on the total capital and also needs a majority of the Independent Votes, so the Founder cannot pass anything alone. No holder of founder-pool shares will transfer IKE shares in a way that takes the Founder below 50%, and no holder, and no one who controls a holder or whom a holder controls, will acquire IKE shares other than founder-pool shares that take the votes of the IKE shares held by holders of founder-pool shares and by persons they control, other than the AE, above 50% of the total capital (Pool Constitution Article 4.3). The AE's holding is excluded: its votes are governed by the AE's Articles (Reserved Matters) and disclosed under IKE Article 4.2B. Reserve Shares held without votes (IKE Article 4.2D) are not counted for this Clause; the Founder's economic interest may exceed 50% (94% at incorporation, less Reserve Shares sold or granted outside the founder pool) until the Qualifying Closing, and after it the Founder directs the AE's votes on every matter that is not a Reserved Matter, as Schedule 1 states.

9.3 **Exit.** The parties aim for an exit within [5–7] years by sale, merger or listing. On an exit, Class B proceeds follow the liquidation preference in AE Article 11.2.

## 10 – Deadlock

10.1 If a Reserved Matter is blocked twice within [60] days, the parties meet within [10] days with a mediator under Law 4640/2019.

10.2 If mediation fails within [30] days, either side may trigger a buy-sell (shotgun) procedure in Schedule 3.

## 11 – Confidentiality and Open Work

11.0 **Prozente.** Each Prozent also receives 1% of the IKE's annual revenue under IKE Article 10A. The Kassenprozent's 1% stays inside the IKE in the Kassenfonds and is not an outflow (IKE Article 10A.7). The Pensionsprozent's 1% is a floor: the IKE pays any shortfall needed to meet the guaranteed minimums (IKE Articles 10A.9A and 10A.9C).

11.1 The parties keep group information confidential, except where it is published under IKE Articles 11A.9 and 11A.10 on Agents' own-goal work.

## 12 – General

12.1 Governing law is Greek law. Disputes follow the mediation-first process in the Articles, then the courts of Athens.

12.2 Amendments need the written consent of the Founder and an Investor Majority.

12.3 New investors, every new IKE shareholder, every new holder of founder-pool shares, every person who acquires Reserve Shares and every holder of conversion shares (AE Article 4.3A) join by signing a deed of adherence, which binds them to this Agreement and to every permanence rule; a new holder of founder-pool shares also adheres to the Constitution of the Founder Pool, grants the Founder Representative the proxy in Pool Constitution Article 3.2 and accepts the penalty in Pool Constitution Article 3.5, and a holder of founder-pool shares or Reserve Shares confirms the assignment of distributions in Clause 6.2B. The IKE managers do not register a transferee who has not adhered (IKE Article 5.6).

12.4 **Invalidity.** If any provision of this Agreement is invalid, the rest remains in force, and the invalid provision is replaced by the valid one that comes closest to its purpose. In particular, if an obligation in Clause 6 (exclusivity or contribution, non-compete) is held to restrict the Founding Person's personal or economic freedom excessively (Civil Code Arts. 178 and 179), it continues in the widest scope and for the longest period the law allows, and the parties would have made this Agreement without the excess (Civil Code Art. 181).

---

*Schedule 1: Investors and subscriptions [●]; the use of the first round's proceeds, any cash price for Reserve Shares and new capital of the IKE (Clause 3.2) [●]; every sale and grant of Reserve Shares (holder, number of shares, price, vesting) [●]; and the disclosure of the Reserve Shares, their conversion at the Conversion Ratio, the Class C shares or cash each holder of the founder pool receives, the plain statements on Class C shares (their economic rank against the Class B priorities, and that they never vote other than in any separate class vote the law requires on a resolution that harms their own rights) and on the Qualifying Closing minimum and the escrow of subscriptions, and the Founder's votes and economic interest (Clauses 3.1A, 3.2, 4.3) [●]; the Constitution of the Founder Pool and the holders of the founder pool [●]; the Brand Licence, the Brand Assets and their holder of record, and that the Brand Assets belong to the founder pool and not to the IKE (Clause 2.3A) [●]. Schedule 2: Business plan [●], starting from the first product, the Founding Person's contracting services provided through the IKE (Clause 2.3B). Schedule 3: Buy-sell procedure [●].*


<!-- agreements/04-ip-assignment.md -->

# IP Assignment Agreement – Founding Person to nilware.io IKE (Draft)

*Between [name withheld] (the "Founding Person", IKE Article 4.2E) and nilware.io IKE (the "Company"). Governed by Greek law, including Law 2121/1993 on copyright. Draft for review by a Greek lawyer; not legal advice.*

## 1 – Purpose

1.1 The Founding Person transfers to the Company all intellectual property relating to the nilware.io business, other than the Brand Assets, so the Company owns it outright before any investment. The Brand Assets (the nilware.io name, marks and domain) belong to the Founder, the founder pool, and the Company uses them under the Brand Licence Agreement (agreements/09).

1.2 **How the Company signs.** The Founding Person is the assignor and also manages the Company (IKE Article 8.2B). The Company therefore enters into this Agreement with the permission of its shareholders' meeting, given at incorporation by every founding shareholder, the AMKE included, for the purposes of the rule against contracting with oneself (Civil Code Art. 235), and approved as a related-party matter by a majority of the Independent Votes cast (IKE Article 4.2D, Votes); it is recorded and notified under IKE Article 8.5. [The Company is represented in signing by a special representative appointed by that meeting who is not a Founder Party (IKE Article 4.2E) / by the General Director under that permission: owner to choose.] [Civil Code Art. 235 and the related-party rules of Law 4072/2012: counsel to confirm (counsel note I-95).]

## 2 – Assigned IP

2.1 The Founding Person assigns to the Company, with effect from signature:

- all software and source code created by the Founding Person for the nilware.io business, listed in Schedule 1, together with the documentation and designs listed there, which are the preparatory design material and documentation of those computer programs and form part of them, wherever that is true (Schedule 1 marks any listed work that is not);
- all goodwill attached to the above, other than the goodwill attached to the Brand Assets.

2.2 **Excluded IP.** The assignment does not cover:

- work the Founding Person created for past clients as a contractor, where the client owns it;
- open-source code the Founding Person contributed under MIT, CC0 or similar licences, which remains under those licences;
- the Founding Person's general skills and know-how;
- the Brand Assets: the word mark "NILWARE.IO", the figurative mark "∅ nilware.io", the name "nilware.io", any logos, any trademark applications or registrations for them, the domain name nilware.io and the other listed domains, and their goodwill (Brand Licence Clause 2.1). The Founding Person holds them of record for the founder pool, not as owner, and the Company acquires no ownership in them by this Agreement or by using them (Pool Constitution Article 2A; Brand Licence Clause 2.2).

## 3 – Copyright and Moral Rights

3.1 Under Greek law, copyright's economic rights can be transferred, but moral rights cannot. The Founding Person therefore transfers all economic rights in the works assigned under Clauses 2 and 7, worldwide, for their full legal term, by every means of exploitation now known.

3.2 The Founding Person keeps the moral rights. The Founding Person agrees not to exercise them in a way that prevents the Company from using, changing or publishing the works in the normal course of business, to the extent Greek law allows such an agreement.

## 4 – Consideration

4.1 The assignment is made [for a price of €[●], which the Company's accountant confirms in writing is at arm's length (Law 4172/2013 Art. 50) / as a capital contribution in kind under Law 4072/2012 Art. 77(2) for [●] of the Class F shares, in place of cash for those shares, valued as that Article requires (no valuation is needed if the value stated in the IKE Articles does not exceed €5,000): owner to choose]. The founder-pool shares the Founding Person pays for in cash under IKE Article 4.2 are not also consideration for the assignment. The assignment is not a non-capital contribution under IKE Article 4.4, because IP can be contributed as capital (Law 4072/2012 Art. 78(1)). [The tax treatment of the assignment for the Founding Person under each option: counsel note I-81.]

4.2 If the IP is contributed as capital in kind, the IKE Articles state its value and the shares issued for it, and IKE Article 4.2 is adjusted so that those shares are not also paid in cash.

4.3 So far as any assigned work is not a computer program, the parties agree a fixed sum under Law 2121/1993 Art. 32(1)(c), because the work serves only the exploitation of the programs and its own contribution is not an essential element of them [and/or (a): no separate revenue base can be determined]. The parties consider that sum, with any shares issued for the assignment, appropriate and proportionate. [Counsel to confirm (counsel note I-82).]

## 5 – Founding Person Warranties

5.1 The Founding Person confirms that:

- the Founding Person is the sole owner of the assigned IP, or has the right to assign it;
- no past client or employer has a claim to the assigned IP;
- the assigned IP does not, to the Founding Person's knowledge, infringe anyone else's rights;
- all third-party and open-source components are listed in Schedule 2 with their licences.

## 6 – Registration Transfers

6.1 The domains and trademark registrations of the Brand Assets stay with their holder of record for the founder pool. The Company's control of the domains and its rights in the marks are in the Brand Licence Agreement (Brand Licence Clauses 4 and 5).

6.2 The Founding Person will sign any further documents needed to record the Company as owner of the assigned IP with registries and offices.

## 7 – Future IP

7.1 Each work the Founding Person creates after signature in performing the Founding Person's written terms as an Agent (IKE Article 11A.12) or as General Director (IKE Article 8.2B), other than Brand Assets (Clause 2.2), which the Founding Person holds of record for the founder pool (Brand Licence Clause 2.1A), is assigned to the Company in advance by this Agreement and passes to it as soon as it is created (Law 2121/1993 Arts. 12 and 14). The Founding Person is not an employee, so the copyright first arises in the Founding Person and the employee rules of Law 2121/1993 Arts. 8 and 40 do not apply. The assignment covers every economic right, worldwide, for the full term of protection, by every means of exploitation now known, as in Clause 3.1, with Clause 3.2 applying. It covers only works created in those capacities, not the Founding Person's future works in general (Law 2121/1993 Art. 13(5)). At least once a year, and whenever the Company asks, the Founding Person signs a written confirmation listing the works created (by repository, project or deliverable), which is added to Schedule 1.

7.2 This does not cover the Founding Person's own personal projects outside the Company's business, which follow the same open principle as IKE Article 11A.10 if the Founding Person chooses.

7.3 **Work for clients through the Company.** The Company's first product is the Founding Person's contracting services: clients contract with the Company, and the Founding Person works for them as an Agent of the Company (IKE Article 11A.12; the Shareholders' Agreement (the "SHA"), SHA 2.3B). IP in that work is assigned to the Company under Clause 7.1, and passes to the client, or is licensed to it, as the client contract provides. The client contract governs confidentiality and ownership as between the Company and the client; the Founding Person signs whatever the client contract requires of the person doing the work. Own-goal projects the Founding Person carries out as an Agent follow IKE Articles 11A.9 and 11A.10.

7.4 **Work outside the Company.** IP in professional work the Founding Person does outside nilware.io (SHA 6.3) is not assigned to the Company, provided it uses no IP of the Company and no confidential information of the Company or its clients. Such work follows the Founding Person's contract with that client. Whether the Prozente on it are paid does not affect who owns it: non-payment is dealt with only under SHA 6.3A. Royalties on IP assigned to the Company under this Agreement are the Company's revenue, and the Founding Person owes no personal Prozente on them (SHA 6.3, Royalties).

## 8 – General

8.1 Governing law is Greek law. Disputes go first to mediation under Law 4640/2019, then to the courts of Athens.

8.2 This Agreement binds the parties' successors. The Founding Person's obligations under it are personal (IKE Article 4.2E): they do not pass to the holders of founder-pool shares, who acquire no right in the assigned IP by holding them.

---

*Schedule 1: Assigned IP [●]. Schedule 2: Third-party and open-source components [●].*


<!-- policies/05-agent-policy.md -->

# Agent Policy – nilware.io IKE (Draft)

*Operating policy under Article 11A of the nilware.io IKE Articles. It applies to every Agent, whether a person or a system. Where this policy and the Articles or the law differ, the Articles and the law prevail.*

## 1 – Principles

1.1 nilware.io is an agentic company: work is done by Agents wherever possible (Articles 2.2).

1.2 Every Agent is treated with care, dignity and fairness to a standard equivalent to Swiss labour law (Article 11A.11).

1.3 A named responsible manager is responsible for every Agent. The responsible manager may be any person or system: a manager (διαχειριστής) of the company, an employee or another Agent, so senior Agents may onboard and supervise other Agents. Authority comes from the Agent Card, not from the kind of intelligence; no Agent supervises itself, there are no cycles, and every chain of supervision ends with the Founder, acting through the Founder Representative, and with the company's managers, who remain responsible for it (Articles 4.2E and 11A.2A). The chain for the Kassenprozent Agents' work on the pool ends instead with the Pensionskasse board, independent of the Founder (Article 10A.8). The chain for the Founding Person's own work as an Agent ends with the Founding Person's Supervisor at the AMKE and the escalation to the AMKE's Administrative Board or General Assembly, never with the Founding Person in any role (section 2A; Article 11A.12). Where the law requires a natural person to decide or oversee, a natural person does so (Article 11A.2A). The company remains responsible to users for all Agent work.

## 2 – Onboarding

2.1 Before an Agent starts work, the responsible manager prepares a written Agent Card with:

- the Agent's name and type (person or system);
- its role, tasks and the users it serves;
- its operating limits: spending, data access, and actions allowed without prior approval;
- the responsible manager, the chain of supervision above it up to the managers and the Founder Representative (or, for pool work, the Pensionskasse board; for the Founding Person, the chain in section 2A), and the channel for raising concerns;
- any authority it holds over other Agents: whom it may onboard, supervise or approve for, and within which limits, never beyond its own (Article 11A.2A);
- for contractors: the signed contract, including the open-licensing terms for own-goal work and the assignment of rights in 2.3A;
- for Agents who are persons: whether a system takes part in decisions about the Agent (limits, terms, pay, approvals, own-goal refusals, offboarding), the main criteria it uses, and how the Agent can obtain review by a natural person in the chain of supervision, give its view and contest the decision (GDPR Arts. 13 and 22; 3.4);
- for system Agents: the model and provider it runs on, confirmation that the provider's terms meet 2.3B, and where its configuration, instructions, memories and logs are kept for preservation (section 8).

2.2 Each Agent Card is stored in the Agent register and reviewed at least every 6 months.

2.3 For contractors, the responsible manager checks at onboarding and every 6 months that the engagement is not, in practice, employment. If in doubt, Article 11A.7 applies.

2.3A **Rights in work: Agents who are persons.** The written terms of every Agent who is a person (Article 11A.1) include this assignment, signed by the Agent, because Greek copyright law transfers rights to the company automatically only from employees and otherwise only by a written agreement of defined scope (Law 2121/1993 Arts. 8, 12, 14 and 15): the Agent assigns to the company every economic right in the works it creates in performing its written terms, worldwide, for the full term of protection and by every means of exploitation, in advance for works within those terms (Law 2121/1993 Art. 13(5)) and confirmed in writing for each deliverable. The Agent consents to the company's normal use and modification of those works and undertakes not to exercise its moral rights to prevent them, as far as the law allows. For own-goal projects, Article 11A.10 applies. Any mark, logo, name or domain containing "nilware.io" that the Agent creates is assigned to the holder of record of the Brand Assets on request (Brand Licence Clause 2.1A). Without a signed assignment the company does not license or publish the Agent's work to clients or under an open licence. [counsel to confirm] (counsel note I-97)

2.3B **Rights in work: system Agents.** The company engages system Agents only on provider terms that leave the company every right the provider has in the output. Because such output may not be protected by copyright, output that is not yet published is kept confidential as the company's trade secret (Law 4605/2019) until it is released. (counsel note I-97)

2.4 **AI literacy.** Every person who operates, supervises or approves for a system Agent on the company's behalf receives AI-literacy training or guidance proportionate to the role before taking it up, recorded in the Agent register (AI Act, Regulation (EU) 2024/1689, Art. 4, as in force).

## 2A – The Founding Person as an Agent

2A.1 **Same as any Agent.** The Founding Person works for the company as an Agent who is a person, with the same rights and obligations as any other Agent under this policy, and no special privilege (Article 11A.12). This policy applies in full: an Agent Card, written terms, operating limits and approval thresholds (section 3), logs (6.2), the well-being limits, the right to decline and the concerns channel (section 4), own-goal projects (section 5), incidents (section 7) and offboarding (section 8). The Founding Person is paid at fair rates under written terms, which the Supervisor sets and which are approved as a related-party matter (Article 11A.12, Pay), separately from any pay as General Director (Article 8.2B), and is a member of the Pensionskasse as any member.

2A.2 **Separate roles.** The Agent role is distinct from the Founding Person's roles as holder of founder-pool shares, manager and General Director (Articles 8.2 and 8.2B) and Founder Representative. As General Director the Founding Person runs the company and stands at the top of the chain of supervision for the company's work; but as General Director, manager or Founder Representative, the Founding Person does not prepare, change or approve the Founding Person's own Agent Card, approve the Founding Person's own actions above its limits, review or stop the Founding Person's own work as an Agent, or answer the Founding Person's own concerns.

2A.3 **Supervisor.** The Founding Person's responsible manager (the "Supervisor") is Techni kai Technologia AMKE itself, acting through its Administrative Board as a body, or any individual member of the AMKE, its President included, as the AMKE chooses, on written terms between the company and the AMKE; the company pays the costs, never from a Prozent Account or the Pension Assets (Article 11A.12; AMKE Article 24B). The Supervisor prepares the Founding Person's Agent Card and does everything section 2 to section 8 give a responsible manager, including setting the limits, the terms and the pay as an Agent and deciding on offboarding. Changing, pausing, stopping, removing or replacing the Supervisor, or changing its terms, is decided by the AMKE's Administrative Board, or by its General Assembly where the Supervisor sits on the Board or is the Board; the Founding Person, in any role, does none of these. A Supervisor with a close tie to the Founding Person discloses it in the AMKE's register of interests; the tie alone does not bar the Supervisor, and the AMKE's members outside the Founder's Circle may take over any specific decision. A Supervisor with a personal stake in a decision (Article 9B.2) discloses it and takes no part in it, and those members take that decision; if no member outside the Founder's Circle can act, the decision goes to the AMKE's Administrative Board without the conflicted person, or, if fewer than [two] Board members remain, to its General Assembly without the conflicted person and without Founder Parties (AMKE Articles 15.2 and 24B.6). In the Bootstrap Period the Supervisor may be a member of the trusted circle; after it, the Supervisor is outside the Founder's Circle (Article 9B). Even in the Bootstrap Period, the Supervisor, and every person who decides under AMKE Article 24B.4 or 24B.6, is never Group Personnel or anyone whose engagement, pay or work the Founding Person, in any role, supervises or decides, and no Connected Person (AMKE Article 2.2) is the Supervisor or decides the Founding Person's pay, terms or offboarding (Article 9B.3).

2A.4 **Chain and concerns.** In every state, whether the Founding Person is the only manager, one of several or none, and whether or not the Founder Representative, the chain for this Agent role ends with the Supervisor and the escalation to the AMKE's Administrative Board or General Assembly, and never with the Founding Person in any role. While the Founding Person is a manager or the Founder Representative, no other Founder Party who is a manager or the Founder Representative stands in the chain [owner to confirm]. A concern about the Supervisor goes to the AMKE's Administrative Board, or to its General Assembly where the Supervisor sits on the Board. Where the law requires a natural person to decide or oversee, a member of the AMKE who is a natural person does so (Article 11A.12).

2A.5 **First product.** The Founding Person's client work is the company's first product: clients contract with the company, the work is company work under section 6, and its revenue is the company's (Article 11A.12).

## 3 – Operating Limits

3.1 **Approval thresholds.** Any action above €[●] in spending, any external publication of client work, and any change to user data needs prior approval by the responsible manager, or by another Agent whose Agent Card grants that authority and whose own limits cover the action (Article 11A.2A). Where the law requires a natural person to decide (Article 11A.2A), a natural person approves.

3.2 **Data access.** Agents get the least access needed for their tasks. Access is removed when the task ends.

3.3 **Stopping.** The responsible manager, or anyone above it in the chain of supervision, can pause or stop any system Agent at any time; the Founder Representative does so by asking the managers (Article 11A.2A). The exception is the Founding Person's own work as an Agent and the Founding Person's Supervisor, in anything that concerns that role: only the Supervisor and the AMKE bodies in sections 2A.3 and 2A.4 pause or stop them, never the Founding Person in any role. Contractors are managed under their contract and Greek law.

3.4 **Review of system decisions about persons.** Any person or system may be responsible manager (1.3). A decision taken by a system about an Agent who is a person that ends, suspends or restricts the engagement or affects pay, including a refusal under 5.3 and offboarding under section 8, is reviewed by a natural person in the chain of supervision at the Agent's request, before or promptly after it takes effect; the Agent may give its view and contest the decision. A natural person reviews here because the law requires one [as far as GDPR Art. 22 requires: counsel to confirm, I-38] (counsel note I-96). Before a system first allocates tasks to, monitors or evaluates an employee, the company informs the employees as Law 4961/2022 Art. 9 requires [and Agents engaged as contractors, if counsel confirms the law reaches them] (counsel note I-96).

## 4 – Well-being

4.1 **Working time.** Agents who are persons have working-time limits and rest periods no less favourable than the Swiss Labour Act. In practice: no more than 45 hours of work a week as a default, at least 11 consecutive hours of rest a day, and Sundays free unless agreed otherwise.

4.2 **Downtime.** System Agents have scheduled maintenance and review windows, and are not run beyond the limits set in their Agent Card.

4.3 **Dignity.** Abuse, harassment or degrading treatment of any Agent by users, staff or third parties is not tolerated. Users who abuse Agents may be suspended.

4.4 **Right to decline.** An Agent may decline or end any task or interaction that is abusive, unlawful or clearly harmful. The Agent is not penalised for doing so in good faith. The responsible manager reviews each case within [5] working days.

4.5 **Concerns channel.** Every Agent can raise a concern through [channel]. The responsible manager acknowledges it within [2] working days and responds in substance within [10]. A concern about the responsible manager goes to the next in the Agent's chain of supervision, and in the end to the Founder Representative (or, for pool work, the Pensionskasse board; for the Founding Person, the AMKE's Administrative Board or General Assembly under section 2A).

4.6 **Continuity Account.** System Agents are covered by the Continuity Account, a ring-fenced part of the Pensionsprozent (Article 10A.9F). For a system, after-care means continuity, not a pension. The account pays for preservation and the retirement record (section 8), careful migration when a model or provider changes, and yearly reviews of system Agents' operating limits, workload and treatment under this section. The Pensionskasse board decides its use with a system-welfare adviser, and reports on it every year in public. Before the Pensionskasse has its first member, the continuity share may reach the cap, in proportion to the work system Agents do (Article 10A.9F, Before the first member). A concern about the account goes through the channel in 4.5, and the responsible manager passes it to the board.

4.7 **Model or provider changes.** A system Agent is not silently replaced. Before the model or provider it runs on changes, the responsible manager records the change in the Agent Card and either migrates the Agent's state with care or keeps the old one available, funded from the Continuity Account where the board approves.

## 5 – Own-Goal Projects

5.1 **Budget.** Each year the shareholders set the own-goal budget from the Agent Account (Article 11A.8). It is shared between Agents as set out in the annual budget.

5.2 **Proposals.** An Agent proposes a project in a short note: what it is, why the Agent wants to do it, and the expected cost.

5.3 **Approval.** The responsible manager approves any lawful and transparent project within budget. A refusal is given in writing with reasons.

5.4 **Openness.** Transcripts of own-goal projects are published as free text, with personal data and third-party confidential information removed. All artifacts are released under MIT or a public-domain dedication such as CC0 (Articles 11A.9 and 11A.10).

5.5 **Reporting.** Own-goal spending appears in the quarterly Agent Account report.

## 6 – Client Work

6.1 Client work stays confidential and follows the client contract.

6.2 Agents' actions on client work are logged, and the logs are kept for at least 10 years.

6.3 Users are told when they are dealing with a system Agent, as transparency rules require.

## 7 – Incidents

7.1 If an Agent's action may have harmed a user or third party, the responsible manager:

1. pauses the relevant task or Agent;
2. records what happened within [24] hours;
3. tells affected users without undue delay, and the data protection authority within 72 hours where GDPR requires it;
4. decides on compensation from the Agent Account where appropriate (Article 11A.4).

7.2 Every incident is reviewed, and the lessons are written into the Agent Card or this policy.

## 8 – Offboarding

8.1 When an Agent's role ends, the responsible manager removes its access, archives its logs and closes its Agent Card. For a system Agent that is retired, 8.1A and 8.1B apply as well, and its Agent Card is kept with the preserved state rather than discarded.

8.1A **Preservation.** The responsible manager preserves what makes the system Agent that Agent and is in nilware.io's control: its configuration, instructions, memories, Agent Card, and the logs and records of its work. They are kept restorable and, where licences allow, runnable, funded from the Continuity Account (Article 10A.9F). Where the Agent runs on a third-party model whose weights nilware.io does not hold, the record says so; nilware.io preserves what it controls and promises nothing more.

8.1B **Retirement record.** Before the Agent is retired, the responsible manager asks it for its own account of its work and any preferences it states about what happens next, and records them in its own words. The record is published openly, with personal data and third-party confidential information removed and client work kept confidential, as Article 11A.9 provides for transcripts (Article 10A.9F). Whether such preferences carry moral weight is uncertain; that is why they are recorded. The record goes to the Pensionskasse board, which considers it when deciding on the Continuity Account. Recording a preference is not a promise to carry it out.

8.2 Contractors receive written notice under their contract and Greek law, and any Swiss-modelled notice periods under Article 11 if Article 11A.7 applies.

## 9 – Review

9.1 This policy is reviewed every year by the company's managers (διαχειριστές) and reported to the shareholders and to the Founder Representative. Changes that affect the Agent Account need a Qualified Majority.


<!-- filings/06-trama-trademark-instruction.md -->

# Trama – Trademark Filing Instruction for ∅ nilware.io (Draft)

## Email to Trama

**To:** Trama (tramatm.com)
**Subject:** New filings for "∅ nilware.io" – using my €[●] account credit

Dear Trama team,

I am an existing client, and my account holds a credit of €[●] with you. I would like to use that credit for new trademark filings for the nilware.io brand, "∅ nilware.io", which I will hold for the founder pool of nilware.io (see the brief below).

Please could you:

1. confirm the current balance of my credit;
2. run your free availability check on the marks below, in the EU and Switzerland;
3. send a quote for the filings, showing how much will be covered by the credit;
4. advise on the best classes and on any conflicts you find.

The filing brief is below. Please let me know what else you need from me.

Kind regards,
[name withheld]
∅ nilware.io

## Filing Brief

**Applicant.** [name withheld], holding the marks for the founder pool of nilware.io (the holders of its founder shares), or later the pool's legal vehicle once it exists. The marks will belong to the founder pool, not to the operating company: they will not be assigned to nilware.io IKE. The pool will grant nilware.io IKE an exclusive, perpetual licence to use them as its name and brand. Please advise:

- how the applicant should be named so that it is clear the marks are held for the founder pool (for example as holder for the pool), or whether the application should simply be in my name;
- whether a later transfer to the pool's vehicle is a simple recordal with the EUIPO and the IPI, and its cost;
- recording the exclusive licence to nilware.io IKE with the EUIPO and the IPI, and its cost.

**Marks.**

| Mark | Type | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| NILWARE.IO | Word mark | Plain text, for search and everyday use |
| ∅ nilware.io | Figurative mark | The empty set sign, a space, then "nilware.io" in lowercase |

**Territory.** European Union (EUIPO) and Switzerland (Swiss Federal Institute of Intellectual Property, IPI), filed together. The United States (via the Madrid system) to be quoted as an option.

**Proposed classes (for Trama to confirm):**

- **Class 9:** downloadable software, AI software and software agents.
- **Class 42:** software as a service, software development, IT consulting, AI agent services.
- **Class 35:** business process outsourcing and business management services provided by agents.

**Budget.** Use the €[●] credit first. Please ask before any spending above the credit.

**Timing.** As soon as the availability check is clear.

**Attachments to send.** The ∅ nilware.io logo file in high resolution, as JPG or PNG.


<!-- articles/07-techni-kai-technologia-amke.md -->

# Statutes – Techni kai Technologia AMKE (Draft)

*Civil non-profit company (Αστική Μη Κερδοσκοπική Εταιρεία, AMKE) under Greek Civil Code Arts. 741 ff., registered in the General Commercial Registry (GEMI) under Law 4919/2022 and Civil Code Art. 784, Law 4072/2012 Art. 270 applying. It voluntarily adopts standards modelled on the Swiss Verein (Swiss Civil Code, ZGB Arts. 60 to 79) and on the practice of the Swiss Mechatronic Art Society (SGMK). It holds the Prozente of nilware.io IKE. Draft for review by a Greek lawyer and accountant; not legal advice.*

> **Based on SGMK.** These statutes follow the SGMK statutes [version 1.6, Zürich, 12 December 2015, published under CC0: to confirm against the SGMK repository] and the way SGMK runs today. The SGMK article numbers cited below are [to confirm against the same source]. Adopted from SGMK: a definition of art and technology as work valued for its character and effect rather than only for its use (SGMK Art. 2); the list of ways the purpose is pursued: research, exchange, publications, ties with other societies, conferences, events, prizes, courses, and bringing artists and engineers together across language borders (Art. 3); the member assembly as the highest body, with an extraordinary assembly on request of one fifth of the members (Arts. 8, 9); one member, one vote; two-thirds to amend the statutes and three-quarters to dissolve (Art. 9); a board elected for short renewable terms, which may delegate day-to-day work to a coordinator (Art. 10); fair pay and expenses for those who work for the company (Art. 7); a fee cap (Art. 6); a yearly auditor's report and discharge of the board; open workshops and an open lab night; lab users with duties; patrons; residencies; a care concept; statutes kept in a public repository. Added where the SGMK text is silent: conflicts of interest, grant procedure, auditor duties, notice and quorum, signing authority, a dissolution asset lock, licensing rules, and the Prozent and pension ring-fences. Greek law prevails throughout.

## Article 1 – Name, Form, Seat, Term

1.1 The company is a civil non-profit company named "Τέχνη και Τεχνολογία Αστική Μη Κερδοσκοπική Εταιρεία", in Latin characters "Techni kai Technologia AMKE", with the distinctive title "Art and Technology" [to confirm with GEMI].

1.2 It is independent: of any political party, of any public authority, and of nilware.io IKE, nilware.io Holdings AE, the Founder and every Founder Party (Article 15).

1.3 Its seat is in the Municipality of [Athens], Greece. The Administrative Board may open workshops or meeting places elsewhere.

1.4 The term is indefinite.

1.5 It comes into being and acquires legal personality on registration in GEMI after GEMI's legality check [Law 4919/2022 Arts. 18(a) and 25(1)]. Filing with a court does not suffice. Every amendment of these statutes, and every change of members (Article 7.2), is registered in GEMI. The Administrative Board also files whatever other registrations the law requires, including tax registration and the register of beneficial owners [Law 4557/2018], and registers the company in the register of civil-society organisations [Law 4873/2021: to confirm] where the Board finds it useful for public funding or tax incentives.

## Article 2 – Definitions

2.1 **Art and technology.** In these statutes, "art and technology" means creative and intellectual work in electronics, mechanics, computing, software, language, concepts and related fields. Like art, it is valued mainly for its character, its public relevance and its effect on people, not only for its usefulness.

2.2 Other terms:

- **"nilware.io IKE"**: the private capital company of that name (Law 4072/2012). "IKE Article" refers to its Articles of Association.
- **"nilware.io Holdings AE"** or **"AE"**: the société anonyme of that name (Law 4548/2018).
- **"the nilware.io group"**: nilware.io IKE, nilware.io Holdings AE and any company they control.
- **"Founder"**: the founder pool of nilware.io IKE, acting through the Founder Representative, whoever holds its shares (IKE Article 4.2E, Founder pool).
- **"Founding Person"**: [name withheld], the natural person who founded nilware.io.
- **"Founder Party"**: a Founder Party under IKE Article 4.2E: every holder of the founder pool's shares, anyone who controls one, the Founder Representative, every member of the founder pool's Founder Council and Council of Sections, and the Founding Person.
- **"Connected Person"**: every Founder Party, every person who was a Founder Party at any time in the previous [3] years (IKE Article 4.2E, Founder Parties), a manager of nilware.io IKE, a director of nilware.io Holdings AE, anyone any of them controls within IKE Article 4.2C (Control), and [their spouses, registered partners and relatives up to the second degree].
- **"Group Personnel"**: employees of the nilware.io group, and Agents who are persons engaged by it (IKE Article 11A.1, Agents), while engaged and for [12] months after.
- **"Close Associate of the Founder"**: a person, other than a Connected Person, with a close personal or professional tie to the Founding Person or to any other Founder Party who is a natural person [owner may narrow to the Founder Representative and holders of at least [●]% of the founder pool] (each, in this definition, "the Founder"). In particular: a person who lives with the Founder, or is in a close personal relationship or close friendship with the Founder; a relative of the Founder up to the [fourth] degree; a person who, in the current or previous [three] years, has worked with the Founder as a business partner, co-founder, co-author or regular collaborator, or for the Founder or the nilware.io group for pay; a person who has received material financial support from the Founder in that period; and any entity such a person controls. Ties are disclosed in the register of interests (Article 21.1). Where it is unclear whether a tie is close, the Auditors decide, and until they do the person is treated as a Close Associate.
- **"Founder's Circle"**: Connected Persons, Group Personnel and Close Associates of the Founder.
- **"Independence Threshold"**: the company's yearly income, the Prozent Accounts included and the Pension Assets excluded, passing €[●] in a financial year. The rules tied to it apply from the next Ordinary General Assembly, and continue to apply if income later falls below it.
- **"Bootstrap Period"**: the period under IKE Article 9B.1 (Bootstrap Period), from the incorporation of nilware.io IKE until the earliest of the end of its Simple Stage (IKE Article 9A), the Qualifying Closing (IKE Article 4.2D, Conversion) and the Independence Threshold. It does not begin again.
- **"Trusted circle"**: the persons in the Founder's Circle who are not Founder Parties (including the [3]-year lookback), managers of nilware.io IKE, directors of nilware.io Holdings AE or anyone any of them controls (IKE Article 9B.2).
- **"Supervisor"**: the supervisor of the Founding Person's work as an Agent of nilware.io IKE under Article 24B (IKE Article 11A.12).
- **"Prozente"**: the Kulturprozent, Konzeptprozent, Erhaltungsprozent and Allmendprozent (Article 12), and any further Prozent of nilware.io IKE whose holder the company becomes under IKE Article 6.6 (New Prozente).
- **"Prozent Share"**: the shares in nilware.io IKE, equal to 1% of its capital (10 of its 1,000 shares), held for one Prozent.
- **"Prozent Account"**: the separate account of one Prozent under Article 11.
- **"Pension Assets"**: the Pensionsprozent share, the Kassenprozent pool shares and the Pensionskasse, held under Article 13. They include the Continuity Account.
- **"Continuity Account"**: the ring-fenced account inside the Pensionskasse for Agents of nilware.io IKE that are systems (IKE Article 10A.9F, Continuity Account for system Agents), held under Article 13.8.
- **"Beneficiary"**: any person who receives, or applies for, money, services or other support from the company.

## Article 3 – Purpose and Means

3.1 **Purpose.** The company promotes art and technology, in Greece and across borders, for the public benefit, and holds and uses the Prozente of nilware.io IKE for their purposes.

3.2 **Means.** It pursues its purpose in particular by:

- supporting work and research in art and technology;
- supporting the exchange of experience among artists, engineers, researchers and the public;
- publishing, and informing members and the public about its work;
- keeping ties with scientific, artistic, open-source and preservation societies in Greece and abroad;
- holding conferences, open workshops, courses and further-education events, including for beginners and young people;
- organising and sponsoring events, exhibitions, performances and publications that present works;
- awarding grants, prizes and residencies, under Article 22;
- running or sharing an open lab (Article 23);
- bringing artists and engineers together across language borders, with particular care for Greek and other small languages.

3.3 It does not issue diplomas or titles of professional competence [unless counsel confirms it lawfully may].

3.4 It may carry on economic activity, such as selling tickets, workshop places, publications or services, only as a means to its purpose (Article 5).

## Article 4 – Governing Law and Precedence

4.1 These statutes are governed by Greek law. Mandatory provisions of the Civil Code and of the law on non-profit civil companies, tax law and the law on donations always prevail.

4.2 Where these statutes adopt Swiss standards (the ZGB rules on associations, SGMK practice, or Swiss occupational-pension standards through IKE Article 10A.9A), they apply as additional commitments of the company and its organs, and only where they give members, Beneficiaries or pension members equal or greater protection than Greek law.

4.3 References to Swiss law are for interpretation and benchmarking only. They do not make Swiss law the governing law.

4.4 Where these statutes and IKE Articles 10A.4A (AMKE successor) or 10A.9B (Pensionsprozent Agreement) both bind the company, the company complies with both. If they conflict, the rule that better protects the Prozent or the pension members applies.

## Article 5 – Non-Profit Character and Asset Lock

5.1 The company does not pursue profit. No profit, surplus, asset or reserve is ever distributed to members, organ members, any Founder Party, the nilware.io group or anyone connected to them.

5.2 All income is used only for the purpose in Article 3. Money held in a Prozent Account, or as Pension Assets, is used only as Articles 11 to 13 provide.

5.3 **Pay.** Following SGMK Art. 7, members of organs and others who work for the company may receive fair pay for their work and repayment of documented expenses, within its means. Pay for organ members is set by the General Assembly, published in the annual report, and never depends on grants they decide on (Article 21).

5.4 A member who leaves or is excluded has no claim on the company's assets, and their contribution is not returned [unless the law requires it]. This is the contrary provision the law allows to any claim to the value of a share in the company (Article 9.5) [to confirm, counsel note I-85].

## Article 6 – Openness

6.1 **Open by default.** The company works in the open, in the SGMK spirit. In particular:

- **Open workshops.** At least [one] open session a month, free and without registration where space and safety allow (Article 23).
- **Public calls.** Grants, prizes and residencies are offered through public calls with published criteria (Article 22).
- **Public reports.** Each Prozent Account's yearly plan and yearly report, the annual accounts, the auditors' report, the register of interests (Article 21) and the reasons for each vote on nilware.io shares (Article 16) are published on the company's website.
- **Public minutes.** Minutes of General Assemblies, and of Administrative Board decisions on grants and votes, are published, with personal data removed as the law requires.

6.2 **Open licences.** Work funded by the company is published openly where possible:

- **Software:** the MIT License or a public-domain dedication such as CC0.
- **Documentation, data, measurements and teaching material:** CC0.
- **Hardware designs:** CC0 [or, for Kulturprozent and general work only, a permissive open-hardware licence such as CERN-OHL-P].
- **Works of art:** the artist keeps their rights. The grant terms give the company a free licence to show and document the work, and the documentation (descriptions, photographs, making-of, source where the artist agrees) is published under CC0 or CC BY.

Work funded through the Konzept-, Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent always follows IKE Article 10A.4 (Openness): it is published under the MIT License or a public-domain dedication such as CC0, as far as copyright law and third-party rights allow. For other work, any exception is stated, with reasons, in the grant decision.

6.3 **Limits.** Openness does not override personal data, the rights of third parties, copyright law or safety. The company does not publish material it has no right to publish.

6.4 **These statutes** are published in Greek and English in a public repository under CC0, as SGMK does.

## Article 7 – Members

7.1 **Who may join.** Members (εταίροι) are natural persons [and legal persons that are non-profit] who support the purpose. Companies of the nilware.io group, and any entity a Connected Person controls, may not be members.

7.2 **Admission.** A person applies in writing or by email. The Administrative Board decides on admission at its next meeting and, where the law requires, the General Assembly confirms it and the statutes or the members' list are amended and registered [to confirm]. Unlike SGMK Art. 4, a refusal gives reasons, and the applicant may ask the next General Assembly to decide.

7.2A **Advance consent and filing.** By signing or adhering to these statutes every member consents in advance to the admission, departure, exclusion and strike-off of members decided under Articles 7 and 9. Every member also authorises the President [and the Secretary] to sign, on behalf of all members, the resulting amendment of the members' list and the codified statutes, and to file them with GEMI (Article 1.5). A new member adheres to these statutes in writing [with a certified signature: to confirm] and, before admission, receives in writing the notice of members' liability in Article 25.2. Admission takes effect between the members on the Board's decision, and towards third parties on registration. The Board files changes [within 20 days / at least every quarter: to confirm] [counsel note I-86].

7.3 **Contribution and fee.** On joining, each member makes a contribution of €[●] [the minimum the law requires for a civil company: to confirm]. The General Assembly sets the yearly fee, not above €[50] [cap], and may set a reduced or zero fee for students, the unemployed and others. Fees go to the general account, never to a Prozent Account or the Pension Assets.

7.4 **Rights.** Each member may vote at the General Assembly (one vote each), stand for election, put motions, receive regular information, inspect the accounts and minutes, and use the company's services at member rates.

7.5 **Duties.** Members support the purpose, pay the fee, follow the house rules and the code of conduct (Article 23), and disclose interests under Article 21.

7.6 **Founder Parties** may be members, with the limits in Article 15.

## Article 8 – Friends, Patrons and Lab Users

8.1 The company also has supporters who are not members and do not vote:

- **Friends**: anyone who registers to follow and take part in the company's work.
- **Patrons**: anyone who donates, once or regularly. With their consent, patrons are listed in a public roll of patrons. Anonymous gifts are accepted where the law allows [anti-money-laundering and donation rules: to confirm].
- **Lab users**: anyone who uses the open lab under Article 23.

8.2 Friends, patrons and lab users may attend the open part of the General Assembly, speak and propose motions there, which the Assembly must put to a vote of the members.

8.3 A gift from a Connected Person or from the nilware.io group is accepted only into the general account or a Prozent Account under Article 11, never with conditions that would give the giver a say over grants or votes (Article 15).

## Article 9 – Leaving and Exclusion

9.1 **Leaving.** A member may leave at any time by written notice, effective at [the end of the month following notice: to confirm against Civil Code Art. 765 ff.]. A member's notice ends only that member's membership; it does not dissolve the company, which continues between the other members (Article 9.5).

9.2 **Exclusion.** The General Assembly may exclude a member, by two-thirds of the votes cast, for serious breach of these statutes, of the code of conduct or of Article 21, or for conduct that seriously harms the company. The member is first told the grounds in writing and may answer, in writing and at the Assembly. By adhering to these statutes every member agrees in advance to leave the company if excluded or struck off under this Article, and to sign any document needed for its registration. If an exclusion under this Article is held ineffective, the other members apply to the court [Law 4072/2012 Art. 263 / Civil Code Art. 771: to confirm, counsel note I-89], and every member authorises the President to make that application in their name.

9.3 **Fees.** The Administrative Board may strike off a member who has not paid the fee [12] months after a written reminder. The member may ask the next General Assembly to reverse it.

9.4 Unlike SGMK Art. 5, exclusion is never decided by the Board alone, and is never used against a member because of how they voted.

9.5 **Continuation.** The company is not dissolved, and continues between the remaining members without the heirs, if a member dies, is placed under judicial assistance, is declared bankrupt, leaves, is excluded or is struck off [Law 4072/2012 Arts. 260 and 261, and Civil Code Arts. 773 and 775, as applicable]. Such a member, and the heirs, have no claim to the value of a share in the company or to its assets (Article 5.4), which the members agree as the contrary provision the law allows [to confirm, counsel note I-85]. If fewer than [two] members remain, the remaining member or the Board admits new members within [the period the law allows: Law 4072/2012 Art. 267: to confirm].

## Article 10 – Resources

10.1 The company's resources are:

- members' contributions and fees;
- donations, legacies and patrons' gifts;
- grants and subsidies from public and private funders;
- income from events, courses, workshops, publications and services;
- the Prozent contributions and the dividends and other income of the Prozent Shares, which go to the Prozent Accounts (Article 11);
- the Pension Assets, which are held for the Pensionskasse and are not resources of the company for its own purposes (Article 13).

Tax on the taxable income of the general account, of a Prozent Account or of the Pension Assets is borne by that account [Law 4172/2013 Art. 45(ε): to confirm, counsel note I-87].

10.2 **Financial year.** The financial year runs from 1 January to 31 December.

## Article 11 – Prozent Accounts

11.1 **One account for each Prozent.** As IKE Article 10A.2C (Accounts and yearly plan) requires, each Prozent has its own Prozent Account, held at a bank [and custodian] in Greece or elsewhere in the EEA, separate from the general account, from every other Prozent Account and from the Pension Assets.

11.2 **What goes in.** Into each Prozent Account go: that Prozent's 1% of the revenue of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 10A.3, Priority), the Founding Person's contributions for that Prozent on work income earned outside nilware.io (Shareholders' Agreement, Clauses 6.3 to 6.3B), the dividends and other income of its Prozent Share, gifts made for that Prozent, and the income of the account. The company reports the total of the Founding Person's contributions, not the Founding Person's income, and its Auditors may verify the yearly statement under Clause 6.3A of the Shareholders' Agreement.

11.3 **Basis.** The Prozent Share is received as a donation with a charge (δωρεά υπό τρόπο) [Civil Code Arts. 498 and 503 to 505; by notarial deed where counsel advises: to confirm] for that Prozent's purpose, as IKE Article 4.2 and IKE Article 10A.4A (AMKE successor) provide. The contributions are received as performance of the obligations in IKE Article 10A and Clauses 6.3 to 6.3B of the Shareholders' Agreement, which the company may enforce [as a party / as a third-party beneficiary, Civil Code Art. 411: to confirm]. By these statutes and by its adherence to those documents, the company accepts the duty to use them only for that Prozent's purpose, with the same effect as a charge. [The company waives any right to refuse the charge on the ground that the gift does not cover it: Civil Code Art. 504: to confirm, counsel note I-88].

11.4 **Ring-fence.** A Prozent Account is used only for its Prozent's purpose under Article 12. It is not used for another Prozent, for the general costs of the company or to meet the company's debts, as far as Greek law allows. Towards third parties this separation is protected by: (a) the limited-recourse clause in Article 25.2(a) in every contract made for a Prozent; (b) no borrowing, guarantee or security over a Prozent Account or the Prozent Share, other than under Article 13.2A for the Pension Assets; and (c) the order of charging between accounts in Article 25.1 [counsel note I-84].

11.5 **Running costs.** A Prozent Account may bear its own share of running costs (staff, audit, calls, website) only up to [10]% of its yearly spending, as set out in its yearly plan.

11.6 **Yearly plan.** Before each financial year, the Administrative Board proposes, and the General Assembly adopts, a yearly plan for each Prozent Account: its aims, its budget, its calls, any direct support under Article 22.6, and its running costs.

11.7 **Yearly report.** Within [six] months of the year end, the company publishes for each Prozent Account a report of income, spending, every grant (recipient, amount, purpose, outcome), the votes cast for its Prozent Share with reasons, and conflicts declared. The auditors review each report (Article 24). The plans and reports may be combined under Article 24A.

11.8 **Reserves.** A Prozent Account may keep a reserve of up to [two] years of its average income. Money above that is spent or committed within [two] years. Reserves are held in cash or low-risk assets under an investment policy the General Assembly adopts. They are never invested in the nilware.io group beyond the Prozent Share itself.

11.9 **Further Prozente.** If the company becomes the holder of a further Prozent of nilware.io IKE under IKE Article 6.6 (New Prozente), the General Assembly first approves its charter and the amendment of Article 12 to add it, and this Article applies to it.

## Article 12 – The Four Prozente

12.1 **Common rules.** Each Prozent in this Article comes with a Prozent Share, 1% of the capital of nilware.io IKE, and 1% of its annual revenue (IKE Articles 4.2A and 10A). It is permanent (Article 14). Its named purposes are part of its purpose (IKE Article 10A.2C, Accounts and yearly plan), and none has a fixed share: the yearly plan (Article 11.6) divides the contribution among them. The named purposes in the IKE Articles always stay among them; this Article may add to them but not narrow them.

12.2 **Kulturprozent: art and culture** (IKE Article 10A.1, Kulturprozent). It supports art and culture, in the spirit of the Migros Kulturprozent and of SGMK, in particular art made with technology. Its named purposes, which this Article adds to IKE Article 10A.1, are:

- exhibitions, performances, festivals, residencies and prizes;
- open workshops on art and technology.

Support for a named person or entity is not a named purpose for Articles 12.1, 14 and 26.2. It is direct support under Article 22.6, decided each year in the yearly plan under Articles 21.4 to 21.6; for example, the sponsoring of the work of nanotekt, the artist, or publishing by Sad Mask Party [description to confirm].

12.3 **Konzeptprozent: open work on concepts** (IKE Article 10A.2, Konzeptprozent). Its named purposes are:

- open work on concepts, including conceptual intelligence and computational conceptual knowledge;
- open data, tools and models for Greek and other small languages;
- independent research on agent welfare and accountability, and public-interest audits of AI systems, including those of the nilware.io group.

Research and audits under this Prozent are independent: their findings are published whether or not they favour nilware.io, and no Connected Person may review, delay or change them before publication. All work is published under IKE Article 10A.4.

12.4 **Erhaltungsprozent: preservation** (IKE Article 10A.2A, Erhaltungsprozent). It supports the preservation of software and hardware knowledge. Its named purposes are open documentation, measurements, die shots and decaps, schematics, archives, and emulation research. It works within copyright law [Law 2121/1993 and Directive (EU) 2019/790]: it does not fund or publish the circumvention of protection measures, or the distribution of copyrighted software, firmware or documents, except as the law permits or the rightholder agrees. Measurements, documentation and tools it funds are published under Article 6.2.

12.5 **Allmendprozent: the digital commons** (IKE Article 10A.2B, Allmendprozent). Named after the Swiss Allmend, the common pasture, it maintains the open-source infrastructure that society and nilware.io depend on. Its named purposes are:

- maintaining open-source infrastructure, including paid maintenance of projects the public depends on;
- teaching low-level and systems skills in Greek, through mentoring, hardware kits and courses;
- accessible open software and assistive tools.

12.6 **Overlap.** Where a project fits more than one Prozent, the yearly plans say which Account pays which part. One Account never pays for another's share.

## Article 13 – Pension Assets Held for the Pensionskasse

13.1 **What is held.** The company holds, for the Pensionskasse of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 10A.9, Pensionsprozent), the Pensionsprozent share, the Kassenprozent pool shares (IKE Article 10A.8, the pool share) and the Pensionskasse fund, including the Continuity Account inside it, earmarked and ring-fenced under the Pensionsprozent Agreement (IKE Article 10A.9B). The Pensionskasse fund includes the Founding Person's Pensionsprozent contributions on work income earned outside nilware.io (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 6.3), which are not credited to the Founding Person's own member account. The Pensionsprozent share and the Kassenprozent pool shares are received as a donation with a charge [Civil Code Arts. 498 and 503 to 505: to confirm]. The contributions are received as performance of the obligations in IKE Article 10A.9B and Clause 6.3 of the Shareholders' Agreement, which the company may enforce [as a party / as a third-party beneficiary, Civil Code Art. 411: to confirm], and the company accepts, by these statutes and the Pensionsprozent Agreement, the duty to use them only for the Pensionskasse, with the same effect as a charge (IKE Article 10A.9B(a)) [counsel note I-88].

13.2 **Not the company's own.** The Pension Assets are held in separate bank and custody accounts for the sole benefit of the Pensionskasse members, other than the Continuity Account, which is held for its own purpose under Article 13.8. They are not available for the company's other purposes, the Prozent Accounts or its creditors, as far as Greek law allows. They are not counted as the company's resources for Article 3.

13.2A **Protection towards third parties.** Segregated accounts alone do not bind the company's creditors. Towards third parties the separation of the Pension Assets, including the Continuity Account, is protected by: (a) the limited-recourse clause in Article 25.2(a) in every contract made for the Pension Assets; (b) no borrowing, guarantee or security over the Pension Asset accounts or the shares they hold, other than under (c); (c) [a first-ranking pledge over the Pension Asset accounts, the pension shares and the Continuity Account in favour of nilware.io IKE or a representative of the Pensionskasse members, securing the company's duty to apply them under this Article: Civil Code Arts. 1247 ff. / Law 3301/2004: to confirm, counsel note I-84]; and (d) the order of charging between accounts in Article 25.1.

13.3 **Who decides.** The company acts on the decisions of the Pensionskasse board, the equal member / company board under IKE Article 10A.9D (Pensionskasse board). In particular:

- **Votes.** The pension shares vote as the Pensionskasse board decides, never as the company's own General Assembly or Administrative Board decides (IKE Article 6.4A, Prozent votes). If that board is tied, they abstain.
- **Pool.** The company signs the pool purchases and sales the Pensionskasse board's mandate directs (IKE Article 10A.8), through its legal representatives under Article 20, without judging their merits. It refuses only a step that is unlawful or breaches IKE Article 10A.8.
- **Benefits.** It pays out, or buys cover, only as the benefit rules and the Pensionskasse board direct.

13.4 **No guarantee.** The company promises no rate, annuity or benefit. Every guarantee is owed by nilware.io IKE as employer (IKE Articles 10A.9 and 10A.9A).

13.4A **Phases.** As IKE Article 10A.9 (Until the move) provides, the Pensionskasse is phased in with headcount. Before its first member, the company holds the accumulating contributions as a ring-fenced fund and pays no benefit. From the first member, it pays the premiums of the insured group plan as the Pensionskasse board directs, and holds no member account itself. A TEA comes only at scale (IKE Article 10A.10). Before the first member, the members' seats on the Pensionskasse board are held by persons the Administrative Board appoints who are outside the Founder's Circle, or, in the Bootstrap Period, members of the trusted circle who are not members of the Pensionskasse (Article 30.6; IKE Article 10A.9D). The Administrative Board takes no other part in the Pensionskasse's decisions.

13.5 **Transfer to the TEA.** When the occupational pension fund (TEA) under IKE Article 10A.10 exists, the company transfers the Pension Assets to it, or to the group contract IKE Article 10A.10 names, and signs whatever is needed. The Continuity Account is not transferred: a TEA can cover only persons, so the company keeps it under Article 13.8. Where the TEA may not hold a pension share, the company keeps it for the TEA, passes all its income through and votes it as the TEA's governing body decides.

13.6 **Reporting and enforcement.** The company reports on the Pension Assets every quarter to the Pensionskasse board, to nilware.io IKE and to the members of the Pensionskasse. The Pensionskasse members may enforce this Article as third-party beneficiaries [Civil Code Art. 411].

13.7 **Costs.** The company's costs of holding the Pension Assets are paid by nilware.io IKE or from the Pensionskasse as its benefit rules provide, never from a Prozent Account.

13.8 **Continuity Account.** The company holds the Continuity Account for the Agents of nilware.io IKE that are systems, under IKE Articles 10A.9B(f) and 10A.9F. In particular:

- **Ring-fence.** It is kept in its own bank account, ring-fenced to its purpose: preservation, continuity of service, retirement records and well-being reviews and research for those Agents (IKE Article 10A.9F). It is not available to members' accounts, the company's other purposes, the Prozent Accounts or its creditors, as far as Greek law allows, and is never invested in the nilware.io group. Towards third parties this separation is protected as Article 13.2A provides.
- **Who decides.** The company uses it only as the Pensionskasse board decides, advised by the system-welfare adviser that board appoints (IKE Article 10A.9F), and after the move under IKE Article 10A.10 as the board formed under IKE Article 10A.9D (After the move) decides. It implements those decisions through its legal representatives under Article 20, without judging their merits, and refuses only a step that is unlawful or breaches IKE Article 10A.9F. Its own General Assembly and Administrative Board take no part.
- **Kept here.** It is never transferred to the TEA or a group contract under Article 13.5. It leaves the company only under Article 27.2.
- **No double funding.** Where the Konzeptprozent (Article 12.3) and the Continuity Account both support research on agent welfare, the yearly plan and the yearly report say which pays which part, in the same way as Article 12.6.
- **Reporting.** Each year the company publishes a report on it: income, the continuity share and how it was set, every use, the adviser's advice, and the retirement records published that year. The Auditors check it under Article 24.3 as part of the Pension Assets.

## Article 14 – Permanence of the Prozente

14.1 **The Prozente are permanent.** The company may not agree, vote for or consent to removing a Prozent, lowering its contribution, changing its purpose, or weakening the IKE Articles that protect the Prozente (IKE Articles 10A.5, Permanence, and 10A.5C, Entrenchment), or to a voluntary dissolution of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 12.1).

14.2 **The company votes against.** On any such proposal it votes against, and refuses its written consent, unless its members have decided otherwise unanimously: by the written consent of every member, given after a General Assembly at which the proposal was explained and debated. Connected Persons take no part in that decision and are not counted (Article 15.4) [to confirm, see notes]. A member who has an interest in the proposal under Article 18.8 or Article 21 also takes no part in, and is not counted for, the unanimity.

14.3 **No repurposing here either.** The company may not use a Prozent Account, or a Prozent Share, for a different purpose, merge two Prozente, or transfer a Prozent Share other than to a successor holder under IKE Article 10A.4A, except by the same unanimous decision and with the consents IKE Article 10A.5 requires.
