14.4 **Pension Assets.** For the pension shares, any vote or written consent on a matter in Article 14.1 is given or refused only as the Pensionskasse board decides under Article 13.3 (IKE Articles 6.4A and 10A.9D), subject to SHA Clause 5.1C as Clause 5.1D provides. Article 14.2 does not apply to them, and accrued pension rights are preserved in any case (IKE Article 10A.5A, Accrued pension rights).

14.5 **Shareholders' Agreement.** The company is bound by Clauses 5.1B to 5.1D of the nilware.io Shareholders' Agreement (voting undertaking, permanence, adherence) for all its nilware.io IKE shares, and honours them.

## Article 15 – Independence

15.1 **Principle.** The company votes its nilware.io shares and awards its grants for the purposes of the Prozente, independently of the Founder and every Founder Party, the managers of nilware.io IKE and the nilware.io group (IKE Article 6.4A, Independence).

15.2 **Founder Parties.** Each Founder Party may be a member, but:

- may not be a member of the Administrative Board, the auditors, any grant committee or any Prozent council;
- takes no part, at any organ, in deciding how the company votes its nilware.io shares, in adopting the voting policy, or in any decision under Article 14;
- takes no part in any grant, payment or contract that concerns a Founder Party, a person connected to one, or the nilware.io group;
- takes no part in electing or removing members of the Administrative Board, the President, the Vice-President or the Prozent stewards;
- may not give the company instructions, and may not hold a proxy for another member.

15.3 **Other Connected Persons** are bound by Article 15.2 in the same way as Founder Parties, including the bar on electing or removing members of the Administrative Board, the President, the Vice-President or the Prozent stewards.

15.4 **No control.** No Connected Person, alone or with others, may control the company within IKE Article 4.2C. In particular, at any General Assembly the votes of Connected Persons and Group Personnel together count for at most [one quarter] of the votes cast; if they would count for more, each of their votes is reduced in proportion [to confirm].

15.5 **Group Personnel** may be members and may hold at most [one] seat on the Administrative Board, never as President or Treasurer. They take no part in deciding how the company votes its nilware.io shares [owner to decide]. Article 30.6 does not lift the limit of [one] seat for decisions under Article 24B, in which Group Personnel take no part (Article 24B.7).

15.6 **Loss of independence.** If the company ceases to meet IKE Article 6.4A, the Administrative Board tells nilware.io IKE and the members at once and puts the cause right within [60] days. If it cannot, Article 27.3 applies to the Prozent Shares and Prozent Accounts, as IKE Article 10A.4A provides.

## Article 16 – How the Company Votes its nilware.io Shares

16.1 **Board decision.** The company votes each Prozent Share at nilware.io IKE by a decision of the Administrative Board, taken at a meeting or by written resolution, with the members who are excluded under Articles 15 and 21 taking no part. The pension shares follow Article 13.3, not this Article.

16.2 **For the Prozent's purpose.** Each Prozent Share is voted in the interest of its own Prozent's purpose and of the long-term health of nilware.io IKE as the source of the Prozente. Where two Prozent Shares would be served differently, each is voted for its own Prozent.

16.3 **Voting policy.** The General Assembly adopts, and reviews every [two] years, a voting policy that sets out how the Board weighs a Prozent's purpose, permanence, openness, agent welfare and good governance. Connected Persons do not vote on it. The Board follows it and explains any departure.

16.4 **Fixed rules.** Whatever the policy says:

- for each Prozent Share, the company votes against every matter in Article 14.1, unless Article 14.2 is met; for the pension shares, it votes or consents on those matters only as the Pensionskasse board decides under Articles 13.3 and 14.4;
- it honours the voting undertaking in Clause 5.1B of the Shareholders' Agreement for new Prozente;
- it never takes instructions from a Connected Person or the nilware.io group;
- it appoints a proxy only to a member of the Board who may vote on the matter, or to an independent person who is not a Connected Person, with written voting instructions.

16.5 **Published reasons.** Within [30] days of each meeting or resolution of nilware.io IKE, the company publishes how each Prozent Share voted and why. Confidential company information is described only in general terms.

16.6 **Information rights.** The Board exercises the company's information rights as a shareholder of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 7.1, Information) at least once a year, and reports what it learns to the members, within the confidentiality IKE Article 7.2 (Loyalty) requires.

## Article 17 – Organs

17.1 The organs of the company are:

- the General Assembly (Γενική Συνέλευση), its highest body;
- the Administrative Board (Διοικητικό Συμβούλιο), led by the President;
- the Auditors;
- the Supervision Committee (Article 24B.6).

17.2 The General Assembly may set up a council for each Prozent, with members and outside experts, to advise the Board on its yearly plan and calls. A council advises; the Board decides.

## Article 18 – General Assembly

18.1 **Highest body.** Following ZGB Art. 64, the General Assembly is the highest body of the company. It decides on:

- electing and removing the Administrative Board, its President and Vice-President, and the Auditors;
- approving the annual report, the accounts, the Auditors' report, and discharging the Board;
- adopting each Prozent Account's yearly plan and approving its yearly report (Article 11);
- the budget, the fee and its cap, and pay for organ members;
- the voting policy (Article 16.3) and the investment policy (Article 11.8);
- admitting members where the law requires it, and excluding members (Articles 7 and 9);
- amending these statutes, and dissolution;
- motions from the Board, from members, and from friends under Article 8.2.

18.2 **Ordinary Assembly.** It meets at least once a year, within [six] months of the year end.

18.3 **Extraordinary Assembly.** The Board calls one when it decides, or within [30] days of a written request by one fifth of the members stating the purpose (SGMK Art. 8; ZGB Art. 64). If the Board does not, the requesting members may call it themselves.

18.4 **Notice.** At least [15] days' written notice, by email or post, with the agenda and the documents. Members may put motions until [10] days before the Assembly, and the Board sends them out at once. The Assembly decides only on items on the agenda, apart from motions to call another Assembly (ZGB Art. 67).

18.5 **Quorum.** The Assembly is quorate when at least [one third] of the members are present or represented. If not, a repeat Assembly held at least [seven] days later is quorate whatever the attendance, except for the matters in Article 18.7.

18.6 **Voting.** Each member has one vote. A member may give a written proxy to another member, and no one may hold more than [one] proxy. Resolutions pass by a simple majority of the votes cast; abstentions are not counted. Elections are by secret ballot if any member asks.

18.7 **Higher majorities.**

- amending these statutes: two-thirds of the votes cast, with at least half of all members present or represented;
- excluding a member: two-thirds of the votes cast;
- dissolution: three-quarters of the votes cast, with at least half of all members present or represented;
- the matters in Article 14 and the entrenched Articles in Article 26.2: as those Articles provide.

18.8 **Exclusion from voting.** Following ZGB Art. 68, a member does not vote on a matter that concerns them, a close relative or an entity they control: their own grant, pay, contract, exclusion, or a claim against them. Articles 15 and 21 apply in addition.

18.9 **Electronic meetings and written resolutions.** The Assembly may meet in person, online or both, with secure voting. A written resolution is valid if every member is consulted and the majority this Article requires is reached within the period stated [to confirm].

18.10 **Open part.** Every Ordinary Assembly has an open part, which friends, patrons, lab users and the public may attend. Votes are taken among the members only.

18.11 **Minutes.** A secretary takes minutes, and a vote counter is appointed for each Assembly, as SGMK does. Minutes are signed by the chair and the secretary and published under Article 6.1.

18.12 **Challenge.** Following ZGB Art. 75, a member who did not agree may challenge a resolution that breaches the law or these statutes in court within [one month] of learning of it [as far as Greek law allows].

## Article 19 – Administrative Board

19.1 **Composition.** The Board has [3 to 7] members, including the President, a Vice-President, a Treasurer and a Secretary. One person may hold more than one of these roles, but the President, the Vice-President and the Treasurer are three different persons (Articles 20.2 and 20.3). Each Prozent has a steward on the Board, who prepares its yearly plan and report. One person may steward more than one Prozent.

19.2 **Election.** The General Assembly elects the whole Board, the President and the Vice-President by name, for [two] years. Members may be re-elected. The Board assigns the other roles among its members. The Assembly may remove a Board member at any time for good reason.

19.3 **Who may sit.** Board members are members of the company. Article 15 applies. At least [two] members, and a majority of those who decide on nilware.io votes, are neither Connected Persons nor Group Personnel nor Beneficiaries of the Prozent concerned in the current or previous [two] years. In the Bootstrap Period, members of the trusted circle may count toward this rule as Article 30.6 provides.

19.3A **Independent majority.** After the Bootstrap Period (Article 2.2), on the schedule in IKE Article 9B.4 and at the latest within [12] months counted from the end of the Bootstrap Period, a majority of the Board's members are outside the Founder's Circle: they are not Connected Persons, not Group Personnel and not Close Associates of the Founder. The register of interests names the Board members who meet this rule. If a vacancy or a change in ties breaks it, the Board tells the members at once, and a General Assembly held within [three] months restores it. Until then, the Board decides on nilware.io votes and grants only if a majority of the members deciding are outside the Founder's Circle.

19.4 **Powers.** The Board runs the company and does everything not reserved to the General Assembly, within the budget and yearly plans. It may delegate day-to-day work to a coordinator, who reports to it and to the Assembly (SGMK Art. 10). It may not delegate decisions on nilware.io votes, grants or exclusions.

19.5 **Meetings.** The Board meets at least [every three months], in person or online, called by the President or any [two] members with [five] days' notice. It is quorate when a majority of its members who may vote on the matter are present. It decides by a majority of the votes cast. No one has a casting vote. On a tie, the proposal fails. Written resolutions are allowed if no member objects to the procedure. If fewer than [two] members may vote on a matter, the Board co-opts under Article 19.7 or the General Assembly decides it.

19.6 **Duty of care.** Board members act with care and in good faith in the interest of the company's purpose and, for each Prozent, of that Prozent's purpose, to the standard of ZGB Art. 69 and Swiss Code of Obligations Art. 717 where it is higher than Greek law.

19.7 **Vacancies.** If a seat falls vacant, the Board may co-opt a member until the next General Assembly, within Articles 19.3 and 19.3A.

## Article 20 – President, Representation and Signing

20.1 **President.** The President chairs the Board and the General Assembly, and represents the company in and out of court [and before GEMI and the tax authorities].

20.2 **Vice-President.** The Vice-President stands in for the President whenever the President is absent or excluded under Article 21, including on every decision on the President's own funding.

20.3 **Joint signature.** The following need the signatures of two Board members, one of them the President or Vice-President and the other the Treasurer [or another member the Board names]:

- any payment or commitment above €[1,000];
- any payment from a Prozent Account, and any dealing in the Pension Assets;
- any proxy, vote or written consent at nilware.io IKE;
- contracts with a member, an organ member or a Beneficiary.

20.4 A Board member who is excluded from a decision under Articles 15 or 21 does not sign for it.

## Article 21 – Conflicts of Interest

21.1 **Register of interests.** The company keeps a register of interests. Every Board member, Auditor, council member, coordinator and member of a grant committee enters, on taking office and within [14] days of any change:

- any grant, pay, contract or other benefit they, a close relative or an entity they control receive or seek from the company;
- any role, pay or interest in the nilware.io group;
- any interest in a Beneficiary, an applicant or a project the company funds or may fund;
- any close personal or professional tie, of the kinds in the definition of Close Associate of the Founder (Article 2.2), to the Founding Person or another Founder Party who is a natural person, to another organ member, or to a Beneficiary or applicant. This applies from the start, in the Bootstrap Period too, and to every person who fills a role under Article 30.6 or acts as Supervisor under Article 24B.

The register is published (Article 6.1), with personal data limited to what the purpose needs. A personal tie is published only by its kind (personal or professional) and the persons it links, not its details.

21.2 **Disclosure.** Before any decision, each person taking part declares any interest in it. The declaration is minuted.

21.3 **Recusal.** A person with an interest in a decision leaves the meeting while it is debated and decided, does not vote, does not sign it, and is not counted in the quorum. A person may never decide, review or sign anything about their own funding, pay or contract, or that of a close relative or an entity they control.

21.4 **Funding a person with an interest.** A grant, pay or contract for an organ member, a close relative or an entity they control needs:

- a decision by at least two-thirds of the Board members who may vote on it;
- the written opinion of an independent reviewer who is not a member of the Board and not connected to the person, that the terms are fair and serve the Prozent's purpose;
- an item in the yearly plan adopted by the General Assembly, or the Assembly's approval;
- publication of the recipient, the amount, the reasons and the reviewer's opinion.

21.5 **Cap.** Funding under Article 21.4 for any one person and those connected to them may not exceed [25]% of a Prozent Account's yearly spending, unless the General Assembly approves more by two-thirds.

21.6 **The President as a Kulturprozent Beneficiary.** nanotekt, the President, may also receive direct support from the Kulturprozent under Article 22.6 (Article 12.2). Therefore:

- every decision on support for nanotekt, or for any project or entity in which nanotekt has an interest [including Sad Mask Party, if so: to confirm], is prepared by the Kulturprozent steward or council and decided under Article 21.4, chaired by the Vice-President, with nanotekt absent;
- nanotekt does not take part in the Kulturprozent's yearly plan items, calls, selection or reports where nanotekt or such an entity is an applicant or Beneficiary;
- the support appears as a separate line in the Kulturprozent's yearly plan and report;
- nanotekt's pay, if any, for work as President is set by the General Assembly under Article 5.3 and kept apart from any artistic support.

21.7 **Breach.** A decision taken in breach of this Article may be reversed by the Board or the General Assembly, and money paid under it reclaimed. A breach is a ground for removal and exclusion (Article 9.2).

21.8 **Gifts.** Organ members do not accept gifts or favours from applicants or Beneficiaries beyond customary hospitality of small value.

## Article 22 – Grants, Prizes and Residencies

22.1 **Rules.** The Board adopts written grant rules for each Prozent, within its yearly plan, and publishes them. The General Assembly may amend them.

22.2 **Public calls.** Except as Article 22.6 provides, grants, prizes and residencies are awarded through public calls that state the purpose, the budget, who may apply, the criteria, the deadline, the selection process and the licence (Article 6.2). Calls are published in Greek and English.

22.3 **Selection.** Applications are assessed by a selection committee of at least [three], including at least [one] outside expert, under the published criteria. Article 21 applies to every committee member. The Board decides on the committee's recommendation and records reasons where it departs from it.

22.3A **Outside majority.** After the Bootstrap Period (Article 2.2), on the schedule in IKE Article 9B.4 and at the latest within [6] months counted from the end of the Bootstrap Period, the selection committees of the Kulturprozent and the Erhaltungsprozent have a majority of outside members: persons who are not members of the Administrative Board and are outside the Founder's Circle.

22.4 **Publication.** Results are published with the recipient, amount, purpose and short reasons. Unsuccessful applicants may ask for the reasons for their case.

22.5 **Terms.** Each grant has written terms: what is funded, payment stages, reporting, the licence, the acknowledgement of the Prozent, and repayment if the money is misused.

22.6 **Direct support.** A Prozent may give support without a call only where the yearly plan, as adopted by the General Assembly, names the Beneficiary, the purpose and the maximum amount, such as the sponsoring of nanotekt or Sad Mask Party publishing under the Kulturprozent, or paid maintenance of a named project under the Allmendprozent. Article 21 applies.

22.7 **Residencies.** Residencies may be informal and co-organised by the resident, in the SGMK way, but the support given, its amount and the resident's name are published.

22.8 **Beneficiaries from the nilware.io group.** A Prozent may fund a Connected Person, Group Personnel or the nilware.io group only through a public call under Article 22.2, with Article 21.4 applied as if the recipient were an organ member, and never for work that nilware.io IKE would otherwise pay for. No Founder Party may ever be a Beneficiary.

## Article 23 – Open Lab, Workshops and Care

23.1 **Open sessions.** The company runs open workshops or open lab sessions, free and walk-in where space and safety allow (Article 6.1).

23.2 **Lab users.** Where it runs or shares a lab, lab users may pay a monthly lab fee set by the General Assembly for access, storage and materials. Lab users share the care of the lab: cleaning days, user meetings and hospitality to visitors, as the house rules set out. Lab fees and costs are reported separately from member fees.

23.3 **House rules.** The Board adopts house rules for the lab and events, covering safety, tools, storage and shared space.

23.4 **Code of conduct and care concept.** The Board adopts, and the General Assembly approves, a code of conduct and a care concept: how the company keeps people safe and welcome, how it protects minors at its events, and how concerns are raised and handled, with a person of trust outside the Board.

23.5 **Courses.** Courses and workshops for young people follow the care concept, and the law on working with minors.

## Article 24 – Accounts, Auditors and Reports

24.1 **Accounts.** The accounts follow Greek Accounting Standards (Law 4308/2014) [and the tax rules for civil companies under Law 4172/2013 Art. 45(ε): to confirm]. They show the general account, each Prozent Account and the Pension Assets separately, and within the Pension Assets the Continuity Account.

24.2 **Auditors.** The General Assembly elects, for [two] years:

- two member Auditors, who are not Board members, Connected Persons or Group Personnel (in the Bootstrap Period they may be members of the trusted circle, but never Board members, Article 30.6); and
- an independent certified auditor (ορκωτός ελεγκτής), where the law requires one [to confirm, including whether holding the Pension Assets does], and in any case once nilware.io IKE has left its Simple Stage (Article 24A).

24.3 **Duties.** The Auditors check the accounts, the separation of the Prozent Accounts and the Pension Assets, compliance with the yearly plans, Articles 21 and 22 and the register of interests, and the published reasons for nilware.io votes. They may inspect all books and records at any time. They report in writing to each Ordinary General Assembly, before the vote on discharge.

24.3A **The Founding Person's Agent role and contributions.** The company accepts the roles that nilware.io IKE Article 11A.12 and Clauses 6.3 to 6.4 of the Shareholders' Agreement give it [counsel note I-45]:

- it supervises the Founding Person's work as an Agent of nilware.io IKE, itself or through a member, under Article 24B;
- only the member Auditors outside the Founder's Circle (Article 2.2), or in the Bootstrap Period member Auditors from the trusted circle (Article 30.6), verify the Founding Person's yearly statement of work income. They keep its details confidential and report to the Board only whether the statement and payments are complete;
- the company receives the Founding Person's declarations of other professional activity, and recovers unpaid contributions for its own Prozent Accounts and the Pensionskasse and, while the Founding Person is a manager of nilware.io IKE, for the Kassenfonds of nilware.io IKE on its behalf, as the Administrative Board decides, Article 21 applying.

24.4 **Annual report.** The Board publishes, within [six] months of the year end, an annual report with the accounts, the yearly report of each Prozent Account (Article 11.7), the Auditors' report, the register of interests and a summary of nilware.io votes.

## Article 24A – Scaled Paperwork

24A.1 **Combined plan and report.** While the company's yearly income, the Prozent Accounts included and the Pension Assets excluded, is below €[●], the yearly plans (Article 11.6) and yearly reports (Article 11.7) of the Prozent Accounts may be made as one combined plan and one combined report. Each Prozent Account is still shown as a separate section and a separate sub-account, and the Pension Assets and the Continuity Account (Article 13.8) have their own section. Each section shows everything Articles 11.6, 11.7 and 13.8 require.

24A.2 **Simple Stage.** While nilware.io IKE is in its Simple Stage (IKE Article 9A), the company's paperwork scales in the same way:

- **One ledger.** It keeps one set of books, with a sub-account for each Prozent Account, the Pension Assets and the Continuity Account. The money stays in the separate accounts these statutes require (Articles 11.1, 13.2 and 13.8).
- **Member Auditors only.** The member Auditors under Article 24.2 do the audit, without a certified auditor unless the law requires one.
- **One annual report.** The annual report under Article 24.4 may be the combined report under Article 24A.1.

24A.3 **What does not scale.** Every substantive rule applies in full: every amount, ring-fence and separate account, permanence (Article 14), independence (Article 15 and Article 19.3A), conflicts of interest (Article 21) and publication (Article 6). A combined plan or report publishes everything the separate ones would.

## Article 24B – Supervising the Founding Person's Agent Work

24B.1 **Role.** The company may supervise the Founding Person's work as an Agent of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 11A.12), on written terms with nilware.io IKE that the Administrative Board adopts. The Supervisor is, as the Administrative Board decides, either the company itself, acting through the Administrative Board as a body, or any individual member of the company, the President included, whom the Board names with that member's consent. A member named as Supervisor acts in the name and on behalf of the company, under a written mandate from the Board and within the written terms with nilware.io IKE, to which only the company is a party. Any pay is paid by nilware.io IKE to the company and passed to the member under Article 5.3.

24B.2 **What the Supervisor does.** The Supervisor does what IKE Article 11A.12 and the Agent Policy give the Founding Person's responsible manager: it prepares the Agent Card, sets and changes the limits, the written terms and the pay as an Agent, reviews the work, approves actions above the limits, may pause or stop the work, answers the Founding Person's concerns and decides on offboarding. It acts for a fair and lawful engagement, and takes no instruction from nilware.io IKE, the Founder or any Founder Party.

24B.3 **Costs.** nilware.io IKE pays the costs of the role [and fair pay for a member who acts as Supervisor, under Article 5.3]. They are never paid from a Prozent Account or the Pension Assets (Articles 11.4 and 13.2), and any pay is published (Article 5.3).

24B.4 **Escalation.** A concern about the Supervisor or a challenge to its decisions, and every decision to change, pause, stop, remove or replace the Supervisor or its terms, goes to the Administrative Board, or to the General Assembly where the Supervisor sits on the Board or is the Board. Nothing under this Article goes back to the Founding Person.

24B.5 **A natural person where the law requires one.** Where the law requires a natural person to decide or oversee, a member of the company who is a natural person does so: the Supervisor, if a natural person, or otherwise a member the Board names.

24B.6 **Conflicts.** A Supervisor who has a close tie to the Founding Person, of the kinds in the definition of Close Associate of the Founder (Article 2.2), enters it in the register of interests (Article 21.1); the tie, once disclosed, does not by itself bar the Supervisor from the Founding Person's matters, and the Supervision Committee may take over any specific decision. The Supervision Committee consists of every member of the company outside the Founder's Circle (in the Bootstrap Period, of the trusted circle under Article 30.6) who may act under Article 24B.7. It is called by any [two] of its members or by the Board on [five] days' notice, may meet or decide online or in writing, decides by a majority of the votes cast, keeps minutes and may name one of its members to take the decision. A Supervisor with a personal stake in a decision (Article 30.6) enters it in the same way and takes no part in it, and the Supervision Committee takes that decision in the same way. If no member of the Supervision Committee can act, the decision goes to the Administrative Board without the conflicted person, or, if fewer than [two] Board members remain, to the General Assembly without the conflicted person and without Founder Parties (Article 15.2). Article 21 applies in addition.

24B.7 **Who may act.** No Founder Party and no manager of nilware.io IKE is the Supervisor or takes part in a decision under this Article (Article 15.2). In the Bootstrap Period the Supervisor may be a member of the trusted circle (Article 30.6); after it, the Supervisor is outside the Founder's Circle on the schedule in IKE Article 9B.4. Even in the Bootstrap Period, the Supervisor, and every person who decides under Article 24B.4 or 24B.6, is never Group Personnel or anyone whose engagement, pay or work the Founding Person, in any role, supervises or decides, and no Connected Person is the Supervisor or decides or votes on the Founding Person's pay, terms or offboarding (IKE Article 9B.3). Article 15.5's limit of [one] Board seat for Group Personnel is not lifted for decisions under this Article (Article 30.6).

24B.8 **Kept apart.** The role gives the company and its members no part in managing nilware.io IKE [and does not make them its managers or Group Personnel: to confirm, counsel note I-45], and no say in how the company votes its nilware.io shares, which Article 16 governs.

## Article 25 – Liability

25.1 Towards third parties, the company's assets answer for its debts as the law provides. Between the accounts, each debt is charged to the account for which it was incurred, and no Prozent Account or Pension Asset account bears a debt of the general account or of another account. Where a creditor is paid from the wrong account, the account that should have borne the debt reimburses it first. Towards third parties the Prozent Accounts and the Pension Assets are protected as Articles 11.4 and 13.2A provide.

25.2 Under Greek law the members (εταίροι) are personally liable for the company's debts towards third parties [jointly, severally and without limit: Civil Code Art. 784 and Law 4072/2012 Arts. 249, 258 and 270 / pro rata: to confirm, counsel note I-83], and these statutes cannot limit that liability towards third parties. To keep it remote:

- **(a) Limited recourse.** Every contract the company signs, other than with consumers, states that the counterparty has recourse only against the company's assets and waives any claim against the members personally.
- **(b) Limits on commitments.** The company takes no loan, gives no guarantee and enters no commitment above €[●] without a decision of the General Assembly.
- **(c) Indemnity.** [nilware.io IKE indemnifies the company and its members for liabilities arising from holding the Pension Assets, the Continuity Account and the Supervisor role (Articles 13 and 24B), under the Pensionsprozent Agreement and the Supervisor terms: to confirm, and mirrored in IKE Article 10A.9B(g).]
- **(d) Insurance.** The company insures its organs against liability [as far as is reasonable] and, where available, insures its general liability.

Every applicant is told of this paragraph in writing before admission (Article 7.2A), and every founding member before signing these statutes (Article 30.1).

25.3 Board members are liable for harm they cause by breach of their duties, under Greek law. Discharge by the General Assembly does not cover facts that were not disclosed to it.

## Article 26 – Amendments

26.1 These statutes are amended by the General Assembly under Article 18.7, and the amendment is registered as the law requires.

26.2 **Entrenched Articles.** Articles 5, 11.3 and 11.4, 12 (as it sets the purpose of each Prozent), 13, 14, 15, 16.4, 19.3A, 21.3 and 21.6, 22.3A, 24A.3, 27.2 and 27.3, and this Article 26.2, may be amended in any way that weakens a Prozent, its ring-fence, the pension ring-fence or the company's independence only by the unanimous decision in Article 14.2, in which a member with an interest under Article 18.8 or Article 21 takes no part and is not counted, and, for Article 13 and Article 27.2, only with the consent of the Pensionskasse board and of nilware.io IKE. Article 14.2 does not apply to how the pension shares vote or consent, which Article 14.4 governs. Support for a named person or entity is not a purpose of a Prozent for this Article (Article 12.2). An amendment that IKE Articles 10A.4A or 10A.9B require may not be weakened at all while the company holds the shares those Articles cover.

## Article 27 – Dissolution and Liquidation

27.1 **Dissolution.** The company is dissolved by a General Assembly resolution under Article 18.7, or on a ground the law makes mandatory (in particular a court decision for serious reason, or the company's bankruptcy). An event affecting a member does not dissolve it (Article 9.5).

27.2 **Pension Assets.** On dissolution, the Pension Assets other than the Continuity Account go to the TEA under IKE Article 10A.10, or, if none exists, to a pension fund or a successor non-profit with the same lock that nilware.io IKE designates under IKE Article 10A.9B(d). The Continuity Account never goes to the TEA or a pension fund: on dissolution it goes to a successor non-profit with the same purpose and lock, designated by nilware.io IKE under IKE Article 10A.9B(f) [and approved by the Pensionskasse board], and the successor holds it under IKE Article 10A.9F. None of the Pension Assets ever go to the company's other purposes, its members or the nilware.io group.

27.3 **Prozent Shares and Prozent Accounts.** On dissolution, or if the company ceases to meet IKE Article 6.4A, each Prozent Share and the unspent balance of its Prozent Account go to a successor non-profit with the same purpose and lock, designated under IKE Article 10A.4A [and approved by an independent person or the Pensionskasse board: to confirm]. The General Assembly may propose a successor.

27.4 **Other assets.** After the debts are paid, any remaining assets go to one or more Greek or EU non-profit bodies with a purpose close to the company's [and, where needed, exempt from tax], chosen by the General Assembly. Unlike SGMK Art. 9, they may never go to members, organ members, Connected Persons or the nilware.io group.

27.5 **Liquidators.** The Board acts as liquidator unless the General Assembly appoints others. Liquidators report to the members and publish a final report.

## Article 28 – Disputes

28.1 Disputes between members, or between members and the company, go first to mediation under Law 4640/2019.

28.2 If mediation fails, the competent courts of [Athens] decide, or an arbitrator if the parties agree.

## Article 29 – Final Provisions

29.1 If any provision is invalid, the rest remains in force, and the invalid provision is replaced by the valid one that comes closest to its purpose.

29.2 Matters not covered here are governed by Civil Code Arts. 741 ff. and Greek law in general.

29.3 The Greek text is filed and prevails. The English text is a translation.

## Article 30 – Founding and Transitional Provisions

30.1 **Founding members.** The founding members, and their contributions, are: [●], including [●]. [The Founding Person: owner to decide whether the Founding Person is a founding member, within Article 15.]

30.2 **First Board.** The first Administrative Board serves until the first Ordinary General Assembly, and in any case no more than [two] years: President, [●]; Vice-President [●]; Treasurer [●]; Secretary [●]; members [●]. Article 19.3 applies from the start, subject to Article 30.6, and Article 19.3A after the Bootstrap Period, on the schedule in IKE Article 9B.4. Every first Board member enters their ties in the register of interests (Article 21.1) on taking office.

30.3 **First Auditors.** [●].

30.4 **Before receiving nilware.io shares.** Before the company receives any share or payment from nilware.io IKE, it:

- signs the Pensionsprozent Agreement (IKE Article 10A.9B) and adopts these statutes, including Articles 13, 14 and 27, and registers them in GEMI (Article 1.5);
- signs the deed of adherence under IKE Article 5.3 and Clauses 5.1B to 5.1D of the Shareholders' Agreement;
- opens the Prozent Accounts and the Pension Asset accounts, including the Continuity Account;
- adopts a first yearly plan for each Prozent Account;
- obtains the accountant's written confirmation of the income tax, VAT and donation tax treatment of: the share donation; the Prozent and Pensionsprozent contributions; the dividends of the Prozent Shares and the pension shares; the payments under Article 24B.3; and the Pension Assets and the Continuity Account [Law 4172/2013 Arts. 45(ε) and 47; Law 2961/2001; VAT Code: to confirm, counsel note I-87].

30.5 **Shares received.** On incorporation of nilware.io IKE, the company receives sixty shares, 6% of its 1,000 shares: ten for each of the Kultur-, Konzept-, Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent, ten as the Pensionsprozent share and ten as the Kassenprozent pool shares (IKE Article 4.2, Initial holdings).

30.6 **Bootstrap.** In the Bootstrap Period (Article 2.2; IKE Article 9B), members of the trusted circle may hold the seats and roles that Articles 13.4A, 15.3, 15.5, 19.3, 22.3, 24.2, 24.3A and 24B otherwise reserve to persons outside the Founder's Circle, or to persons who are not Connected Persons or Group Personnel: seats on the Administrative Board and its committees, the Supervisor, the members' seats on the Pensionskasse board before there is an eligible member, the member Auditors (never a Board member) and independent reviewers under Article 21.4. A member of the trusted circle who is a member of the Pensionskasse does not hold a members' seat on the Pensionskasse board under this Article, before the first member or while no member is outside the Founder Parties (IKE Article 10A.9D). Each of them enters every close tie in the register of interests (Article 21.1) before acting and takes no part in any decision in which they have a personal stake (Article 21.3). In this Article and Article 24B, a "personal stake" is an interest of the person's own, or of their household or an entity they control, in the outcome of the decision; a tie to the Founding Person or another Founder Party disclosed under Article 21.1 is not by itself a personal stake or an interest under Article 21.3, and does not by itself bar the person from the Founding Person's matters (IKE Article 9B.2). [Connected Persons who sit under this Article take no part in deciding how the company votes its nilware.io shares (Article 15.3 continues to apply to that): the default / Connected Persons who sit under this Article may take part in those decisions: owner to choose.] Article 15.5's limit of [one] Board seat for Group Personnel is not lifted for decisions under Article 24B. The hard lines in IKE Article 9B.3 hold throughout: no one supervises or approves for themselves; Founder Parties never take part in deciding how the company votes its nilware.io shares or the pension shares (Article 15.2); the Pension Assets are never paid, lent or made available to the Founding Person or the trusted circle other than as benefits under the benefit rules, and no pension member's rights are decided by a person who would benefit; the insured salary of a member who is a Founder Party never exceeds the arm's-length rate IKE Article 9B.3 sets; the Supervisor, and every person who decides under Article 24B.4 or 24B.6, is never Group Personnel or anyone whose engagement, pay or work the Founding Person, in any role, supervises or decides; no Connected Person is the Supervisor or decides or votes on the Founding Person's pay, terms or offboarding; and the Prozente stay permanent (Article 14). When the Bootstrap Period ends, the Administrative Board publishes within [30] days the schedule in IKE Article 9B.4, and Articles 19.3A and 22.3A, the Supervisor outside the Founder's Circle and the Pensionskasse seats held by elected members follow it within their cure periods, counted from the end of the Bootstrap Period.

---

*Signatures of the founding members: [●]*


<!-- articles/08-founder-pool-constitution.md -->

# Constitution of the Founder Pool – nilware.io (Draft)

*Constitution of the founder pool of nilware.io: the holders of the founder-class shares (Class F) and the Reserve Shares (Class R) of nilware.io IKE, and of the Class A shares of nilware.io Holdings AE. An agreement among those holders under Greek law [legal form: to confirm, Article 16]. Draft for review by a Greek lawyer; not legal advice.*

> **Modelled on the Swiss Federal Constitution.** This constitution borrows the structure of the Swiss Federal Constitution of 18 April 1999 (BV), not its text, and none of Swiss public law applies [article numbers below to confirm against the current BV text]. Adopted in adapted form: the holders as the sovereign, meeting together like a Landsgemeinde (a cantonal assembly of all voters, not a BV institution); basic rights before organs (BV Title 2); a collegial executive of up to seven members deciding as one body, whose president serves for one year and is not re-elected for the year after (BV Arts. 174 to 177); the optional referendum on decisions of the authorities (BV Art. 141), the mandatory referendum (BV Art. 140) and the initiative (BV Arts. 138 and 139); the double majority of the people and the cantons (BV Art. 142); sections with their own rules within the constitution, as cantons have their own constitutions (BV Arts. 3 and 51); revision and transitional provisions (BV Arts. 192 to 197). Added where a state constitution has nothing to say: one block vote at the company, commitments that travel with the shares, independence from the Prozente and the Pensionskasse, the brand held for the pool, free transfer, succession on death, arbitration among holders. Greek law prevails throughout.

## Article 1 – The Founder Pool

1.1 **The Founder Pool.** The Founder Pool is formed by all holders of founder-pool shares, whoever they are and however many. It is "the Founder" in the nilware.io documents: the Articles of nilware.io IKE (the "IKE Articles"), the Articles of nilware.io Holdings AE (the "AE Articles"), the Shareholders' Agreement and the Agent Policy (IKE Article 4.2E). The Founder is the Founder Pool, acting through the Founder Representative (Article 8), not any person.

1.2 **Founder-pool shares.** The founder-pool shares are:

- the founder-class shares (Class F) of nilware.io IKE: ordinary voting shares equal to exactly 50% of its capital, initially 500 of its 1,000 shares (IKE Article 4.2B);
- the Reserve Shares (Class R) of nilware.io IKE held by Holders, until every Reserve Share is contributed to nilware.io Holdings AE at the Qualifying Closing (IKE Article 4.2D, Conversion); a Reserve Share a Holder sells or grants to a person who is not a Holder leaves the Founder Pool (IKE Article 4.2D, Sales and grants);
- the Class A shares of nilware.io Holdings AE (AE Article 4.2), from its formation;
- any share issued to keep the Founder Pool at exactly 50% (IKE Article 4.2B).

1.3 **Holders.** A "Holder" is any person, natural or legal, registered as holder of founder-pool shares who has adhered under Article 4.1. Each Holder's weight in this constitution is the number of Class F shares it holds ("Class F votes"). Class A shares go with the Class F shares (Article 9.2); neither they nor the Reserve Shares carry separate weight. Class F shares whose votes are suspended or lost under Clauses 6.1 and 6.2 of the Shareholders' Agreement carry no weight in the Founder Pool, and do not count for Article 1.6 or the quorum in Article 6.6, while the suspension lasts; nor do shares without weight under Articles 4.5 and 9.3A [and 16.1A, if the owner keeps that option].

1.4 **The Founding Person.** The "Founding Person" is [name withheld], the natural person who founded nilware.io and the first Holder of every founder-pool share. The obligations that are personal stay with the Founding Person and never pass with founder-pool shares: the IP assignment, exclusivity or contribution (providing professional services exclusively through nilware.io IKE, or paying the Prozente on work income earned outside nilware.io), the non-compete, the leaver terms (Shareholders' Agreement, Clauses 6.1 to 6.5) and authorship of the charter. The Founding Person's work for nilware.io IKE is done as an Agent, with the same rights and obligations as any other Agent (IKE Article 11A.12), and gives no right in the Founder Pool. The Founding Person holds the Brand Assets of record for the Founder Pool (Article 2A.3) and, as between the Founding Person, the Holders and nilware.io IKE, has no beneficial interest of its own in them. In the Founder Pool the Founding Person has the rights of a Holder, and no others.

1.5 **Precedence.** This constitution governs the Holders among themselves. Towards nilware.io IKE, nilware.io Holdings AE, the Prozente and the Investors, the IKE Articles, the AE Articles and the Shareholders' Agreement prevail, and nothing in this constitution weakens any duty in them. Mandatory Greek law prevails over all of them.

1.6 **Counting heads.** For every count of Holders or of the Holders in a Section (Articles 6.4, 10.1, 11 and 12), persons acting in concert, a Holder and those it controls (IKE Article 4.2C) or who are its nominees, and spouses or registered partners count as one Holder. A Holder of fewer than [5]% of the Class F votes (and never fewer than twenty Class F shares), or who acquired its holding in the previous [6] months other than as heir or legatee, does not count as a head. Persons who received founder-pool shares without payment from the same Holder in the previous [3] years, other than on death, count as one head together with that Holder. Persons who hold a share in common count as one Holder (Article 9.6). These rules decide only who counts as a head; every Holder still votes its Class F votes.

## Article 2 – Purpose

2.1 **Purpose.** The Founder Pool exists to hold the founder-pool shares together, so that the Founder stays at exactly 50% of nilware.io IKE and speaks with one voice, and to carry out the Founder's role in the nilware.io documents:

- casting the votes of the Class F shares at nilware.io IKE and of the Class A shares at nilware.io Holdings AE, as one block (Article 3);
- proposing the candidates for the majority of the Board of nilware.io Holdings AE that the Class A shares propose, for election by the General Meeting under the voting undertaking in Clause 5.1F of the Shareholders' Agreement (AE Article 8.2);
- proposing candidates for manager of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 8.2A);
- standing at the end of every chain of supervision of the company's Agents (IKE Article 11A.2A; Article 8.3), except the chains this constitution keeps independent of the Founder (Article 8.4);
- having the Brand Assets, which belong to the Founder Pool as a whole and are held for it of record under Article 2A.3, and licensing them to nilware.io IKE (Article 2A);
- giving the Founder's consents and receiving its information under the nilware.io documents.

2.2 **The charter.** The Founder Pool keeps faith with the charter of nilware.io: the six founding Prozente and their permanence, the agentic company, openness, and the independence of the AMKE and the Pensionskasse from the Founder.

2.3 **No business of its own.** The Founder Pool runs no business and holds no assets other than the founder-pool shares its Holders bring to it and the Brand Assets (Article 2A). Each Holder receives the dividends and other distributions on its own shares directly. The Holders share the costs of the Founder Pool in proportion to their Class F votes.

## Article 2A – The Brand

2A.1 **Brand Assets.** The "Brand Assets" belong to the Founder Pool as a whole, held for it of record under Article 2A.3 (Article 2A.2). They are: the word mark "NILWARE.IO", the figurative mark "∅ nilware.io", any other mark, logo or name containing "nilware.io", or the ∅ sign as used with it, that the Founder files, adopts or acquires, or that nilware.io IKE or anyone else holds for the Founder Pool under Brand Licence Clause 2.1A, every application and registration for them, the domain name nilware.io and the other domains listed in the Brand Licence Agreement, and the goodwill attached to them (Brand Licence Clause 2.1).

2A.2 **Assets of the pool.** The Brand Assets belong to the Founder Pool as a whole, not to any Holder, and are held for it of record under Article 2A.3; the Holders' rights in them are contractual and are exercised only through the organs of the Founder Pool. No Holder has a separate share in them that it can sell, pledge, inherit or claim, and they are never distributed to Holders. A Holder's interest in them is its membership of the Founder Pool, through its Class F votes.

2A.3 **Holder of record.** Until the Founder Pool has a legal vehicle, the Founding Person is the registered owner of the Brand Assets and holds them for the Founder Pool, on the Founder Council's instructions [as fiduciary or mandatary without representation: to confirm, counsel note I-42]. Until a vehicle holds them, the Holders know that the Brand Assets are, in law, the holder of record's property. When a vehicle exists under Article 16.2, or another vehicle a mandatory referendum approves (Article 10.2), the Brand Assets are transferred to it. They are also transferred, to a holder of record the Founder Council designates (a vehicle, or a natural person eligible under Article 8.2), when the Founding Person dies or becomes incapable (Article 14.4), ceases to be a Holder, or the Council so decides. If no Founder Council can act, including while the constitution is dormant and its sole Holder has died or become incapable, the interim Founder Representative under Article 14.3, or failing one the arbitrator under Article 15 on the request of any person listed in Article 14.3(d), designates an interim holder of record and uses the power of attorney below, notwithstanding the restrictions in Article 14.3 and Article 10.6; the interim holder of record serves until the Founder Council designates a holder of record. The holder of record signs, on taking office, an irrevocable power of attorney to the Founder Representative for the time being to make these transfers [Civil Code Art. 218: to confirm]. As between the holder of record, the Holders and nilware.io IKE, the holder of record holds the Brand Assets for the Founder Pool and has no beneficial interest of its own in them. Its heirs take them subject to the duty to transfer them under this Article [protection against the holder of record's personal creditors and insolvency: counsel note I-42 (c)]. The holder of record also owes these duties to nilware.io IKE and to Techni kai Technologia AMKE, which may enforce them directly (Brand Licence Clauses 8.3 and 8.4), and while this constitution is dormant (Article 13.1) those duties are owed to them as well as to the Holders [counsel note I-93].

2A.4 **Inseparable.** The Brand Assets travel with the Founder Pool, whoever its Holders become. They are never separated from the founder-class shares (Class F), and never sold or transferred apart from the Founder Pool. A transfer of founder-pool shares, in whole or in part, transfers no Brand Asset; whoever controls the block controls the Brand Assets through the organs of the Founder Pool.

2A.5 **The Founder Council decides.** The Founder Council decides on filing, renewing, maintaining, opposing and enforcing registrations of the Brand Assets, on the domains, on consents and brand guidelines under the Brand Licence Agreement, and on giving notice of a Prozent Breach and referring it to the arbitrator under Brand Licence Clauses 7.2 and 7.3, subject to the optional referendum (Article 10.1). It may not rely on a Prozent Breach that a Founder Party caused, voted for or procured (Brand Licence Clause 7.1).

2A.6 **Referendum.** A Brand Asset is assigned only in ways that keep it with the Founder Pool: to a new holder of record or vehicle under Article 2A.3, or with the whole Founder Pool on a change of form under Article 16.3. Assigning a Brand Asset to a new vehicle or other holder of record, other than a designation under Article 2A.3 on the events listed there, abandoning a Brand Asset (including letting a registration or a domain lapse on purpose), and amending the Brand Licence Agreement, are decided only by a mandatory referendum (Article 10.2), and always subject to the Brand Licence Agreement. No Brand Asset is encumbered or given as security (Brand Licence Clause 8.1) [owner may confirm]. Any other assignment or encumbrance is a breach of this constitution and of the Brand Licence Agreement. It does not bind the Holders among themselves, but towards third parties it takes effect as the law provides (Civil Code Art. 177). The person who made it accounts to the Founder Pool for whatever it receives, reverses it and transfers the Brand Asset back on request, and answers for all loss. A transferee or chargee takes subject to the Brand Licence once it is recorded under Brand Licence Clause 5.4.

2A.7 **The Brand Licence.** The Founder Pool has granted nilware.io IKE an exclusive, perpetual, royalty-free, worldwide licence of the Brand Assets, which ends only under its Clause 7 (Brand Licence Agreement). Every Holder is bound by it, and the Founder Pool honours it. No organ of the Founder Pool may end or suspend it at its discretion.

2A.8 **Costs.** Costs of the Brand Assets that nilware.io IKE does not pay under the Brand Licence Agreement are costs of the Founder Pool (Article 2.3).

## Article 3 – One Block

3.1 **One block.** All Class F shares vote at nilware.io IKE as one block, and all Class A shares vote at nilware.io Holdings AE as one block. Every vote of the block is cast the same way, by the Founder Representative, as the Founder Pool has decided under this constitution. A majority inside the Founder Pool decides for all of it.

3.2 **Proxy.** Each Holder grants the Founder Representative for the time being an irrevocable proxy, given also in the interest of the other Holders [Civil Code Art. 218: to confirm], to vote its Class F and Class A shares and to sign the written resolutions and consents of the block. No Holder votes its founder-pool shares itself. Each Holder signs a new proxy whenever the Founder Representative changes.

3.3 **Who decides.** The Founder Council decides how the block votes (Article 7), except on the matters this constitution reserves to the Pool Assembly or to a referendum (Articles 6 and 10).

3.4 **Exceptions.** Reserve Shares carry no votes, whoever holds them, until nilware.io Holdings AE holds them after the Qualifying Closing (IKE Article 4.2D). The votes of shares whose rights are suspended or lost under Clauses 6.1 and 6.2 of the Shareholders' Agreement are cast as those Clauses provide. There is no other exception.

3.5 **A vote cast otherwise.** A vote cast for founder-pool shares other than by the Founder Representative is not counted by the company [as far as Greek law allows] (IKE Article 4.2E). A Founder Representative who casts the block other than as decided is answerable to the Holders under Article 15, and the vote stands at the company. If the company counts a vote a Holder casts itself in breach of Article 3.2, that Holder is in breach of this constitution and of the Shareholders' Agreement. It owes a contractual penalty of [●] (Civil Code Arts. 404 ff.), and the other Holders or the Founder Council may seek specific performance and interim measures (Article 15.4), so that it votes with the block at any adjourned or repeat meeting and confirms the proxy in Article 3.2.

## Article 4 – Commitments that Travel with the Shares

4.1 **Adherence.** No one becomes a Holder until they have adhered in writing to this constitution, to the Shareholders' Agreement by deed of adherence (Clause 12.3), including Clauses 5.1B to 5.1D (and Clause 5.1E with them), to the Brand Licence Agreement, and to the IKE Articles and the AE Articles as they bind the Founder. The managers of nilware.io IKE do not register a transfer to a person who has not adhered (IKE Article 5.7). A Holder who disposes of all its founder-pool shares ceases to be a Holder, but stays bound by Article 15 for disputes about its time as Holder, and stays a Founder Party for the lookback in Article 4.4. A Holder that is a legal person tells the Founder Council, and the managers of nilware.io IKE, of any change of its control (IKE Article 4.2C) within [14] days (IKE Article 5.7). Before any change of its control closes, it procures, and undertakes in its deed of adherence to procure, that its controllers and the acquirer seek the ballot under Article 9.3 where that Article applies, and make the offer to the Investors under Clause 9.1A of the Shareholders' Agreement where that Clause applies.

4.2 **Permanence.** Every Holder is bound by every rule that makes the Prozente permanent (IKE Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C, AE Article 7.6, Shareholders' Agreement Clause 5.1C). The block votes against, and refuses its written consent to, every matter in Clause 5.1C of the Shareholders' Agreement, unless a mandatory referendum has approved it with the consent of every Holder (Article 10.2).

4.3 **Exactly 50%.** No Holder transfers, acquires or subscribes shares in a way that takes the Class F shares away from exactly 50% of the capital of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 4.2B). The block may vote for a capital increase of nilware.io IKE only after Holders have committed irrevocably in writing to subscribe all the new Class F shares it needs. Each Holder may subscribe in proportion to its Class F votes, and the others may take up what a Holder does not; no Holder is obliged to subscribe. Otherwise the block votes against the increase, unless it is structured under IKE Article 4.2B so that the Founder stays at exactly 50% without new Class F shares. No Holder, and no one who controls a Holder or whom a Holder controls (IKE Article 4.2C), holds capital shares of nilware.io IKE other than founder-pool shares, or acquires any that would take the votes of the IKE shares held by Holders and by persons they control above 50% of the total capital [owner may confirm]. This excludes the AE's holding, whose votes are governed by the AE Articles (Reserved Matters, AE Article 7.4) and disclosed under IKE Article 4.2B: the AE is not counted as a person the Holders control for this Article, and the Qualifying Closing does not breach it.

4.4 **Independence.** Every Holder, everyone who controls a Holder, the Founder Representative, every member of the Founder Council and of the Council of Sections, and the Founding Person is a "Founder Party" (IKE Article 4.2E). Every rule of the nilware.io documents that keeps a body, a vote or a person independent of the Founder applies to each of them: in particular the independence of the AMKE and its Prozent votes (IKE Article 6.4A, AMKE Article 15), of the Pensionskasse, its board, the pension shares and the Kassenprozent pool (IKE Articles 10A.8 and 10A.9D), and of the independent proxy holder (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 5.1B). A person who was a Founder Party at any time in the previous [3] years is treated as one for every such rule, and stays a Connected Person and in the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2; IKE Article 4.2E, Founder Parties).

4.5 **Who may not be a Holder.** nilware.io IKE, nilware.io Holdings AE, Techni kai Technologia AMKE, the Pensionskasse, any holder of a Prozent share, any pension fund under IKE Article 10A.10, and anyone any of them controls, may not hold founder-pool shares. A Holder that becomes barred, including by a change of its control, has no weight in the Founder Pool from that moment (Article 1.3), and sells its founder-pool shares to a person who may hold them within [6] months; failing that, the other Holders may buy them, in proportion to their Class F votes, at fair value set by a valuer who is not a Founder Party.

4.6 **Beneficial ownership.** Each Holder gives the Founder Council and the managers of nilware.io IKE and nilware.io Holdings AE, on adherence and within [14] days of any change, the information on itself and its own beneficial owners that they need under Law 4557/2018 [articles to confirm]. After every election and on every change, the Founder Council tells the managers which natural persons direct the block (the Founder Representative, and any person or persons acting in concert holding a majority of the Class F votes), so that the companies can keep their entries in the Central Register of Beneficial Owners accurate (IKE Article 5.1) [whom to declare when no Holder exceeds 25%, and whether the Founder Pool or the holding of the Brand Assets is a legal arrangement similar to a trust: (counsel note I-68)].

## Article 5 – Basic Rights of Holders

5.1 **Equal treatment.** Every Class F share carries the same rights in the Founder Pool. No decision of the Founder Pool may favour or disadvantage a Holder, or a group of Holders, except in proportion to their Class F votes.

5.2 **Information.** Every Holder receives, within [30] days, everything the Founder Representative receives from nilware.io IKE and nilware.io Holdings AE as shareholder (IKE Articles 7.1 and 8.4), the minutes of the Founder Council, and how the block voted on each matter and why. Every Holder keeps company secrets as IKE Article 7.2 requires.

5.3 **Voice.** Every Holder may attend and speak at the Pool Assembly, put motions to it, and stand for election to the Founder Council.

5.4 **The right to sell.** Every Holder may sell, give or leave its founder-pool shares, in whole or in part, to anyone who may be a Holder, under Article 9, and may independently sell or grant its Reserve Shares to anyone under IKE Article 4.2D (Sales and grants). The internal procedures of this constitution may delay a sale under Article 9.3, but never stop it.

5.5 **Minority protection.** Without the consent of each Holder affected, no decision of the Founder Pool or of a Section may: change the weight of a share; impose a payment or duty on a Holder beyond this constitution; restrict a Holder's right to sell beyond Article 9; or treat Holders differently. A Holder who did not agree may challenge any decision that breaches this constitution under Article 15, within [60] days of learning of it.

5.6 **No forced exit.** No Holder can be excluded from the Founder Pool or made to sell or give up its founder-pool shares, except:

- under the drag-along of nilware.io IKE or nilware.io Holdings AE (IKE Article 5.5, AE Article 6.3);
- the contribution of every Reserve Share to nilware.io Holdings AE at the Qualifying Closing in exchange for AE shares at the Conversion Ratio, non-voting Class C shares for Reserve Shares held by Holders, or, at a Holder's option and only as far as the round provides the cash, their sale to it for cash at the round price (IKE Article 4.2D, Conversion; AE Article 4.3B);
- the transfer of Reserve Shares to a new Prozent (IKE Article 4.2D, New Prozente);
- the leaver terms of the Founding Person, as they reach shares received from the Founding Person without payment (Shareholders' Agreement, Clauses 6.1 to 6.2A);
- the sale of the shares of a Holder that has become barred (Article 4.5);
- the purchase of shares acquired in breach of Article 9.3 (Article 9.3A);
- [the offer of the Class F shares of a Holder whose participation has ended (Article 16.1A): if the owner keeps that option]; and
- seizure, enforcement or insolvency under the law.

## Article 6 – Pool Assembly

6.1 **Highest body.** The Pool Assembly is all the Holders. Like a Landsgemeinde, it is the highest body of the Founder Pool, and every Holder may take part in person or by proxy.

6.2 **Powers.** The Pool Assembly:

- elects and removes the members of the Founder Council, and each year elects its President and Vice-President from among them (Articles 7 and 8);
- decides the matters of the mandatory referendum (Article 10.2), by ballot of all Holders;
- decides on initiatives and optional referendums (Articles 10.1 and 10.4);
- recognises Sections and approves their rules (Article 12);
- receives the yearly report of the Founder Council and discharges it;
- amends this constitution (Article 11).

6.3 **Ordinary Assembly.** The Pool Assembly meets at least once a year, at least [15] days before the ordinary shareholders' meeting of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 6.1).

6.4 **Extraordinary Assembly.** The Founder Council calls an extraordinary Assembly when it decides, or within [20] days of a written request by Holders of [10]% of the Class F votes or by [3] Holders (counted under Article 1.6). If the Council does not, the requesting Holders may call it themselves.

6.5 **Notice and meetings.** At least [15] days' written notice, by email, with the agenda and the documents. The Assembly may meet in person, online or both, and may decide by written or electronic ballot. It decides only on items on the agenda.

6.6 **Votes.** Each Class F share has one vote. Unless this constitution says otherwise, the Assembly decides by a majority of the Class F votes cast, and a tie is a rejection (on the block's votes, Article 10.6). The Assembly is quorate when Holders of at least half of the Class F votes take part; a repeat Assembly at least [7] days later is quorate whatever the attendance. A ballot under Article 10 has no quorum.

6.7 **Proxies.** A Holder may give a written proxy to another Holder or to a lawyer. No one holds proxies for more than [one third] of the Class F votes.

6.8 **Minutes.** Minutes are kept of every Assembly and sent to every Holder.

## Article 7 – Founder Council

7.1 **Collegial executive.** The Founder Council is the executive of the Founder Pool. It has 1 to 7 members, as the Pool Assembly decides, elected by the Assembly for [four] years and re-eligible. If the Assembly has not decided the number of seats, the Council has [three] members, or as many as there are Holders if fewer. Its members are natural persons; they need not be Holders [owner may confirm]. A person barred under Article 8.2 may not be a member of the Founder Council. Where there are Sections, the Assembly takes care that they are fairly represented. In an election, the candidates with the most Class F votes are elected; a tie between candidates for the last seat, or for President, is decided by lot.

7.2 **Deciding as a body.** The Founder Council decides as one body, by a majority of its members voting. No member decides alone for the Council. A member who disagrees may have the disagreement minuted, but represents the Council's decision outside it. On a tie, the President's vote decides [owner may confirm].

7.3 **Powers.** The Founder Council decides everything not reserved to the Pool Assembly or to a referendum, in particular:

- how the block votes at nilware.io IKE and nilware.io Holdings AE, other than on the matters in Article 10.2;
- the Class A proposals of candidates for the Board of nilware.io Holdings AE (AE Article 8.2), after consulting the Council of Sections (Article 12.4);
- the candidates the Founder proposes for manager of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 8.2A);
- the policy within which the Founder Representative oversees the chains of supervision of Agents (Article 8.3);
- the Brand Assets, within Article 2A.6 (Article 2A.5);
- preparing the Pool Assembly and the ballots, and carrying out their decisions.

7.4 **Timing.** The Founder Council decides how the block votes on a matter within [3] days of the Founder Representative receiving the notice of the meeting or the proposed written resolution, and publishes the decision to the Holders at once. With the IKE's 20 days' notice (IKE Article 6.2), that leaves time for a demand within [5] days and a ballot of [7] days that ends before the meeting (Articles 10.1 and 10.5). On a matter in Article 10.2, the Council puts its proposal to the ballot on the same timetable.

7.5 **Duties.** Members of the Founder Council act with care and in good faith, in the interest of the Founder Pool as a whole and of the long-term health of nilware.io, within the charter (Article 2.2). They disclose any conflict of interest and abstain on it, as IKE Article 8.3 requires of managers. They report to the Pool Assembly every year.

7.6 **Removal and vacancies.** The Pool Assembly may remove a Council member at any time. A vacant seat is filled at the next Assembly; until then the Council continues with the remaining members.

7.7 **Failed election.** If no Founder Council, or no President, has been elected within [90] days of the date on which the Assembly had to elect one (Articles 6.2, 13.3 and 14.3), the arbitrator under Article 15, on the request of any Holder, appoints an interim Founder Council of one or more members, or an interim Founder Representative, eligible under Article 8.2, who serve until the Assembly elects. Until the arbitrator appoints, the Founder Representative in office, or the interim Founder Representative under Article 14.3, continues.

## Article 8 – Founder Representative

8.1 **President and Representative.** The President of the Founder Council is the "Founder Representative". The President is elected by the Pool Assembly for one year from among the Council members, and may not be President for the following year, unless the Council has only one member or no other member is willing [owner may confirm]. The Vice-President stands in whenever the President is absent or excluded by a conflict, and, as the Swiss practice is, is the natural candidate for President the next year.

8.2 **A natural person.** The Founder Representative is always a natural person, of full legal capacity, who is not barred under Article 4.5 and is not a bad leaver under Clause 6.2 of the Shareholders' Agreement [for [●] years after the bad-leaver date: owner to decide].

8.3 **Role.** The Founder Representative:

- casts every vote of the block, and signs its written resolutions and consents, as the Founder Pool has decided (Article 3);
- represents the Founder towards nilware.io IKE, nilware.io Holdings AE, the AMKE, the Investors and third parties;
- is the end of every chain of supervision of the company's Agents (IKE Article 11A.2A; Agent Policy, section 1.3), other than the chains that stay independent of the Founder (IKE Articles 10A.8 and 10A.9D) and the chain for the Founding Person's own work as an Agent, which ends with the Founding Person's Supervisor at the AMKE (IKE Article 11A.12). Within the policy the Founder Council sets, the Founder Representative oversees how the managers and the responsible managers supervise Agents, receives the Agent register and the incident records, and may ask the managers to pause or stop any Agent or to report on any Agent's work. The Founder Representative oversees as the Founder's representative, not as a manager, unless also appointed manager; the managers remain responsible for supervision under IKE Article 8.3.

8.4 **What stays independent of the Founder.** The oversight in Article 8.3 never reaches the Kassenprozent Agents' work on the Kassenprozent pool, the Pensionskasse, its board and the pension shares, the Continuity Account, or the AMKE and its Prozent votes. Those chains end with the Pensionskasse board or the AMKE's own organs (IKE Articles 6.4A, 10A.8, 10A.9D and 10A.9F), and no Founder Party takes part in them (Article 4.4).

8.5 **Notice to the company.** The Founder Council notifies nilware.io IKE and nilware.io Holdings AE in writing of the Founder Representative and of every change, with the new proxies under Article 3.2. If the Council cannot act, the interim Founder Representative under Article 14.3, or any Holder, notifies the company, with the death certificate, the court decision or the certificates under Article 14.4. The company relies on the last notice it has received.

## Article 9 – Transfers

9.1 **Free transfer.** Founder-pool shares are freely transferable: they may be sold, given or inherited, in whole or in part, and pledged or held in usufruct. No consent of nilware.io IKE, of nilware.io Holdings AE or of the other Holders is needed (IKE Article 5.7). The conditions in Articles 9.2 to 9.5 apply.

9.2 **Conditions.** The acquirer adheres under Article 4.1 and is not barred under Article 4.5. A Holder who transfers part of its holding transfers Class F and Class A shares as nearly in proportion as it holds them as whole shares allow, rounding [to the nearest whole share], so that a transfer of any number of Class F shares can be made [owner may confirm, or allow the two classes to be transferred separately]. The proportion is worked out on everything the Holder has transferred to the same acquirer, and to persons acting in concert with it, in the previous [12] months, so that splitting a transfer into smaller ones never leaves Class A shares behind. Reserve Shares are not tied to the Class F shares: a Holder may sell or grant them independently, in whole or in part, to anyone, with the proceeds to that Holder (IKE Article 4.2D, Sales and grants), and a transfer of Class F shares need carry none. A Holder with no Class F shares is not counted as a Holder for Articles 6.4, 10.1, 11 and 12. With 1,000 capital shares in nilware.io IKE, every 0.1% of its capital is a whole share (IKE Article 4.1). The transferring Holder tells the Founder Council within [14] days.

9.3 **Large sales.** A transfer by any title other than inheritance or legacy (a "sale" in this Article, its acquirer the "buyer") that would give one buyer, alone or with persons acting in concert with it, more than [●]% of the Class F shares, counting what it acquired in the previous [12] months, needs a mandatory referendum (Article 10.2). Acquiring control (IKE Article 4.2C) of a Holder counts as acquiring its founder-pool shares, and shares held by persons a buyer controls count as the buyer's. The seller, the buyer, and persons acting in concert with or controlled by either, take no part in the ballot, and their Class F votes are not counted among the votes cast. If the referendum rejects it, the other Holders may buy the shares offered, at the same price and on the same terms, or at fair value where no price per share is paid, in proportion to their Class F votes, within [60] days. Shares they do not take may then be sold to the buyer as offered. If no Holder may vote in the ballot, Article 9.3 does not apply. A sale or grant of Reserve Shares alone is not a sale under this Article.

9.3A **Breach of Article 9.3.** Founder-pool shares acquired in breach of Article 9.3, directly or by acquiring control of a Holder, carry no weight in the Founder Pool (no Class F votes in the Assembly, ballots or elections, and no head under Article 1.6) until a ballot under Article 9.3 approves the acquisition. If the ballot rejects it, the other Holders may buy those shares from the buyer or the Holder concerned, in proportion to their Class F votes, at the price paid, or at fair value set by a valuer who is not a Founder Party where no price per share was paid, within [60] days.

9.4 **Change of control.** A buyer who acquires a majority of the Class F shares controls the block. Acquiring control of Holders counts as acquiring their founder-pool shares, as in Article 9.3. The Investors' tag-along on such a sale is in Clause 9.1A of the Shareholders' Agreement and IKE Article 5.4A [owner may confirm].

9.5 **Pledges, usufruct and enforcement.** A pledgee, usufructuary or assignee of dividends adheres under Article 4.1 for the rights it receives, and the votes always stay with the block. A buyer in enforcement or insolvency takes the shares subject to this constitution and becomes a Holder on adherence.

9.6 **Shares and parts of shares.** Founder-pool shares are whole capital shares. Persons who hold one share in common, including heirs of an undivided estate, count as one Holder for Articles 6.4, 10.1, 11, 12 and 13 until the share is divided (Article 1.6), and act through one common representative (Law 4072/2012 Art. 75(5)). Until the co-holders designate their common representative, the Founder Council sends every notice under this constitution to each co-holder it knows of, and no time limit under Articles 10 and 14 runs against them until [30] days after the death has been notified to the Founder Council. While an undivided estate is the only Holder, its common representative, if eligible under Article 8.2, is the Founder Representative (Article 13.1). The capital of nilware.io IKE is divided into 1,000 shares, so that the Founder Pool can be split finely (IKE Article 4.1).

9.7 **Register.** The Founder Council keeps a register of Holders and of their Class F, Class R and Class A shares, and of every sale and grant of Reserve Shares by a Holder (holder, number of shares, price), matching the share registers of nilware.io IKE and nilware.io Holdings AE.

## Article 10 – Referendum and Initiative

10.1 **Optional referendum.** Holders of [5]% of the Class F votes, or [3] Holders (counted under Article 1.6), may demand that any decision of the Founder Council be put to a ballot of all Holders. The demand suspends the decision. It is made:

- for a decision on how the block votes at nilware.io IKE or nilware.io Holdings AE, within [5] days of its publication (Article 7.4); the ballot then runs for [7] days and ends before the meeting or the deadline for the written resolution;
- for any other decision, within [30] days of its publication.

If a ballot on a block vote still cannot end before the vote must be cast, the Founder Representative asks for the matter to be adjourned where the company's rules allow. Otherwise the block does not vote as the Council decided: it abstains, and votes against any matter that changes the IKE Articles or the AE Articles [owner may confirm]. If the ballot ends in time and rejects the Council's decision on a block vote (Article 10.3), the block likewise abstains, and votes against any matter that changes the IKE Articles or the AE Articles. On a Required Matter the block always votes under Article 10.7, whatever the ballot. On a matter in Article 10.2, Article 10.6 applies.

10.2 **Mandatory referendum.** The following are decided by a ballot of all Holders, and only after the Founder Council has consulted the Council of Sections (Article 12.4):

- amending this constitution (Article 11);
- the block's vote on any matter of nilware.io IKE that needs the Qualified Majority (IKE Article 6.5) or two-thirds of the capital, including a new Prozent (IKE Article 6.6), and the block's vote on the Reserved Matters of nilware.io Holdings AE (AE Article 7.4);
- the block's consent to any matter in Clause 5.1C of the Shareholders' Agreement, which needs the consent of every Holder (Article 4.2);
- electing the Founder Council (Article 7.1);
- a sale under Article 9.3;
- assigning a Brand Asset to a new vehicle or other holder of record (other than a designation under Article 2A.3 on the events listed there), abandoning a Brand Asset, and amending the Brand Licence Agreement (Articles 2A.3 and 2A.6);
- changing the legal form of the Founder Pool, or ending it (Article 16).

10.3 **Majority.** A ballot is decided by a majority of the Class F votes cast, except where Articles 4.2 and 11 require more, and except elections, which follow Article 7.1. A tie is a rejection. In an optional referendum on a decision of the Founder Council on how the block votes (Article 10.1), the decision stands unless a majority of the Class F votes cast is against it, so a tie leaves it in force [owner may confirm].

10.4 **Initiative.** Holders of [10]% of the Class F votes may propose in writing an amendment of this constitution, or a decision on how the block votes or what it proposes at nilware.io IKE or nilware.io Holdings AE, within the nilware.io documents. The Founder Council puts the initiative to a ballot within [90] days, and may put a counter-proposal beside it. An initiative may not propose anything the IKE Articles, the AE Articles or the Shareholders' Agreement forbid.

10.5 **Ballots.** Ballots are held in writing or electronically, over at least [10] days, or over [7] days for a ballot on how the block votes on a matter at nilware.io IKE or nilware.io Holdings AE, which ends before the meeting or the deadline for the written resolution (Article 7.4).
