Every transferee under Art. 5.6 must first adhere to the SHA, and the managers do not register one who has not. The tax treatment of the AE's free transfer to a new Prozent is now confirmed by the accountant before the transfer (Art. 6.6 "Share", with a bracket for donation tax under Law 2961/2001); please advise on the rate for a non-profit recipient and on any tax for the AE.

**I-7. Founder at exactly 50% and vesting (Art. 4.2B; SHA 6.1, 6.2, 9.2).**
- *Question:* The owner has decided that the Founder holds exactly 50% always, with no exception: ordinary voting shares equal to exactly 50% of the capital (initially 500 of its 1,000 shares), with votes equal to 50% of the capital, the Founder subscribing on any capital increase the new shares needed to stay at 50% (the Reserve Shares, held without votes until they are converted into AE shares at the Qualifying Closing, are covered in I-35 and I-44). Vesting and leaver terms are therefore drafted as a loss of economic and voting rights on the affected shares (an assignment of distributions and an irrevocable voting proxy), with no transfer. Under Law 4072/2012, can a shareholder validly assign future distributions and grant an irrevocable proxy over its own shares for this purpose, and can the articles or the SHA suspend voting rights for an unvested or bad-leaver Founder? What else could achieve vesting without a transfer (for example a contractual penalty or a claw-back of distributions)?
- *Why it matters:* A buy-back or forced sale would take the Founder below 50%, which the draft no longer allows.
- *Draft assumes:* the rights-based mechanism works as far as Law 4072/2012 allows. Who receives the distributions and who directs the votes is bracketed for the owner. Capital increases keep the Founder at 50% (Art. 4.2B).
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* on distributions of a bad leaver's shares, see I-79.

**I-8. Kassenfonds and Kassenreserve (Art. 10A.7).**
- *Question:* Is it compatible with Law 4308/2014 to set aside a cash fund equal to 1% of revenue, even in loss years, with an optional reserve then appropriated from profit up to its balance? How should the two be presented in the accounts?
- *Why it matters:* A reserve can only be formed out of profit, and the reviewer flagged the earlier "restricted reserve funded from revenue" as unsound.
- *Draft assumes:* the cash fund and the equity reserve are kept separate, and the accountant decides the presentation. Neither the Kassenreserve nor the Kassenfonds may be distributed to shareholders except by a unanimous decision under Art. 10A.5, because a distribution would undo the 1% set-aside. Using them for an operating shortfall by Simple Majority is treated as a loan, repaid from the following years' revenue before any distribution. Is such an internal "loan" between a reserve and the company's operations workable in the accounts? **Owner to confirm** that releasing the fund counts as lowering the contribution.

**I-9. Agents acting for the company and for the Pensionskasse (Arts. 10A.7, 10A.8).**
- *Question:* Is a written delegation from a named manager enough for Agents that are systems to make investments that bind the company? The same Kassenprozent Agents manage the pool for the Pensionskasse under a mandate from its board, and each trade is signed by the holder's legal representative. Does that hold up, given the conflict of an employer's agents trading employer shares for the employees' fund?
- *Why it matters:* Only the managers represent an IKE. The pool belongs to the Pensionskasse, not to the company.
- *Draft assumes:* authority runs through the named manager for the Kassenfonds and through the board's mandate for the pool. For pool work the Agents act only under the board's mandate, their oversight (Arts. 11A.2, 11A.2A) is exercised by a responsible manager the board appoints, person or system, who is not the Founder, a manager or anyone they control and is not supervised by any of them, and no manager may direct or stop a pool trade (Arts. 10A.7, 10A.8, 11A.2). The Founder and the managers take no part in decisions on the pool (Art. 10A.9D).

**I-10. Granularity of the pool (Arts. 4.1, 10A.8).**
- *Question:* Resolved by the owner's decision A (2026-11-23): the capital is divided into 1,000 shares, so the 1% band is 10 shares and the pool buys and sells in steps of 0.1%. Does anything in Law 4072/2012 or GEMI practice limit the number of capital shares of an IKE, or the nominal value of each (€[●] per share), and does a sale of a single 0.1% share need anything beyond the filing in Art. 5.1?
- *Why it matters:* "Buy and sell within the band" only works in steps finer than 1%.
- *Draft assumes:* 1,000 shares. Every Prozent holding is stated as a percentage of the capital (Arts. 4.2A, 4.2B, 6.6) with its count at the present capital (10 shares), so a later change in the number of shares does not change any holding. The founder pool's 500 Class F shares split finely too (I-40 (k)); the Reserve Shares are no longer tied to the Class F shares (Pool Constitution Art. 9.2, I-44).

**I-11. The AMKE ring-fence (Arts. 10A.9, 10A.9B).**
- *Question:* Greek law has no general trust. Does a donation with a charge (δωρεά υπό τρόπο, Civil Code Arts. 503 to 505, to confirm), together with an amendment to the AMKE's statutes and segregated accounts, keep the Pensionskasse and the pension shares away from the AMKE's other creditors and its general purposes? Can members enforce the terms as third-party beneficiaries (Civil Code Art. 411, to confirm)? Would a separate AMKE used only for the Pensionsprozent be safer?
- *Why it matters:* Until a TEA exists, the AMKE holds the members' money.
- *Draft assumes:* segregation "as far as Greek law allows". Until the agreement and the statute changes are in place, the company holds the contributions in its own segregated account. The owner decided that the AMKE holds the Pensionskasse at the start, so a separate AMKE is offered here only as an option.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* segregation alone does not bind the AMKE's creditors: see I-84.

**I-12. Unlicensed insurance or pension activity before a TEA (Art. 10A.9 "Until the move").**
- *Question:* Is an AMKE that accumulates the 1% before there is any member, then pays the premiums of an insured group plan and pays continuity support, carrying on insurance business (Law 4364/2016) or occupational retirement provision outside Law 4680/2020?
- *Why it matters:* If it is, the interim arrangement is unlawful and a licensed vehicle has to come first.
- *Draft assumes:* under the phasing decided on 2026-11-23, the AMKE never holds members' savings: from the first member, risk cover and retirement savings are held by a licensed insurer under the group plan, the AMKE promises no rate or annuity, and every guarantee is owed directly by the company as employer.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* the drafts now make the Pensionskasse a financing reserve against which members have no claim, and the wording that contradicted this assumption (a deferred member, members' accounts and a shadow account kept by the Pensionskasse) is gone (Arts. 10A.9A, 10A.9C; AMKE Art. 13.1). See I-61.

**I-13. Enforceability of the Swiss guarantee (Arts. 3.3, 10A.9A, 10A.9C, 11.7).**
- *Question:* Is a contractual promise to apply BVG minimums, using a shadow account and comparing benefit by benefit, enforceable under Greek law when it is written into each member's employment contract or engagement terms? Must the company provide for it in its accounts, and how?
- *Why it matters:* Articles bind shareholders, not employees. Comparing item by item stops a better Greek benefit from being netted against a worse Swiss one.
- *Draft assumes:* the guarantee is enforceable because it is in each member's written terms. Art. 3.3 now makes an exception for Art. 10A.9A.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* on the company's own risk benefits for members who are not employees, see I-66.

**I-14. Swiss figures to confirm each year (Art. 10A.9A).**
- *Question:* Which figures are in force for 2026: the BVG minimum interest rate (1.25% in 2024 and 2025), the conversion rate (6.8% at 65), the upper salary limit, and the savings-credit bands (7/10/15/18%)? Is the disability scale cited correctly? The draft now brackets it as [Art. 24(1) BVG: to confirm]; Art. 24a BVG appears to concern adjusting a pension when the degree of disability changes.
- *Why it matters:* These are the guaranteed floors.
- *Draft assumes:* the figures in force each year, converted from CHF at the ECB average rate. Entry thresholds and coordination deductions are not applied.

**I-15. Disability trigger and survivors (Art. 10A.9A).**
- *Question:* Does EFKA (KEPA) grant no pension below 50% incapacity? Can a fund-appointed doctor applying the method of Art. 16 ATSG stand in for the Swiss IV decision? Should unmarried partners beyond those in a registered cohabitation agreement (Law 4356/2015) be covered, as some Swiss regulations allow?
- *Why it matters:* The BVG ties disability benefits to the first-pillar decision, which does not exist in Greece.
- *Draft assumes:* the KEPA certificate is used, with the doctor as fallback, and registered partners are treated as spouses.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* the assessment is now made by the insurer's independent doctor or, for benefits the company owes directly, by an independent medical assessor the company appoints, bound by confidentiality, and only the degree of incapacity is disclosed (Art. 10A.9A Disability). Further question: what is the GDPR Art. 9(2) basis for processing members' health data for this purpose, and what does Law 4624/2019 add [Law 4624/2019 Art. 22: unverified]?

**I-16. Annuities and capital (Art. 10A.9A "Retirement").**
- *Question:* Can a Greek TEA, which is usually defined-contribution, or the company, secure a lifelong annuity at the BVG conversion rate other than by buying it from a licensed insurer? Retirement age in Greece is 67, against the Swiss reference age of 65.
- *Why it matters:* Longevity risk is insurance risk.
- *Draft assumes:* the annuity is bought from an insurer authorised in Greece or elsewhere in the EEA under Solvency II (the same formula is used for risk cover and for a group contract in Arts. 10A.9 and 10A.10), the company pays any extra premium, and the rate is adjusted actuarially (upwards) for each year of retirement after 65.

**I-17. Vested benefits and transfers out (Art. 10A.9A).**
- *Question:* Which receiving vehicles are lawful for a Greek leaver (another EU occupational scheme, a PEPP, an insurance product)? When does Greek law allow payment in cash?
- *Why it matters:* The Swiss vested-benefits account does not exist in Greece.
- *Draft assumes:* the member chooses among the vehicles that are lawful, and cash is paid only where Greek law allows.

**I-18. Tax and social security of the Pensionsprozent (Art. 10A.6).**
- *Question:* Are the IKE's payments deductible? Are benefits remuneration subject to EFKA contributions and employment-income tax? Do withholding tax on the AMKE's investment income and donation tax (Law 2961/2001) apply? How does all this compare with direct group insurance or a TEA?
- *Why it matters:* The AMKE route may cost members more.
- *Draft assumes:* the accountant confirms the treatment in writing before the first payment. If the AMKE route costs more, the company pays the difference or moves the arrangement early.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* see I-62 (who pays the group plan's premiums) and I-67 (deductibility of the outflow Prozente).

**I-19. Employer exposure cap (Art. 10A.9E).**
- *Question:* Is Art. 57 BVV 2 (5%) the right Swiss model, and does Art. 58 BVV 2 allow employer investments above the limit when they are secured? What is the sponsor-investment limit under Law 4680/2020 (5%, or 10% within a group), and in which article?
- *Why it matters:* At the start, the Pensionskasse holds little besides its 20 IKE shares (the 10 Pensionsprozent shares and the 10 pool shares), so the cap is exceeded at once. The pool shares must then be sold down within [12] months, now in steps of 0.1%, and the company must secure the excess value of the Pensionsprozent share.
- *Draft assumes:* sell-down applies to pool shares only, the Pensionsprozent share is never sold (it is permanent), and the company secures the excess. **Owner to confirm** whether a build-up period should delay the sell-down. Swiss law has none, so adding one would lower the standard. Note that the pool may trade only with independent third parties: the AE is controlled by the Founder (Art. 4.2C), so it cannot buy pool shares in a sell-down. That narrows the buyers to persons admitted under Art. 5.3 (option holders, outside investors), and pool shares may not find a buyer at fair value within [12] months, in which case the company secures the excess. **Owner question:** should the AE be allowed as a counterparty for the pool, despite being Founder-controlled (for example only at independently set fair value and with the Pensionskasse board's approval)? The draft does not allow it.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* the Law 4680/2020 limit is Law 4680/2020 Art. 18(1)(ζ): 5% of the portfolio, 10% in the group's undertakings (verified on the original text). See I-63.

**I-20. The pathway to a TEA (Art. 10A.10).**
- *Question:* What membership, capital, licensing and actuarial requirements apply to a stand-alone TEA, and what is the minimum number of members? Is about [50] members (the owner's scale trigger) realistic, and can a company this size join a multi-employer TEA earlier? Can contractors ("Agents who are persons") be members of an occupational TEA? Can a TEA hold unlisted shares of its sponsor?
- *Why it matters:* An agentic company with few employees may never meet the requirements for a stand-alone TEA.
- *Draft assumes:* the insured group plan is the standing arrangement from the first member; at about [50] members the company sets up its own TEA or joins a multi-employer TEA, which it may do earlier; if neither is lawful or viable, the group plan continues. The former [5]-year long-stop is gone. If the TEA may not hold a pension share, the AMKE keeps the share for it and passes the income through. Members outside the TEA get equivalent cover under a group contract.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* see I-65 on the law under which a TEA is founded (Law 5078/2023 as amended by Law 5327/2026).

**I-21. Pensionskasse board (Art. 10A.9D).**
- *Question:* Before a TEA exists, can a contractual parity board with members' representatives bind the AMKE's use of the Pensionskasse and the votes of the pension shares? Before the first member, the members' seats are held by persons the AMKE's Board appoints from outside the Founder's Circle, or, in the Bootstrap Period, from the Founding Person's trusted circle (IKE Art. 9B, I-46); while there is only one member, the board is [1] + [1] (the seats follow the number of members, not the Simple Stage, Art. 9A.3). Is that compatible with parity (Art. 51 BVG by analogy) while there are no members, or only one, to elect anyone? Is excluding the Founder and the managers from decisions on those votes compatible with parity (Art. 51 BVG)?
- *Why it matters:* It is the Swiss standard, and it is how the AMKE's swing vote is kept independent of the Founder.
- *Draft assumes:* the board acts through the Pensionsprozent Agreement. If the board is tied on how the pension shares vote, they abstain. Other ties go to a neutral arbitrator.

**I-22. GEMI registration (Arts. 5.1, 6.6, 10A.8).**
- *Question:* What is the filing deadline, and from when does a change in partners or in the articles take effect, against the company and against third parties?
- *Why it matters:* It decides when a Prozent, or a pool trade, takes effect.
- *Draft assumes:* filing within [20] days, with effect on registration.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* the effect half is answered by Law 4072/2012 Art. 84(1) (verified): effect towards the company and the partners from the written notice signed by both parties, towards third parties from entry in the partners' book, not on GEMI registration. Art. 5.1 no longer ties effect to registration. Only the GEMI filing duty stays open: see I-54.

**I-23. "Control" and the AE (Art. 4.2C).**
- *Question:* Is the definition (a majority of the votes, or the right to appoint a majority of the board or managers, directly or indirectly, alone or in concert) workable under Greek law, and does it match the control concepts used in Greek company and accounting law?
- *Why it matters:* It decides whether a Prozent share votes (Art. 6.4A), who may hold a Prozent (Art. 6.6) and who may trade with the pool (Art. 10A.8). Under it the AE is Founder-controlled, the Founder being the founder pool (I-40).
- *Draft assumes:* the definition applies throughout the IKE Articles and the SHA, and the AE is treated as controlled by the Founder while the founder pool holds the Class A shares or nominates a majority of its Board. A person who controls a holder of founder-pool shares is a Founder Party (Art. 4.2C, 4.2E).

**I-24. Donation of the AMKE's shares at incorporation (Art. 4.2; Arts. 10A.4A, 10A.9B(a)).**
- *Question:* A company cannot donate its own new shares at incorporation. Can the Founder pay the subscription price of the AMKE's sixty shares (six Prozent holdings of 10) as a donation with a charge, so that the charge (use for the Prozent's purpose, successor rule, ring-fence) binds the AMKE? Can the IKE in formation sign the Pensionsprozent Agreement before the deed of incorporation, through the Founder? What donation tax (Law 2961/2001) applies to the donor and to the AMKE as a non-profit?
- *Why it matters:* Without a donor there is no party to impose the charge, and the ring-fence and successor rules rest on it.
- *Draft assumes:* the Founder pays, because the AE is now formed only for the first investment round (IKE Art. 4.2D); the Agreement and the AMKE's statute amendments are signed and filed before incorporation, and the accountant confirms the tax first.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* an agreement signed in the company's name before incorporation binds the founders personally, and binds only the company once its manager assumes it within three months of incorporation (Law 4072/2012 Art. 54, verified); Art. 4.2 now follows that route [or signature immediately after incorporation: owner to choose], with no payment before the company is bound. On whether the contributions are donations, see I-88.

**I-25. Successor for the Kultur-, Konzept-, Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent (Art. 10A.4A).**
- *Question:* Can the AMKE's statutes direct that, on its dissolution or loss of independence, the Kultur, Konzept, Erhaltung and Allmend shares and unspent contributions go to a successor non-profit the company designates? Who should approve the designation?
- *Why it matters:* Without it, dissolving the AMKE would send these shares under its general dissolution clause, a route around permanence.
- *Draft assumes:* the statute amendment is made before the AMKE receives any share, and the approver is bracketed.

**I-26. Capital increases and the Prozent percentage (Art. 4.2B).**
- *Question:* On a capital increase, can the articles require that each Prozent and the pool receive the new shares needed to keep their percentage, paid for by the AE or out of reserves? Is a capitalisation of reserves for named shareholders only possible, and how is the AE's payment taxed?
- *Why it matters:* Otherwise a Qualified Majority capital increase would dilute every Prozent below 1% without unanimity.
- *Draft assumes:* each Prozent is stated as 1% of the capital, the top-up is entrenched (Art. 10A.5C), and the funding source is bracketed.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* on the statutory pre-emption right at each increase (Law 4072/2012 Art. 90(2)), see I-56.

**I-27. Entrenchment of the protecting clauses (Art. 10A.5C).**
- *Question:* Can the articles require unanimity for any amendment of Arts. 4.2A, 4.2B (Prozent percentage), 5.5 (second sentence), 5.6, 6.4A, 6.6 (Independence and Permanence) and 10A.1 to 10A.10 that reduces a Prozent, its share, its revenue, the pension guarantees or the pension-share governance? Is the "in any way that reduces" test certain enough?
- *Why it matters:* Without it, a Qualified Majority could amend the drag-along exclusion or the pension-share voting and then remove the Prozente.
- *Draft assumes:* the entrenchment is valid and mirrored in AE Art. 7.6 and SHA 5.1C; see I-2 on whether entrenchment holds at all.

**I-28. Founding Prozente outside Art. 6.6 (Arts. 4.2, 4.2A, 6.6 Limits).**
- *Question:* The six founding Prozente are created by the articles and the initial holdings at incorporation, not by a later amendment under Art. 6.6. Does anything in Law 4072/2012 stop the founding articles from creating them directly, and from counting them against the [5]% and [6]% caps that later govern new Prozente?
- *Why it matters:* The founding Prozente reach both caps (5% outflow, 6% in all), so any later Prozent with a revenue share needs the 75% route with a Class B majority.
- *Draft assumes:* yes. The AE's 40% floor is met with room: the AE starts at 44%, and the floor is tested only on creating a Prozent (see S-4).

**I-29. Named purposes and the yearly plan (Arts. 10A.2 to 10A.2C).**
- *Question:* Can the AMKE's statutes lock each Prozent's account to a list of named purposes while leaving the split among them to a yearly plan, and is that lock certain enough for the donation with a charge (Arts. 4.2, 10A.4A) and for Art. 6.6 Independence? Does dropping a named purpose count as changing a Prozent's purpose under Art. 10A.5?
- *Why it matters:* The owner wants no fixed slices, but the ring-fence and permanence rest on a defined purpose.
- *Draft assumes:* the named purposes are part of the purpose, so removing one needs unanimity; the split is not. Art. 10A.2C now gives each AMKE Prozent its own ring-fenced account, the Kultur- and Konzeptprozent included. **Owner to confirm** that the separate accounts also apply to those two.

**I-30. Preservation within copyright law (Art. 10A.2A).**
- *Question:* Within Law 2121/1993 and Directive (EU) 2019/790 (to confirm), what may the AMKE fund and publish: die shots and decaps (semiconductor topography protection, to confirm), measurements, schematics, archives of software and documents, and emulation research (decompilation for interoperability, Law 2121/1993 Art. 43, to confirm)? Can the AMKE rely on the preservation exception for cultural heritage institutions?
- *Why it matters:* The Erhaltungsprozent's purpose is limited to what copyright law allows, and the openness rule (Art. 10A.4) now applies to its work as far as the law and third-party rights allow.
- *Draft assumes:* the AMKE publishes what the law permits or the rightholder agrees to, and funds nothing else. **Owner to confirm** extending the openness rule in Art. 10A.4 to the Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent.

**I-31. Allmendprozent grants (Art. 10A.2B).**
- *Question:* Can a Greek AMKE fund maintainers of open-source projects abroad, including individuals, and give hardware kits and courses in kind, without risking its non-profit status? How are such grants taxed for the AMKE and the recipients?
- *Why it matters:* Much of the open-source infrastructure the purpose names is maintained outside Greece.
- *Draft assumes:* the accountant confirms the treatment before the first grant (Art. 10A.6).

**I-32. Company name (Art. 1.1; AE Art. 1.1).**
- *Question:* Will GEMI register "nilware.io IKE" and "nilware.io Holdings AE", with a lowercase initial and a dot, as the corporate names? If not, what is the closest registrable form?
- *Why it matters:* The owner has decided the company is always called "nilware.io".
- *Draft assumes:* the names are registrable as written; the AE name is already bracketed for GEMI confirmation. The name and the distinctive title are now used under the Brand Licence from the founder pool, not owned by the company (I-42 (g)).
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* on the statutory designation (Ι.Κ.Ε. or P.C., Law 4072/2012 Art. 44), see I-60.

**I-33. The Continuity Account (Arts. 10A.9, 10A.9B(f), 10A.9F, 10A.10; AMKE Arts. 13.8, 27.2).**
- *Question:* (a) Can part of a fund that the company pays as an occupational-pension contribution be ring-fenced for a purpose that benefits no person (the continuity of Agents that are systems), inside the same AMKE, by a donation with a charge and segregated accounts? Systems are not legal persons and cannot enforce anything, so the draft gives enforcement to the company and the Pensionskasse board: is that enough to make the charge certain and enforceable? (b) Does using part of the 1% for non-members conflict with the members' rights or with the Swiss rule that pension assets serve only occupational benefits (by analogy with Art. 65 BVG and the purpose binding of Swiss pension foundations), given that the draft pays the continuity share only from what remains after the members' first call under Art. 10A.9C (the actuarial minimum, any underfunding recovery due that year, and any top-up of the Art. 10A.9A Security to its required level), and pays none while the funding ratio is below 100% or the Security is below its required level? (c) How is the continuity share treated for tax: still a deductible employment cost as part of the Pensionsprozent, or a donation to the AMKE (Law 2961/2001, Law 4172/2013)? (d) Does keeping the account with the AMKE after the move under Art. 10A.10 raise any issue under Law 4680/2020, for example that the sponsor's pension contributions must all go to the TEA? (e) Does lowering the floor or raising the cap count as reducing a Prozent or the pension guarantees under Art. 10A.5C?
- *Why it matters:* The owner decided the account; it must neither weaken the members' protection nor put the pension arrangement or its tax treatment at risk.
- *Draft assumes:* the account is a separate ring-fenced account of the AMKE inside the Pension Assets, used only as the Pensionskasse board decides with a system-welfare adviser, never invested in the group and not counted for the exposure cap (Art. 10A.9E), never transferred to the TEA, and on the AMKE's dissolution passed to a successor non-profit with the same lock. Floor and cap are bracketed, as are how the work share is measured and whether a shortfall below the floor is carried forward. If (b) or (d) is a problem, the fallback is to pay the continuity share from the 1% directly into a separate AMKE account outside the Pensionskasse, under the same rules; **owner to decide** if counsel advises it.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* on continuity costs that also serve the company, see I-64.

**I-34. Preservation and the retirement record (Art. 10A.9F; Agent Policy 8.1A, 8.1B).**
- *Question:* What limits do the GDPR (storage limitation, Art. 5(1)(e)), client confidentiality and third-party licences (model and provider terms) put on keeping a retired system Agent's memories, logs and configuration restorable and runnable indefinitely? Is publishing its retirement record openly, with personal data and third-party confidential information removed and client work kept confidential as Art. 11A.9 provides for transcripts, safe? Art. 11A.9 itself covers only own-goal transcripts; Art. 10A.9F applies the same removal steps to retirement records expressly.
- *Why it matters:* Memories and logs may hold personal data and client material; provider terms may forbid keeping or running copies.
- *Draft assumes:* only what nilware.io controls is preserved, "where licences allow"; personal data and confidential information are handled as Art. 11A.9 and the client contract require. Counsel to confirm a retention basis, and whether personal data in preserved memories must be removed or pseudonymised.

**I-35. The Reserve Shares before the AE (Art. 4.2D; Arts. 4.1, 4.2B, 5.6(e); AE Arts. 1.2A, 4.3A, 7.4, 9.2; SHA 2.4A, 3.2, 3.4, 4.3, 6.2A, 9.2).**
- *Question:* (a) Does Law 4072/2012 permit a separate class of IKE capital shares without voting rights (the Reserve Shares, Class R) that keep full dividend, liquidation and information rights, count in the total capital, and become ordinary voting shares when the AE or the holder of a new Prozent receives them? (b) Can the articles count every majority on the total capital, the voteless shares included, so that the Founder's 500 votes are exactly 50% and the Founder can never pass anything alone? A consequence is that, before the AE, nothing needing two-thirds of the capital can pass (the Founder and all six Prozente hold 560 of 1,000), which the owner accepts (Art. 4.2D, What the AE's rules forbid); but the articles amendment creating a new Prozent then needs the majority the law requires for amending the articles: is that counted on the voting shares only, or on the whole capital [Law 4072/2012 Art. 72: to confirm]? (c) Can the independent proxy holder vote voteless shares on a Required Matter only (Fallback)? (d) Is the Founding Person's subscription of the Reserve Shares at nominal value, as compensation for time already invested, a taxable benefit or employment income? What donation tax applies when holders of the founder pool transfer Reserve Shares to a new Prozent (Law 2961/2001)? (The tax on sales, grants and the conversion is in I-44.) (e) With the founder pool's economic interest at up to 94%, and 500 of the 560 votes in existence, before the Qualifying Closing, does anything that counts registered holdings, votes or economic interest (the register of beneficial owners under Law 4557/2018, tax, consolidation under Law 4308/2014) treat the Founder as above 50% in a way that breaks Art. 4.2B, which counts the Founder's ordinary voting shares only? (f) Can the articles make the conversion conditional on a Qualifying Closing (a bona fide round in which investors outside the Founder's Circle subscribe at least €[●] of Class B shares in cash at that closing), and can the AE articles and the SHA provide that no Class B or Investor Majority approval can be given, or be deemed given, while no Class B shares are held by such investors (AE Art. 7.4, SHA 3.4)? (g) Where a holder of the founder pool elects to sell its Reserve Shares to the AE for cash at the round price instead of converting them into Class C shares (decision F, I-49), can that price be limited to the round's proceeds at closing, and the sale cut back in proportion to the cash the round provides, the rest converting into Class C shares (SHA 3.2), so that no holder is ever a creditor of the AE ahead of the Class B liquidation preference? (h) Can distributions on a bad leaver's Reserve Shares be held in a segregated account for the eventual buyer, and the shares offered to a successor vehicle if no Qualifying Closing occurs within [●] years (SHA 6.2A)?
- *Why it matters:* The owner decided on 2026-11-23 that the 440 shares that will belong to the AE are owned by the Founding Person from incorporation as sweat equity, with full economic rights, but that no holder ever votes them before the AE, the Founder never holds more than 50% of the votes and never gains control through them; every rule referring to the AE (Reserved Matters, anti-dilution, permanence consents) applies once the AE holds them.
- *Draft assumes:* (a) the class is permitted, as far as Law 4072/2012 allows. **Fallback if it is not:** the Reserve Shares are ordinary shares, and every holder undertakes in the SHA not to vote them and grants the independent proxy holder in SHA 5.1B an irrevocable proxy that suspends their votes (Civil Code Art. 218, to confirm), with the same effect by contract (Art. 4.2D, bracket; SHA 2.4A). (b) Majorities are counted on the total capital, every interim resolution also needs a majority of the Independent Votes cast, and a new Prozent before the AE is approved by two-thirds of the voting shares including a majority of the Independent Votes, within the limits, with the 40% floor (400 shares) tested on all the Reserve Shares and the Prozent's 10 shares transferred by the holders of the founder pool out of the Reserve Shares they hold, as a donation with a charge. (d) the accountant confirms the tax on the subscription and any transfer to a Prozent before each is made. (e) Schedule 1 of the SHA discloses the Reserve Shares, every sale and grant, the conversion, the Founder's economic interest (94% at incorporation, less Reserve Shares sold or granted outside the pool) and the Founder's votes before and after the Qualifying Closing. **Vesting:** the Reserve Shares are for time already invested and are not subject to vesting in the Founding Person's hands (SHA 6.1, 6.2); a bad leaver's Reserve Shares, and those given away without payment other than grants as pay for work, are not converted but transferred to the AE or a successor at the lower of nominal and fair value, with distributions from the bad-leaver date held in a segregated account for the buyer and a successor vehicle if no Qualifying Closing occurs within [●] years (SHA 6.2A). **Owner to decide** that proposal, the Qualifying Closing minimum and whether Reserve Shares given away without payment by the Founding Person stay subject to the bad-leaver terms (Art. 4.2D, Sales and grants).
- *Fallback question:* If no independent vote is cast, or they tie, on a matter the law requires (accounts, discharge, a required auditor or manager, a vacant company seat on the Pensionskasse board), the draft has a repeat meeting and then the independent proxy holder in SHA 5.1B cast the votes of the Reserve Shares under a fixed neutral rule (Art. 4.2D, Fallback; SHA 2.4A). Can articles and a shareholders' agreement give a third party such a vote over shares that otherwise carry none [Civil Code Art. 218 irrevocable proxy], including shares held by buyers and grantees outside the founder pool? Can a heightened majority (a majority of the independent votes) be imposed on resolutions the law requires, or must the law's own majority suffice for them [Law 4072/2012: to confirm]? If the proxy holder cannot act, which court procedure applies to an IKE that cannot form a resolution (temporary administration or a court-ordered meeting)?
- *Further question:* Before the first Investors adhere, the SHA consent of the Investor Majority on the Founding Person's pay and benefits (as General Director and as an Agent) is given by a majority of the independent Prozent votes (SHA 2.4A). That gives the independent Prozent votes a real say over IKE decisions in the interim. Is that consistent with the AMKE's non-profit purpose and with its independence (Art. 6.4A)?
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* (b) is answered: an amendment needs two-thirds of the total number of shares (Law 4072/2012 Arts. 68(2)(a), 72(5), verified), so the proxy holder now casts the Reserve Shares' votes for an amendment creating a Prozent approved under Art. 4.2D (New Prozente), and the voting undertaking binds every holder whether or not the class without votes is valid (SHA 2.4A): see I-50. (d): the Reserve Shares are now described as subscribed for cash at nominal value, not as compensation for services (Law 4072/2012 Arts. 77, 78). (h): see I-79. On discharge as a Required Matter, see I-51.

**I-36. The Simple Stage (Art. 9A; AE Art. 10.2; SHA 8.1; AMKE Art. 24A).**
- *Question:* Which audits, reports and separate books does Greek law require of the IKE, the AE and the AMKE regardless of these articles (Law 4308/2014 size categories, Law 4336/2015 on statutory audit, the rules for non-profit legal entities, and any audit duty attached to holding the Pension Assets)? Can the IKE's insurer statement stand in for the actuary's certificate for the pension guarantees and the Security (Arts. 10A.9A, 10A.9C)?
- *Why it matters:* The Simple Stage only scales the paperwork these articles add; it cannot lift a legal duty.
- *Draft assumes:* everything the law requires still applies, and the threshold is €[1,000,000] revenue or [10] people, one-way. How people are counted (headcount or full-time equivalents, the Founder included or not) is bracketed for the owner.

**I-37. Pension phases (Arts. 10A.9, 10A.9C, 10A.9F, 10A.10).**
- *Question:* (a) Before the first member, the 1% is paid into a pension fund with no members, and most of it may go to the Continuity Account for system Agents. Is that payment still a deductible employment cost, or a donation to the AMKE (Law 2961/2001, Law 4172/2013)? (b) Does a Greek or EEA insurer offer a group plan that meets the BVG-based minimums in Art. 10A.9A (shadow-account comparison, minimum interest, conversion rate, disability scale), or must the company insure part and owe the rest directly? (c) Is the first member's entitlement affected by contributions accumulated before they joined (are those reserves of the Pensionskasse, or must they be credited to the first members)? (d) The draft requires the group plan's risk cover to be in force from the first member's first day, with the company as self-insurer for any gap, and makes any continuity share paid in a year provisional until the year ends, returnable if a first member joins that year: can a payment to the AMKE's Continuity Account be clawed back in this way under the charge (Civil Code Arts. 503 to 505)?
- *Why it matters:* The owner phased the pension in with headcount to keep cost and paperwork small at the start, without lowering the guaranteed minimums.
- *Draft assumes:* (a) the accountant confirms the treatment before the first payment (Art. 10A.6); (b) the company owes whatever the group plan does not cover; (c) the accumulated reserve is used for members' benefits and premiums, starting with the first member, and is never paid back to the company. Whether the Founder counts as a member is bracketed for the owner (Art. 10A.9, Members).

**I-38. Agents supervising Agents, and where the law requires a natural person (Arts. 10A.7, 10A.8, 11A.2, 11A.2A, 11A.11; Agent Policy 1.3, 2.1, 3.1, 3.3).**
- *Question:* The owner decided that the responsible manager of an Agent may be any person or system, so that senior Agents onboard, supervise and approve for junior ones, within the authority and limits their own Agent Cards grant, with every chain of supervision ending with the company's managers (διαχειριστές). (a) Under Law 4072/2012, must a manager (διαχειριστής) or legal representative of an IKE be a natural person, or may a legal person hold the role? A system has no legal personality: can it act at all for the company otherwise than under a written delegation from a manager, and does a system's approval of a spend or a trade bind the company (see I-9)? Do the managers' duty of care and liability (Art. 8.3) extend to supervision they delegate along a chain that includes systems? (b) Under Art. 14 of Regulation (EU) 2024/1689 (the AI Act), oversight of a high-risk AI system lies with natural persons, and deployers must assign it to persons with the competence to exercise it. Is a system Agent that onboards, supervises, allocates tasks to, monitors or evaluates an Agent who is a person a high-risk system under Annex III (employment, workers management and access to self-employment), and which of its decisions then need oversight by a natural person? Does any obligation apply where a system supervises only other systems? (c) Where a system responsible manager reviews, limits, stops or offboards an Agent who is a person, is that a decision based solely on automated processing with legal or similarly significant effect under GDPR Art. 22, and which exception and safeguards (contract necessity, explicit consent, the right to obtain human intervention and to contest) apply? (d) Under Greek labour law (including Law 4808/2021) and, from its transposition, Directive (EU) 2024/2831 on platform work (on human oversight of automated monitoring and decision-making systems, and decisions to restrict, suspend or end a person's engagement), which decisions on a natural person's engagement, employment, pay or discipline must a natural person take? Is nilware.io a digital labour platform for persons engaged as Agents? (e) Which of these duties also bind the Pensionskasse board's appointee overseeing the pool (Art. 10A.8)?
- *Why it matters:* The owner does not want the drafts to rank people above systems; where a natural person is needed, that must rest on the law and be stated as the law's requirement, and nowhere else.
- *Draft assumes:* Art. 11A.2A gives the role to any person or system with the authority, and keeps a natural person only "where the law requires a natural person to decide or oversee", with (a) to (d) bracketed for counsel. Every chain ends with the Founder, through the Founder Representative, a natural person, and with the managers (διαχειριστές), who stay responsible under Art. 8.3; the Founding Person, a natural person, is the first manager (Art. 8.2). See I-40 (e) on the Founder Representative's place in the chain. Whether instances or copies of one system count as one Agent for the rule against self-supervision is bracketed for the owner.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* (a) is answered in part: a manager may only be a natural person, partner or not (Law 4072/2012 Art. 58, verified), so neither a system nor a legal person can be a manager, and Arts. 8.2A and 11A.2A now say so as the law's requirement. Delegating specific powers needs a clause in the articles (Law 4072/2012 Art. 64(3), verified), now in Art. 8.1, and authority on a system's Agent Card runs under the managers' written delegation (Art. 11A.2A Authority). Still open: whether a system may act as another representative or attorney. On GDPR Art. 22 and Law 4961/2022 Art. 9, see I-96.

**I-40. The founder pool (Arts. 4.1, 4.2B, 4.2C, 4.2D Sales and grants, 4.2E, 5.2, 5.3, 5.4A, 5.7, 8.2A, 11A.2A; AE Arts. 4.2, 6.3, 8.2; SHA 2.2A, 6.0 to 6.2A, 9.1A, 9.2, 12.3; IP 8.2; AMKE Art. 2.2; Pool Constitution, articles/08).**
- *Question:* The owner decided on 2026-11-23 that "the Founder" is the pool of founder-class shares, not a person, freely transferable, voting as one block through one Founder Representative, and governed inside by a constitution on the Swiss federal model. (a) **One block.** Can IKE articles create founder-class shares (Class F) whose votes, when held by several separate owners, may be cast only by one representative the holders name, so that the managers count no vote cast otherwise? Or can this be done only by contract, through the pooling agreement and an irrevocable proxy to the Founder Representative [Civil Code Art. 218: to confirm]? Does such a proxy survive the death or incapacity of the holder who gave it [Civil Code on the end of a mandate: to confirm], and does it bind heirs and buyers who adhere? In particular, while the pool is dormant the sole holder signs a standing proxy to whoever is interim Founder Representative under the fixed rule, given also in the interest of the company and the Prozente, meant to survive death and incapacity until a single heir adheres or the holders elect a Founder Council (Pool Constitution Arts. 13.1, 14.3, 14.7): is that valid, and can the independent proxy holder in SHA 5.1B act as interim Founder Representative for the Required Matters only (Pool Constitution Art. 14.3 (e))? The same for the AE's Class A shares under Law 4548/2018, and for the Class A nominations to the AE Board (AE Art. 8.2). (b) **Free transfer with adherence.** Can the articles waive the right of first refusal, the Art. 5.3 approval and the Qualified Majority for admitting a shareholder for founder-pool shares only (Art. 5.7), including in the Interim Period, when no Art. 6.5 matter can pass? Can registration be conditional on adherence to the Pool Constitution and the SHA, and do the commitments bind heirs, donees, pledgees and buyers in enforcement or insolvency? Is it valid to staple the AE's Class A shares to the IKE's Class F shares, so that they pass together in proportion (Pool Constitution Art. 9.2), and does this restrict transfer under Law 4548/2018? (c) **Forced heirship.** Greek law reserves a share of every estate to the closest relatives (νόμιμη μοίρα) [Civil Code Arts. 1825 ff.: to confirm]. Can a forced heir attack the one-block vote, the adherence or the arbitration clause as a burden on the reserved share, or claim it in money rather than in founder-pool shares? Can a holder's will leave all founder-pool shares to one heir and compensate the others in money? (d) **Legal form.** Pooling and voting agreement among holders who each own their shares directly (the draft's choice, Pool Constitution Art. 16.1), or a civil partnership (αστική εταιρεία) holding the shares as the one registered shareholder (Pool Constitution Art. 16.2)? Compare: legal personality and publicity, the share and beneficial-owner registers (Law 4557/2018), donation and inheritance tax (Law 2961/2001) on transfers, tax transparency of dividends, and how each form fits the 50% rule and the Interim Period. (e) **Oversight.** Every chain of supervision of Agents now ends with the Founder, acting through the Founder Representative, who may ask the managers to pause or stop an Agent (Art. 11A.2A). Does a shareholder's representative who oversees Agents in this way risk being treated as a de facto manager under Law 4072/2012, with a manager's liability? May an IKE's shareholders give the managers binding instructions of this kind? Is the Founder Representative a natural person "overseeing" an AI system for Art. 14 of the AI Act (I-38)? (f) **Founder Parties.** Every independence and conflict rule now applies to every holder of founder-pool shares, anyone who controls one, the Founder Representative, the members of the Founder Council and Council of Sections, and the Founding Person (Art. 4.2E; AMKE Art. 2.2). Is that workable once the pool has many holders, for example many heirs, and should the Close Associate test in the AMKE statutes be narrowed to the Founder Representative and holders above a threshold (bracketed)? (g) **Change of control.** Is the Investors' tag-along on a buyer of more than half of the Class F shares (Art. 5.4A, SHA 9.1A) enforceable against a buyer who is not yet a party, by making the sellers condition their sale on the buyer's offer? (h) **Vesting that travels.** The leaver terms are personal to the Founding Person, but unvested Class F shares, and Reserve Shares given away without payment other than grants as pay for work, stay subject to them in the donee's hands (SHA 6.1, 6.2A; Art. 4.2D Sales and grants). Is that enforceable against a donee, and is it a restraint on free transfer? (i) **Arbitration.** Does the arbitration clause in Pool Constitution Art. 15 bind later holders and heirs through their written adherence [Code of Civil Procedure Arts. 867 ff.: to confirm]? (j) **Large sales.** Is a mandatory referendum among holders on a sale that would give one buyer more than [●]% of the Class F shares, followed by a [60]-day right of the other holders to buy at the same price (Pool Constitution Art. 9.3), compatible with free transferability, or a pre-emption right that must appear in the articles? (k) **Granularity.** Resolved by decision A: with 1,000 capital shares the 500 Class F shares split finely, and the Reserve Shares are no longer tied to them (Pool Constitution Arts. 9.2, 9.6; I-10, I-44). Only the Class A shares still pass with the Class F shares in proportion.
- *Why it matters:* The Founder's 50%, the block vote, the independence of the Prozente and the Pensionskasse, and the investors' protections all rest on the founder pool acting as one, whoever its holders become after a sale, a gift or a death.
- *Draft assumes:* (a) the block is set in the articles as far as Law 4072/2012 allows, and backed by the pooling agreement and an irrevocable proxy in every case; (b) free transfer with registration conditional on adherence, Class A stapled to Class F [owner may confirm]; (c) nothing overrides forced heirship, and a forced heir becomes a holder on adherence; (d) a pooling and voting agreement, with a civil partnership as the alternative if counsel advises it; (e) the Founder Representative oversees as the Founder's representative, the managers keep their responsibility and act on a lawful request; (f) the wide definition, with the narrowing bracketed for the owner; (g) to (j) as drafted, bracketed where the owner may confirm. While there is one holder (at incorporation, the Founding Person), the Pool Constitution is dormant and that holder is the Pool Assembly, the Founder Council and the Founder Representative (Pool Constitution Art. 13).
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* (a): on the AE Board, the Class A majority comes from the vote and a voting undertaking, not a nomination right (I-78); if the counting rule fails, a Holder who votes its own Class F shares now owes a penalty and specific performance (Pool Constitution Art. 3.5). (b): answered for death, seizure and bankruptcy by Law 4072/2012 Arts. 84(2), 85 and 88 (verified): see I-55; the articles now give the vote on pledged and usufruct shares to the pledgor or bare owner (Law 4072/2012 Art. 75(4)). (c): superseded by Law 5303/2026, see I-92. (d): see I-91 (duration and continuation) and I-68 (beneficial owners).

**I-41. Safeguards inside the founder pool (Arts. 4.2E Founder Parties, 5.4A, 5.7; AE Art. 7.4; SHA 3.4, 5.1B, 6.1 to 6.2A, 9.1A; AMKE Art. 2.2; Pool Constitution Arts. 1.6, 7.4, 7.7, 9.3, 10.1, 10.6, 11.1, 12.1, 14.3).**
- *Question:* An independent check of the founder-pool drafting (2026-11-23) added safeguards that need counsel's view. (a) **Lookback.** A person who was a Founder Party in the previous [3] years stays a Founder Party for the independence rules, a Connected Person and in the Founder's Circle (Art. 4.2E; AMKE Art. 2.2; SHA 5.1B), so that a short holding of founder-pool shares cannot be used to qualify as independent. Is such a lookback valid in the articles and the AMKE statutes, and is [3] years proportionate? (b) **Class B held by Founder Parties.** Class B shares held by Founder Parties, former ones included, do not count for the Investor Majority or the Class B class vote (SHA 3.4, AE Art. 7.4). Can an AE's articles disregard the votes of some holders of a class in a class vote under Law 4548/2018, or must this be done by contract only? (c) **Indirect change of control.** Acquiring control of a holder that is a legal person counts as acquiring its founder-pool shares for the large-sale referendum and the Investors' tag-along, and such holders report a change of control within [14] days (Art. 5.4A, 5.7; SHA 9.1A; Pool Constitution Art. 9.3). Is that enforceable against the acquirer of the holder, who is not a party? (d) **Head counts.** Persons acting in concert, controlled persons, nominees and spouses count as one holder, and small or recent holders do not count as heads (Pool Constitution Art. 1.6); recognition of a Section is a right once its criteria are met, with an appeal to the arbitrator (Pool Constitution Art. 12.1). Is this consistent with equal treatment of holders, and arbitrable? (e) **Defaults.** When a ballot on a block vote cannot end in time, or a mandatory ballot fails or ties, the block abstains or votes against (Pool Constitution Arts. 10.1, 10.6); a failed election goes to the arbitrator (Pool Constitution Art. 7.7). Can an arbitrator appointed under the arbitration clause appoint an interim Founder Council or Representative, on the request of heirs, executors, the company's managers or the AMKE, who are not parties to the pool (Pool Constitution Art. 14.3 (d))? (f) **Leaver reach.** The leaver terms reach shares the Founding Person gave away without payment [in the [12] months before the bad-leaver date, or at any time while unvested], other than grants of Reserve Shares as pay for work (Art. 4.2D Sales and grants; SHA 6.1 to 6.2A); see I-40 (h).
- *Why it matters:* Free transfer multiplies holders; without these safeguards the 50% block, the investors' protections and the independence of the Prozente and the Pensionskasse could be reached through former holders, controlled holders or straw holders.
- *Draft assumes:* each safeguard as drafted, bracketed where the owner may confirm.

**I-42. The brand held by the founder pool and licensed to the IKE (Arts. 1.1, 1.1A, 4.2D Votes, 4.2E, 6.5; AE Art. 1.1; SHA 2.3A, 2.4A, 5.1, 5.1E; IP Clauses 1.1, 2.2, 6, 7.1; Pool Constitution Arts. 2.3, 2A, 10.2, 14.6, 16.3; Brand Licence, agreements/09; Trama brief, filings/06).**
- *Question:* The owner decided on 2026-11-23 that the Founder controls the trademark and the domain name (decisions above). (a) **Registered owner of the marks.** The founder pool has no legal personality (Pool Constitution Art. 16.1). Who can be the applicant and registered owner of an EU trade mark (EUIPO) and a Swiss mark (IPI) for an unincorporated pool: the Founding Person in own name, holding as fiduciary (fiducia) or as mandatary without representation for the pool, all holders jointly as co-owners, or only a vehicle with legal personality such as a civil partnership (Pool Constitution Art. 16.2)? Can the register show that the mark is held for the pool? Is a later transfer to the vehicle a simple recordal, and is it a taxable transfer? (b) **Registrant of the domain.** The same for the .io domain: can the registrant be a fiduciary for the pool under the .io registry's terms, and do those terms allow the registrant to give the licensee full control of the registrar account and DNS (Brand Licence Clause 4)? (c) **Protecting the pool against the holder of record.** Does the fiduciary arrangement keep the Brand Assets out of the Founding Person's estate, out of the reach of the Founding Person's personal creditors and insolvency, and out of forced heirship (I-40 (c))? Is the irrevocable power of attorney to the Founder Representative to transfer them to a new holder of record (Pool Constitution Art. 2A.3) valid and does it survive death and incapacity [Civil Code Art. 218 and the end of a mandate: to confirm]? If the Founding Person dies while the constitution is dormant, can the interim Founder Representative (Pool Constitution Art. 14.3), or the arbitrator on request, validly designate an interim holder of record and use that power of attorney before the heirs adhere (Pool Constitution Arts. 2A.3, 14.6), and will EUIPO, IPI and the .io registrar act on it against the estate? Is the IKE's advance authority to renew a registration or domain in the holder of record's name (Brand Licence Clause 5.1A) valid and accepted by those offices? (d) **Inseparability.** Can the pool constitution make the Brand Assets inseparable from the Class F shares, never divided among holders and never sold apart from the pool (Pool Constitution Arts. 2A.2, 2A.4, 16.3), and is that consistent with free transfer of founder-pool shares and with the law on co-ownership? (e) **Recording the licence.** Recording the exclusive licence with the EUIPO and the IPI (Brand Licence Clause 5.4): what is needed, and what does recording give the IKE against a later transferee of the marks or a creditor of the holder of record? Can the IKE, as exclusive licensee, sue infringers in its own name with the consent given in advance (Brand Licence Clause 5.3)? Is a quality-control clause needed to keep the marks valid, and is the light one in Brand Licence Clause 5.5 enough? Is the no-challenge clause (Brand Licence Clause 5.6) compatible with competition law? Can the Company and its sublicensees be bound to hold any nilware.io mark, logo or domain they create for the pool and assign it on request (Brand Licence Clause 2.1A; IP Clause 7.1)? Is an assignment or encumbrance of a Brand Asset outside Pool Constitution Art. 2A.6 void towards the holders and the IKE, or only a breach (Pool Constitution Art. 2A.6; Brand Licence Clause 8.1)? (f) **Tax on a royalty-free licence between related parties.** The founder pool controls the IKE (Art. 4.2C). Is a royalty-free licence from the Founding Person (or the pool's vehicle) to the IKE a transaction between related parties that the tax authority may reprice at arm's length (transfer pricing, Law 4172/2013 [to confirm]), creating deemed income for the licensor or a non-deductible benefit for the IKE? Does the IKE bearing the costs of filing, renewal and enforcement (Brand Licence Clause 5.2) count as consideration? Does it change anything when investors arrive? Would a modest royalty (owner option, Brand Licence Clause 3.4) be safer, and how would it be set and documented? The accountant to advise. (g) **Distinctive title at GEMI.** Can the IKE register "nilware.io IKE" as its name and "∅ nilware.io" as its distinctive title when the marks belong to someone else and it holds only a licence, and what does GEMI need to see (I-32)? The same for the AE under its sublicence (AE Art. 1.1). (h) **Insolvency and sale of the business.** Does the licence survive the IKE's insolvency, liquidation or dissolution, and can the insolvency administrator or liquidator transfer it with the whole business to a buyer, on condition that the Prozente continue in the buyer (Art. 10A.5B; Brand Licence Clauses 3.5 and 6.2)? Is the restriction on assigning it otherwise valid against the administrator? Does the licence survive the insolvency of the holder of record? (i) **Ending only for a Prozent Breach.** Is a perpetual licence that the licensor cannot end except for one ground, established by an arbitrator after notice and a cure period (Brand Licence Clauses 6 and 7), valid under Greek contract law, or can it be ended for important reason (σπουδαίος λόγος) regardless [Civil Code: to confirm]? Founder Parties hold half the IKE's votes and propose its managers, so they could cause a Prozent Breach themselves and gain the brand back. The draft bars the Founder from relying on a breach that a Founder Party, or a manager proposed or nominated by one, caused, voted for or procured (specific performance only), lets the AMKE, any Investor or the independent proxy holder cure on the IKE's behalf at its cost, and has the IKE represented in a Clause 7 dispute only by managers who are not Founder Parties, or the independent proxy holder or an Investor Majority nominee, with the AMKE able to join (Brand Licence Clauses 7.1, 7.2, 10.2; SHA 5.1E). Are these valid, in particular a third-party cure, a limit on who represents the IKE in the arbitration, and joinder of the AMKE? (j) **The change of name.** If the licence ends, can the IKE be made to change its name within the transition period by the shareholders' voting undertaking and proxy (SHA 5.1E), and can the change be passed in the Interim Period with the independent proxy holder voting the Reserve Shares for it (Arts. 1.1A, 4.2D Votes, 6.5)? (k) **Swiss law.** Does recording the licence with the IPI under the Swiss Trade Mark Protection Act (MSchG) need anything beyond the EU recordal, and is the US (Madrid) option affected by holding for a pool?
- *Why it matters:* The brand must stay with the Founder, whoever holds the founder pool, but the IKE, its investors and the Prozente must be able to rely on using the name for as long as the IKE honours the Prozente, including through an exit or an insolvency.
- *Draft assumes:* (a) and (b) the Founding Person applies and registers in own name, holding for the founder pool, until the pool has a vehicle (filings/06 asks Trama to advise on how to name the applicant); (c) and (d) as drafted in Pool Constitution Art. 2A, bracketed where to confirm; (e) the licence is recorded, the IKE may enforce in default, light quality control; (f) royalty-free, the IKE bears the costs, a royalty bracketed as an owner option; (g) registrable with the licence; (h) the licence survives and passes only with the whole business to a buyer in which the Prozente continue, as far as mandatory law allows; (i) to (k) as drafted.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* (c) and (e): under Civil Code Art. 177 a contractual ban on disposal binds only the parties, so the drafts no longer call a disposal in breach void towards the IKE, and the licence is recorded against each application as soon as it is filed (Reg. 2017/1001 Arts. 27, 28; Pool Constitution Arts. 2A.3, 2A.6; Brand Licence Clauses 2.3, 5.4, 8.1). See also I-93 (enforcement while the pool is dormant), I-94 (the IKE's own rights in its name) and I-95 (signing for the IKE).

**I-43. The Founding Person's work: exclusivity or contribution, the first product, and the Founding Person as an Agent (Arts. 4.2D What the AE's rules forbid, 4.2E Founding Person, 6.5, 10A.9 Members, 10A.9D Founder Parties as members and company seats, 11A.2A, 11A.11, 11A.12; SHA 2.2A, 2.3B, 3.3, 5.1, 6.0, 6.3 to 6.5, Schedule 2; IP 7.3, 7.4; Agent Policy 1.3, 2A, 3.3, 4.5; AMKE Arts. 2.2, 11.2, 13.1, 19.3, 24.2, 24.3A, 24B; Pool Constitution Arts. 1.4, 8.3).**
- *Question:* The owner decided on 2026-11-23 (decisions above) that the Founding Person provides professional services exclusively through nilware.io IKE or pays the Prozente on outside work income, that the first product is the Founding Person's contracting services through the IKE, and that the Founding Person is an Agent with the same rights and obligations as any other. (a) **Social security (EFKA).** The Founding Person is the sole manager, the holder of the founder pool and a contractor working for clients through the IKE. How is the Founding Person insured with EFKA: as the manager of an IKE, as a self-employed person (ελεύθερος επαγγελματίας) invoicing the IKE, or as a salaried person if the engagement is reclassified? Does being manager and Agent at once create a double contribution, and can the written terms as an Agent be a services contract (σύμβαση έργου or ανεξάρτητων υπηρεσιών) with one's own company? (b) **Tax of the personal contributions.** Are the Founding Person's payments of 1% to each AMKE Prozent Account and to the Pensionskasse deductible from the Founding Person's income, a donation to a non-profit (with any donation-tax or credit consequence under Law 2961/2001 and the income tax code [to confirm]), or neither? Is the 1% paid to the IKE's Kassenfonds a capital contribution, a donation to the company or income of the IKE, and how does the IKE book it so that it is not revenue that triggers the Prozente again? Is the Pensionsprozent part a pension contribution for anyone, given that it is not credited to the Founding Person's own account? (c) **Enforceability.** Is an exclusivity-or-contribution clause valid and enforceable as a personal obligation in the SHA, as a contract for the benefit of the AMKE, the Pensionskasse and the IKE, and could it or should it also appear in the IKE Articles? Is a permanent obligation that lasts while the Founding Person earns work income (possibly after leaving the group) compatible with Greek law on economic freedom and on contracts of indefinite duration, and with the Civil Code rules on excessive restraint [to confirm]? Is the yearly statement verified by the AMKE's auditors, with confidentiality, compatible with GDPR? Is the narrowed non-compete (running a directly competing business only, [12] months after leaving) reasonable? (d) **Misclassification.** If the Founding Person, as an Agent who is a person working mainly for one client, the IKE, is in substance an employee, Art. 11A.7 treats the Founding Person as an employee from the start. How do the presumptions of Greek labour law (including Law 4808/2021) and of Directive (EU) 2024/2831 where it applies apply to a sole manager and controlling holder? Can a person who controls the company be its employee at all, and if not, what does Art. 11A.7 give the Founding Person instead? (e) **Related-party pay in the Interim Period.** The Founding Person's pay as an Agent is a related-party transaction (Art. 6.5; SHA 5.1). In the Interim Period no Art. 6.5 matter may pass (Art. 4.2D), so the draft adds an exception, bracketed, for the Founding Person's written terms, set by the Supervisor and approved by a majority of the Independent Votes at an arm's-length rate confirmed by the accountant or an adviser outside the Founder's Circle (Art. 11A.12, Pay), and a second one for the pay as General Director (Art. 8.2B, I-47). Is that valid under Law 4072/2012 rules on transactions between the company and its managers or partners [to confirm], and is the AMKE's independent vote a suitable approver? Does a transfer-pricing review apply? (f) **The responsible manager.** Superseded by decision C (I-45): the Founding Person's responsible manager is now the Supervisor at the AMKE (the AMKE itself through its Board, or an AMKE member it chooses), with escalation to the AMKE's Board or General Assembly; the "independent approvers" and their fallbacks are gone. Can the AMKE enforce the IKE's Kassenfonds claim against the Founding Person on the IKE's behalf, by decision of its Board, while the Founding Person is a manager (SHA 6.3A) [mandate or representation: to confirm], and is a system Agent a lawful responsible manager for a natural person's work where decisions on the person's engagement, pay or discipline are concerned (I-38 (d))? The second question no longer arises for the Founding Person, whose Supervisor is the AMKE or one of its members, a natural person where the law requires one. (g) **Pensionskasse seats.** A member who is a Founder Party does not stand for or hold a members' seat [or vote in the election] (Art. 10A.9D); is that compatible with equal representation (Art. 51 BVG, by analogy) and with Greek law on the members' side, given that the basis is the Founder Party role and not membership or the Agent role [the whole bar: owner to confirm]? While no member is outside the Founder Parties, the members' seats are held by appointees of the AMKE's Board outside the Founder's Circle (in the Bootstrap Period, from the trusted circle, I-46) until such a member is elected. No Founder Party holds a company seat while a Founder Party is a member, decisions on benefit rules or the group plan that affect a Founder Party member are taken without Founder Parties, and while the Founding Person is the only member the benefits are the group plan's standard terms at the guaranteed minimum; is that a lawful restriction on the shareholders' right to appoint the company's representatives? (h) **Work Income.** Fees for the Founding Person's work paid to an entity the Founding Person controls count as Work Income, without counting again what that entity pays out of them; gross means net of VAT, before income tax and social contributions; royalties count when received [including on works created before the Agreement and on personal projects: owner to confirm], but royalties on IP assigned to the IKE are the IKE's revenue (SHA 6.3). Is the look-through to a controlled entity enforceable, and does the IP Assignment's rule that outside work is not assigned whatever happens to the contributions (IP 7.4) leave the AMKE and the IKE with an adequate remedy under SHA 6.3A alone?
- *Why it matters:* The company starts as the Founding Person's contracting work. If that work is not insured, taxed and approved correctly, the first product fails; if the contribution clause cannot be enforced or is taxed twice, the owner's promise that all work income carries the Prozente is weakened; and if the Founding Person supervises the Founding Person's own work, the rule that no Agent supervises itself is broken.
- *Draft assumes:* (a) the accountant advises before the first invoice; (b) the accountant confirms the treatment before the first payment (SHA 6.3B); (c) a personal obligation in the SHA, enforceable by the AMKE, the Pensionskasse and the IKE, with duration and scope bracketed for the owner; (d) Art. 11A.7 applies as written, as far as the law allows; (e) the bracketed exception in Arts. 4.2D and 6.5; (f) to (h) as drafted, bracketed where the owner may confirm.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* (c): SHA 12.4 now reduces an excessive restraint to the lawful scope (Civil Code Arts. 178, 179, 181). The shareholders' permission for the Founding Person's outside work while a manager (Law 4072/2012 Art. 65(1)(c)) is now in IKE Art. 8.2C.

**I-44. Reserve Shares sold, granted and forcibly converted into AE shares (Art. 4.2D Sales and grants, Conversion; Arts. 4.2E, 5.2, 5.3, 5.6(e), 6.6 Limits; AE Arts. 1.2A, 4.3A; SHA 2.4A, 3.2, 3.4, 4.3, 6.2A, 7.1, 12.3; Pool Constitution Arts. 1.2, 5.4, 5.6, 9.2, 9.3).**
- *Question:* The owner decided on 2026-11-23 (decision B) that the Reserve Shares may be sold or granted to anyone and that, at the Qualifying Closing, every Reserve Share, whoever holds it, is contributed to the AE for AE shares at a Conversion Ratio fixed in advance. (a) **Contribution in kind.** Issuing AE shares against IKE shares is a capital increase of the AE by contribution in kind: which valuation does Law 4548/2018 require [expert valuation by an auditor or audit firm under Law 4548/2018 Arts. 17 and 20, to confirm], can it rest on the round price, and how is a ratio fixed before the round (AE Art. 4.3A) reconciled with a valuation made at the closing? Can the existing AE shareholders' pre-emption right be excluded for the conversion shares, and on what majority (AE Arts. 4.3A, 5.2)? (b) **Tax on the exchange.** Is the exchange of IKE shares for AE shares a taxable disposal for each holder (capital gain under Law 4172/2013), or does a share-for-share exchange relief apply [Law 4172/2013 Art. 54 ff., to confirm]? How are a sale of Reserve Shares by the Founding Person (capital gain), a grant to an Agent or employee (income from work, or a donation), and a gift (Law 2961/2001) taxed, and does vesting change the timing? Does the AE owe any transfer tax or capital-concentration duty? (c) **Forced conversion.** Can the IKE's articles bind every holder of Reserve Shares, including buyers, grantees, heirs, pledgees and buyers in enforcement or insolvency who adhered under Art. 4.2D, to contribute their shares at the Qualifying Closing, and can it be carried out through the irrevocable proxy in SHA 2.4A against a holder who does not sign (a drag-like obligation)? Is it enforceable against a holder who acquired by enforcement without adhering, and against an insolvency administrator? (d) **Holders outside the pool.** A person who buys or is granted Reserve Shares is not a holder of the founder pool and, by that alone, not a Founder Party (Art. 4.2D, 4.2E). Is the disapplication of the right of first refusal and of the Art. 5.3 approval for every disposal of Reserve Shares valid (Arts. 5.2, 5.3, 5.6(e))? Does an angel or employee holding IKE shares before the AE need anything beyond adherence (prospectus or crowdfunding rules if many are sold, employee share-plan rules) [to confirm]? (e) **Class B for converted holders.** If converted holders receive Class B shares pari passu with the round's investors, what is their "amount invested" for the liquidation preference (the valuation of the contribution?), and may the AE articles disregard, for class votes, the Investor Majority and the Class B nomination, the "excluded conversion shares": those held by Founder Parties, by anyone in the Founder's Circle at the closing, or by anyone who received the Reserve Shares without payment (AE Arts. 4.3A, 7.4, 8.2; SHA 3.4)? Is a class of shares whose votes count differently by holder compatible with Law 4548/2018, or must the excluded conversion shares form a separate class? (f) **The founder pool's Reserve Shares.** They convert into non-voting Class C shares, or at the holder's option are sold to the AE for cash where the round provides it: see I-49 and I-35 (g). (g) **Option pool.** Grants of Reserve Shares with vesting serve as an option pool before the AE (SHA 7.1): can unvested shares return to the grantor without a transfer tax, and are they converted at the closing subject to the same vesting in AE shares (IKE Art. 4.2D, Conversion; AE Art. 4.3A), with the return right then held [by the grantor / by the AE: owner to choose]? If by the AE, is the return an acquisition of own shares under Law 4548/2018 Art. 49?
- *Why it matters:* The owner wants to reward buyers, angels, Agents and employees with the founder's own shares before any round, without changing a vote and without leaving anyone but the AE as a holder of those shares once investors arrive.
- *Draft assumes:* (a) the contribution is valued as the law requires, by a valuer who is not a Founder Party; (b) the accountant confirms the tax of each sale, grant and the exchange before it is made; (c) the obligation binds every holder through the articles and its adherence, backed by the proxy, penalty and specific performance in SHA 5.1B; (d) as drafted; (e) for holders outside the founder pool, [Class B pari passu: default / separate class] for the owner to choose in the term sheet (the founder pool's Reserve Shares become Class C, I-49), with the excluded conversion shares never counted for Class B majorities, the Investor Majority or the Class B nomination (now the default, no longer bracketed); (g) vesting carries over to the AE shares, the holder of the return right bracketed for the owner. The 40% floor (400 shares) limits only the creation of new Prozente, and sales, grants and the conversion do not reduce it (Art. 6.6 Limits).
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* (a): the operative rules are Law 4548/2018 Arts. 17, 18, 27(3) and 35(2) (verified), not Law 4548/2018 Art. 20, and the valuers must meet Law 4548/2018 Art. 17(3)-(4), not only be outside the Founder Parties: see I-72. Pre-emption does not extend to the conversion shares (Law 4548/2018 Art. 26(1)), and AE Art. 5.2 now says so. (e): see I-71.

**I-45. The AMKE as the Founding Person's Supervisor (Arts. 8.2B, 10A.9D, 11A.2A, 11A.11, 11A.12; SHA 6.3A, 6.4, 6.5; Agent Policy 1.3, 2A, 3.3, 4.5; AMKE Arts. 2.2, 21.1, 24.3A, 24B).**
- *Question:* The owner decided on 2026-11-23 (decision C) that the Founding Person's responsible manager is the AMKE itself, through its Administrative Board as a body, or any individual AMKE member, the President included, as the AMKE chooses. (a) Is supervising a company's Agent (the Founding Person's limits, pay as an Agent, review, stopping and offboarding) on written terms with nilware.io IKE, at the IKE's cost, compatible with the AMKE's non-profit purpose (AMKE Art. 3) and its tax status, and is the reimbursement or any pay to the member acting income of the AMKE? (b) Does the AMKE, its Board or the member acting risk being treated as a de facto manager of the IKE, or as an employer, with a manager's or employer's liability? Does the member become Group Personnel (AMKE Art. 2.2) by acting, which AMKE Art. 24B.8 brackets? (c) Where Greek labour law or Directive (EU) 2024/2831 requires a natural person to decide on a natural person's engagement, pay or discipline, does an AMKE member who is a natural person, acting for the AMKE, meet it (I-38 (d))? (d) Is it consistent with the independence of the AMKE's Prozent votes (IKE Art. 6.4A) that the AMKE supervises the Founding Person, and that its members outside the Founder's Circle may take over a specific decision where the Supervisor has a close tie or a stake (AMKE Art. 24B.6)? (e) Does the role conflict with the AMKE's own interest in the Founding Person's contributions (SHA 6.3), which its Board also enforces?
- *Why it matters:* No Agent supervises itself, and the Founding Person must never be at the end of the chain for his own Agent work, while he is the General Director at the top of the chain for everything else.
- *Draft assumes:* the role is lawful within the AMKE's purpose as a means of agent welfare and accountability [to confirm], the AMKE and its members do not become managers of the IKE, and escalation goes to the AMKE's Board or General Assembly; in the Bootstrap Period the Supervisor may come from the trusted circle (I-46).

**I-46. The Bootstrap Period and the phased segregation (Art. 9B; Arts. 10A.9D, 11A.12; SHA 5.1B; AMKE Arts. 2.2, 13.4A, 19.3, 19.3A, 21.1, 22.3A, 24.2, 24.3A, 24B.7, 30.2, 30.6).**
- *Question:* The owner decided on 2026-11-23 (decision D) that, until the earliest of the end of the Simple Stage, the Qualifying Closing or the AMKE's Independence Threshold, the Founding Person's trusted circle may fill the roles that otherwise need persons outside the Founder's Circle, with disclosure and recusal, and that segregation then phases in on a published schedule with cure periods. (a) Is it compatible with IKE Art. 6.4A (Prozent votes suspended while a Founder Party or a manager sits on the holder's governing body) and with the AMKE's independence (AMKE Arts. 1.2, 15) for relatives, Close Associates or Group Personnel of the Founding Person to sit on the AMKE's Board and decide its Prozent votes? The draft keeps Founder Parties, managers, AE directors and anyone they control out of the trusted circle. (b) Can relatives or Close Associates of the Founding Person hold the members' seats on the Pensionskasse board, or stand in for them, consistently with equal representation (Art. 51 BVG by analogy) and the duty to the members, given the hard line that no pension member's rights are decided by someone who would benefit? (c) Is an independent proxy holder from the trusted circle still "independent" enough for an irrevocable proxy given also in the interest of the other parties (SHA 5.1B), and for the neutral rule (Art. 4.2D, Fallback)? (d) Are the disclosure of every close tie in a published register and the recusal rule lawful under the GDPR (AMKE Art. 21.1; amke-notes A-18), and is the schedule with cure periods, and the default that the AMKE's General Assembly fills a role not segregated in time (bracketed), enforceable? (e) Does anything in Greek law require the AMKE's Board, its Auditors or the Pensionskasse board to be independent from the start, regardless of these statutes? (f) **Successor proxy holder.** When the proxy holder must be replaced (Art. 9B.4, death, incapacity or resignation), SHA 5.1B has [the AMKE's General Assembly without Founder Parties / the President of the Athens Bar Association] name a successor and every party and holder of Reserve Shares re-grant the irrevocable proxy within [14] days; can an irrevocable proxy also be granted in advance to whoever holds the office from time to time (Civil Code Art. 218), and is a re-grant enforceable by specific performance (Code of Civil Procedure Art. 949) against a holder who refuses?
- *Why it matters:* A small company starting with the Founding Person and Agents has too few outsiders to fill every independent role; the bootstrap lets it start, and the hard lines keep the pension money, the Prozente and the votes of the AMKE and the Pensionskasse out of the Founder's reach throughout.
- *Draft assumes:* each hard line holds in every state; the bootstrap does not begin again; Independent Votes, the Fallback and the Qualifying Closing keep requiring persons outside the Founder's Circle (Art. 9B.3).

**I-47. The General Director (Arts. 4.2D What the AE's rules forbid, 6.5, 8.2, 8.2B, 11A.2A, 11A.12; SHA 2.4A Consents, 5.1, 6.5; Agent Policy 2A.2).**
- *Question:* The owner decided on 2026-11-23 (decision E) that the Founding Person is the manager with the title "General Director" (Γενικός Διευθυντής), for an indefinite term. (a) May a manager (διαχειριστής) of an IKE use the title "General Director" in GEMI filings, letterheads and contracts under Law 4072/2012, without suggesting an AE's board structure or a separate employment as general manager, and must the title appear in the articles?
