(b) Pay as General Director is separate from the Founding Person's fees as an Agent: how is a manager's pay treated for tax and EFKA next to the Agent fees (I-43 (a)), and is approval by a majority of the Independent Votes in the Interim Period, and with the Investor Majority's consent afterwards, a valid related-party approval under Law 4072/2012 (bracketed in Arts. 4.2D and 6.5)? (c) Does keeping the two hats apart (Art. 8.2B: the General Director at the top of the chain for the company's work, the AMKE Supervisor deciding the Founding Person's own Agent matters) hold if the managers' duties under Art. 8.3 make the General Director answerable for all supervision in the company?
- *Why it matters:* The Founding Person runs the company, but must not decide his own Agent terms or pay.
- *Draft assumes:* the title is usable [to confirm], removal follows Art. 8.1 unchanged, both pays are related-party matters at arm's length, and the written terms say which work is paid as General Director and which as an Agent.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* on the majority for either pay and the count on discharge, see I-51.

**I-48. Bootstrap hard lines on the Founding Person's own matters (Arts. 9B.2, 9B.3, 10A.9A Insured salary, 10A.9D, 11A.2A No cycles, 11A.12 Pay, Supervisor, Conflicts; Agent Policy 2A.3; AMKE Arts. 13.4A, 15.3, 15.5, 21.3, 24B.6, 24B.7, 30.6).**
- *Question:* (a) Is a "personal stake" limited to the person's own interest or that of their household or an entity they control (Art. 9B.2; AMKE Art. 30.6), so that a disclosed tie to the Founding Person is not by itself a conflict, a sufficient standard of conflict under the AMKE law and for the related-party approvals of the Founding Person's pay (Art. 6.5; Law 4072/2012) [to confirm]? (b) Is the bar on Connected Persons (spouse, registered partner, relatives up to the [second] degree) acting as Supervisor or deciding or voting on the Founding Person's pay, terms or offboarding, including through the AMKE's and the Pensionskasse's Independent Votes, workable where the AMKE's Board is small, and does the fallback to the Board without the conflicted person or to the General Assembly without Founder Parties (AMKE Art. 24B.6) satisfy the AMKE law's rules on quorum and on members' votes? (c) Can a Founder Party's insured salary in the Pensionskasse be capped at an arm's-length rate confirmed by the accountant or an outside adviser (Art. 9B.3) without breaching the equal treatment of members or the group plan's terms? (d) The default bars Connected Persons serving in the bootstrap from deciding the AMKE's nilware.io votes (AMKE Art. 15.3, bracketed for the owner): if the AMKE's Board then has no one who may decide, does Art. 6.4A suspend its Prozent votes, and does the Fallback apply?
- *Why it matters:* The bootstrap lets the Founding Person's trusted circle fill independent roles; without these lines his Supervisor or the people approving his pay could be people he supervises, pays or is related to, and inflated pay would raise his pension credits out of the shared 1%.
- *Draft assumes:* each line holds in the Bootstrap Period too; Group Personnel never decide under AMKE Art. 24B; the arm's-length confirmation in Art. 11A.12 (Pay) comes from outside the Founder's Circle.

**I-49. Non-voting Class C shares for the founder pool's Reserve Shares (Art. 4.2D Conversion; AE Arts. 1.2A, 4.3A, 4.3B, 6.1, 7.4, 7.5, 7.6, 8.2, 10.4, 11.2; SHA 2.4A Conversion, 3.1A, 3.2, 3.4, 4.3, Schedule 1; Pool Constitution Art. 5.6).**
- *Question:* The owner decided on 2026-11-23 (decision F) that Reserve Shares held by the founder pool at the Qualifying Closing convert into a non-voting class of AE shares (Class C) with full economic rights, or at the holder's option are sold to the AE for cash where the round provides it. (a) **A non-voting class in an AE.** Law 4548/2018 allows shares without voting rights only as preference shares (Law 4548/2018 Art. 38) [to confirm]: can Class C be a class of preference shares without votes whose rights are defined on their own terms, whether or not other conversion shares are issued, as [ordinary shares ranking after the Class B priorities / the shares issued to converted holders outside the founder pool, Class B priorities included where those are Class B: owner to choose] (AE Arts. 4.3B, 10.4, 11.2), so that it has no "preference" at all in the first option, or must it carry a distinct priority, and is there a cap on the proportion of non-voting shares in the capital? (b) **Votes the law keeps.** Which votes of the Class C holders cannot be excluded: a separate meeting of the class on a resolution that harms its rights (Law 4548/2018 Art. 38 and the class-meeting rule [Law 4548/2018 Art. 72: to confirm]), and any others? Can the articles exclude Class C from the quorum and majority of every General Meeting resolution, the AE Art. 7.5 two-thirds and the AE Art. 7.6 unanimity included (counted on the shares that carry votes), and from the minimum that makes a closing the Qualifying Closing? (c) **Conversion into Class B on transfer.** Can the AE articles provide that a Class C share converts one for one into a Class B share at the request of a buyer outside the Founder's Circle who is a "qualifying buyer": one that paid at least [the round price / fair value as an independent valuer determines], is not a nominee of a Founder Party, has no agreement or understanding with a Founder Party on voting, proxies, reacquisition or options over the share, and was not financed by a Founder Party, and that certifies this to the Board on request (AE Art. 4.3B, SHA 3.4), without a General Meeting resolution each time: is that an amendment of the articles, a capital-structure change that needs a class meeting of Class B (whose class grows), or an automatic conversion authorised in advance [to confirm]? Does a converted share take the Class B liquidation preference "up to the amount invested", and what is that amount for a share first issued for a contribution in kind (see I-44 (e))? Can the articles test the buyer's price, nominee status, side agreements and financing in this way, rely on its certificate, and treat a share whose holder later fails the test as an excluded conversion share? Is a rule that a converted share counts for Class B votes only while held by a qualifying buyer outside the Founder's Circle compatible with Law 4548/2018, or does it need a separate class (as I-44 (e) asks for the excluded conversion shares)? (d) **Free transfer.** Can Arts. 6.1 and 6.2 (pre-emption on transfer, Board approval) be disapplied for Class C shares only? (e) **Tax.** Does issuing Class C rather than Class B to the founder pool change the tax of the exchange (I-44 (b)), and is the conversion of Class C into Class B a taxable event for the buyer or the AE? (f) **Cash option.** Can the cash price be limited to what the round's terms provide, with elections cut back in proportion and the rest converted into Class C (SHA 3.2), so that the AE never owes a deferred balance (I-35 (g))? (g) **Subscriptions below the minimum.** Can Class B subscriptions at a closing be made conditional on the Qualifying Closing minimum being reached at that closing (counted [after / before: owner to choose] deducting any cash price for Reserve Shares), with the money in escrow and refunded if it is not reached by [date] (SHA 3.1A, AE Art. 1.2A), given that an AE formed for the round needs paid-up capital of at least €25,000 at formation?
- *Why it matters:* If investors buy only part of the round, the founder pool keeps the value of the rest of the Reserve Shares without gaining votes in the AE that could outvote the investors on their protections (the Reserved Matters, the Investor Majority, the Class B nomination). That only works if the class is lawful and truly non-voting, and if conversion into Class B in a buyer's hands cannot be used to bring the pool's stake back into the investor vote.
- *Draft assumes:* (a) Class C is a class of preference shares without votes under Law 4548/2018 Art. 38 that ranks economically with the other conversion shares [to confirm]; (b) Class C holders vote only where the law gives them a vote that cannot be excluded; (c) conversion into [Class B: owner to confirm] on the request of a qualifying buyer outside the Founder's Circle (price, no nominee, no side agreement, no Founder Party financing, certified on request), counted for Class B votes only while so held, with no anti-dilution adjustment (SHA 4.3); (d) as drafted; (e) the accountant confirms; (f) as drafted; (g) conditional subscriptions in escrow, refunded if the minimum is not reached by [date], with the alternative bracketed for the owner. Class C economic rights are defined on their own terms and never rank ahead of the Investors' Class B priorities; the IKE, the SHA and Schedule 1 now state that Class C never votes other than in any separate class vote the law requires on a resolution that harms its own rights. The round must reach the Qualifying Closing minimum through outside investors alone (SHA 3.1A); otherwise nothing converts and the Reserve Shares stay voteless in the IKE.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* (a): see I-69. (b): the class-meeting rules are Law 4548/2018 Arts. 25(3)-(4), 38(8) and 124(1) (verified), not Law 4548/2018 Art. 72: see I-75. (c): see I-71. (f): see I-70 on Law 4548/2018 Art. 19. (g): Law 4548/2018 Arts. 20(2)-(3) and 28(1) are verified; SHA 3.1A and AE Art. 1.2A now pay the money into the AE's special capital account and fit the long-stop within the payment period.

**I-50. Voting the Reserve Shares on a new Prozent, and the voting undertaking in every case (Art. 4.2D Votes, New Prozente; SHA 2.4A Reserve Shares, Deadlock, 5.1B).**
- *Question:* Law 4072/2012 Arts. 68(2)(a) and 72(5) count the two-thirds for an amendment of the articles on the total number of shares (verified on the consolidated text as amended up to Law 4541/2018; later amendments not checked). In the Interim Period only 560 of the 1,000 shares vote, so an amendment creating a new Prozent can pass only if the Reserve Shares are voted. The draft now lets the independent proxy holder cast the votes of the Reserve Shares for that amendment, once the Prozent is approved under Art. 4.2D (New Prozente), as it already does for a Required Matter and for the change of name (Art. 1.1A). (a) Can the proxy holder validly cast, for these purposes only, votes of shares the articles make voteless [Civil Code Art. 218: to confirm]? (b) Each holder's undertaking not to vote the Reserve Shares other than through the proxy holder now applies in every case, not only if the class without votes is invalid (SHA 2.4A Reserve Shares). Is such an unconditional undertaking, with the penalty and specific performance of SHA 5.1B, enforceable against buyers, grantees and heirs who adhere? (c) Has any amendment after Law 4541/2018 changed Law 4072/2012 Art. 72(5)?
- *Why it matters:* Without the proxy holder's votes no new Prozent can be created before the AE exists. And if the class without votes were annulled only later, by a court under Law 4072/2012 Art. 74, an undertaking that applied only on invalidity would have left 440 shares free to vote in the meantime.
- *Draft assumes:* the two-thirds is counted on the total number of shares, which answers I-35 (b); the proxy holder, never a Founder Party, casts the Reserve Shares' votes for the resolution only on a Required Matter, the change of name and an amendment creating a Prozent already approved under Art. 4.2D (New Prozente); the undertaking and irrevocable proxy bind every holder whether or not the class without votes is valid (I-35 (a)).

**I-51. Pay of the General Director and discharge of the Founding Person (Arts. 4.2D Fallback, 8.2B, 11A.12 Pay; SHA 2.4A Consents).**
- *Question:* A manager is unpaid unless the articles or a partners' decision provide otherwise (Law 4072/2012 Art. 64(4)), and a partners' decision needs an absolute majority of the total number of shares (Law 4072/2012 Art. 72(4)), 501 of 1,000. The law bars a partner's vote only on its own discharge, on a special representative against it and on its exclusion (Law 4072/2012 Art. 72(3)); it requires no abstention on pay (all verified). The draft now has the Founder Representative cast the Class F votes on either pay only as a majority of the Independent Votes cast has decided (Interim Period) or with the consent of the Investor Majority (after the Qualifying Closing), under a voting undertaking in SHA 2.4A (Consents); the Founding Person takes no part, as manager, in the management decision on either pay. (a) Is a resolution passed in this way a valid partners' decision under Law 4072/2012 Art. 64(4), and the approval under Law 4072/2012 Art. 95(2)? Is the undertaking that ties the Class F votes to the Independent Votes enforceable? (b) On the Founding Person's own discharge, Law 4072/2012 Art. 72(3) bars the Class F votes. Are the barred shares disregarded in the Law 4072/2012 Art. 72(4) count? The statute says so only for the vote cap in Law 4072/2012 Art. 72(2). If they are not, the proxy holder's 440 votes and the 60 Prozent votes make 500, one short of 501.
- *Why it matters:* As first drafted, the Founding Person abstained from every decision on either pay, so at most 60 votes were cast and no partners' decision on pay could ever reach 501.
- *Draft assumes:* pay is approved as above; discharge is optional (Law 4072/2012 Art. 67(2)), so if the barred shares are not disregarded the Fallback cannot produce the Founding Person's discharge, and none is given in the Interim Period. Adds to I-47 (b).

**I-52. The Prozent contributions and the shares of their holder (Arts. 4.2A, 10A.1 to 10A.3A, 10A.5, 10A.9; SHA 11.0).**
- *Question:* The AMKE is a partner (it holds all 60 Prozent and pool shares). It receives 4% of revenue for the four AMKE Prozente and 1% for the Pensionskasse it holds, every year, in loss years too, with no counter-performance. Only profits shown in the annual accounts may be distributed (Law 4072/2012 Art. 100(3)); extra profit rights need the articles and last at most ten years (Law 4072/2012 Art. 100(4)); a partner repays what it received in breach, hidden payments of profit included, and creditors may claim it by indirect action (Law 4072/2012 Art. 100(5)-(6)) (verified). The draft now says each contribution is the company's own obligation under a written Prozent Agreement with the body that carries the Prozent's purpose, owed to it in that capacity whether or not it holds shares, and is not a distribution, a return of contributions or a right attached to any share (Arts. 10A.3, 10A.3A). (a) Is that enough to keep the payments outside Law 4072/2012 Art. 100(6), given that the articles still give each Prozent's holder shares equal to 1% (Art. 4.2A) and tie permanence to those shares (Art. 10A.5)? (b) Would it be safer for the money to go to a body that is not the shareholder, for example the AMKE keeping the shares and a separate purpose vehicle receiving the contributions? (c) Is a reading as an extra profit right under Law 4072/2012 Art. 100(4), with its ten-year limit, excluded? On characterisation (contract or donation) see I-88; on deductibility, I-67.
- *Why it matters:* If the payments are a hidden distribution, the AMKE must repay them and the company's creditors can claim them; if they are an extra profit right, they end after ten years. Either reading defeats permanence.
- *Draft assumes:* the contributions are owed under the Prozent Agreements as the company's own expenses, with an agreement signed for the four AMKE Prozente before the first payment, as for the Pensionsprozent (Art. 10A.9B); no contribution is paid while the company is in threatened inability to pay its debts (Law 4072/2012 Art. 102) or where payment would frustrate its creditors (Law 4072/2012 Art. 95(3)), the amount staying owed (Art. 10A.3; see I-53).

**I-53. Contributions when the company is in difficulty (Arts. 10A.3, 10A.5B, 10A.7, 10A.9C).**
- *Question:* The outflow contributions are gratuitous for the company. (a) Can contributions paid before a bankruptcy be avoided under Law 4738/2020 (Law 4738/2020 Arts. 117 ff. [to confirm]; headings of Law 4738/2020 Arts. 117 and 118 verified), and what suspect periods apply to gratuitous acts? (b) What is the rank of unpaid contributions in an insolvency: ordinary unsecured claims, or subordinated as gratuitous? (c) Does the part of the Pensionsprozent owed under members' written terms (Art. 10A.9C, Enforceable by members) rank as an employee claim? (d) Is a deferral while the company has ceased payments or is in the situation in Law 4072/2012 Art. 102 (verified), with the amounts accruing and paid in full afterwards, consistent with the permanence rule (Art. 10A.5)?
- *Why it matters:* "Priority" in Art. 10A.3 ranks the contributions only ahead of distributions to partners. Paying them while insolvent could be avoided later and expose the manager to liability.
- *Draft assumes:* the contributions have no priority over creditors; they are deferred, not reduced, while the company cannot pay its debts, and a deferral is not a lowering under Art. 10A.5; the Kassenfonds and the Kassenreserve stay assets of the company available to its creditors (Art. 10A.7).

**I-54. When transfers take effect, and the GEMI filing (Arts. 4.2D, 5.1, 10A.8).**
- *Question:* Law 4072/2012 Art. 84(1) (verified) answers the effect half of I-22: a transfer or encumbrance takes effect towards the company and the partners from the written notice signed by both parties (e-mail allowed), and towards third parties from its entry in the partners' book (Law 4072/2012 Art. 66(1)(a)), not on GEMI registration. What remains: under the current GEMI law (Law 4919/2022), must a change of partners of an IKE also be filed with GEMI, by when, and with what effect [unverified]?
- *Why it matters:* It fixes when a Prozent transfer, a pool trade, a Reserve Share sale or the conversion takes effect, and when the Interim Period ends.
- *Draft assumes:* effect as Law 4072/2012 Art. 84(1) provides; the managers enter a transfer in the partners' book at once once the conditions are met, and file any change the law requires with GEMI within [20] days (Art. 5.1); the Interim Period ends on entry in the partners' book [and any GEMI filing the law requires] (Art. 4.2D).

**I-55. Heirs of founder-pool shares and Reserve Shares (Arts. 4.2D Sales and grants, 5.6, 5.7; Pool Constitution Arts. 5.6, 14.1).**
- *Question:* The articles may restrict transfers and encumbrances between living persons (Law 4072/2012 Art. 84(2)), so registration may depend on adherence for those. On death, the manager must register the heir without delay once the heir's right is checked (Law 4072/2012 Art. 85(1)); the articles may only provide a buy-out at full price by a person the company designates within one month of learning of the death (Law 4072/2012 Art. 85(2)) (verified). The drafts now register heirs as Law 4072/2012 Art. 85(1) requires, bind them by the articles from registration and ask them to adhere, and keep a Law 4072/2012 Art. 85(2) designation only as the backstop for an heir who does not adhere [within [3] months] or is barred by Art. 4.2E. (a) Is a designation that lapses if the heir adheres in time valid under Law 4072/2012 Art. 85(2), given the one-month deadline? (b) Can the articles suspend an heir's votes and distributions until the heir adheres? (c) Is the full-value method set in the articles (a valuer who is not a Founder Party) binding on the heirs? (d) Is the backstop consistent with the owner's rule that founder-pool shares are freely inherited?
- *Why it matters:* As first drafted, the managers would have refused a registration the law requires and could be liable for it. I-40 (b) asked the general question; Law 4072/2012 Art. 85 answers it for death.
- *Draft assumes:* heirs are registered and asked to adhere; voluntary transfers between living persons stay conditional on adherence (Law 4072/2012 Art. 84(2)); the buy-out is a backstop only, for founder-pool shares to holders of founder-pool shares pro rata first.

**I-56. Pre-emption on capital increases (Art. 4.2B; SHA 5.1B).**
- *Question:* Every partner has pre-emption pro rata on a capital increase not in kind; it may be limited or abolished only by a resolution under Law 4072/2012 Art. 72(5) (two-thirds of the total number of shares), and if partners whose percentage falls object, Law 4072/2012 Art. 89(1) applies by analogy (Law 4072/2012 Art. 90(2), verified). Art. 4.2B fixes in advance who receives the new shares (Class F to 50%, each Prozent and the pool to 1%). The draft now requires every increase resolution to limit pre-emption as far as needed for that allocation, and every SHA party undertakes, with the proxy, penalty and specific performance of SHA 5.1B, to vote for it. (a) Is an advance undertaking to vote for the limitation at every increase enforceable? (b) What does the objection rule in Law 4072/2012 Art. 89(1), by analogy, give a partner whose percentage falls? (c) Must the new Prozent and pool shares be paid in full in cash at the increase (Law 4072/2012 Art. 77(4)), and can the AE or another payer pay for them as a donation to the holder (I-26)?
- *Why it matters:* Without the limitation the statutory pro-rata right overrides Art. 4.2B and the Founder's exactly 50%.
- *Draft assumes:* each increase resolution limits pre-emption by the Law 4072/2012 Art. 72(5) majority under the undertaking, and the new Prozent and pool shares are paid in full in cash by the AE or another payer [or out of reserves: I-26].

**I-57. Unanimity for the amendment that creates a Prozent (Arts. 6.6, 10A.5, 10A.5C).**
- *Question:* The majority for an amendment is two-thirds of the total number of shares (Law 4072/2012 Arts. 68(2)(a) and 72(5), verified), which answers the majority half of I-1; Art. 6.6 now says so. Under Law 4072/2012 Art. 72(6), last sentence, introducing a higher majority or unanimity by amendment requires unanimity of the partners. The amendment creating a new Prozent makes the unanimity rule in Art. 10A.5 and the entrenchment in Art. 10A.5C apply to it. Is that "introducing" unanimity, so that the amendment itself needs every partner, or is it enough that Arts. 10A.5 and 10A.5C say, in the initial articles, that they apply to every Prozent, including any later created under Art. 6.6?
- *Why it matters:* If Law 4072/2012 Art. 72(6) applies, a Prozent created by two-thirds alone might not be permanent.
- *Draft assumes:* Arts. 10A.5 and 10A.5C apply, from the initial articles, to every Prozent including later ones, so a later amendment adds a Prozent without introducing a new unanimity rule.

**I-58. Pool purchases and the company's own shares (Arts. 10A.8 Money, 10A.9B, 10A.9C).**
- *Question:* An IKE may not acquire its own shares directly or indirectly, and shares so acquired are cancelled automatically (Law 4072/2012 Art. 87, verified). The company funds the Pensionskasse and bears its shortfalls (Art. 10A.9C). Could a pool purchase be treated as an indirect acquisition for the company's account because of that guarantee, and what must be true of the money for it not to be (paid irrevocably to the holder, no risk or return for the company) [unverified]?
- *Why it matters:* Automatic cancellation would break the 1% band and the Founder's exactly 50%.
- *Draft assumes:* no pool share is bought with money still in the company's own account (Art. 10A.9B, last paragraph) or not yet irrevocably paid to the holder, and the company bears no risk or return of pool shares (Art. 10A.8 Money).

**I-59. One partner voting in blocks (Art. 6.4A).**
- *Question:* All 60 Prozent and pool shares belong to one partner, the AMKE. Each share carries one vote (Law 4072/2012 Art. 72(2), verified), but the statute does not say whether one partner may cast its shares differently on the same resolution. May the AMKE cast the pension shares as the Pensionskasse board decides and the other Prozent shares as its own organs decide, and may the articles suspend the votes of only some of one partner's shares? If not, the AMKE casts one vote per resolution, and its statutes and the Pensionsprozent Agreement must set how the blocks combine.
- *Why it matters:* If split voting is not allowed, the managers' count is open to annulment (Law 4072/2012 Art. 74).
- *Draft assumes:* split voting and per-share suspension are valid. Adds to I-4.

**I-60. The legal designation in the name (Art. 1.1; AE Art. 1.1).**
- *Question:* The name may be wholly or partly in Latin characters, but must contain "Ιδιωτική Κεφαλαιουχική Εταιρεία" in full or "Ι.Κ.Ε.", and "Private Company" or "P.C." for international dealings (Law 4072/2012 Art. 44, verified). Will GEMI register "nilware.io Ι.Κ.Ε." with the lowercase initial and the dot, and may the drafts and day-to-day dealings use "nilware.io IKE" in Latin letters as a short form [GEMI practice: unverified]? The same for the AE's designation under Law 4548/2018.
- *Why it matters:* The owner decided the company is always called nilware.io; the statutory designation is a separate requirement.
- *Draft assumes:* "nilware.io Ι.Κ.Ε." (Ιδιωτική Κεφαλαιουχική Εταιρεία), "nilware.io P.C." for international dealings, and "nilware.io IKE" as the short form in the drafts. Adds to I-32.

**I-61. The Pensionskasse as a financing reserve, not a benefit provider (Arts. 10A.9 Until the move, Uses, 10A.9C, 10A.9F; AMKE Arts. 13.3, 13.4A).**
- *Question:* An IORP is an institution of any legal form, operating on a funded basis, set up separately from the sponsor, that provides retirement benefits under an agreement with employees or the self-employed (Law 4680/2020 Art. 5(1)(α)); it needs approval (Art. 8(1)), and additional own funds where it itself covers biometric risk (Art. 14(1)) (verified on the original text, ΦΕΚ Α' 72/2020; later amendments not checked). The drafts now make the Pensionskasse a reserve that finances the company's pension obligations, the group plan's premiums and the Continuity Account: no member has a claim against the AMKE or the reserve; the AMKE pays no benefit to a member, carries no biometric or investment risk and holds no individual or deferred account; continuity benefits for persons (income support in long illness, transition support) are insured under the group plan or paid by the company; underfunding is measured on the company's guaranteed liabilities against the reserve, the insured cover and the Security together; deferred members, member accounts and the BVG shadow account sit with the insurer, the TEA or the company, not the Pensionskasse. Does a sponsor-financed reserve held by a third-party AMKE, against which members have no claim, fall outside Art. 5(1)(α) and outside the insurance licence requirement (Law 4364/2016 [article: unverified])?
- *Why it matters:* If the AMKE-held fund is an IORP or an insurer, the interim arrangement is unlawful and the owner's phasing fails (I-12).
- *Draft assumes:* as above; counsel confirms before the first payment from the reserve. Extends I-12.

**I-62. Who pays the group plan's premiums (Arts. 10A.6, 10A.9 Until the move, 10A.9B(a), 10A.9C).**
- *Question:* The income-tax exclusion for employer pension and risk funding covers contributions to voluntary TEAs and to the funds in Law 3029/2002 Art. 6(20), and premiums under group pension contracts issued by insurance undertakings (Law 4172/2013 Art. 14(1)(ε), (ι), (ια) as amended by Law 5078/2023 Arts. 106 and 107; the 20%-of-pay cap in Law 5078/2023 Art. 105 as amended by Law 5178/2025 Art. 35; €1,500 per employee for health, life and disability cover under (ια); AADE circular Ε.2107/2025; verified via the circular, paragraph letters for the accountant to confirm). The draft now makes the company the policyholder, paying the premiums and the members' first call directly, counting toward the Pensionsprozent's 1%, with only the remainder paid to the AMKE. (a) Does the exclusion survive if the AMKE is the policyholder instead (the bracketed alternative)? (b) How are premiums for Agents who are persons engaged as contractors treated: part of their gross receipts or not (the circular does not say)? (c) Can the reserve accumulated before the first member be applied toward premiums, by reimbursing the company or paying the insurer on its behalf, without losing the exclusion or the company's deduction? (d) Does donation tax arise on the payments to the AMKE (Law 2961/2001; rate for an AMKE unverified)?
- *Why it matters:* Giving the same euro to the AMKE as a donation and treating it as an employer cost could cost the members the exclusion, the company its deduction, and add donation tax on every payment.
- *Draft assumes:* payments to the insurer are employer contributions, not donations; Art. 10A.9B(a) covers only the shares and payments to the AMKE; the accountant confirms before the first payment. Adds to I-18 and I-37 (a).

**I-63. A pension share the TEA may not hold (Arts. 10A.9E, 10A.10; AMKE Art. 13.5).**
- *Question:* Investment in the sponsoring undertaking may not exceed 5% of the portfolio, and investment in the group's undertakings 10% (Law 4680/2020 Art. 18(1)(ζ), transposing Directive 2016/2341 Art. 19(1)(g); verified on the original text), which answers part of I-19. The draft now has the AMKE keep a pension share the TEA may not hold as its own ring-fenced asset, not as the TEA's nominee: its income is paid to the TEA as an additional contribution on the company's behalf, and it is voted by the board formed under Art. 10A.9D, not on the TEA's instructions. Would a supervisor still attribute this holding to the TEA under Law 4680/2020 Art. 18(1)(ζ)? Has Law 5078/2023 or Law 5327/2026 amended Law 4680/2020 Art. 18(1)(ζ)?
- *Why it matters:* A nominee holding with full pass-through and TEA control would not cure the breach it was meant to avoid.
- *Draft assumes:* the holding is the AMKE's own and is not attributed to the TEA [as far as Law 4680/2020 allows]. Adds to I-19.

**I-64. Continuity costs that also serve the company (Art. 10A.9F Continuity of service; AMKE Arts. 5.1, 13.8).**
- *Question:* Migrating a system Agent's state, or keeping an old version available, when a model or provider changes keeps the IKE's systems serving its clients. Paid from the Continuity Account, which the IKE funded, that would be a benefit to the nilware.io group from an AMKE asset (AMKE Art. 5.1) and a circular payment. The draft now has the company bear the part that serves its business [or reimburse the Account at cost], and the Account pay only for continuity beyond the company's business need (preservation copies, archives and retirement records in the AMKE's own name). (a) Is that allocation enough for the AMKE's non-profit character, and for the deduction or donation treatment of the company's payments [Law 4172/2013: unverified]? (b) How should the split be documented?
- *Why it matters:* A circular payment undermines the AMKE's non-profit status and the tax treatment of the Pensionsprozent.
- *Draft assumes:* as above, decided by the Pensionskasse board with the system-welfare adviser. Adds to I-33.

**I-65. The law under which a TEA is founded (Arts. 10A.9D After the move, 10A.10; AMKE Art. 13.5).**
- *Question:* Law 4680/2020 sets the prudential and governance rules. Founding, sponsoring undertakings and licensing of TEAs are now in Law 5078/2023 Arts. 6 to 8, as amended by Law 5327/2026 (whose Art. 10 replaces Art. 7 of Law 5078/2023; existence and article titles verified, contents not read in full). (a) What are the current Art. 7 and Art. 8 conditions, and is there a minimum membership? (b) Is the professional or sector-affinity requirement gone, as summaries of the 2026 bill say [unverified], so that the company can join a multi-employer TEA early? (c) Can Agents who are persons, engaged as contractors, be members (Law 4680/2020 Art. 5(1)(α)(αβ) allows self-employed members by agreement, verified)?
- *Why it matters:* It decides whether and when the move to a TEA is possible for a company this size.
- *Draft assumes:* the company establishes or joins a TEA under Law 5078/2023, as amended (including by Law 5327/2026), and Law 4680/2020 [to confirm]; the TEA's governing body under the law then in force takes over, with member representation no less than Art. 10A.9D [as far as that law allows]. Adds to I-20.

**I-66. The company's own risk benefits for members who are not employees (Arts. 10A.9 Until the move, 10A.9A, 10A.9C, 11A.7).**
- *Question:* The company promises disability and survivors' pensions and a guaranteed conversion rate to employees and to Agents who are persons engaged as contractors, and self-insures any gap in insured cover. (a) An employer's own promise to its employees is a recognised book-reserve arrangement (Directive 2016/2341 Art. 2(2)). Can the company also carry disability and survivors' risk, and the conversion-rate guarantee, for members who are not employees, as a recurring term of service contracts, without an insurance licence (Law 4364/2016 [article: unverified]), or must these be fully insured? (b) How must the company provide for the guarantee in its accounts (Law 4308/2014 [article: unverified])? (c) Do pension, disability and sick-pay-style benefits for contractors add to the reclassification risk under Art. 11A.7? (d) Any part of the guaranteed liabilities above the Security's [●]% is an unsecured claim against the company: is that adequately disclosed to members?
- *Why it matters:* Unlicensed risk-carrying would make the self-insurance unlawful, and employment-like benefits make reclassification more likely.
- *Draft assumes:* the company self-insures any gap. If counsel advises against it, the suggested default for the owner is that risk benefits owed to members who are not employees are fully insured under the group plan. Extends I-13.

**I-67. Tax deductibility does not cap the Prozente (Arts. 10A.6, 10A.9C).**
- *Question:* The outflow Prozente, and the Pensionsprozent before any member, have no counter-performance. Corporate social responsibility expenses are deductible (Law 4172/2013 Art. 22(α), as replaced by Law 4646/2019 Art. 11) only in a year in which the company shows an accounting profit (AADE circular Ε.2107/2021; both verified from the amending law's heading and the circular, not the consolidated text). (a) Is each outflow Prozent a CSR expense, a donation or a sponsorship, and is it deductible in profit years and in loss years? (b) What is the shortfall the company pays under Art. 10A.9C where the beneficiaries are contractors or there are no employees?
- *Why it matters:* "within the limits of Greek tax ... law" under the heading "Law prevails" could be read as capping each contribution at what is deductible, which would let a manager or a buyer cut the Prozente in any loss year.
- *Draft assumes:* deductibility does not limit any contribution; a contribution that is not deductible in a year is still paid in full, subject only to mandatory company and insolvency law (Arts. 10, 10A.3, 10A.5B); the shortfall is an employment cost where it concerns employees and otherwise a cost of the company (Art. 10A.9C). Extends I-18.

**I-68. Beneficial owners of the IKE and the founder pool (Arts. 4.2D Sales and grants, 5.1, 5.7; SHA 12.3; Pool Constitution Arts. 2A.3, 4.6).**
- *Question:* Identifying the beneficial owners and registering them, and every change, in the Central Register of Beneficial Owners is a mandatory Greek duty (Law 4557/2018 Art. 20, verified as the governing article; the deadline for changes, 60 days after the 2020 amendment, may have changed under Law 5193/2025 Art. 198 [unverified]). The drafts now have the managers count economic interests (Reserve Shares included) and control through the block vote, and require every acquirer and every Holder to give beneficial-owner data on adherence and on any change; the Founder Council tells the managers who directs the block. (a) Whom must the IKE, and later the AE, declare when no Holder exceeds 25% but the block holds 50% under a voting agreement: the Founder Representative, the Founder Council, or the Holders acting in concert? Does control "through other means" (Law 4557/2018 Art. 3 [unverified]) reach the Founder Representative? (b) Is the Founding Person, with about 94% of the economic interest through voteless Reserve Shares, a beneficial owner before the Qualifying Closing? (c) Is the founder pool, or the Founding Person's fiduciary holding of the Brand Assets for it, a legal arrangement similar to a trust that must itself register (Law 4557/2018 Art. 21 [unverified])?
- *Why it matters:* The managers cannot comply without the data, and a wrong declaration is sanctionable.
- *Draft assumes:* the managers declare and update within the period the law sets; the information duties are in Pool Constitution Art. 4.6, the SHA deed of adherence (Clause 12.3) and the IKE Articles. Extends I-40 (d) and I-42 (c).

## articles/02-nilware-holdings-ae.md

**A-1. Class B votes on Prozente (Arts. 4.3, 7.5, 7.6).**
- *Question:* Under Art. 38 of Law 4548/2018, can preference shares carry a vote limited to listed matters (new Prozente, permanence) and count in the quorum base for those matters?
- *Why it matters:* The two-thirds majority is counted across all classes together, so the quorum and the majority depend on Class B counting.
- *Draft assumes:* yes, and the higher minimums of Law 4548/2018, including those for repeat meetings (Law 4548/2018 Art. 130), prevail.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* on anti-dilution through convertible Class B, see I-76.

**A-2. Unanimous General Meeting (Art. 7.6).**
- *Question:* Can AE articles require a resolution "passed by all of the company's shares, of every class", and entrench that clause?
- *Why it matters:* It is the AE half of permanence.
- *Draft assumes:* yes. Unanimity is counted on the whole capital, not on the votes represented.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* see I-73 (the quorum cap in Law 4548/2018 Art. 130(5)) and I-74 (the Board's authority towards the IKE, Law 4548/2018 Art. 86(3)).

**A-3. Free transfer of an IKE share (Arts. 2.1, 7.5).**
- *Question:* Does stating it in the purpose give enough corporate basis for the Board's gratuitous transfer of IKE shares equal to 1% of the IKE's capital to each new Prozent, and for paying for the Prozente's and the pool's top-up shares on an IKE capital increase (IKE Art. 4.2B)? Is the AMKE, or any other Prozent holder, a related party under Arts. 99 to 101 of Law 4548/2018?
- *Why it matters:* A gift by an AE board needs a corporate basis, and related-party rules may require authorisation and disclosure.
- *Draft assumes:* the purpose clause covers it, and the Board authorises under Law 4548/2018 Arts. 99 to 101 where those apply.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* the related-party Reserved Matter now also requires the Board authorisation in Law 4548/2018 Arts. 99 and 100 and excludes the counterparty and related shareholders from the vote and quorum (Law 4548/2018 Art. 100(5)) (AE Art. 7.4).

**A-4. Founder control of the 75% override (Art. 7.5).**
- *Question:* Does requiring a majority of the Class B votes cast for the 75% override protect investors well enough?
- *Why it matters:* The reviewer noted that if Class A holds 75% of the AE votes or more, the Founder alone could otherwise break the limits.
- *Draft assumes:* a Class B majority is required to go beyond the limits. The two-thirds route for ordinary creation is unchanged (owner decision).

**A-5. Forming the AE for the first round (Arts. 1.2A, 4.3A).**
- *Question:* Can an AE formed later be bound by decisions taken under IKE Art. 4.2D before it existed, by every sale and grant of Reserve Shares made under it, and by the SHA through a deed of adherence? Can its articles require it to receive every Reserve Share at the Qualifying Closing as a contribution in kind for conversion shares at a ratio fixed in its articles at formation (AE Art. 4.3A), issuing non-voting Class C shares for those held by the founder pool, or, at the holder's option, buying them for cash only out of the round's proceeds (I-49)?
- *Why it matters:* The interim rules only work if the AE inherits them.
- *Draft assumes:* yes, through the deed of adherence and the AE's articles (see I-35 (f), I-44).
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* see I-70 (Law 4548/2018 Art. 19 on a cash purchase in the first two years) and I-72 (valuation of the contribution).

**I-69. Class C must carry a real preference (AE Arts. 4.3B, 10.4, 11.2; SHA 2.4A Conversion, 4.3, Schedule 1).**
- *Question:* Every AE share votes except preference shares issued without votes (Law 4548/2018 Arts. 36(1), 38(4)); common shares always vote (Law 4548/2018 Art. 37(3)); a preference means taking the dividend and/or the return of paid-in capital, share premium included, before the common shares (Law 4548/2018 Art. 38(1)); Law 4548/2018 Art. 38 sets no cap on the proportion of non-voting preference shares (all verified, no amendment since 13.06.2018). This answers I-49 (a): a Class C that only takes part pro rata after Class B has no priority over Class A, so it would be a common share and would vote. The first bracketed option for Class C now gives a priority return of the capital and share premium paid up on Class C before the Class A shares [and a non-cumulative priority dividend of [●]%], ranking after the Class B priorities; the second option is unchanged. What remains: is a small priority, such as the return of paid-up capital only, enough in doctrine to make Class C a preference share [unverified]?
- *Why it matters:* If Class C were a common share it would vote and count for quorum, which defeats decision F.
- *Draft assumes:* Class C are preference shares without voting rights under Law 4548/2018 Art. 38(1) and (4), with the owner's two options kept in brackets.

**I-70. Buying Reserve Shares for cash in the AE's first two years (AE Art. 1.2A; SHA 3.2).**
- *Question:* In the first two years after formation, an AE's acquisition of an asset for more than 1/10 of paid-up capital from founders, shareholders holding over 1/20, board members, their close family or companies they control is absolutely void unless the General Meeting approves it beforehand and the asset is valued under Law 4548/2018 Arts. 17 and 18, with the report published (Law 4548/2018 Art. 19(1), (2), (4); outside the related-party regime, Law 4548/2018 Art. 99(3)(g); verified). The draft now requires a prior GM resolution and a Law 4548/2018 Art. 17/18 valuation for any cash purchase of Reserve Shares within two years of formation, the price never exceeding the valuation, any excess cut back under SHA 3.2. (a) Law 4548/2018 Art. 19 is silent on whether the selling shareholder may vote on the approval (the exclusion in Law 4548/2018 Art. 100(5) applies only to related-party transactions): should the AE Articles exclude the sellers' votes? (b) Can the same valuers do the Law 4548/2018 Art. 17 valuation of the conversion shares and the Law 4548/2018 Art. 19 valuation in one report?
- *Why it matters:* Without the approval and valuation the purchase is void, anyone with a legal interest may invoke it, and the AE has a restitution claim.
- *Draft assumes:* the approval is on the agenda of the GM that resolves the round's increase. Adds to A-5 and I-49 (f).

**I-71. Class B shares whose votes depend on the holder (AE Arts. 4.3A, 4.3B, 7.4, 8.2; SHA 3.4).**
- *Question:* Equality applies to all shares of the same class, and preference shares of the same issue series confer the same rights (Law 4548/2018 Arts. 36(1)-(2), 38(2), verified). This answers I-44 (e) and the last question of I-49 (c): a Class B share cannot weigh differently in the class vote or the board seat depending on who holds it. The draft now uses a separate class, Class B-X, with Class B's economic rights and general votes but no part in the separate Class B majority or the Class B board seat, and mandatory conversions under Law 4548/2018 Art. 38(3): excluded conversion shares are issued as Class B-X; a Class B share converts into Class B-X when a Founder Party (or a person who was one in the previous [3] years) acquires it, or when its holder ceases to be a qualifying buyer or fails to certify on request; a Class C share converts into Class B at a qualifying buyer's written request, and into Class B-X if the buyer later fails the test; each conversion takes effect when the Board records the triggering fact. (a) Is a mandatory conversion triggered by a change of holder, or by the holder's status, a lawful "term of conversion" under Law 4548/2018 Art. 38(3) [unverified]? (b) Does each conversion need a separate meeting of a class whose rights it affects (Law 4548/2018 Arts. 25(3), 38(8))?
- *Why it matters:* If the holder-based exclusions fell, the founder side's Class B votes would count in the class vote, which defeats the owner's intent.
- *Draft assumes:* Class B-X with mandatory conversion; the contractual Investor Majority test in SHA 3.4 stays as a contract term.

**I-72. Valuing the contribution of Reserve Shares (AE Art. 4.3A).**
- *Question:* An in-kind contribution to an AE is valued by two certified auditors or an audit firm, or two independent certified valuers, who meet the independence test in Law 4548/2018 Art. 17(4), no more than six months before the contribution (Law 4548/2018 Art. 17(3), (4), (9)), and in a mixed cash and in-kind increase before the resolution (Law 4548/2018 Art. 27(3)); it may not be counted above its valuation (Law 4548/2018 Art. 17(7)), and no share is issued below par (Law 4548/2018 Art. 35(2)); the exemption in Law 4548/2018 Art. 18(1) covers only securities on a regulated market (all verified). This answers I-44 (a): the Conversion Ratio must give way to the valuation. The draft now makes the ratio a maximum, reduced (share premium first, then the ratio, the same for every Reserve Share and every holder) if the valuation falls short. (a) Can Law 4548/2018 Art. 18(2) (fair value already set by a recognised independent expert) be used instead, for example on the round's pricing? (b) Can the valuation rest on the round price?
- *Why it matters:* A fixed ratio above the valuation would stall the closing or make it defective.
- *Draft assumes:* valuers meeting Law 4548/2018 Art. 17(3)-(4) [or Law 4548/2018 Art. 18(2), if counsel advises]; the ratio as a maximum.

**I-73. The quorum for the unanimous General Meeting (AE Art. 7.6; SHA 5.1C).**
- *Question:* The quorum the articles may set for Law 4548/2018 Art. 130(1) matters may not exceed two-thirds of the paid-up capital (Law 4548/2018 Art. 130(5)); authorising the Board to vote at the IKE on the Art. 7.6 matters, and an ordinary amendment of the articles, are not on the closed list in Law 4548/2018 Art. 130(3); the articles may raise majorities (Law 4548/2018 Art. 132(3)) (verified). A resolution "passed by all of the company's shares that carry votes", counted on the whole capital, is in effect a 100% quorum. The draft now requires (a) a resolution passed unanimously by the votes represented at a meeting where at least two-thirds of the paid-up voting capital is present or represented, and (b) the written consent of every holder of voting shares delivered to the Board before it votes, as a limit on the Board's authority; the same for amending Art. 7.6. Is limb (b) lawful outside the GM quorum rules, or is it a disguised quorum?
- *Why it matters:* An excessive quorum may be read down or held void, which would weaken the AE half of permanence (A-2).
- *Draft assumes:* the two limbs as above, with SHA 5.1C carrying limb (b) by contract. Adds to A-2.

**I-74. The AE Board voting at the IKE in breach of its limits (AE Arts. 7.6, 9.2; IKE Arts. 6.6, 10A.5).**
- *Question:* Limits on an AE Board's authority set by the articles or by a GM resolution cannot be relied on against third parties, even if published (Law 4548/2018 Art. 86(3), verified). An IKE vote the AE Board casts in breach of AE Arts. 7.6 or 9.2 is therefore probably valid at the IKE, leaving only director liability [Law 4548/2018 Arts. 96 and 102: unverified], injunctions and the SHA undertakings. (a) Should the IKE Articles (IKE Arts. 6.6, 10A.5) make the AE's vote on these matters ineffective unless the AE delivers a certified copy of the required AE GM resolution (and, for AE Art. 7.6, the written consents)? (b) Should the SHA give the independent proxy holder an irrevocable proxy over the AE's IKE votes on these matters only?
- *Why it matters:* The pass-through of the investor protections and of permanence to the IKE rests on that internal limit, so the block may need to sit on the IKE side.
- *Draft assumes:* the internal limit as drafted; the owner's rule is unchanged. Adds to A-2.

**I-75. Votes the law keeps for non-voting classes (AE Arts. 4.3B No votes, 7.4, 7.6; SHA 2.4A Conversion).**
- *Question:* The class votes are confirmed (Law 4548/2018, verified): a separate meeting of the preference holders concerned, deciding by two-thirds of the preference capital represented, on abolishing or limiting their preference (Law 4548/2018 Art. 38(8)); approval in a separate meeting of each class whose rights are affected by a capital increase or an authorisation of one (Law 4548/2018 Art. 25(3)-(4)); holders of non-voting shares may attend General Meetings but do not count for quorum (Law 4548/2018 Art. 124(1)). Law 4548/2018 Art. 72, cited in I-49 (b), is not the class-meeting rule. These votes give the Class C holders, the founder pool, a statutory blocking vote on later senior rounds. The draft adds [owner to confirm] an undertaking by each Class C holder to vote in any such separate meeting for a resolution that has the Art. 7.4 approval, Class B majority included. Is that undertaking enforceable (penalty, specific performance), and does it bind a buyer of Class C?
- *Why it matters:* Without it the founder pool could use the statutory class vote against the investors on their protections.
- *Draft assumes:* Class C votes only in the separate class meetings the law requires; the Art. 7.4 capital-increase item adds those approvals; the undertaking is bracketed. Narrows I-49 (b).

**I-76. Anti-dilution for Class B (AE Art. 5.3 and the Class B conversion terms; SHA 4.2).**
- *Question:* An AE cannot simply re-rate existing shares: no issue below par (Law 4548/2018 Art. 35(2)), equality within a class (Law 4548/2018 Art. 36(1)), pre-emption and its exclusion on any new cash issue (Law 4548/2018 Arts. 26 and 27); preference shares may be convertible on terms set in the articles (Law 4548/2018 Art. 38(3)) (verified). SHA 4.2 had no formula. The draft now makes Class B convertible preference shares, converting into ordinary voting shares of a new class [O] (not Class A, which is the founder pool's), with a broad-based weighted-average adjustment written out in the AE Articles. (a) Can an issue below the Class B price by the IKE, a different company, lawfully trigger an adjustment of the AE Class B conversion ratio [unverified]? (b) Is a compensatory issue to Class B only compatible with Law 4548/2018 Arts. 26, 27 and 36?
- *Why it matters:* As drafted before, the investors' anti-dilution protection was unenforceable at corporate level.
- *Draft assumes:* the formula in the AE Articles; for an IKE issue, [an adjustment on the AE's look-through value / a compensatory issue: owner to choose]. Adds to A-1.

**I-77. The AE's minimum dividend (AE Art. 10.4).**
- *Question:* An AE must pay a minimum dividend of 35% of net profits, after the deductions Law 4548/2018 Art. 161(1) lists, in cash, unless the General Meeting reduces or waives it with an increased quorum and majority (rule verified from secondary sources; quorum and majorities unverified). (a) Which quorum and majorities apply? (b) Does a waiver need a separate vote of Class B, or of Class C, as a class (Law 4548/2018 Arts. 25(3), 38(8))? (c) How does it interact with the Class B dividend priority?
- *Why it matters:* The AE is expected to keep and reinvest its income (SHA 3.2); without a waiver each year it must pay out at least 35%.
- *Draft assumes:* the minimum dividend applies unless the General Meeting reduces or waives it as Law 4548/2018 Art. 161 allows (Art. 10.4).

**I-78. How Class A secures the Board majority (AE Arts. 8.1, 8.2; Pool Constitution Arts. 2.1, 7.3; SHA 5.1F).**
- *Question:* The articles may give named shareholders a right to appoint directors directly only up to two-fifths of the Board, and the appointing holders then do not vote on the other seats (Law 4548/2018 Art. 79(1)-(2)); otherwise the General Meeting elects the Board (Law 4548/2018 Art. 78(1)), and a "nomination" that binds it has no basis (verified). The drafts now give Class B a Law 4548/2018 Art. 79 appointment right for [1] seat, and have Class A, as one block through the Founder Representative, propose candidates for a majority of the Board, with every shareholder party to the SHA undertaking to vote for them [or Law 4548/2018 Art. 79 appointment of two-fifths plus the undertaking for the rest: owner to choose]. (a) Can the undertaking secure the majority, given Law 4548/2018 Art. 79(2) and Law 4548/2018 Art. 80 (no list elections where an appointment right exists)? (b) Can the Class B joint act be signed by holders of a majority of the Class B shares for all of them (Law 4548/2018 Art. 79(5))? (c) Since Class B votes only on listed matters and Class C never votes, Class A in practice elects every seat other than the Class B seat: is the undertaking then only a backstop?
- *Why it matters:* The owner decided that the Founder, as Class A holder, nominates the AE Board majority; that must rest on a lawful mechanism.
- *Draft assumes:* the Class A majority comes from the vote, backed by the undertaking, not from a nomination right. Adds to I-40 (a).

## agreements/03-shareholders-agreement.md

**S-1. Enforcing the voting undertaking (Clause 5.1B).**
- *Question:* Is an irrevocable proxy given also in the interest of the other parties (Civil Code Art. 218, to confirm) valid for this purpose? Is a penalty under Civil Code Art. 404, with specific performance by a judgment that stands in for the vote (Code of Civil Procedure Art. 949), the right enforcement pair?
- *Why it matters:* A vote cast against the undertaking is still valid at the meeting. The remedies come afterwards.
- *Draft assumes:* the proxy is given to an independent proxy holder named in Clause 5.1B ([●], a notary or lawyer who is not the Founder, a manager or anyone they control), not to the IKE managers, because the first manager is the Founding Person and the pension shares must vote independently of every Founder Party (IKE Arts. 4.2E, 6.4A, 10A.9D). Can such a proxy, covering the pension shares, be reconciled with the Pensionskasse board's control of their vote? The alternative is to exclude the pension shares from the proxy and have the board's chair hold it.

**S-2. The AMKE as a party (header, Clause 5.1D).**
- *Question:* Can the AMKE bind the pension-share votes by contract when, under the Pensionsprozent Agreement, those votes follow the Pensionskasse board, and the board's duty runs to the members?
- *Why it matters:* The owner wants every IKE shareholder bound. The board's duty to the members may prevail.
- *Draft assumes:* the board's decisions are subject to the undertakings as far as mandatory pension law allows. After the AE is formed and holds the Reserve Shares, the Founder and the AE (94%) carry any amendment; before it, nothing needing two-thirds of the capital can pass (I-35(b)).

**S-3. Entrenchment inside the SHA (Clause 5.1D).**
- *Question:* Does "despite Clause 12.2, amendable only with the written consent of every party" hold?
- *Why it matters:* It mirrors the entrenchment in the articles.
- *Draft assumes:* yes.

**S-4. Option pool (Clause 7.1).**
- *Question:* Options are now satisfied only from shares transferred by the AE, or, before the AE, by grants of Reserve Shares from their holders' own holdings (I-44 (g)). Transfers by the AE lower its holding. How should this interact with the 40% floor (400 shares) for new Prozente in IKE Art. 6.6?
- *Why it matters:* An option pool of [10]% would take the AE from 44% (440 shares) to 34% (340), below the floor, which as drafted applies only when a Prozent is created.
- *Draft assumes:* the floor is tested only on creating a Prozent. **Owner to decide** whether shares transferred under the option pool count toward the floor.

**S-5. Leaver terms without a transfer (Clauses 6.1, 6.2).**
- *Question:* See I-7. Is it enforceable for the Founder to keep title to all shares while assigning distributions and granting an irrevocable voting proxy on unvested shares, and how is the leaver payment for the rights given up characterised for tax?
- *Why it matters:* It is the only way to keep vesting while the Founder stays at exactly 50%.
- *Draft assumes:* yes, as far as Law 4072/2012 allows. The recipients and the person directing the votes are bracketed for the owner.
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* see I-79 (an assignment of distributions as the default, and the ten-year limit in Law 4072/2012 Art. 100(4)).

**S-6. The Agreement before the AE and the Investors (Clause 2.4A).**
- *Question:* Can the Agreement be signed by the Founder, the IKE and the AMKE alone, with the AE and the Investors adhering later, and can its amendment clause be the Founder and the AMKE until then? Is the AMKE, as a non-profit, a suitable party to consent to the pay and benefits of the Founding Person and other Founder Parties in the interim?
- *Why it matters:* Otherwise the Founder alone could amend the Agreement before any investor exists.
- *Draft assumes:* yes; Clauses 5.1C and 5.1D still need every party.

**I-79. Distributions on a bad leaver's Reserve Shares (Clauses 2.4A Leaver terms, 6.1, 6.2A; IKE Art. 4.2D Economic rights).**
- *Question:* Profit share follows the shares, and only the articles may limit a partner's participation, for at most ten years (Law 4072/2012 Art. 100(4), verified). A declared dividend is the holder's claim against the company; a segregated account at the IKE "for the eventual buyer" gives that buyer no title, and "distributions due to the AE" before the AE exists have no lawful recipient. The draft now makes the default a conditional assignment of future distribution claims on the affected shares, granted on signing or adhering, to [an escrow agent / the independent proxy holder as escrow holder] for the eventual buyer, paid by the IKE on notice, binding anyone who receives the shares without payment; [if the owner prefers the IKE to hold the money, an IKE article under Law 4072/2012 Art. 100(4) excludes those shares from profits for at most ten years]. (a) Is the conditional assignment effective against the holder's creditors and heirs, and against a donee who never signed? (b) Is the escrow holder a suitable assignee? (c) Under the alternative, what happens after ten years?
- *Why it matters:* As first drafted, a bad leaver or a donee could claim the withheld dividends from the company.
- *Draft assumes:* the assignment is the default. Adds to I-7, I-35 (h) and S-5.

**I-80. Funding the IKE by capital or by a shareholder loan (Clause 3.2).**
- *Question:* Compare a shareholder loan from the AE to the IKE with a capital increase as to stamp duty on loans between non-bank parties and any exemption for shareholder loans [unverified], the deductibility of interest (Law 4172/2013 Art. 23(α)) and arm's-length pricing between connected persons (Law 4172/2013 Art. 50) [both cited, not re-checked].
- *Why it matters:* Clause 3.2 leaves the choice to the AE Board with Reserved Matters approval, with no tax check.
- *Draft assumes:* the AE Board decides [after the company's accountant has confirmed the tax treatment of each in writing: owner option].

## agreements/04-ip-assignment.md

**P-1. The bare name without ".io" (Clause 2.2; Brand Licence Clause 2.1).**
- *Question:* The name "nilware.io" is no longer assigned to the IKE: it is a Brand Asset of the founder pool, licensed to the IKE (I-42). Should the Founding Person also bring any rights in the bare name without ".io" (as used historically in the charter poem 00b) into the founder pool's Brand Assets, so that no one else can claim it?
- *Why it matters:* The company is always called "nilware.io", but an earlier use of the bare word could otherwise stay with the Founding Person personally.
- *Draft assumes:* "nilware.io" only. **Owner to decide.**

**P-2. The brand taken out of the assignment (Clauses 1.1, 2.2, 6).**
- *Question:* The marks, the name and the domain are now Excluded IP, held of record by the Founding Person for the founder pool (Pool Constitution Art. 2A). Does the consideration in Clause 4, and any valuation of the IP as a non-cash contribution, need to change now that the brand is not contributed?
- *Why it matters:* The value of the IP contribution, if valued under IKE Art. 4.4, would otherwise include assets the IKE does not receive.
- *Draft assumes:* the assignment covers the code, documentation and designs only; the accountant confirms the valuation (I-42 (f)).
- *Audit update (2026-11-23):* see I-81 (the consideration) and I-82 (remuneration for works that are not computer programs).

**I-81. The consideration for the IP Assignment (Clauses 4.1, 4.2; IKE Arts. 4.2, 4.4).**
- *Question:* A contribution in kind to an IKE must be an asset capable of valuation, valued under the AE rules unless its value in the articles does not exceed €5,000 (Law 4072/2012 Art. 77(2)); only prestations that cannot be capital, such as services, can be non-capital contributions (Law 4072/2012 Art. 78(1)) (verified). The earlier options either paid for the founder-pool shares twice (in cash under IKE Art. 4.2 and with the IP) or treated IP as a non-capital contribution under IKE Art. 4.4. The draft now offers [a price of €[●], confirmed in writing by the accountant as at arm's length (Law 4172/2013 Art. 50) / a capital contribution in kind under Law 4072/2012 Art. 77(2) for [●] Class F shares in place of cash: owner to choose]. (a) What is the tax of the assignment for the Founding Person under each option? (b) Can the tax authority reprice a nominal price as a connected-party transaction? (c) Which valuation applies under Law 4072/2012 Art. 77(2) today (the text cites C.L. 2190/1920 Arts. 9 and 9a, read as Law 4548/2018 Arts. 17 ff. [unverified])?
- *Why it matters:* An undisclosed contribution in kind escapes the statutory valuation, and a sale for €1 by the 94% economic owner may be repriced.
- *Draft assumes:* the owner chooses between the two options; if capital in kind, IKE Art. 4.2 is adjusted so the shares are not also paid in cash. Adds to P-2.

**I-82. Remuneration for assigned works that are not computer programs (Clauses 2.1, 4.3).**
- *Question:* Authors are owed appropriate and proportionate remuneration as a percentage, with a fixed sum allowed only in cases (a) to (c), and a waiver is void (only the author may invoke it); computer programs are excepted (Law 2121/1993 Art. 32(1)-(2), as replaced by Law 4996/2022 Art. 21; verified). The draft now describes the documentation and designs, where true, as preparatory design material and documentation of the programs forming part of them, and for any other assigned work agrees a fixed sum under Law 2121/1993 Art. 32(1)(c) [and/or (a)], which the parties consider appropriate and proportionate together with the shares. (a) Is preparatory design material part of the program (Law 2121/1993 Art. 2(3) [from memory, unverified])? (b) Is the stated ground enough?
- *Why it matters:* A nominal fixed sum for works outside the exception can be challenged by the author or the author's heirs.
- *Draft assumes:* as above, confirmed together with P-2 and I-81.

## articles/07-techni-kai-technologia-amke.md

The AMKE's own notes are in [agents/amke-notes.md](agents/amke-notes.md) (cited here as "AMKE note A-n"). The notes below come from the Greek-law audit of 2026-11-23.

**I-83. Members' personal liability for the AMKE's debts (Arts. 7.2, 25.1, 25.2, 30.1).**
- *Question:* GEMI applies Law 4072/2012 Art. 270 to civil companies with legal personality, so that the general-partnership chapter (Law 4072/2012 Arts. 249 to 269) applies by analogy (Ministry of Development GEMI circular 43302/31-05-2024, verified). Partners are then liable without limit, jointly and severally, alongside the company (Law 4072/2012 Art. 249(1)), and no agreement limits that towards third parties (Law 4072/2012 Art. 258) (verified). This is the prevailing view for an AMKE but disputed; the Civil Code alternative is pro-rata liability (Civil Code Art. 759 [unverified]). The draft now states the rule [jointly, severally and without limit / pro rata: to confirm] and builds the protections the law allows: a limited-recourse clause in every contract other than with consumers; no loan, guarantee or commitment above €[●] without the General Assembly; [an indemnity from nilware.io IKE for liabilities from holding the Pension Assets and the Continuity Account and from the Supervisor role, to mirror in IKE Art. 10A.9B]; insurance; and written notice to every applicant before admission. (a) Which regime applies to an AMKE? (b) Can a pro-rata or other limitation apply to a non-profit civil company? (c) Is a small partner circle, with non-partner Friends (Art. 8), needed to keep members of the public outside this liability? (d) Is the IKE indemnity lawful, and is it a contract under Law 4072/2012 Art. 95?
- *Why it matters:* Open membership would otherwise put unlimited liability for the Pension Assets and the Supervisor role on every member of the public who joins, without their knowing.
- *Draft assumes:* as above. AMKE note A-3 cites "Law 4072/2012 Art. 271"; the article is 270.

**I-84. The ring-fence towards creditors (Arts. 11.4, 13, 13.2, 13.8, 25.1; IKE Arts. 10A.9, 10A.9B).**
- *Question:* Greek law has no trust and no statutory segregation of an AMKE's earmarked accounts, so every account in the AMKE's name answers to any AMKE creditor; a donation with a charge binds only the donee (Civil Code Arts. 503 to 505). The draft now keeps the allocation between accounts internal (each debt charged to the account for which it was incurred, with reimbursement if a creditor is paid from the wrong one), retitles Art. 13 "Pension Assets Held for the Pensionskasse", and protects the separation towards third parties by limited-recourse clauses, a ban on borrowing, guarantees and security over these accounts, and [a first-ranking pledge over the Pension Asset accounts, the pension shares and the Continuity Account in favour of nilware.io IKE or a representative of the Pensionskasse members: Civil Code Arts. 1247 ff. / Law 3301/2004: to confirm]. (a) Is such a pledge valid and effective against the AMKE's other creditors, and who should hold it? (b) Is a separate AMKE holding only the Pension Assets, with no other debts (the option in I-11), the safer implementation?
- *Why it matters:* Segregation alone does not bind creditors, and the pension money is the most exposed.
- *Draft assumes:* as above. Extends I-11 and AMKE note A-4.

**I-85. Member events do not dissolve the AMKE (Arts. 5.4, 9.1, 9.5, 27.1).**
- *Question:* Which regime governs exit and dissolution of an AMKE? Through Law 4072/2012 Art. 270: a partner's death, bankruptcy or judicial assistance causes that partner's exit unless the contract provides otherwise (Law 4072/2012 Art. 260(1)); a partner may leave by declaration (Law 4072/2012 Art. 261(1)); a leaver from a company of indefinite term is paid the value of the stake (Law 4072/2012 Art. 261(2)) (verified); a single remaining partner, Law 4072/2012 Art. 267 [content unverified]. Under Civil Code Arts. 767, 773 and 775 [not re-verified], notice, death, bankruptcy or judicial assistance dissolve the company unless continuation was agreed. The draft now states continuation for every member event, says that a member who goes and the heirs have no claim to the value of a share or to the assets, as the contrary provision the law allows, and limits dissolution to the statutes' grounds or a ground the law makes mandatory. (a) Can Law 4072/2012 Art. 261(2) and the value-of-stake rules be excluded for a non-profit? (b) What period does the law allow to restore the minimum number of members?
- *Why it matters:* Dissolution would move the Prozente and the Pension Assets away, and a leaver's claim to value could reach them.
- *Draft assumes:* as above. Adds to AMKE note A-2.

**I-86. Admitting members as an amendment of the company contract (Arts. 7.2, 18.1).**
- *Question:* The company is a contract among the partners (Civil Code Art. 741), and formation and amendments are registered in GEMI after a legality check (Law 4919/2022 Arts. 18(a) and 25(1), verified via the GEMI circular). The draft now has every member consent in advance to admissions, departures, exclusions and strike-offs decided under Arts. 7 and 9, and authorise the President [and the Secretary] to sign and file the resulting amendment of the members' list and the codified statutes; a new member adheres in writing [with a certified signature: to confirm] and first receives the notice in Art. 25.2. Is advance consent with a filing mandate valid between the members, and does GEMI accept it? When must changes be filed [within 20 days / at least every quarter]?
- *Why it matters:* Without it, admissions may be ineffective between the partners or towards third parties.
- *Draft assumes:* as above. Adds to AMKE note A-2.

**I-87. The AMKE's income tax status (Arts. 1.5, 10.1, 24.1, 24B.3, 30.4).**
- *Question:* Law 4172/2013 Art. 45(ε) covers civil companies, profit or non-profit, and the purpose-income exclusion in case (γ) does not apply to them; income of Law 4172/2013 Art. 45 entities is business income (ΠΟΛ.1044/2015, verified). Member fees, donations and sponsorships may fall outside gross income (ΠΟΛ.1059/2015; AADE Ε.2008/2025 §23 [not re-verified]). (a) Are the dividends on the Prozent and pension shares, the IKE's payments for the Supervisor role, ticket, course and lab income and interest taxable business income of the AMKE, and at what rate [22%: unverified]? (b) Does VAT apply to its services? (c) Are the Prozent and Pensionsprozent contributions non-taxable, which depends on their characterisation (I-88)? (d) Is pension money taxed inside the AMKE? (e) What does registration in the register of civil-society organisations (Law 4873/2021 [unverified]) change?
- *Why it matters:* AMKE note A-9 assumes the AMKE is taxed as a non-profit legal entity; under Law 4172/2013 Art. 45(ε) every inflow other than fees and genuine donations is taxable, and the pre-share checklist asked only about donation tax.
- *Draft assumes:* before the AMKE receives shares, the accountant confirms in writing the income tax, VAT and donation tax treatment of the share donation, the contributions, the dividends, the Art. 24B.3 payments, and the Pension Assets and the Continuity Account (Art. 30.4); tax on each account's taxable income is borne by that account (Art. 10.1).

**I-88. Contributions owed, not donated (Arts. 11.2, 11.3, 13.1; IKE Arts. 4.2, 10A.9B(a); SHA 6.3 to 6.3B).**
- *Question:* A donation needs a notarial deed, except a donation of a movable completed by delivery (Civil Code Art. 498, verified indirectly), and needs liberality. Calling the Founding Person's future contributions (SHA 6.3 to 6.3B) and the IKE's yearly 1% (IKE Art. 10A) donations makes the claim to them unenforceable without a notarial deed, and an obligatory payment lacks liberality. The draft now receives the Prozent Share as a donation with a charge [by notarial deed where counsel advises], and the contributions as performance of the obligations in IKE Art. 10A and SHA 6.3 to 6.3B, which the AMKE enforces [as a party / as a third-party beneficiary, Civil Code Art. 411: to confirm], accepting the purpose restriction in its statutes with the same effect as a charge. (a) Is that characterisation sound, and does it bind the AMKE as a charge would? (b) What is its effect on donation tax and income tax (I-87)? (c) IKE Art. 10A.9B(a) still calls the Pensionsprozent payments to the AMKE a donation with a charge: should it follow? (d) Can the AMKE waive the right to refuse a charge that the gift does not cover (Civil Code Art. 504 [unverified])?
- *Why it matters:* The AMKE's right to recover the contributions (Art. 24.3A), on which the owner relies, would otherwise fail.
- *Draft assumes:* as above. Adds to I-24 and AMKE note A-9; see also I-52.

**I-89. Exclusion without a court (Arts. 9.2, 9.3, 18.7).**
- *Question:* The statutory route to excluding a partner is a court decision (Law 4072/2012 Art. 263 through Law 4072/2012 Art. 270 [title verified, wording unverified]; Civil Code Art. 771 [not re-verified]). Is exclusion by a two-thirds Assembly majority, or strike-off by the Board for unpaid fees, effective in an AMKE, or is exclusion by the court mandatory? The draft now adds every member's advance agreement to leave if excluded or struck off and to sign what registration needs, and authorises the President to apply to the court in the members' name if an exclusion is held ineffective. Are that agreement and that authorisation valid?
- *Why it matters:* An ineffective exclusion leaves the person a partner, with a vote and the liability in I-83, and exclusion enforces the independence and conflict rules (Arts. 15, 21.7).
- *Draft assumes:* as above. Adds to AMKE note A-2.

**I-90. Registration and legal personality (preamble, Arts. 1.5, 26.1).**
- *Question:* GEMI is the only competent register for civil companies formed after 04.04.2011; filing the statutes with a court does not create the company; formation takes effect on GEMI registration after a legality check (Law 4919/2022 Arts. 18(a), 25(1)); legal personality needs the Civil Code Art. 784 conditions; a model statute exists (Ministerial Decision 5186/2024, ΦΕΚ Β' 556) (GEMI circular 43302/31-05-2024, verified). This settles the registration question in AMKE note A-1, and the draft now says so. What remains: (a) fees; (b) which other registers apply; (c) do bespoke statutes, rather than the MD 5186/2024 model, pass GEMI's legality check?
- *Why it matters:* AMKEs have in practice obtained tax numbers on court-sealed statutes without GEMI registration; that does not create the company.
- *Draft assumes:* registration in GEMI under Law 4919/2022 and Civil Code Art. 784, Law 4072/2012 Art. 270 applying; every amendment and every change of members is registered. Updates AMKE note A-1.

## articles/08-founder-pool-constitution.md

**I-91. The founder pool as a civil company: duration and continuation (Arts. 2.3, 16.1, 16.1A).**
- *Question:* Holders who combine their votes for a common purpose and share its costs (Art. 2.3) are likely an internal civil company without legal personality (Civil Code Arts. 741 ff.; prevailing view, not settled), whatever Art. 16.1 calls it. By default, any partner may then terminate a company of indefinite duration at any time; termination for important reason cannot be excluded; untimely termination without important reason gives damages (Civil Code Art. 767(2)); and a partner's death dissolves the company unless continuation is agreed (Civil Code Art. 773) [the split between Civil Code Arts. 765 and 766: unverified]. The draft now adds a term [the duration of nilware.io IKE / [30] years renewed for [10]-year periods], lets a Holder leave only for important reason, and provides that no notice, death, incapacity, insolvency or exit dissolves the pool [with the leaving Holder's Class F shares carrying no weight and offered to the other Holders at fair value: owner to choose]. (a) Is the pool a civil company? (b) Are a fixed term and a continuation clause enough to stop one Holder ending it? (c) How does the important-reason termination right, which cannot be excluded, interact with the SHA undertakings and the block vote?
- *Why it matters:* One Holder's notice, or any death, could otherwise end the block vote and the pool's organs and proxies.
- *Draft assumes:* the pooling and voting agreement stays the owner's chosen form, with Art. 16.1A as above. Extends I-40 (d).

**I-92. Forced heirship after Law 5303/2026 (Art. 14.5).**
- *Question:* Law 5303/2026 (ΦΕΚ Α' 81/22.05.2026) replaces Book Five of the Civil Code; its core applies from 16 September 2026. The forced share is now a monetary claim against the estate, with a shorter limitation period, and inheritance contracts are possible and cannot be revoked unilaterally (verified through secondary sources; new article numbers, the form of inheritance contracts, the fractions and the limitation period unverified). I-40 (c) describes the earlier law. (a) How is the money claim valued for unlisted IKE shares bound by the pool, and do the pool restrictions lower that value? (b) What is the transitional rule for deaths before 16 September 2026? (c) Can an inheritance contract bind the succession to founder-pool shares, and in what form? (d) Should a sale by an estate to meet forced-share claims be exempt from the large-sale rules in Art. 9.3 [owner to decide]?
- *Why it matters:* The risk is now liquidity: an estate whose main asset is founder-pool shares may have to sell them to pay forced-share claims.
- *Draft assumes:* nothing reduces the forced share; a Holder may settle the succession by will or by inheritance contract; the other Holders may offer, pro rata, to buy from the estate at fair value set by a valuer who is not a Founder Party. Replaces I-40 (c).

## agreements/09-brand-licence.md

**I-93. Who can enforce the brand holding while the pool is dormant (Clause 8.3; Pool Constitution Arts. 2A.3, 13; filings/06).**
- *Question:* While the pool is dormant, the Founding Person is its only Holder, so holding the marks "for the Founder Pool" means holding them for himself; the EU filings come before the IKE exists; and the AMKE, whose Prozente the arrangement protects, is not a party. The draft now has the Founding Person sign, before the Trama filings, a dated declaration of fiduciary holding (mandate without representation, Civil Code Arts. 713 ff.) in favour of the Holders from time to time and of nilware.io IKE once registered, addressed to and accepted by the AMKE, and gives the AMKE a direct right to enforce the holder of record's duties as a third-party beneficiary (Clause 8.3; Civil Code Art. 411 [to confirm]). (a) Is that enough against the holder of record's heirs and creditors (I-42 (c))? (b) Is the declaration taxable (I-42 (f))?
- *Why it matters:* A fiduciary duty owed only to oneself has no one to enforce it.
- *Draft assumes:* as above. Extends I-42 (a) and (c).

**I-94. The IKE's own rights in its name and distinctive title (Brand Licence Clauses 1.1, 2.2, 5.6; IKE Art. 1.1).**
- *Question:* The right in a company's name and distinctive title arises in the company itself, by registration and use (Law 146/1914 Art. 13 [from memory, not fetched]); it cannot belong to the Founder.
