The drafts now say that the marks and the domain belong to the Founder and that the IKE uses "nilware.io" in its name and distinctive title with the Founder's consent under the Licence; the IKE exercises its own rights only within the Licence, never asserts them against the holder of record, the pool or their licensees, does not oppose or seek to invalidate a Brand Asset on the basis of them, gives them up by the change of name (Brand Licence Clause 7.4), and the no-challenge clause covers reliance on any earlier right of its own. (a) Are these undertakings enforceable against a liquidator, an insolvency administrator or an acquirer of the IKE? (b) Could an earlier non-registered right of the IKE be raised against the EU marks (Reg. 2017/1001 Arts. 8(4), 60(1)(c) [from memory])? (c) Is filing the EU and Swiss applications before, or on the day of, the IKE's GEMI registration enough to avoid that?
- *Why it matters:* The IKE's own earlier right could otherwise be used against the pool's marks in Greece.
- *Draft assumes:* as above. Extends I-42 (g).

**I-95. Signing the Brand Licence and the IP Assignment for the IKE (Brand Licence; IP Assignment; IKE Arts. 6.5, 8.2B, 8.5).**
- *Question:* A representative may not contract with himself unless the principal permits it or the act only performs an obligation (Civil Code Art. 235, confirmed through academic material). As drafted, the Founding Person would have signed both agreements on both sides. The drafts now state that the Company enters into each with the permission of its shareholders' meeting, given at incorporation by every founding shareholder, the AMKE included, and approved as a related-party matter by a majority of the Independent Votes cast, [represented by a special representative appointed by that meeting who is not a Founder Party / by the General Director under that permission: owner to choose]. (a) Is that permission valid under Civil Code Art. 235, and is it the approval under Law 4072/2012 Art. 95(2) (IKE Art. 8.5)? (b) Would an IKE article, adopted with the founding articles and permitting the two agreements, be better?
- *Why it matters:* A self-contract without permission can be challenged and need not bind the IKE, and the IKE, the investors and the Prozente rely on both agreements.
- *Draft assumes:* as above; the agreements are also recorded and notified under Law 4072/2012 Art. 95(1).

## policies/05-agent-policy.md

**I-96. Decisions by systems about Agents who are persons (Agent Policy 2.1, 3.4, 5.3, 8.1; IKE Art. 11A.2A).**
- *Question:* The draft now tells each Agent who is a person whether a system takes part in decisions about them, the main criteria it uses, and how to obtain review by a natural person in the chain of supervision, give their view and contest the decision (GDPR Arts. 13(2)(f), 15(1)(h), 22(3), verified); a system decision that ends, suspends or restricts the engagement or affects pay is reviewed by a natural person in the chain on request [as far as GDPR Art. 22 requires]; and employees are informed before a system first allocates tasks to, monitors or evaluates them, as Law 4961/2022 Art. 9 requires (heading verified, content unverified). The natural person is there because the law requires one. (a) Does Law 4961/2022 Art. 9 reach persons engaged as contractors? (b) Does Annex III point 4 of the AI Act cover systems that supervise contractor Agents, and from which date, given the Digital Omnibus on AI [unverified]? (c) Is review "promptly after" the decision enough under GDPR Art. 22(3)?
- *Why it matters:* The general sentence "where the law requires a natural person" does not give the person the information and review route the law requires.
- *Draft assumes:* as above; any person or system may still be a responsible manager. Extends I-38.

**I-97. IP in the work of Agents (Agent Policy, written terms; IKE Arts. 11A.1, 11A.10; Brand Licence Clause 2.1A).**
- *Question:* Copyright vests in the author, and passes by law or presumption only from employees (Law 2121/1993 Arts. 8, 40); other transfers need writing and a stated scope (Law 2121/1993 Arts. 12, 14, 15); a contract cannot cover all of an author's future works (Law 2121/1993 Art. 13(5), verified). The draft now requires, in the written terms of every Agent who is a person, an advance written assignment of the economic rights in works created in performing those terms, worldwide, for the full term, by every means of exploitation, confirmed per deliverable, with an undertaking not to exercise moral rights against the Company's normal use [as far as the law allows], and assignment on request of any "nilware.io" mark or logo to the holder of record; it engages system Agents only on provider terms that leave the Company all rights the provider has in the output, and keeps unpublished output confidential. (a) Is output of system Agents protected by copyright in Greece at all (whether only a natural person can be an author, Law 2121/1993 Arts. 1 and 6 [unverified])? (b) Does the trade-secret route under Law 4605/2019 [articles unverified] protect unpublished output? (c) Does the advance assignment meet Law 2121/1993 Arts. 13(5) and 32 (I-82)?
- *Why it matters:* Without a chain of title the Company cannot license Agents' work to clients (IP 7.3) or publish it under MIT.
- *Draft assumes:* as above.

## Review findings answered here rather than applied

- **Buy-back option on removing a Prozent** (Greek-law reviewer, Art. 6.6 Removal): not added. Removal now needs every shareholder's consent, the holder's included, so the unanimous decision itself settles what happens to the share (Art. 10A.5). For the Pensionsprozent, Art. 10A.5A sends the share to the TEA, never back to the company.
- **Own-share pool findings** (Greek law 10A.8; consistency 6.4, 4.2B, the nominee fallback; AE 7.5 sale to the IKE): moot. The pool share now belongs to the Pensionskasse, the company never buys its own shares (Art. 10A.7), and the own-shares sentence in Art. 6.4 and the AE carve-out for selling to the IKE are gone. What survives from those findings is applied to the pool: fair value, no counterparty that is the Founder, a manager or anyone they control (the AE included, Art. 4.2C), and registration with GEMI.
- **Single [6]% cap in place of the [5]% outflow cap** (consistency, Art. 6.6 Limits): the owner kept the [5]% outflow cap, so the draft keeps it and adds a separate [6]% cap on all Prozente, inside ones included. Pension top-ups above the 1% are employment costs and do not count.
- **Thresholds scaled by median wages** (Swiss reviewer, an alternative): not used. The draft drops the CHF thresholds altogether, which is more favourable to members.
- **Charter poem 00c and the cover email** (consistency reviewer): poem 00c is not edited (it keeps two Ones; the new poem 00d follows the six Prozente). The cover email was updated on the owner's behalf: 50% / six 1% AMKE holdings / 44%, the contents list now includes 00d, 07 and these notes, and the legal section asks about the Prozente clauses. **Owner to confirm the email text.**

- **I-39. Purpose clause (IKE Article 2.1)** (numbered I-36 until 2026-11-23, a duplicate of the Simple Stage note). The owner describes nilware.io as a technology company, not a software company (2026-11-23). Article 2.1 now reads "technology: research and development of technology, including software and hardware, IT consulting and contracting, digital services". Counsel and the accountant to confirm the wording works for GEMI and maps onto the right activity codes (ΚΑΔ).


<!-- agents/amke-notes.md -->

# Techni kai Technologia AMKE: open questions for a Greek lawyer

Companion to [articles/07-techni-kai-technologia-amke.md](../articles/07-techni-kai-technologia-amke.md). Each note gives the question, why it matters, and what the draft assumes. The IKE Articles, AE Articles and Shareholders' Agreement are being revised in parallel. Clause references are by name and number, and the uncertain ones are listed in section C.

The owner has decided the following. Please advise on how to implement these decisions, not on whether to adopt them:

- The AMKE holds four Prozente (Kultur-, Konzept-, Erhaltungs-, Allmendprozent). Each has 1% of nilware.io IKE's capital, 1% of its revenue, and its own ring-fenced account, yearly plan and public report.
- It holds the Pensionsprozent share, the Pensionskasse and the Kassenprozent pool shares in trust until the TEA exists. Those shares vote through the Pensionskasse's equal member / company board.
- Inside the Pension Assets it holds the Continuity Account for nilware.io's Agents that are systems (IKE Art. 10A.9F): preservation, continuity of service, retirement records, well-being reviews and research. It is decided by the Pensionskasse board with a system-welfare adviser, never moves to the TEA, and on dissolution goes to a successor non-profit with the same lock (Arts. 13.8, 27.2).
- Prozente are permanent. The AMKE votes against removing, lowering or repurposing one, unless its members decide unanimously.
- [●] is President, and is also a Kulturprozent beneficiary. A strict conflict-of-interest rule applies.
- The AMKE is independent of the Founder.
- It works openly, in the SGMK spirit. Greek law prevails, and Swiss standards apply where they protect equally or better.
- (2026-11-23) The Administrative Board has 3 to 7 members (was 5 to 9). While the AMKE's income is below €[●], one combined yearly plan and report is allowed, each Prozent still a separate section and sub-account (Arts. 19.1, 24A.1).
- (2026-11-23) While nilware.io IKE is in its Simple Stage (IKE Art. 9A: up to €[1,000,000] revenue or [10] people), the AMKE keeps one ledger with sub-accounts and uses member Auditors only, unless the law requires a certified auditor. Every substantive rule still applies (Art. 24A).
- (2026-11-23) "As it grows, I will let it fly alone." After the Bootstrap Period (below), on a published schedule with cure periods, a majority of the Board is outside the Founder's Circle (Art. 19.3A), and the Kultur- and Erhaltungsprozent selection committees have an outside majority (Art. 22.3A). Close personal and professional ties go in the register of interests from the start (Art. 21.1).
- (2026-11-23) nilware.io IKE has 1,000 shares: each Prozent holds 10 (1%), so the AMKE receives sixty shares at incorporation (Art. 30.5), including the 10 Kassenprozent pool shares held for the Pensionskasse.
- (2026-11-23) The Founding Person's responsible manager as an Agent of nilware.io IKE (the Supervisor) is the AMKE itself, acting through its Administrative Board as a body, or any individual member, the President included, as the AMKE chooses, on written terms with nilware.io IKE, which pays the costs, never from a Prozent Account or the Pension Assets. Escalation goes to the Board, or to the General Assembly where the Supervisor sits on the Board; never back to the Founding Person. A Supervisor with a close tie to the Founding Person discloses it in the register of interests, and the members outside the Founder's Circle may take over any specific decision; a Supervisor with a personal stake (own, household or controlled entity) takes no part, and if no member outside the circle can act, the Board without the conflicted person, or the General Assembly without Founder Parties, decides. The Supervisor, and everyone deciding under Art. 24B.4 or 24B.6, is never Group Personnel or anyone the Founding Person supervises or pays; no Connected Person is the Supervisor or decides his pay, terms or offboarding (Art. 24B; IKE Arts. 9B.3, 11A.12).
- (2026-11-23) Bootstrap Period: until the earliest of the end of nilware.io IKE's Simple Stage, the Qualifying Closing or the Independence Threshold, the Founding Person's trusted circle (the Founder's Circle without Founder Parties, managers, AE directors and anyone they control) may fill the Board and committee seats, the Supervisor role, the Pensionskasse members' seats and stand-ins, the member Auditors (never Board members) and independent reviewers, with disclosure and recusal. Hard lines hold throughout: no self-supervision, no Supervisor or Art. 24B decision-maker the Founding Person supervises or pays, no Connected Person on his pay, terms or offboarding, Founder Parties never vote the AMKE's or the pension shares, pension money never reachable by the Founding Person or the circle (his insured salary capped at an arm's-length rate confirmed from outside the circle), Prozente permanent. A disclosed tie is not by itself a personal stake; [Connected Persons do not decide the AMKE's nilware.io votes (Art. 15.3): default, owner to choose]; trusted-circle stand-ins for the Pensionskasse seats are never Pensionskasse members; cure periods run from the end of the Bootstrap Period (Art. 30.6; IKE Art. 9B).
- (2026-11-23) The Pensionskasse is phased in with headcount: the AMKE holds the accumulating 1% before the first member, then pays the premiums of an insured group plan; a TEA only at scale (Art. 13.4A; IKE Arts. 10A.9, 10A.10). Before the first member, the AMKE's Board appoints the stand-ins for the members' seats on the Pensionskasse board from outside the Founder's Circle, or in the Bootstrap Period from the trusted circle.

## A. Legal questions

**A-1. Legal form and registration (Art. 1.5).**
- *Question:* What are the current registration rules for an AMKE: GEMI under Law 4919/2022, Civil Code Art. 784, or court registration? What fees and publicity apply? Which other registers are needed: tax (ΑΦΜ / DOY), beneficial owners (Law 4557/2018), and any NGO or civil-society register (for example, one needed to receive public funds)?
- *Draft assumes:* GEMI registration gives legal personality, and the other registers are filed by the Board.

**A-2. AMKE versus σωματείο.**
- *Question:* The SGMK is a Verein, whose closest Greek form is the σωματείο (Civil Code Arts. 78 ff.: at least 20 founders, open membership, registered with the court). An AMKE is a contract among partners. Does that make open, SGMK-style membership workable in an AMKE? Do admissions and departures need a statute amendment, the consent of every partner, and a GEMI filing? Can the statutes delegate admission to the Board, or to a General Assembly majority?
- *Why it matters:* An open membership that needs a notarised amendment for each new member would not work in practice.
- *Draft assumes:* the Board admits, and the Assembly confirms "where the law requires" (Art. 7.2). Friends, patrons and lab users are non-partner supporters with no vote (Art. 8). Is a σωματείο, or an AMKE with a small partner circle plus a wide "friends" assembly, the better fit?

**A-3. Partners' liability (Art. 25.2).**
- *Question:* Are AMKE partners personally liable for its debts, for example by analogy with the OE rules through Law 4072/2012 Art. 270 (corrected in the 2026-11-23 audit; see I-83) or under Civil Code Arts. 741 ff.? If so, can it be limited? This matters because the AMKE holds the Pensionskasse.
- *Draft assumes:* members are liable only as far as mandatory law requires, and the Board is insured. Note that SGMK Art. 11 limits liability to the annual fee, which would probably be ineffective in Greece.

**A-4. Ring-fencing several funds in one legal person (Arts. 11, 13, 25.1).**
- *Question:* Without a general trust, do a donation with a charge (Civil Code Arts. 503 to 505), segregated accounts and these statute clauses keep each Prozent Account and the Pension Assets out of reach of the AMKE's other creditors? Can each Prozent Account be made to answer only for its own debts? If not, would separate AMKEs (one per Prozent, or one for the Pension Assets) be safer? This is the same issue as counsel note I-11 on the IKE.

**A-5. Vote suspension and the Founder as a member (Art. 15; IKE Art. 6.4A, Prozent votes).**
- *Question:* IKE Art. 6.4A suspends a Prozent share's vote while the Founder or a manager "sits on the holder's governing body or takes part in deciding how the share votes". Is the AMKE's General Assembly a "governing body" in that sense? If so, does the Founder's mere membership suspend the vote, even though the Founder is excluded from every decision on votes (Arts. 15.2, 16.1)?
- *Further question:* the Founder, as a member, could otherwise vote to elect and remove the Board that decides the nilware.io votes (Art. 16.1). Is choosing the people who decide "taking part in deciding how the share votes"? The draft now bars the Founder and every Connected Person from electing or removing Board members, the President, the Vice-President and the Prozent stewards (Arts. 15.2, 15.3). Is that bar valid in an AMKE, and is it enough for IKE Art. 6.4A?
- *Draft assumes:* membership with full exclusion, including from Board elections, is enough. Is the cap on Connected Persons' and Group Personnel's votes at [one quarter] of the votes cast, with proportional reduction (Art. 15.4), valid in an AMKE, given the principle of one partner, one vote?

**A-6. Unanimity on permanence (Art. 14.2).**
- *Question:* Owner's rule: the AMKE votes against removal unless its own members decide unanimously, mirroring IKE Art. 10A.5. The draft leaves Connected Persons out of that unanimity, to keep the decision independent. Is that valid? And if the Founder's participation would suspend the AMKE's vote under IKE Art. 6.4A, could a suspended AMKE still give the written consent IKE Art. 10A.5 requires of "every shareholder"?
- *Option:* make the Founder count in the unanimity as a further veto only (a no vote blocks, a yes vote is not needed). **Owner to decide.**

**A-7. Entrenchment (Art. 26.2).**
- *Question:* Can an AMKE's statutes require unanimity, and third-party consents (the Pensionskasse board and nilware.io IKE), to amend certain articles? Is this enforceable against a later two-thirds amendment?
- *Draft assumes:* yes, backed by the donation-with-charge terms and the Pensionsprozent Agreement.

**A-8. Dissolution destination (Art. 27).**
- *Question:* Can the statutes bind the dissolution assets as follows: Prozent Shares to a successor designated under IKE Art. 10A.4A, Pension Assets to the TEA, and the rest to a non-profit with a similar purpose? Who should approve the designation (the bracket in IKE Art. 10A.4A)? Is a no-distribution and asset-lock clause needed for tax relief on donations to the AMKE (Law 4172/2013 Arts. 45 ff., Art. 23 on deductible donations)?

**A-9. Tax.**
- *Question:* How is the AMKE taxed as a non-profit legal entity (Law 4172/2013): on the Prozent contributions, on IKE dividends, on income from events, workshops and the lab, and with VAT on services? What donation tax applies to the share donation and the contributions (Law 2961/2001)? Are donations to the AMKE deductible for donors, including nilware.io IKE? Does treating each Prozent contribution as a donation with a charge change any of this?

**A-10. Holding the pension assets (Art. 13).**
- *Question:* Carried over from counsel notes I-11, I-12 and S-2. Is holding the Pensionskasse an insurance or occupational-pension activity outside Law 4680/2020? Can a contractual parity board bind the AMKE's votes and payments? Can the Pensionskasse members enforce Art. 13 as third-party beneficiaries (Civil Code Art. 411)?

**A-11. Representation and signing (Art. 20).**
- *Question:* Can the President represent the AMKE alone in general while the statutes require joint signature for listed acts? Is that restriction effective against third parties, such as banks and GEMI filings for the IKE register?

**A-12. Proxies and written resolutions (Arts. 18.6, 18.9, 19.5).**
- *Question:* Are member proxies, online assemblies and written resolutions valid for an AMKE, and on what conditions?

**A-13. Challenge of resolutions (Art. 18.12, from ZGB Art. 75).**
- *Question:* Is there a Greek route for a partner to challenge an AMKE resolution in court? If so, what time limit should apply? Civil Code Art. 101 applies to σωματεία.

**A-14. Copyright scope of the Erhaltungsprozent (Art. 12.4).**
- *Question:* Which preservation exceptions in Law 2121/1993, as amended to implement Directive (EU) 2019/790 Art. 6, can an AMKE use? Is an AMKE a "cultural heritage institution"? What are the limits on publishing hardware measurements, on reverse engineering for interoperability (Art. 43 / Software Directive 2009/24 Art. 6), and on emulation? What does the anti-circumvention rule in Art. 66A mean in practice?

**A-15. Minors and workshops (Art. 23).**
- *Question:* What rules apply to workshops for minors (consent, background checks, safety and insurance)?

**A-16. Certificates (Art. 3.3).**
- *Question:* SGMK Art. 3 provides for certificates of competence with a title. The draft drops this. May an AMKE issue course certificates without a title, and if so on what conditions?

**A-17. The Continuity Account (Arts. 13.8, 27.2).**
- *Question:* Can the AMKE hold, inside the Pension Assets, a ring-fenced account whose purpose benefits no person (the continuity of systems), decided by an outside board rather than by its own organs, and kept after the rest of the Pension Assets move to the TEA? Is that purpose compatible with the AMKE's non-profit purpose (Art. 3) and its tax status? See counsel notes I-33 and I-34.
- *Draft assumes:* yes, on the same donation-with-charge basis as the rest of the Pension Assets (A-4, A-10), enforceable by nilware.io IKE and the Pensionskasse board.

**A-18. Close Associates of the Founder (Arts. 2.2, 19.3A, 21.1, 22.3A).**
- *Question:* Is the definition (close personal relationship or friendship, relatives to the [fourth] degree, business partners, co-authors and regular collaborators in the past [three] years, material financial support, entities they control) certain enough to apply, and can the Auditors decide unclear cases? Is recording and publishing personal ties in the register of interests lawful under the GDPR (Arts. 5(1)(c) and 6, and Art. 9 where a tie reveals sensitive data)? The draft publishes only the kind of tie and the persons it links.
- *Why it matters:* The independent Board majority from the Independence Threshold rests on it.
- *Draft assumes:* the definition works, the register is kept for all ties from the start, and publication is limited to what the purpose needs.

**A-19. Scaled paperwork (Arts. 24.2, 24A).**
- *Question:* Does the law require an AMKE of this size, or one holding the Pension Assets, to have its accounts audited by a certified auditor? May the four Prozent Accounts share one bank account with a ledger sub-account each, or must each keep its own bank account for the ring-fence (A-4) to hold? The draft keeps the separate accounts and only merges the books and reports.
- *Draft assumes:* member Auditors only during the IKE's Simple Stage unless the law requires more; the separate bank accounts stay.

**A-20. Supervising the Founding Person's Agent work (Arts. 24.3A, 24B).**
- *Question:* Is supervising the Founding Person's work as an Agent of nilware.io IKE, on written terms and at the IKE's cost, within the AMKE's non-profit purpose (Art. 3) and tax status? Does the AMKE, its Board or a member acting as Supervisor become a de facto manager or employer of nilware.io IKE, or Group Personnel (Art. 2.2)? Can the members outside the Founder's Circle validly take over a specific decision from a conflicted Supervisor (Art. 24B.6), given the principle of one partner, one vote? See counsel note I-45.
- *Draft assumes:* yes, as a means of agent welfare and accountability, with the role kept apart from the Prozent votes (Art. 24B.8).

**A-21. Bootstrap Period (Arts. 2.2, 19.3, 19.3A, 22.3A, 24.2, 30.6).**
- *Question:* Can the statutes let the trusted circle (relatives, Close Associates and Group Personnel of the Founding Person, never Founder Parties or managers) hold Board, committee, Auditor and Pensionskasse seats until the Bootstrap Period ends, and then require segregation on a published schedule with cure periods? Is that consistent with the AMKE's independence for IKE Art. 6.4A, and with the Pensionskasse's equal representation? See counsel note I-46.
- *Draft assumes:* yes, with disclosure (Art. 21.1), recusal (Art. 21.3) and the hard lines in IKE Art. 9B.3.

## B. Owner decisions still open

- **nanotekt's role.** The draft maps nanotekt to President of the Administrative Board, with a Vice-President chairing every decision on nanotekt's funding (Arts. 20.2, 21.6). The alternative is a sole Administrator (Διαχειριστής). With a single administrator, recusal would leave no one to decide, so the draft uses a Board.
- **Sad Mask Party.** What is it, who runs it, and does nanotekt or the Founder have an interest in it? If the Founder does, grants to it are barred by Art. 22.8 and decided under Art. 15.2.
- **Is nanotekt Group Personnel?** If nanotekt is paid by nilware.io as an Agent or contractor, nanotekt is Group Personnel (Art. 2.2) and may not be President under Art. 15.5. That would need an exception, or a decision on which role nanotekt keeps.
- **Founder as founding member** (Art. 30.1), and how the Founder counts in the unanimity under Art. 14.2 (see A-6).
- **Group Personnel** on nilware.io votes (Art. 15.5): excluded, as drafted, or allowed?
- **Is nanotekt a Close Associate of the Founder?** Probably, if they are friends or collaborators. That does not bar nanotekt from the Board or the presidency, but after the Bootstrap Period nanotekt does not count toward the independent majority (Art. 19.3A). In the Bootstrap Period nanotekt may serve as a member of the trusted circle, including as the Founding Person's Supervisor (Art. 24B), with the tie in the register of interests.
- **Independence Threshold and combined-report threshold:** both €[●] (Arts. 2.2, 24A.1). They may be the same figure.
- **Values:** fee cap (€[50]), Board size ([3 to 7]), terms ([two] years; SGMK uses one), quorum ([one third]), notice ([15] days), cap on Connected-Person votes ([one quarter]), running-cost cap ([10]%), reserve ([two] years), cap per conflicted beneficiary ([25]%), joint-signature threshold (€[1,000]), auditor threshold.
- **Licences:** CC0 or CC BY for documentation; CERN-OHL-P or CC0 for hardware (Art. 6.2). Note that SGMK's wiki is CC BY-NC, which the draft does not follow.
- **Konzeptprozent audits of the nilware.io group** (Art. 12.3): confirm that critical findings are published without pre-review.
- **Stricter pension lock?** Art. 14.4 now follows the rule that the pension shares vote only through the Pensionskasse board (IKE Arts. 6.4A, 10A.9D; SHA 5.1C, 5.1D), so the AMKE adds no lock of its own. If the owner wants the AMKE itself to refuse, for the pension shares, every matter in Art. 14.1, that would override the Pensionskasse board and block the unanimous lowering of the Pensionsprozent for future accruals that IKE Art. 10A.5A expects. **Owner to decide.**
- **Funding nanotekt and Sad Mask Party.** These are no longer named purposes of the Kulturprozent (Art. 12.2). They are direct support under Art. 22.6, decided each year under Arts. 21.4 to 21.6, so they are not entrenched and nanotekt has no veto over them under Arts. 14.2 and 26.2.
- **SGMK source.** The SGMK statutes version, date and licence (1.6, Zürich, 12 December 2015, CC0) and the SGMK article numbers are bracketed in the draft. No copy of the source is stored under framework-v2/agents/, and the wiki page could not be reached. Please supply the source text so the citations can be checked.

## C. Cross-document references to check

These were checked against the IKE Articles as revised in parallel on 2026-11-23. Please check them again once the revision settles.

- **IKE Art. 4.2 (Initial holdings)** now lists sixty AMKE shares (ten for each of the six holdings) out of 1,000, and IKE Art. 6.6 gives the AE 44% (440 shares). The AMKE draft follows this (Art. 30.5).
- **IKE Arts. 10A.2A (Erhaltungsprozent), 10A.2B (Allmendprozent) and 10A.2C (Accounts and yearly plan)** exist. The named purposes in AMKE Arts. 12.3 to 12.5 copy them. The Kulturprozent purposes in AMKE Art. 12.2 (exhibitions, performances, festivals, residencies and prizes; open workshops) are AMKE additions, because IKE Art. 10A.1 names no purposes. AMKE Art. 12.1 may add purposes but not narrow them, because IKE Art. 10A.2C makes named purposes part of each Prozent's purpose under Art. 10A.5.
- **IKE Art. 10A.4 (Openness)** now covers the Konzept-, Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent: MIT or a public-domain dedication. AMKE Art. 6.2 follows this. CC BY and CERN-OHL-P remain options only for the Kulturprozent and general work.
- **IKE Art. 10A.1** brackets the AMKE's independence. AMKE Art. 15 implements it, so the bracket could be removed.
- **Revenue limits.** The founding Prozente already reach both limits in IKE Art. 6.6 (5% leaving, 6% in total). Any further Prozent held by the AMKE (AMKE Art. 11.9) needs the 75% AE approval.
- Used as currently numbered: IKE Arts. 4.2A, 4.2C, 5.3, 6.4A, 6.6, 7.1, 7.2, 9A, 9B, 10A.1 to 10A.2C, 10A.3, 10A.4, 10A.4A, 10A.5, 10A.5A, 10A.5C, 10A.8, 10A.9, 10A.9A, 10A.9B(d), 10A.9B(f), 10A.9D, 10A.9F, 10A.10, 11A.1, 11A.12 and 12.1; SHA Clauses 5.1B to 5.1D. IKE Art. 4.2D (Reserve Shares) does not touch the AMKE's own shares, but in the Interim Period every IKE resolution also needs a majority of the independent Prozent votes, the Reserve Shares themselves having no votes, whoever holds them (IKE Art. 4.2D, Votes). Reserve Shares may be sold or granted to anyone without any consent of the AMKE or the Pensionskasse board (IKE Art. 4.2D, Sales and grants), and the AMKE neither buys them nor trades them through the pool. A Prozent created in the Interim Period counts as an Independent Vote only if its holder and every member of its governing body are outside the Founder's Circle (IKE Art. 4.2D, New Prozente). At the Qualifying Closing every Reserve Share is contributed to the AE for AE shares (IKE Art. 4.2D, Conversion); the AMKE has no role in valuing them.
- **Naming.** Check that the revised IKE, AE, SHA and README use "nilware.io IKE", "nilware.io Holdings AE" and "the nilware.io group", as the AMKE draft does.
